# Portable consistency — implementation plan

> **Planning baseline — 2026-09-11.** Implements the reviewed [portable consistency proposal](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) at `f0cba2488`. This is the fresh plan following the restart; no implementation slice is claimed complete. The proposal owns the product contract and acceptance matrices. This plan owns execution order, prerequisites, review checkpoints and the next bounded task.

**Scope.** The checker validates the **rules and computations** of a DocumentModel, conforming to the Kernel for A12 models — including the type-compatibility laws operators impose on their operands. It must support the whole model structure as context, resolving references against it and reading its type facts, but it does not validate whether a group, field or type declaration is itself legal: that is whole-model consistency and a separate product. The only exclusion is a Kernel law that cannot fire for an A12 model. No slice, stage or record below widens this; the authority is the [proposal's scope](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#4-required-functionality-and-scope-boundaries).

**NFR #1 — the interpreter's performance is never compromised.** The proposal's highest-ranked non-functional requirement outranks every checker goal, including the checker's own H5 budgets: no comparable interpreter row regresses by more than 5% against the restart baseline, per row with no compensation between rows, and missing or inconclusive evidence fails rather than passes. Ordinary evaluation takes on no checker dependency, analysis preparation or retained source graph. Where a stage below could trade interpreter cost for checker convenience, the interpreter wins by default and the trade is escalated, not made in passing. Obligation and evidence rules: [H4](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#hard-acceptance-conditions-confirmed-by-the-owner).

The fixed interpreter comparison baseline is `c8e3b99795fe9c2ce3e9db32632a6dd8f6537043`. The abandoned attempt at `6b4c6838737b5db7ad264ba7e207446b3cfcb27d` is read-only source/evidence material. Recover individual original-code changes only after identifying their owner and retained tests; do not restore its module graph, preview API, migration harness or performance qualification.

## 1. Working boundaries

- Build a standalone KMP checker with its own admission, syntax, binding and immutable analysis state. The default is a port of our original infrastructure. A neutral shared leaf is optional and starts with no permitted types until A establishes its bounded allowlist.
- Share selected model facts and measured constraints between designated Java façades and the checker. Preserve existing Java public contracts and deliberate authoring policies. Designate consumers explicitly; do not claim repository-wide consolidation.
- Keep interpreter execution preparation independent. Check both products' production closures and shipped-file ownership, including copied sources, declarations and generated output. Build-tool reuse does not transfer ownership of shipped API files.
- Preserve default Kernel checking in JVM/native dmtool. Portable checking is an explicitly selected consumer of the published checker; it never substitutes automatically for Kernel or vice versa.

**This work is a MIGRATION, and the boundary above is about contracts rather than implementations (recorded 2026-09-12, after it was read the other way for six slices).** It is **swift and feature-scoped**, which is the half that is just as easy to get wrong in the other direction: port exactly what the demanded feature needs and no more — `selectively` and `relevant` are load-bearing, and relevance is decided by what Kernel-free checking on TS/browser JS/Node/JVM demands (admission, preparation, binding, syntax, the checks, the codes), never by language tidiness. An estate-wide conversion programme is as far outside this scope as refusing to port at all; modelkit's authoring surface, `edit`, `schemakit`, the CLI and the Kernel-bound adapter are not in it. The proposal states the method in its own words — *"Selectively port the relevant dmkits foundations into Kotlin Multiplatform"* — and its delivery targets are *"TypeScript, browser JavaScript, Node.js **and Java/Kotlin**"*, so **Java is a CONSUMER of the KMP checker, not a parallel implementation of it**: `:consistency` builds a `jvm` target beside `js`, and a JVM consumer calls the ported Kotlin directly. NFR #1 makes the framing explicit where it bounds regressions *"during the migration"* — it is a fence protecting the already-Kotlin interpreter while the port proceeds, never an argument against porting.

**So "preserve existing Java public contracts" means the CONTRACT a consumer sees, which a façade over ported Kotlin satisfies and the KMP `jvm` target satisfies outright.** Reading it as "preserve the Java implementations" converts a ported family into a permanent duplicate. **How much of that has actually happened is measured, not assumed, and it is a minority:** of nine landed `CheckFamily` laws sampled against Java main source, **six have no Java counterpart at all** (`MVK_ERROR_CODE_MISSING`, `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT`, `MVK_INVALID_INDEX_FIELD`, `MVK_INVALID_SORT_FIELD`, `MVK_ERROR_WRONG_ROUNDING_MODE`, `MVK_SUPP_LANGUAGES_MISSING` — net-new capability), and **three are enforced by the typed builders too** (`MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING`, `MVK_RULE_NAME_INVALID`, `MVK_ERROR_EMPTY_PRECONDITION`, in `Rule.java`/`Computation.java`). So the duplication is real but bounded, and its denominator is the MVK ledger's **31 `pre-checked` rows** — laws a Java builder enforces that this checker may re-implement — not the family count. C1's extraction is the plan's mechanism for collapsing each one. *(Corrected 2026-09-12: the first version of this paragraph asserted all nine had live Java originals, which was written without measuring and is wrong in the self-critical direction — the same unverified-claim failure it was documenting.)*

**Corrected and CLOSED 2026-09-13 — the sample above under-counted, and the full sweep changes the conclusion.** The paragraph's "three" came from sampling nine families; intersecting every `MVK_*` token in the five kits' main sources with the checker's `commonMain` returns **twelve** codes. But only one of the twelve was ever collapsible, and the sweep's value is in why the other eleven are not. Six name the code only in a javadoc, a `StructuralKernelFix` hint or a catalog gotcha — explaining A12's verdict, never refusing anything. Three state A12's law on both sides over carriers with no shared residue: a builder reads its own typed objects where the checker reads stored DM-JSON, so `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING`'s builder half is "the typed list is non-empty" while the checker models what A12 actually collects (declared locales only, later entry wins, blank text counting as none). **And one is a deliberate divergence that must never be collapsed:** `LocalizedText.checkDeclared` THROWS on a duplicate locale, A12 admits it and silently keeps one text, so the checker reports it advisory-only — sharing that predicate would install an over-rejection, the one direction this project forbids. That leaves `MVK_ERROR_EMPTY_PRECONDITION`, whose shared residue after each side's carrier reading is `count >= 2`; extracting that would be cargo-culting the leaf's mechanism rather than using it, so it stays restated. **The reusable conclusion: "stated on both sides" is not the same question as "should be shared", and the sweep's real product is a per-code disposition rather than a collapse count.** `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` now holds every one of the twelve to a recorded disposition and fails the build when a thirteenth appears.
- Start each port with the existing implementation and test owner. Translate our own code and reuse expectations. Add a test only for a named uncovered requirement, translation defect or platform difference; use red/green TDD for new behavior and fixes.
- Complete one finite slice before opening another. Freeze its families, fixtures, oracles and exclusions before implementation. An expanding acceptance denominator triggers the repository's convergence procedure, not another patch round.

Use the proposal's [scope boundaries](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#4-required-functionality-and-scope-boundaries) and [completion criteria](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#10-completion-criteria) when partitioning work; sequencing does not change either contract.

## 2. Execution order and landing boundaries

Rows below are dependency stages, not permission to combine every named slice into one commit. Each independently meaningful, verified feature lands with its documentation. B1–B3 and C1–C2 retain the proposal's separate acceptance scopes even where their delivery is interleaved with B or C. The detailed case denominator remains in [proposal §7](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) and [§12](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#12-project-synergies-additional-features-and-focused-cleanups).

**The Status column is this plan's state carrier** — the one place to look for what remains, updated in the same commit as each landing. A row is **done** only when its exit evidence has actually run; **part** names what is still outstanding rather than rounding up, because a stage marked done on a partial landing is how the remainder stops being scheduled. The §3 record below each landed stage carries what it decided and measured; this column carries only where the work has reached.

**It carries that in two tiers since 2026-09-16, because one of them had stopped being readable.** The cell holds the token and the single clause that decides whether the stage is schedulable; everything further sits in [Where each stage has reached](#stage-detail) below the table, one subsection per stage that needs more than a clause. The split is not cosmetic — this column grows with every landing while the other four are the frozen plan, so it had reached 4 700 characters on stage 7 against four characters on the stages nobody has started, and a reader picking work had to scan a paragraph to learn a stage was blocked. Both tiers are still the state carrier: update the cell's clause and its subsection in the same commit, and keep the clause honest on its own, since that is the only part most readers will see.

### Fastest path to completion — the critical path, and the three things that shorten it (2026-09-16) <a id="fastest-path"></a>

**The remaining stages are not a queue, and treating them as one is what makes the plan look long.** Read off the slice dependencies the [proposal](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) states: `G` needs only F's published contract (shipped with 5a), `H` needs immutable preparation and complete static results (shipped), `I` needs H, `J` needs shared binding and E's dependency relationships, `L` needs J, and `N` needs everything. So the genuine critical path is **H → J → L → N** with `I` hanging off H and `G`/`F1-c`/`F3` entirely parallel to all of it — four sequential landings, not twelve.

Three levers cut it further, and each is a scheduling decision rather than a scope cut:

| lever | why it works | what it saves |
|---|---|---|
| **Land F1-b and H as ONE slice** | they are the same mechanism at two surfaces — "check a candidate without persisting it" — and the hard part is neither surface but the attribution the [F1-b record](#f1b-attribution) measures. Implement the differential once beneath both, then two thin consumers: `rule check --engine portable` and H's library entry point | one semantic slice instead of two, and one acceptance matrix instead of two overlapping ones |
| **Pay the native recapture exactly ONCE, last** | `F1-d` and `N` both require the mandatory recapture → build → gate sequence, and it is the single most expensive step in the estate (~36–38 min, and it cannot be shortened because its coverage tracks the test suite). It is already owed: widening the capture stamp's digest on 2026-09-16 invalidated the committed stamp by construction | one capture instead of two or three. **The corollary is a rule: do not run it until the last main-source slice has landed**, because any later source change stales it again. *Paid 2026-09-16, and the lever's PREMISE has changed with it. The capture was an indivisible 36–38-minute process that no agent-harness mechanism can hold, which is what made paying it twice so expensive and what killed it three times; it is now sliceable and resumable ([NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM](NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM.md#capture-slices) §5.2), so a second one costs machine time but can no longer fail. It was run with `L-b` still open because `L-b` is blocked rather than queued, so "wait for the last main-source slice" had no date attached; the rule stands for a slice that is actually schedulable* |
| **Stop treating row 5 as open work** | its seam is drained rather than empty — every one of its 15 remaining candidates carries a named blocker, and reaching any of them needs the METAMODEL axis nothing in this estate touches. The row's own detail already says capacity comes from rows 7–13 | removes the plan's one open-ended item from scheduling. Left marked `doing`, it invites exactly the wasted slice row 6's stale cell nearly caused |

**Two stages cannot be pulled earlier, so do not try.** Row 8's remaining perf bars are `L` (after-edit) and `N` (combined), which the owner's [H5 ratification](#h5-ratification) deliberately left UNSET — they are measurable only once those slices exist, so row 8 closes as a by-product of rows 12 and 13 rather than as work of its own. *(Closed 2026-09-16. This read: "row 7's remainder is contract-blocked on F2's own accumulation contract … not unblocked by effort elsewhere." Both clauses were wrong, in the two ways a blocker usually is. It was stated at the granularity of a whole DIRECTION, which hid a schedulable half — comparing where A12 keyed a declaration needs no account of its stopping. And the remaining half rested on an unmeasured PREMISE: A12's stop was assumed to hide sibling declarations, and over 23 independent pairs it never did. The generalizable lesson is that a contract-blocked item should be split and its premise measured before its size is believed; neither costs much, and here they cost a row that had been parked.)*

**Resulting order, six landings:** F1-b + H → (G + F1-c + F3, parallel) → J → I → L → one recapture + F1-d + N, which closes row 8 with it. *Reached 2026-09-16: F1-b + H, then G + F1-c together, then J, then I-a and I-b — which closes row 11 — then all three F3 partitions, then the recapture and F1-d with it. The order's own tail is what changed: it assumed the recapture would be the last thing paid, and it was instead paid while `L-b` is blocked, because a gate with no date is not a gate. **What remains is `L-b`, `N`, and the three acceptance clauses the coverage registry owns — and NONE of them is schedulable by an implementing session.** *(Corrected 2026-09-16, hours after the line above first claimed `N` was: `N`'s own acceptance row reads "all preceding required slices are implemented", and a blocked predecessor is inherited rather than stood beside. The mistake was reading the plan's row, which lists `N`'s inputs, instead of the proposal's, which states its precondition.)* **`F1-e` landed later that same day** on explicit owner go-ahead ([the record](#f1e-wording)), and that **does not** make `N` schedulable: `L-b` also precedes `N` and is still blocked, so `N`'s precondition now fails on `L-b` where it used to fail on `F1-e`. Clearing one conjunct schedules nothing — which is the same reading error as the correction above, one blocker later.

### Where each stage has reached <a id="stage-detail"></a>

The table above carries each stage's token and the clause that decides whether it is schedulable; the rest of what a stage has reached is here, one subsection per stage that needs more than a clause. The split exists because the token is what a session reads to pick work, and it stopped being readable once five stages carried a paragraph each — the four stages still marked `open` say everything they need to in the table and get no subsection.

#### Stage 4 — B/B3 preparation and C2 diagnostic delivery <a id="stage-4"></a>

**done** — B3's closure, rehosting and parse-once landed; C2's `StructuralKernelFix`/`RkCode` delivery landed 2026-09-13 as a generated projection on the report's `corrective` member (see its record). The CATALOG half is deliberately not delivered: no finding names an operator id, so its 293 KB would be packaged with nothing to join it against — re-open when a finding carries one. **Both include levels and both mount exclusions landed 2026-09-15** ([the level](#model-root-include), [the exclusions](#mount-exclusions)), so the frozen source/fact set covers includes, imported typedefs, repeated mounts and the `attachment` shield, and **all twenty comparable cases of the shared matrix now agree with the Kernel's own expansion** — which is this row's Java-query reconciliation, since that matrix is what `WorkspacePreparationParityTest` holds equal between the real `expand` and the JVM reader. Parse-once is asserted by identity (`PreparationReuseTest`) and A2's ownership decision is delivered. The provider roots A12 refuses now suspend by name too ([that record](#unmountable-root)), including the two that were mounting silently. The terminal review this row's checkpoint names has run and every finding is settled against its own oracle ([the record](#mount-review)) — including a live over-report on ten of those cases that the reconciliation could not see, and three questions the matrix cannot answer, which went to the Kernel rather than to judgement

#### Stage 5 — C/C1 local semantic families <a id="stage-5"></a>

**part, and nothing in it is schedulable** — every remaining candidate is blocked on the metamodel axis, which is the row's own cell above. 103 families live (`CheckFamily` in [`Check.kt`](../consistency/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/consistency/Check.kt) is the denominator and each constant carries its own scope; counted 2026-09-15, so re-derive rather than cite this). The report's coverage gaps are five, and four of those are standing rather than schedulable: per-operator argument arity (deliberately not reproduced), a `SumOfProducts` shape with no oracle, the NUMBER half of a computation's self-read, whose pre-empting scale gate needs derived-scale inference (the rest of that law landed 2026-09-15 as `COMPUTATION_SELF_READ` — see [its record](#computation-self-read)), and the families not yet implemented; C1's extraction landed 2026-09-13 as the `:predicates` leaf (see the 5a record); D's stored-member denominator is enumerated and DRAINED — and so, as of 2026-09-15, is A12's unprefixed `FehlertextErrorType` estate: 28 of 50 reported and all 22 others classified with a cited reason (see its C record), so the error-text vocabulary is no longer where the open capacity is — all seven formerly unread members closed (see its record). **Where the capacity IS, re-derived over all 306 ledger rows 2026-09-15** (see [the containment record](#error-target-containment) for the method, its arithmetic and the caveat that makes it an upper bound): 15 open candidate rows once the measured-unreachable are removed (re-derived 2026-09-15 after the metamodel custom-condition, hint-list and table-enum blocks closed — see [those](#metamodel-custom-conditions) [three](#hint-list-values) [records](#table-enum-operand), and the calculation block's before them; rows are re-OPENED as well as closed, so the number moves both ways. The arithmetic: of the 306 rows, 99 name a code the checker does not state, 76 are excluded by their disposition CELL and 8 by row PROSE — recomputed from the live checker sources and the whole ledger by `LedgerResidueFigureGuardTest`, which fails this row when it drifts, so the "re-derive" instruction above is now a build gate rather than a habit), the largest blocks being `erased` and `kernel-delegated` rather than the `n/a-structural` bucket an earlier pass named — re-derive rather than cite that split, since each landing moves it. **The residue is DRAINED as a work source as of 2026-09-15, which is not the same as empty** (see [the sweep record](#legacy-only-sweep)): every one of the 15 carries a named blocker rather than pending work — seven declined by design or unmeasured after a stated attempt, six dormant in the JAVA estate where [KF418](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf418) makes a zero raiser count no evidence of unreachability, and two metamodel-tier codes whose carriers are measured unreachable. Reaching any of them needs the METAMODEL axis, which no source read or stored-model probe in this estate touches; that is the re-open trigger. Capacity for the next slice therefore comes from plan rows 7–13, not from this column

#### Stage 6 — E relational families <a id="stage-6"></a>

**part** — twelve relational families landed 2026-09-14: `REPETITION_KEY_PLACEMENT` ([KF347](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf347)), `ERROR_TARGET_ITERATION` ([KF348](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf348)), `REPETITION_SCOPE_GROUP` ([KF349](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf349)), `FILTER_WILDCARD` ([KF97](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf97)), `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY` ([KF351](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf351)) and `REPETITION_NOT_UNIQUE_COMBINATION` ([KF352](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf352)) and `PARALLEL_ITERATION_UNIQUENESS` ([KF353](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf353)) and `H_MODEL_PATH_SUFFIX` ([KF356](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf356)) and `FIELD_OPERAND_KIND` ([KF357](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf357)) and `FILTERED_OPERAND_KIND` ([KF358](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf358)) and `CATEGORY_REFERENCE` ([KF359](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf359)) and `ENUM_CONSTANT_DOMAIN` ([KF360](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf360), since renamed `SCALAR_VALUE_LIST_GATES`), each silent on the shapes another class pre-empts — except `REPETITION_NOT_UNIQUE_COMBINATION` and `H_MODEL_PATH_SUFFIX`, which report a true second defect where A12 stops at an earlier gate. **What the remaining candidates are blocked ON is now known and is not evidence** — re-derive with the residue enumeration rather than citing this cell: **the clause blocker is LIFTED** (`CallOperand` carries a clause-reduced `base` and `clauses`, so both the `@From` scope and the `Having` filter are readable and each has its first family), and the `SumOfProducts` pair-laws — recorded here as deferred for want of star-RELATIONAL analysis — **landed afterwards as `SUM_OF_PRODUCTS_OPERANDS`** with all four classes and the relational level count (`repeatableLevelsBetween`, which counts from the DECLARING group rather than the model root); its own record carries the chain, and this cell simply went unrefreshed. **Corrected 2026-09-15, after the stale cell nearly sent a session to build it again** — the Status column is the state carrier, so a landing that leaves an earlier row's prose describing the old world costs the next reader a slice

#### Stage 7 — F2 durable differential <a id="stage-7"></a>

**done** — **the missing direction was schedulable all along, and landed 2026-09-16.** It had been recorded as one contract-blocked item; measuring it split it, because a comparison scoped to the declarations A12 KEYED needs no account of A12's stopping — A12 said everything it had to say where it spoke. That half now asserts (`unreached` in the class-agreement sweep) and it was not a formality: the first run found a deliberate under-report the subset direction cannot see by construction, and with it a false claim in [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md)'s `MVK_MISSING_ERROR_FIELD` row, which had the checker drawing that code for an unresolvable error target when it draws it for a BLANK member only — contradicting the sentence directly above it in the same row. The accumulation contract the rest of it waited on then turned out to be **unnecessary rather than unbuilt**: measured over 23 independent mutation pairs, A12's early stop does not reach across declarations at all, so its accumulated verdict is constructible from single-defect observations and that direction asserts too. Only the intra-text stop stays out, as a scope statement. The differential lane exists and carries three sweeps of this row's own, all hosted by `:adapter:checkerSilenceGuardTest` because each reads the three model trees at runtime: silence on the models A12 builds, silence on the mutants A12 still builds, and CLASS agreement on the mutants A12 rejects (see the F2 record, which carries the acceptance matrix and what each sweep cannot see). A fourth joined that task on 2026-09-15 from [H5-a](#h5-ordering) — silence on SYNTHESIZED models two orders of magnitude larger than any committed one, which reads no repo file and is therefore this row's population extended along size rather than along shape. The class sweep gained three message-aimed mutation kinds on 2026-09-15, and an iterating-carrier kind the same day, and now compares 116 mutants rather than 32 — the error MESSAGE was a position no declaration-shaped kind reached — and that same day it was keyed by DECLARATION rather than by model ([the record](#class-agreement-by-declaration)), which delivers the per-declaration count invariant and makes A12's own class named about the wrong element a disagreement; A12 keys its notifications by PATH, measured, so the id map that slice was scoped around was deleted unbuilt. **Profile scoping is CLOSED as scoped-out, 2026-09-15, and the reason is [A3](#a3)'s rather than this row's.** There is exactly one pinned profile — whatever the sibling checkout carries — and a second A12 version is not merely absent but inadmissible while M is deferred, that being a licensing boundary rather than a preference. Machinery that SELECTS a profile would therefore be built for a dimension this project may not vary, which is the speculative half. The half that is real with one profile is the trigger for when the profile MOVES, and that was missing entirely: [KERNEL-UPGRADE](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)'s coupling map named no guardian for the portable checker, so a kernel bump would have left every family's measured basis — and this lane's pinned map — silently about the old A12. It now carries that row. Re-open the selection question only if M is undeferred. The SILENCE direction, where the checker said nothing about 16 of the 98 rejected mutants, is CLOSED as of 2026-09-15; it was under-reporting rather than a wrong verdict, and was disclosed rather than asserted throughout. **Made actionable 2026-09-15:** the silence is now PINNED by class rather than counted (`SILENT_CLASSES` in that sweep), which turns it into the row's work list — at first `MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED` ×7, `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` ×4, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED` ×3, `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN` ×2. All four are classes the checker already states elsewhere, so none is a missing family and every one is a shape an existing family does not reach; the map moves both ways, so a family LOSING reach is a regression here instead of an unnoticed drop in a number nobody reads. **All four came off the same day and the map is now EMPTY — the checker names an A12 class for every one of the 116 rejected mutants, at the declaration A12 named it about** (16 → 9 → 7 → 3 → 0, then 2 more found and closed on a widened population). The last class needed two facts rather than one: a filter leaf must stop pre-empting the not-referenced family's iteration test, AND that family must yield to the filtered-parallel gate A12 answers first — having only the first made it name a wrong class beside a right one, which a mirror row caught. Keyed by class, all seven `MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED` mutants turned out to be one mutation kind on one shape, and reproducing that shape on a shared fixture drew a DIFFERENT class — the grid that owns this law had measured every row on a rule that does not iterate, a constant nobody had named ([KF424](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf424)). Chasing it down one layer then found the real locus: the reference gate nine families ask keyed on the DECLARING group where A12 keys on the target's coordinates, so fixing one shared helper also drained the `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN` ×2. **That is the argument for pinning by class rather than counting**: the count said a gap existed, the pin named a shape, and the shape led to a helper no pinned class mentioned. Four families moved, none of them new. The last step was the cheapest of the three and came free with the enumeration: all seven remaining mutants were ONE mutation kind, and the four that closed differed from the three that did not only in carrying the outer marker — which the scope family read as a reason to skip the whole condition rather than just the filter body it binds

#### Stage 8 — F standalone public delivery and qualification <a id="stage-8"></a>

**part** — H5's calibration ordering was overtaken by events (the spike was never run and the checker exists), and the replacement is [recorded](#h5-ordering): derive from a basis independent of the checker, qualify cold/prepared/resources, leave after-edit and combined UNSET so the blocking rule keeps L and N closed. No ratified NUMERIC anchor exists, so the collectors come first and their output is informative until per-number ratified. **Three of the five boundaries were MEASURED first (2026-09-15/16)** — cold standalone use, prepared use and resources/delivery, each with its lane and its dated figures ([results](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md)) — and **the owner ratified them per-number on 2026-09-16, split by what each quantity can honestly bear** ([the record](#h5-ratification)): an absolute 1 MiB ceiling on delivery, the already-ratified growth ratio on both timing boundaries, and nothing absolute on either latency. H5-d then gave the cold lane the assertion it lacked on 2026-09-16, and **H5-e collected the fourth boundary the same day**, because L-a had just shipped the operations there was previously nothing to run ([figures](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#after-edit-2026-09-16)): a keystroke answered from a held analysis costs **0.47–0.49×** of the stateless call across an 8× span of model size, while a saved edit is a wash at ~1.0× because `withSource` re-prepares by construction. It asserts nothing, which is the same posture the cold lane held before H5-d. **The fifth and last boundary — F's combined observation — was then collected on 2026-09-16 too**, and the reason it could be is that its precondition had quietly been met: it "needs F's package", which 5a and F3 had already shipped, so the row had stopped being blocked and had simply gone unnoticed as schedulable. `:adapter:checkerCombinedPerfTest` measures what [proposal §4](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) asks — an application holding two prepared representations and paying for two parses — as an **additivity ratio** rather than a latency, since no checker duration is certified and a ratio survives a change of machine. Result: **strictly additive, 0.998×** ([figures](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#combined-2026-09-16)), which CONFIRMS §4's explicit non-promise rather than exposing a defect — there is no sharing and equally no interference. **Its arm-order control is the part to carry forward:** the first, uncontrolled run reported 0.963× because the `both` arm always ran last and inherited the other two arms' warm-up, which reads as the two preparations sharing work. The lane now measures both orders in one run, and one of them alone would have published a finding the proposal says is not promised. **All five boundaries are measured and, since 2026-09-17, all five are dispositioned** — the keystroke ratio ratified against a work-count ceiling, the combined ratio declined because §4 certifies it to be ~1.0 by design and four controlled runs straddle that line ([the record](#h5-ratification)). Row 12 is not held here. **What still holds this block is an observation the boundary framing hid**: §8's resources row requires "retained memory, release behavior, supported peak observations and installed/downloaded production closure size", and the 2026-09-16 landing measured the last of those four. The closure figure is complete on its own terms — the checker's npm manifest declares no dependencies, so the tarball is the whole closure — and **retained memory, which had never been measured for the checker in any module** on a host that supports it, was measured the same day by `:adapter:checkerMemoryPerfTest` ([figures](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#memory-2026-09-17)). Counting BOUNDARIES rather than required OBSERVATIONS is what let a 1-of-4 row read as done, and that denominator lesson is worth more than the figure — the gap was named in this stage's own collector table, which listed the footprint family as the observation's owner, and still read as closed. **And the lesson recurred within the day on a second axis**: the cold boundary's own row names three HOSTS, every lane here was a JVM lane, and that went unnoticed for the same reason — so the plain-Node arm was built and measured, and the browser arm followed the same day ([the record](#stage-8-h5-host)) — the cold row now holds all three hosts it names, each with its own measurement rather than one inherited from another carrier.

**What the memory figures say, and the one thing that keeps the bar unset (2026-09-17).** <a id="memory-disposition"></a> Memory is **linear** in model size — 3.972x retained and 3.984x allocated over a 4x scale-up, reproduced across three runs to within 0.05 % and 0.2 % — where the same workload grows 5.33x in prepared time. So every super-linearity this checker has shown was a time phenomenon and never a space one, and the 2026-09-15 cubic term had no memory counterpart. **The disposition, decided rather than deferred:** the honest bar here is the GROWTH RATIO against the already-ratified `PerfBounds.SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX`, on exactly the reasoning that qualified the cold and prepared ratios — the mechanism was ratified long before `:consistency` existed, so applying it is a scope transfer that needs a measurement on the new carrier and now has one. An absolute byte ceiling is **declined**: unlike a packed tarball's length, a post-GC heap reading moves with the JDK, the collector and the heap size, so it could only ever be a ratchet against this machine. A quadratic-memory checker returns entirely correct answers and then exhausts a heap, which is the same invisible-to-correctness failure class the growth floors already exist for. **Both ratios are ASSERTED as of 2026-09-17**, so the decision has an enforcement locus rather than a sentence. Getting there needed one refactor and turned up one defect. The refactor: `PerfHarness.Growth` named its two representative values `smallMs`/`largeMs`, so bytes would have printed as milliseconds in the one place a breach is read — they are now `smallValue`/`largeValue` and the seam takes the lane's `unit`, across 29 sites and the guard that pins them. The defect was **in this lane's own control**: the first armed version handed the helper a precomputed median, so its three "independent re-measurements" came back byte-identical and the retry protocol was vacuous while reading exactly like a working control. The estimator is now a closure that re-samples, and the lowered-floor control prints three genuinely different estimates (3.977 / 3.984 / 3.986). The lane also pins `TimingEnforcement.GATED` rather than taking the growth floor's ambient `forGrowthFloor()`, which flags instead of failing whenever `CI` is set: that allowance exists because a self-referential TIMING floor is partly a property of the machine, and a byte ratio is not, so the default would have disarmed this bar on every hosted run. Measuring first also paid for itself: the cold lane found the check was cubic in declarations, and a budget ratified from its first figure would now be ten times too loose

**The acceptance half's one located gap: there was no browser arm — located and CLOSED on 2026-09-17.** <a id="stage-8-browser"></a> *(Corrected 2026-09-17: this paragraph's lead said "there is no browser arm". It records how the gap was found and is kept for that; the two paragraphs below carry the declaration and the replay that closed it the same day.)* [F's acceptance](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) requires the corpus to run "through Java, packed Node and browser", and [this plan](#stage-4) already assigns that replay to F — "F owns their packed Node/browser corpus replay, using the same inputs rather than inventing another matrix" — with no deferral anywhere. The measurable fact is one line of build script: `consistency/build.gradle`'s `js` block declares `nodejs()` and nothing else, where `interpreter/build.gradle` declares `browser()`. A target the module does not compile for cannot have replayed anything, so this arm is missing rather than untested, and no run needs commissioning to establish that.

**The target is DECLARED as of 2026-09-17, which was the half missing from the BUILD rather than from a lane.** `consistency/build.gradle`'s `js` block now calls `browser()`, and three consequences were measured before it landed. It compiles clean and `:consistency:check` stays green, so the browser compilation carries no forbidden edge — `verifyConsistencyDependencies` allowlists `jsRuntimeClasspath`, which now includes it. It is not cosmetic: the distribution gains Kotlin's `kotlin-dom-api-compat` module, so the **packed tarball moves from 634 443 to 638 760 bytes, +4 317 (+0.68 %)** — and then to **640 783** the same day, when [the shipped-file enumeration](#stage-8-shipped) found the package had never carried a licence**, and the ratified 1 MiB ceiling still holds with 1.64× headroom against 1.65× before — [the delivery entry](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#delivery-2026-09-16) carries that correction. And `jsBrowserTest` is **deliberately disabled**, on two measured grounds rather than by copying `:interpreter`: enabling Karma grows the committed `kotlin-js-store/yarn.lock` from **524 to 2077 lines**, pulling in a transitive tree the lock carries none of today, and the shared suite under Karma would exercise the compiler's output where F's clause names the packed tarball. (`:interpreter` disables the same task for a different reason — its commonTest imports `node:*` host probes — so its conclusion transfers and its rationale does not, which is why this one is measured here rather than cited.)

**The bundled replay LANDED 2026-09-17, and the transfer changed one thing worth recording: the corpus is now ONE file rather than two that agree by inspection.** `:consistency:browserConsumerTest` packs the tarball, `pnpm add`s it into a staged `consumer-tests/browser`, bundles it with Vite and replays it in a real system Chrome — the interpreter's mechanism (IF224/IF225), without its ratified browser first-answer bar, which is a different product's number, and without any assumption of tree-shaking, which F's non-goals rule out. F's clause asks for "the same corpus" through Node and browser, and two hand-written case files cannot be SHOWN to be the same, so the cases moved into a host-neutral module both arms import ([`consumer-tests/shared/cases.ts`](../consistency/consumer-tests/shared/cases.ts)), importing neither the package under test — each host resolves it its own way, which is the whole point of installing a tarball — nor an assertion library, `node:assert` not existing in a browser. The fixture moved beside it, so the bytes the browser fetches are the bytes Node reads. Each arm now supplies only a host: Node reads the fixture off disk and projects each verdict into `node:test`; the browser fetches it, renders the verdicts, and a Playwright driver reads them back out of the DOM.

**Both arms were controlled with the same mutant, and it is one only the shared cases assert.** `located.start + 1` in `ReportJson`'s span emission took the Node arm red (`the span sliced "oSuchField"`) and then Chrome red on the same case, so neither arm is a vacuous pass and the shared module is demonstrably what both of them run. What made the arm viable at all is a property measured before any of it was written: **zero host-API references in any of the six packed ES modules** (`require(`, `node:`, `process.`), so the Node distribution bundles for a browser unchanged. The landing measurements are in [the browser-replay entry](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#browser-2026-09-17).

**F's oracle for "JS matches JVM" asks for the FULL report contract, not for fields — discharged 2026-09-17.** The oracle table's wording is exact: *"execute the same fixtures through Java, packed Node and real-browser consumers, comparing the full portable report contract"*. Named-field assertions cannot meet it, because what they are blind to is every member they do not name. So there is now one corpus and three readers: [`consumer-tests/shared/parity`](../consistency/consumer-tests/shared/parity) carries a manifest, the input BYTES, and the expected report per case, generated from the PACKED package by `:consistency:syncCheckerParityCorpus`; the two packaged arms read it at runtime and `ParityCorpusTest` compares it on jvm + js from a generated embedding of the same bytes. **The inputs are stored rather than derived**, so a mismatch can never be one arm having built a slightly different model — the failure mode a parity test is most likely to misattribute. Comparison is over parsed documents on every arm: object key order is not part of the wire contract and array order is.

**Two members nobody could have predicted from source turned out identical across platforms**, and they are the reason the corpus is worth its 92 kB: a parse failure's `explain` is kotlinx-serialization's own message text, and `checksCompleted` is a 103-entry `Set` whose iteration order is insertion order by convention rather than by contract. Both are character-identical on Kotlin/JVM and Kotlin/JS. The control is four-way — `put("complete", true)`, one boolean no named assertion in either consumer project mentions, took `jvmTest`, `jsNodeTest`, the packed Node arm and Chrome red with the same path — which is what shows the four readers share one corpus rather than three that agree ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#parity-2026-09-17)). **The corpus asserts no semantics and cannot**: every value in it came from the checker, so the claim-per-case assertions stay where they were.

**The ownership/closure clause's missing third instrument landed 2026-09-17, and it found that the package had never carried a licence.** <a id="stage-8-shipped"></a> F's oracle for "standalone checking is interpreter-independent" asks for three things and the estate had two: `verifyConsistencyPurity` (what the classes LINK) and `verifyConsistencyDependencies` (what a consumer RESOLVES). The third is named in the oracle with its own justification — *"plus a shipped-file provenance enumeration over the published package … failing on any file the interpreter owns, because a copied file carries no dependency edge for a closure guard to see"*. `verifyConsistencyShippedFiles` is that instrument, over **both** carriers, because a denominator closed on the npm tarball says nothing about the jvm jar.

**The shape is the load-bearing part, not the existence.** It is a CLOSED enumeration: every entry must be one something decided to ship, and an unrecognised one fails. A **required-set** check — "is what I expect present" — stays green while an extra file rides along, which is precisely the case a copied source produces, and it is what `verifyInterpreterNpmPackage` is. Closing it immediately paid: the pack ran over Kotlin's compiler-output directory, so the published package shipped a `devDependencies: {typescript}` and **no licence at all** — no `license` field and no `LICENSE` file, in an MIT project — for the module's whole life. Packing now runs over a staged set with a hand-written publication contract, the way `:interpreter`'s does. Three positive controls fire, and the interpreter-owned check is ordered ahead of the generic one deliberately: behind it the specific check would be unreachable, which is an advertised invariant with no enforcement locus ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#shipped-2026-09-17)).

**And the checker's OWN half of that clause had a hole, found by planting the edge rather than by reading the guards (2026-09-17).** NFR #1 — no `:interpreter` dependency — had **no enforcement locus at all on the JS target**: `implementation project(':interpreter')` on `jsMain` resolved onto `jsRuntimeClasspath` with `verifyConsistencyDependencies` and `verifyConsistencyPurity` both green. `verifyKernelFreeClasspath` examined only `ModuleComponentIdentifier`s, so an intra-repo edge was never looked at, and a group allowlist could not have helped — every module here publishes under one group, so `:predicates` and `:interpreter` are indistinguishable by group. The helper now takes an explicit project-path allowlist, the sibling with the same blindness (`verifyPredicatesPurity`, whose filter drops a component carrying its own group) was swept with it, and both arms run BEFORE their module arm because a project edge drags its transitive modules in and the module arm would otherwise report the fallout ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#project-edge-2026-09-17)). The lesson is the advertise-nothing-you-cannot-point-at one: the build file stated this invariant was covered "by construction", on the premise that `:interpreter` is a JVM artifact that cannot sit on a Kotlin/JS classpath — and it is Kotlin Multiplatform with a published JS target.

**The row's API-compatibility clause turned up a defect one layer below the API dumps (2026-09-17).** F's oracle asks for a "compiled public API comparison where relevant", and the relevant thing here is that `dmkits-consistency-gate`'s golden dump names `…dm.consistency.ModelSource` and `…dm.consistency.ConsistencyReport` in its PUBLIC signatures, at `api` scope, on purpose. Those types lived in a module that **published nothing at all**: both that façade and `dmkits-interpreter-preflight` declared `io.github.mbackschat.a12.dm:consistency-jvm` at COMPILE scope in their generated POMs, so a Maven consumer could not resolve the types their own API returns. `:consistency` now publishes `dm-consistency`, and a new root guard reads the generated POMs and refuses any in-repo coordinate outside the published set — the rule was already written in `predicates/build.gradle` and in [RELEASE-SPEC](RELEASE-SPEC.md) §3, and nothing enforced it. Adding the publication then tripped the root-binding trap this repository documents AND showed that the guard against it was disarmed by a comment containing the plugin's name ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#pom-closure-2026-09-17)).

**The clause's last item — the checker's own golden dump — LANDED 2026-09-17, and the exposure was narrower and sharper than "it has no dump".** <a id="stage-8-abi"></a> The two façade goldens do name the checker's types, but only **by reference**: `api/dmkits-consistency-gate.api` carries `check(…consistency.ModelSource)` and `report(): …consistency.ConsistencyReport`, and `api/dmkits-interpreter-preflight.api` carries `CheckOutcome` and `ConsistencyReport` the same way. A type's *name* in a signature is all that pins — so `ConsistencyReport` could lose `getFindings()`, change its type or gain a member, and **both published goldens stay byte-identical** while every consumer calling `run.report().findings()` breaks. That is the invariant that had no enforcement locus, and it is not the one the earlier disposition described.

`:consistency` now enables Kotlin's ABI validation and commits both dumps, `api/consistency.api` and `api/consistency.klib.api`, member-level. **Enabling it was not enough on its own**: Kotlin does not put `checkLegacyAbi` on `check`, so the committed `api/` directory would have read as pinned and been verified by nobody — the parsed-but-never-consumed shape this repository has shipped before. `:consistency:check` now depends on it, and since CI already names that task the gate rides into CI without a workflow edit. **Each carrier got its own control**, because [IRG3](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md) records a KLIB dump silently losing the member depth its JVM twin kept: deleting `getFindings ()Ljava/util/List;` from the JVM golden fails naming that member, and deleting the KLIB `<get-findings>` line fails naming that file. Neither verdict was allowed to carry to the other.

**And the dump's first reading is a finding in its own right, recorded rather than acted on:** the published ABI is **183 public classes** — 159 in `…dm.consistency`, 24 in `…dm.consistency.syntax` — against a shipped TypeScript surface of exactly **two functions**, `checkModelJson` and `checkEditJson`. Almost all of it is public by omission rather than by intent; Kotlin's default visibility is public, and the module was written as an internal implementation before it became a published artifact on 2026-09-17. The gate is deliberately **unfiltered** so that this stays visible: a filter drawn now would encode the accident as the contract, and the noise of an internal change moving the dump is the pressure that gets internals marked `internal`. **Not fixed here, because deciding what the checker's contract IS is a design call rather than a side effect of adding a gate** — and the measurement above is what that decision needs. **Re-open trigger:** the first dump update whose diff is entirely internal churn, or a decision to publish the checker's Java API to a consumer outside this repository ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#abi-2026-09-17)).

**B0's retained controls are replayed through the packaged arms as of 2026-09-17 — the clause B0 hands to F by name.** B0's acceptance says it, and F's row 8 lists it: *"the packed Node/browser replay of these same cases belongs to F, which owns the delivery tooling, so B0 does not silently depend on a later slice's deliverable"*. It had not run, and could not have: `ReferenceBindingTest` drives `prepare(...).resolve(base, name)`, an internal API no consumer can reach, so a green grid there says nothing about the artifact. Five committed models now drive the same five rows end to end through a rule's condition, which is the only way a resolution outcome is observable from outside, and both packaged arms run them.

**Two things about that transfer are worth keeping.** B0's type-level clause — "the slice's type cannot emit VALID, established by the absence of the VALID factory" — is a claim about a Kotlin API that no JSON string carries, so it was TRANSLATED rather than transferred: no row reports `VALID` and every row reports `complete: false`, asserted across the whole grid. And authoring the positive control was not trivial — the first attempt was INVALID for an unrelated reason (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`, the rule's error field not being named by its own condition), so "a model A12 accepts" is a conjunction and a control is only a control once every other constraint is met. The mutant is the over-rejection direction: removing the declaring-group tier makes the fallback run first and reports `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE` on a model A12 accepts ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#b0-replay-2026-09-17)).

**The mirror is HALF built, and the measurement says which half — corrected 2026-09-17, within the hour.** The oracle's neighbouring row asks for *"the mirrored guard on the INTERPRETER's published JVM/JavaScript production closure and shipped files, with its own forbidden-dependency positive control"*. The **shipped-file half already exists**: `verifyInterpreterNpmPublication` compares committed `api/npm-package-files.txt` and `api/maven-package-files.txt` by exact text equality over the full sorted list, so a copied checker file fails it on arrival, in both carriers. The **forbidden-dependency half does not**: `verifyInterpreterPurity` forbids `com.mgmtp.`, `groovy.` and `kotlinx.coroutines.` and never names the checker, and only its `commonMain` rule carries an allowlist — so a `jvmMain`, `jsMain` or `commonTest` edge on `io.github.mbackschat.a12.dm.consistency` is unrefused today. Its instrument is also an import-TEXT scan, the shape this module's own guards deliberately replaced with jdeps. *(This paragraph first said the whole mirror was unbuilt, on the strength of reading `verifyInterpreterNpmPackage` alone and attributing its shape to the module.)* **That half landed the same day**: `verifyInterpreterDependencies` closes the interpreter's intra-repo denominator to `[':interpreter']` on all four JVM/JS configurations, the purity lint's `jvmMain`, `jsMain` and `commonTest` sets now name the checker, both arms carry a planted control, and the gate is on `check` and in push CI. It declares no module allowlist on purpose — the interpreter's external denominator is a separate slice ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#mirror-2026-09-17)). One obligation stays open and is not ours to close in this session: `interpreter/CLAUDE.md` states the lint's forbidden list verbatim and is now short by the checker prefix. The row's remaining clauses are untouched: B0's retained controls, and API compatibility.

**It is off `:consistency:check` and off push CI, for the same reason at both levels: it needs pnpm and a system Chrome, and this CI installs neither today.** That matches the one module already shipping a browser artifact — `:interpreter`'s browser consumer gate appears in no workflow either, and `GateTierWiringTest` exempts its documentation half as "release rehearsal". Wiring it into push CI is therefore a CI-cost decision rather than a correctness one: it needs a corepack step and a Chrome step, and whether those two work cannot be settled without a push. **The other acceptance clauses in this row — the Java arm, B0's retained controls, and the ownership/closure and API-compatibility guards — are still not assessed**, and saying so is deliberate: the browser arm is now run rather than reasoned about, and the rest need the kind of run-and-compare this note does not stand in for.

**The row's H5-qualification clause was the last named one, and following it found that every H5 lane was on one HOST (2026-09-17).** <a id="stage-8-h5-host"></a> This row had recorded that "every budget H5's required list names is now set: cold, prepared, after-edit, memory and package size" — five **quantities**, and true of all five. §8's cold row asks for something the quantity list cannot express: *"installed package load through preparation and first complete consistency result, **on JVM, plain Node and browser**"*. Three hosts, and the enumeration is short and checkable: `git ls-tree -r --name-only efcdaaf1d | grep -iE 'checker.*perf'` returns **five** lanes — after-edit, cold start, combined application, growth and retained memory — all under `adapter/src/test/kotlin/.../perf/` and therefore all launched from the JVM, and `git grep -lE 'performance\.now|process\.hrtime' efcdaaf1d` returns eight files of which **none** is in `consistency/` — no JS timing instrument of the checker existed at all. *(Corrected 2026-09-17: this said "six lanes", which no denominator supports — five existed then, seven do now, and the looser pattern `checker.*perf|perf.*checker` returns an eighth file that is `CheckerColdStartMain.kt`, a child entry point rather than a lane. Cite the revision too: `git ls-files --with-tree=<rev>` unions the tree with the INDEX and duly returned seven for the pre-lane state while looking like a historical query — the same suspect-the-parse class, one command later.)* `:adapter:checkerColdNodePerfTest` (H5-f, in the slice table above) is the second host and `:adapter:checkerColdBrowserPerfTest` (H5-g) the third, measuring **3.90×** and **3.88×** — both essentially linear — against the already-ratified ratio, each with its own floor-lowered control ([Node](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-node-2026-09-17), [browser](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-browser-2026-09-17)).

**The generalizable part is that this is the SAME denominator error this row already confessed, one axis over.** The resources boundary read as done on one of its four required observations because the row counted boundaries; the cold boundary read as done on one of its three named hosts because the row counted quantities. Both times the account fitted every row that existed and every row shared an unnamed constant — which is the confound no control inside the set can catch, and the reason the question to ask of a finished-looking H5 row is *what is true of every lane I have that a reader of "measured" would not assume?* The **browser** host followed the same day as H5-g, so the COLD row's three-host grid is complete rather than two of three — that row's grid and not H5's, a distinction the record below exists to keep — and building it answered the doubt that had made it look like the weaker arm. The worry was that a browser observation would be dominated by navigation and therefore measure Chrome rather than the checker; the page's clock starts in module execution, *after* navigation, so navigation is per-observation wall cost and not part of the quantity at all. Measuring settled in one smoke run what no amount of reasoning about it would have.

**And H5-g's control earned its cost immediately, which is the transferable part (2026-09-17).** The lane was green, its ratio matched the Node arm to two decimals, and its free positive control — a model-independent `module` column — already said the timer was split correctly. Lowering the floor to watch it refuse produced a bare `NoSuchElementException` instead: the assertion helper re-measures on a breach, and the first version had sampled once outside the assert block, so attempt one drained the per-scale queues. **The happy path would have passed indefinitely and the lane would have crashed opaquely the first time growth ever breached — the one moment it exists for.** No amount of green, and no other control in the lane, could have exposed that; only planting the breach could. Worth remembering when a new lane looks obviously correct: what a floor-lowered control tests is not the number, it is the machinery that would have to run on the bad day ([the record](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-browser-2026-09-17)).

**A pin had to be built before anything could be timed on Node, and the absence of one was itself the finding.** H5 takes every budget through IF137's six points and (2) is a pinned runtime; the estate's Node discovery selected the NEWEST installed version, which on the measuring machine is v26.5.0 while `.github/workflows/ci.yml` provisions `24.20.0` for every JS lane. So an unpinned perf lane would have timed a runtime CI never runs and nothing would have recorded which. `checkerNodeMajor` in `gradle.properties` is the single value that both resolves the binary and drives `NodeIdentity`'s refusal — deliberately not a constant in the test, because a task resolving one runtime while the lane approved another is green in the one way that cannot be noticed. `NodeIdentityTest` hands `requireSupported` a wrong runtime on its production path, so the pin has a test that reddens when it stops refusing. It carries **no** substitution seam, unlike `JdkIdentity`: that class reads the ambient process's properties and can only be falsified through a mutable field, while this one takes the probed version as a parameter because it comes from a child process. The seam was copied across with its justification attached before that was noticed — untestable-by-construction is a property of the older class's INPUT, not of a pin.

**The host axis governs the WHOLE budget list, and the sentence that settles it is owner-confirmed — so H5's host axis is OPEN, and this was nearly escalated as a question instead (2026-09-17).** <a id="stage-8-h5-host-axis"></a> Closing the cold row's three hosts raised the obvious next question: do the hosts attach only to that row, or to all five quantities? Two statements were in hand and they disagreed — [H5](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) attaches the hosts to the whole list (*"cold first result, prepared checking, after-edit analysis, memory and package size **on JVM, plain Node and browser** under §8"*), while §8's own boundary table repeats the host list on **only one** of its six rows, "Cold standalone use", and states the rest in host-neutral terms. That looked like a genuine ambiguity worth an owner round trip, and an escalation was drafted.

**It is not ambiguous. A third statement settles it, and it sits under the heading "Hard acceptance conditions confirmed by the owner":** *"Use the existing measurement owners to cover JVM, plain Node and browser consumers: cold load/preparation through first result, warm validation/computation/single-rule operations, retained/peak memory **where the host supports measurement**, release behavior and delivered dependency size."* The hosts attach to the whole list, with an explicit per-host feasibility qualifier on memory and no qualifier on anything else. The table's silence on five rows is the weakest of the three signals and cannot outrank an owner-confirmed sentence; and §8's closing paragraph asks for "host/collector rules" to be frozen per budget, which presumes the host axis is live for each.

**The near-miss is the transferable part, and it is the narrow-prior-art register exactly.** The record answered, confidently and on-topic — §8's table is precisely the artifact H5 defers to — and that terminated the enquiry before the owner-confirmed section was read. A governing statement that fits the question is not the same as the governing statement, and the tell here was available: an escalation about what the owner meant, drafted without grepping the section whose heading says what the owner confirmed. *(The drafted escalation also reasoned from the ratification record's per-quantity bars as evidence for the table reading — inferring intent from artifacts, which the oracle table says is not ours to infer. The owner had simply written it down.)*

**So the grid is 5 × 3 and ten of fifteen cells are measured** (eight when this was first written; both of H5-h's arms landed the same day), and the gaps are not where one would guess — the three timing/memory quantities have no JS observation at all, while package size has the *reverse* gap, the ratified ceiling being on the npm tarball so the JS artifact is bounded and no JVM jar or closure size is bounded anywhere.

| quantity | JVM | plain Node | browser |
|---|---|---|---|
| cold first result | `:adapter:checkerColdStartPerfTest` | `:adapter:checkerColdNodePerfTest` | `:adapter:checkerColdBrowserPerfTest` |
| prepared checking | `:adapter:checkerGrowthPerfTest` | `:adapter:checkerPreparedNodePerfTest` | `:adapter:checkerPreparedBrowserPerfTest` |
| after-edit | `:adapter:checkerAfterEditPerfTest` | — H5-i | — H5-i |
| retained memory | `:adapter:checkerMemoryPerfTest` | — H5-j | qualifier: no reliable retained-heap observation exists in a browser |
| package size | — H5-k (no jar/closure ceiling exists) | `:consistency:verifyConsistencyDeliveryCeiling` (the packed npm tarball) | the same tarball; its BUNDLED transfer is separately measured at 649 648 bytes and is bounded by nothing |

**Six required observations are missing, so they are named as such** — [A's rule](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) is that "an owner that does not yet cover a boundary is a named missing observation, not an invitation to build a framework", and none of the six needs new machinery: H5-f's installed-package child harness and H5-g's browser sampler generalize to the prepared and after-edit shapes, and the JVM closure size is a byte count of an artifact the build already assembles. They are rows H5-h through H5-k in the slice table above. **Row 8's H5-qualification clause therefore remains open on the host axis**, which is the third reading of that clause in one day and the last one the denominator can move under: quantities, then the cold row's hosts, now every quantity's hosts.

**Four follow-ups the cold review of H5-f/H5-g surfaced, each its own landing rather than folded in (2026-09-17).** <a id="stage-8-cold-followups"></a> The review's in-scope findings were settled in the slice; these four are separate mechanisms, so the convergence gate says they get their own commits and not a growing denominator.

| follow-up | locus | why it is not a one-liner |
|---|---|---|
| **the liveness fuse that cannot fire — LANDED 2026-09-17** | four sites, not three: `CheckerColdStartPerfTest` (pre-existing, from the day it was written), `CheckerColdNodePerfTest` **twice** (the observation child and the version probe) and the browser lane's tool runner | each read the child's whole output BEFORE `waitFor(timeout)`, so a stuck child blocks in `readText()` and the advertised fuse is unreachable code. The obvious repair — swap the two — is the classic pipe deadlock instead. Fixed at the shared root: `ChildProcess.runWithFuse` redirects to a file, which `ProfileCliPerfTest` in the same package had been doing correctly all along. Red/green on the real defect, not just on the fix: the control was first run against a deliberate re-implementation of the old shape and `the fuse fires on a child that never exits` FAILED while the 4 MiB flood case PASSED — which is why the defect was invisible, the old shape being dead-fused rather than deadlock-prone. [The rule it generalizes](AGENT-RUN-DISCIPLINE.md) had been in this repository for a year, about shells |
| **the checker ABI goldens have no depth control — LANDED 2026-09-17** | `CheckerAbiGoldenDepthTest`, in the root module's always-on `apiGuardTest` with `consistency/api` declared as an input | IRG3 records a KLIB dump that silently collapsed to bare type headers while its JVM twin kept member depth, which `checkLegacyAbi` cannot see because it compares each golden with its own dump. The invariant is cross-carrier member PRESENCE, not name equality — the latter needs a model of how one declaration renders into two formats, which the interpreter's sibling guard also declines to build. Four mutants, four kills, on four different assertions: a KLIB header collapse, `findings` renamed in KLIB, `getFindings` renamed on the JVM, and a parser mutant that drops the matched-type floor from ~150 to 0 |
| **`report`/`format`/`requiredProperty`/the run record, three times over — LANDED 2026-09-17** | extracted to `ColdLane`; each arm keeps its own observation record, since those genuinely differ | the third copy earns the extraction, and it accreted behind green tests exactly as the standing rule warns. Following it found the THIRD instance of the report-shape hole: the JVM child printed no verdict at all, so its silence preflight had the same gap closed on the two JS arms an hour earlier — a sibling sweep the root-cause rule demands and the review had not reached. Closing it also found a would-be interop defect in the fix: reading the verdict needs a status NAME, `ReportJson.wireName` is private precisely so nothing reflects over the class, and a mapping written into a perf lane would be a second statement of a shape the checker owns. So the arms pin their own surface's spelling — `"INCONCLUSIVE"` from the wire, `"Inconclusive"` from the `data object` — and `ColdLane` carries both constants with the reason. All three lanes re-measured green afterwards ([the record](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-fuse-2026-09-17)) |
| **no guard that every authored `.ts` is type-checked — LANDED 2026-09-17** | `ConsumerTypeScriptCoverageGuardTest`, in `docGuardTest`, over BOTH browser consumer projects with sources and configs declared as inputs | `verifyConsistencyBrowserTypeScriptSources` refuses authored JavaScript and says nothing about coverage, so `vite.config.ts` — the file deciding what gets emitted — was checked by neither tsconfig. The invariant the project advertises is "sources are TypeScript"; the one that matters is "and the compiler sees them". Coverage is judged against the configs a project's `check` script actually INVOKES, because a tsconfig nobody runs leaves every symptom of the original defect in place, and that is the easier mistake to make second time round. The interpreter's browser project was swept in the same pass and is covered — it has no `vite.config.ts`, relying on Vite's default |

**One adjacent exposure is recorded and NOT closed here: the FUNCTIONAL consumer tasks still take the newest installed Node.** That is a real local/CI divergence — `nodeConsumerTest` and the browser arm exercise v26 on this machine and v24.20.0 in CI — and it is deliberately left alone, because choosing the project's supported Node set is an owner decision rather than a side effect of adding a perf lane. **Re-open trigger:** a consumer-test failure that differs between a local run and CI, or a decision to state a supported Node range.

#### Stage 10 — G Node CI example and H candidate checking <a id="stage-10"></a>

**done** — H's mechanism landed 2026-09-16 as part of F1-b rather than as a slice of its own, which is the merge [the fastest path](#fastest-path) prescribed: "check a candidate without persisting it" is one operation, and the hard part is neither surface but the attribution the [F1-b record](#f1b-attribution) measures. It ships as `checkEdit(baseline, edited)` in `:consistency`, exported to JavaScript as `checkEditJson` and documented in [CONSISTENCY-API](CONSISTENCY-API.md); the Node consumer test exercises both directions of it through the packed tarball, which is the only thing in the build that can catch an unconsumable artifact. What H's row still owes is a decision rather than code — whether an editor integration needs anything beyond this string-in/string-out surface — and that question belongs with `I`, which is H's only consumer. **`G` landed 2026-09-16** as [`ci-gate.mjs`](../consistency/consumer-tests/node/ci-gate.mjs) in the same consumer project, with its own tests inside `:consistency:nodeConsumerTest` — a runnable pipeline step, library-only, that tells a rejection from a tool failure from a run that established nothing, and whose payload cannot claim validation at all ([the record](#g-no-pass)). **Both rows closed the same day, and neither closed by writing more code.** The decision they were waiting on was answered by [I-a](#ia-draft): an editor integration needs exactly one thing beyond string-in/string-out, and it is the model context the text is being typed into, not a session protocol. The row's remaining clause — that candidate checking "agrees with fresh checking" — is discharged **by construction rather than by a lock**, and deliberately has no test: `checkEdit(baseline, edited)` is `attribute(check(baseline), check(edited))`, so the fresh report of the edited source IS what it publishes as `EditReport.edited`. There is no second code path that could disagree, and a test asserting the agreement would compare `check(edited)` against itself — the vacuous-control shape this plan has already paid for twice.

#### Stage 11 — J change-impact reports and I editor diagnostics <a id="stage-11"></a>

**done** — J landed 2026-09-16 as `impactOf(baseline, edited)` in `:consistency`, over a dependency graph built from the three reference carriers ([the record](#j-containment)). It reports what a candidate change REACHES, which is the question `checkEdit` does not answer: an edit report says what broke, and the two part company exactly where a declaration is reached without becoming invalid — the ordinary case for a rename or a kind change. **`I` is partitioned and its first half landed the same day** as `checkDraft(source, at, member, text)`: DSL text an editor has typed applied to the prepared fact base rather than to the source, attributed like any other edit, across all four DSL carriers ([the record](#ia-draft)). That also ANSWERS the decision H's row was waiting on — an editor needs one thing beyond string-in/string-out, and it is not a session protocol but this: the model context the text is being typed into. **I-b then landed the same day**, and it is a change to a finding's own shape rather than another entry point:

| I slice | what it lands | why it is its own commit |
|---|---|---|
| I-a | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — `checkDraft`, the prepared-fact-base seam it needs, and the incomplete-is-not-invalid contract | it settles what an editor ASKS; a finding's shape is untouched |
| I-b | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — a finding's source span, and the defect-keyed attribution it forced ([the record](#ib-spans)) | it changes `Finding` and therefore the published wire shape, and it forces a decision `checkEdit` depends on: a finding whose text merely MOVED must not read as one resolved and another introduced |

#### Stage 9 — F1 dmtool coexistence <a id="stage-9"></a>

**part** — F1-a landed 2026-09-16: `model check --engine kernel|portable`, the `:cli` → `:consistency` edge, and the inconclusive result contract (`Family.INCONCLUSIVE` at exit 3, no `valid` key). Substitution is refused structurally rather than compared: `ConsistencyEvidence.requestedBy` throws when the executed engine is not the requested one, so the mutant dies even where both engines agree. F1-b landed the same day, and **F1-c with it**: `:consistency:verifyCheckerArtifactIdentity` pins the three projections of this module's version that a consumer can meet — the npm package they install, the jvm coordinate dmtool resolves, and the constant its receipt cites ([the record](#f1c-projections)). **F1-d landed 2026-09-16 too** ([the record](#f1d-qualification)), **and F1-e with it later that day, so F1 is complete** ([the record](#f1e-wording)). Its packaged/native identity half was never F1-c's to discharge — a build-resolution equality says nothing about a native binary — and it is now measured rather than argued: the same two engine requests through the packaged launcher and the native binary return byte-identical envelopes and equal exit codes, with the recapture that the new module edge triggered ([NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM](NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM.md) §5.1) paid first. **F3 is partitioned in three and F3-a landed 2026-09-16** ([the record](#f3-partition)): `dmkits-consistency-gate` is the façade a published-dmkits caller reaches for, returning the checker's verdict as a rulekit `ValidationResult` with a `PORTABLE_CHECKED` receipt and a `PORTABLE_CHECKER` origin. F3-b (the schemakit gate) and F3-c (the interpreter preflight) landed the same day, so **F3 is complete** but for the VALID clause below. That record also names the one acceptance clause that is not F3's to satisfy — "a supported accepted model yields portable VALID" is blocked on the coverage registry, since this checker cannot emit `Valid` at all


Packed delivery belongs to F. A2's earlier resource check is a bounded packed-resource experiment using existing tooling, not an early implementation of the full package estate. B0's JVM/common tests and F2's adapter lane do not depend on F. F owns their packed Node/browser corpus replay, using the same inputs rather than inventing another matrix.

## 3. Task records — A's finite inventory contract, then each landed stage

**Recommendation:** finish the following records before production translation. They settle what will be shared, what can be claimed and what will measure success. This is an inventory of selected responsibilities and the profile denominator, not a new general-purpose verification framework.

| Record | Required contents | Exit oracle |
|---|---|---|
| Port and consumer boundary | Enumerate selected source responsibilities, imports/callers, existing test owners, designated Java consumers and residual owners. Decide explicitly whether `ConditionBridge` is designated. Record proposed checker-owned types and every optional neutral-leaf type | Read every included source/caller; report search commands, matches found/read and anchored exclusions. A leaf with no established benefit stays absent. No reliance on orphaned `build/` output or archived modules as current source |
| Constraint/profile denominator | Map the laws and grammar productions the Kernel can raise on an A12 model’s rules and computations to current enforcement, Kernel firing sources, raw-input carriers, prerequisites, authoring policies, witnesses and exclusions. Include conversion/non-MVK and applicable generation-path restrictions | Every entry has a disposition; unresolved entries prevent a complete coverage claim. A code count is not a law count |
| A1 erased-bucket disposition | Re-derive the proposal's dated enumeration and disposition every matching row. Rows are in scope by default; an exclusion cites a Kernel path that is dead for A12 input | Reachability/acceptance claims have the appropriate Kernel evidence. Builder inability, renderer avoidance and an absent rulekit authoring surface are each barred as an exclusion basis |
| A2 diagnostic ownership | Map catalog guidance, `StructuralKernelFix`, applicable `RkCode` knowledge and consumer projections. Prefer direct resource reuse; use deterministic projection when delivery/size or Java-owned guidance requires it | Bounded packed Node resource check plus source/projection freshness and contextual guidance controls; acyclic graph, no new hand-maintained dictionary |
| A3 single pinned profile | Record the one sibling-defined A12 version (`2025.06-ext5` / kernel `30.8.1`), its artifact identity, oracle access and finite witness matrix | Identity and oracle access verified against the sibling. A second A12 version is out of scope while M is deferred |
| A4 corpus ownership | Identify retained fixtures and typed authors, canonical sources, stable case ids, profile applicability and explicit accepted/rejected/incomplete expectations | Reuse one input owner across common replay, adapter differentials and packed consumers. Add only minimal raw-input mutations needed to reach shapes builders cannot author |
| H4 comparison matrix | Fixed restart revision, row/workload identities, hosts, collectors, matching runtime/launch rules, and the resolved-production-set plus built-artifact-digest comparison | Every required row has an existing measurement owner or a named missing observation. The mechanical closure/artifact comparison decides whether an affected sweep is necessary |
| H5 budget proposal | Workload identities, candidate budget recommendations and a bounded calibration-spike specification for cold, prepared and after-edit checking; include resources/release and combined use | A fixes the scenarios, collectors and calibration task described in §5. Sampling, reproducibility evidence and owner ratification are later prerequisites of F qualification, not claims discharged by this inventory |

Use the [proposal research sources](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#11-sources) as the starting map. Existing reference owners include [UndeclaredReferenceTest](../interpreter/src/commonTest/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/UndeclaredReferenceTest.kt) and [ShortNameTierLawsTest](../src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/ShortNameTierLawsTest.java). These are reuse candidates with the exact limits recorded in proposal §3.4; neither is asserted to cover the new complete reference/report contract alone.

A produces the slice-specific acceptance records needed to start B, not empty tables to be filled during implementation. Preserve these in this plan while they are planning decisions; place the machine-readable profile/check registry with its consuming implementation.
