Keep measured results in a dated evidence document and link them, following [documentation discipline](DOC-DISCIPLINE.md). Do not create a second status tracker.

### Boundary record — ported responsibilities (measured 2026-09-12, corrected after review)

Enumerated with `git grep -l <symbol> -- '*/src/main/*' '*/src/commonMain/*'` and `git grep -l <symbol> -- '*Test.java' '*Test.kt'` at `15af15abd`, excluding `/build/` and the native reachability-metadata JSON. **Caller counts exclude each symbol's own declaring file and KDoc-only mentions** — the first pass counted both and overstated four of five rows.

| Responsibility | Source | Callers | Test files | Modules | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Model queries and type facts | [ModelIndex](../core/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/core/ModelIndex.java), 401 lines | 18 | 50 | 5 | Port the query behavior into the portable context |
| Expansion | [ModelReader](../core/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/core/ModelReader.java), 789 lines | 7 | 16 | 5 | Reuse the expansion behavior inside checker-owned preparation |
| Reference binding | [BoundModel](../rulekit/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/dsl/BoundModel.java), 679 lines | **80** | **552** | 8 | **Do not port the class.** Extract the binding responsibility beneath it; the façade and its API stay |
| Bare-reference resolution | [ParsedModel](../interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/ParsedModel.kt), 181 lines | 2 | 0 by name | 2 | Stays in the interpreter under NFR #1 — one of four owners |
| Undeclared-reference query | `DmInterpreter.undeclaredReferences()` | 2 | 1 | 2 | Reuse the lookup logic in the checker; never call `DmInterpreter` |

**`ModelIndex` is the scope decision made concrete.** It declares **31** public signatures — 27 instance methods plus 4 statics. Eleven instance methods are pure type facts (`kindOf`, `numberScale`, `numberMin`/`numberMax`, `stringMinLength`/`stringMaxLength`, `stringPattern`, `temporalFormat`, `stringNoValueValidation`, `enumValues`, `enumCategories`) and six are structure facts (`isRepeating`, `maxRepetitions`, `isField`, `isGroup`, `nameOf`, `usageTypeOfGroup`) — exactly the "support in full, do not validate" surface §4 names. **One static must not be overlooked: `resolvePath(elementPath, reference)` is reference resolution, the subject of the frozen first family**, and an instance-only count of 27 hides it.

**`BoundModel` settles "responsibilities, not classes" empirically.** 80 callers across eight modules and 552 test files make porting the class impossible and extracting beneath it the only move — [proposal §5.2](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#52-port-responsibilities-not-entire-public-classes)'s prescription, now with a measured basis. Its compiled Java API guards that the extraction preserved behavior.

**`ParsedModel` has no test file naming it**, so its coverage is indirect. A recall limit of the by-name search, not evidence it is untested; B1 must identify its actual coverage owner.

### A1 — method finding: the legacy exclusion is per-SITE, not per-code (measured 2026-09-12)

Under the conformance rule the 60 erased rows split into 52 that are **in scope by default** — needing no evidence, since exclusion is what requires it — and 8 whose ledger cell claims legacy/H-model. Only those 8 need work.

**A package-path heuristic looked like it would settle them, and a control refuted it.** The kernel separates legacy checks structurally (`…/check/bedingung/hdv/…`, `CheckAnzahlAngegebenOpHModelImpl`), so "every site under `/hdv/` or named `HModel`" was the obvious mechanical criterion. Measured with `grep -rl <code> a12-kernel --include='*.java'`, excluding `/build/` and the three enum declaration files:

| Code | Files | Under `hdv`/`HModel` |
|---|---|---|
| `MVK_CONDITION_WITH_WILDCARD` | 1 | 1 |
| `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` | 2 | 2 |
| `MVK_CONDITION_WITHOUT_REPEATABLE_ROOTGROUP` | 2 | 1 |
| `MVK_DIFFERENT_ROOT_GROUPS` | 3 | 2 |
| `MVK_INVALID_PARAMS_FOR_OP` | 3 | 2 |
| `MVK_INVALID_UNSPECIFIED_TYPE` | 1 | **0** |
| *control* `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` (in scope) | 3 | **1** |
| *control* `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE` (in scope) | 1 | 0 |

Two rows kill the criterion. `MVK_INVALID_UNSPECIFIED_TYPE` is called "legacy-only" by the ledger and has **no** `hdv` file at all; `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` is squarely in scope — it is §3.4's own worked witness — and **has** one. So the heuristic both over- and under-matches.

**The finding is that the unit of disposition is the firing SITE, not the code.** Most of these codes are referenced from several files, and a code can be raised from a legacy path and a live one. [Proposal §7](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) already says one code can represent several laws firing from several sources; this measures it. A1 therefore reads each candidate's sites individually, and the ledger's "legacy-only" prose is a lead rather than a basis.

**Recall limits.** `grep -rl` counts **files mentioning the token**, not firing sites: a file may reference a code without raising it, and an indirection through a constant would not be matched. No check implementation body has been read yet, so no row above is dispositioned — this measures only that the cheap criterion fails.

### H4/H5 — the six boundaries mapped to existing collectors (measured 2026-09-12)

Read the collector estate before assigning commands, as §5 requires: `interpreter/build.gradle`'s registered tasks, `adapter/build.gradle`'s performance lanes, `scripts/perf*.sh` and `scripts/interpreter-*footprint*.sh`, and [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md)'s named NFRs (`NFR-S1`/`S2` startup, `NFR-P1`/`P2` warm throughput and scaling, `NFR-F1`/`F2` relative footprint).

| Proposal boundary | Existing owner | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Interpreter-only use | `interpreterPerformance`, `performanceJvmThroughput`, `performanceAggregateReport`, `interpreterFootprint`, `footprintJvmFork*`, `footprintAggregateReport`, `verifyInterpreterPerformanceClaims`, `syncInterpreterBrowserSize`; adapter's `perfGateTest`, `performanceEligibilityTest`, `interpreterNativePerformance` | **Fully owned.** This is H4's row set; it needs no addition |
| Resources and delivery | the footprint family above plus `syncInterpreterBrowserSize` and [interpreter-delivery-footprint.sh](../scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.sh) | **part** — the DELIVERY half landed 2026-09-16: the checker's three bases joined that collector rather than a parallel one — its JVM jar, its Kotlin/JS KLIB, and the real packed npm tarball H2's Node and browser consumers receive, at 634 443 bytes ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#delivery-2026-09-16)). Both baseline arms were controlled before landing, since `:consistency` is younger than the releases the collector compares against. **That half is COMPLETE rather than partial, and for a verified reason**: §8 requires the *installed/downloaded* closure because "dropping the evaluator does not by itself drop its third-party dependencies", and the checker's generated manifest declares `dependencies: {}` and `peerDependencies: {}` (read 2026-09-17), so the tarball IS the closure. **The RETAINED-MEMORY half was a named missing observation on 2026-09-17 and was MEASURED the same day** by `:adapter:checkerMemoryPerfTest`, which reuses the interpreter footprint estate's own quantity pair rather than inventing one: a held snapshot retains 1 754 984 bytes at 200 programs and one check thread-allocates 68 406 144, both **linear** over a 4x scale-up at 3.972x and 3.984x ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#memory-2026-09-17)). §8's "release behavior" clause is discharged by the lane's release gate, controlled by deliberately retaining the snapshot; its budget is UNSET pending [the disposition](#memory-disposition) |
| Cold standalone use | none for the checker; `NFR-S1`/`S2`'s startup harness is the pattern but measures the interpreter | **Named missing observation** |
| Prepared use | none for the checker; `NFR-P1`/`P2`'s throughput harness is the pattern | **Named missing observation** |
| After-edit use | none — nothing measures incremental re-analysis | **Named missing observation**, owned by L |
| Combined application | none — requires both products by definition | **Named missing observation** |

That was the position on 2026-09-12: one boundary fully owned, one with a collector awaiting checker rows, four missing observations. Per [proposal §7](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) each missing one earns a bounded addition to its existing owner — never a new benchmark framework, which is what every row below was built as. **Three closed on 2026-09-15/16** — cold standalone use ([H5-b](#h5-ordering)), prepared use ([H5-c](#h5-ordering)) and resources/delivery (the row above) — leaving after-edit use, which is L's, and combined application, which needs F's package by definition. Both stay UNSET rather than scoped out, so H5's blocking rule keeps L and N closed until they are measured.

**H4's mechanical closure comparison has its parts already.** The comparison that decides whether a checker-only edit requires an interpreter sweep is the interpreter's resolved production dependency set plus the digest of its built artifacts, and `interpreter/build.gradle` already carries a `sha256File` helper feeding `generateInterpreterReleaseChecksums`, which writes "one stable, sorted SHA-256 manifest for npm, Maven, and API-doc payloads". H4 reuses that manifest as the artifact-digest half and adds the resolved-configuration half beside it; it does not introduce a new digest mechanism.

### H5 — the calibration ordering was overtaken by events, and what replaces it (owner-decided 2026-09-15) <a id="h5-ordering"></a>

**The spike §5 scheduled cannot do its job any more, and pretending otherwise would be the failure H5 names.** [H5](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#hard-acceptance-conditions-confirmed-by-the-owner) requires a budget to be fixed *before* the implementation it gates is measured, and §5 scheduled a bounded disposable spike after A to supply it. A finished at Order 1; Orders 2–6 then built the real checker (103 families). The spike was never run, so a spike measured NOW would be a throwaway estimate of something that already exists — a number less representative than the real one, and arbitrary as a bar.

**Owner decision, 2026-09-15: derive candidate budgets from a basis that exists independently of the checker, never from the checker's own measurement, and qualify only the boundaries that can be measured today** — cold checking, prepared checking, and resources/delivery. After-edit analysis is Order 12's (L) and combined application needs F's package, so both stay explicitly UNSET, which under H5's blocking rule keeps L and N closed until they are measured. That is the point of leaving them unset rather than scoping them out.

**Correction, same day, before any number was written: there is no ratified NUMERIC basis to anchor to.** The derivation was proposed as "anchor to the interpreter's ratified NFR figures". Those figures are not ratified — [INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE](INTERPRETER-PERFORMANCE.md) states the posture plainly: NFR-S1/S2, P1/P2 and F1/F2 are **UNVERIFIED CANDIDATES, informative until per-number certified**, and **`PerfBounds` is the only ratified NFR/release-level floor**. `PerfBounds` is an interpreter-versus-kernel ratio, growth and anchor-tolerance mechanism, not an absolute latency budget for anything. So what the estate actually offers is:

| candidate anchor | what it really is | usable as a checker release budget? |
|---|---|---|
| NFR-S1/S2, NFR-P1/P2, NFR-F1/F2 | uncertified candidate numbers | **No** — a bar derived from them inherits their status |
| `PerfBounds` / `PerfAnchors` | a RATIFIED mechanism: committed per-runner anchor, `factor 1.65 + 1 ms` tolerance set from 322 measured observations, plus a `0.6` stale edge that fires when an anchor stops describing the code | **As a regression bar, yes; as a release budget, no** |
| NFR #1's 5% no-regression rule | owner-confirmed, and about INTERPRETER preservation | Yes for H4's row set, which is already fully owned; it says nothing about checker latency |

**So the executable sequence, and it commits to no budget.** The collectors have to exist before any number can be produced, and building them is required under every option: add the checker's cold and prepared observations to their named owners above, and checker rows to the delivery-footprint collector rather than a parallel one. Then measure and publish the results with exactly the status the interpreter's own numbers carry — **informative, uncertified** — and bring them to the owner with the anchor question answered by data rather than by analogy. Until a number passes IF137's six points it is an unset budget for the blocking rule, and nothing here asserts otherwise.

**The three measurable boundaries, partitioned into landable slices, with the workload basis settled (2026-09-15).** Each is independently landable and none asserts a latency budget — H5-c, H5-d, H5-f and H5-g assert the ratified growth *ratio*, which is a different quantity. *(H5-f and H5-g were added 2026-09-17: the partition below was by BOUNDARY, and §8's cold row names three HOSTS, so two further slices existed that counting boundaries could not see. H5-h through H5-k followed the same day, once an owner-confirmed sentence settled that the host axis governs **every** quantity and not only the cold row — [the record](#stage-8-h5-host-axis). None of the four has landed.)*

| slice | what it adds | oracle | explicit non-goals |
|---|---|---|---|
| H5-a: workload generation — **LANDED 2026-09-15** | the shaped models at `N` and `4N` from the EXISTING typed synthesizer, whose `extract(programsPerKind)` was split so the model TEXT is reachable without its profile (`adapter…perf.ProfileSynthesisTargetProfiles.modelJson`) | the emitted model loads and the checker's report on it is the expected one — a semantic preflight, per IF137 point 4. `adapter.laws.CheckerSilenceOnSynthesizedModelsTest`, in the `checkerSilenceGuardTest` lane: `BoundModel.ofJson` first at each scale, then no rejecting finding. Green at 200 and 800 programs | no new synthesizer, no new fixture domain, no timing |
| H5-b: cold first result — **LANDED 2026-09-15**, and it found something | a fresh-process lane clocking model text → complete `ConsistencyReport`, ≥50 independent processes, nearest-rank p95 with median reported: `:adapter:checkerColdStartPerfTest`, one JVM per observation, resolving `:consistency` alone so a kernel artifact on the child's classpath fails the lane | the preflight above must pass in the timed process, and the report is compared, not counted — it was, empty at every scale. [Measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-2026-09-15) | no budget, no CI schedule, no shared framework extraction. It writes no anchor deliberately: an anchor is a bar |
| H5-d: the cold lane's missing assertion — **LANDED 2026-09-16** | the same children, launched as **interleaved N/4N pairs** rather than two campaigns, and the median of the within-pair ratios asserted against `SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX` — the ratified estimator, now applied on both timing boundaries. It costs nothing: the same `2 × processes` JVMs were already being launched, one scale after the other | the ratio the lane now computes — **2.75×** at 200 → 800 programs, reproduced within 1.5 % over four independent estimates and 408 fresh JVMs — reported per attempt, so a re-measured breach never describes an accumulated run. A control run with the floor lowered under the observed ratio refuses, which is the only evidence the assertion is live ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-paired-2026-09-16)) | no duration asserted, no anchor, and no claim that the cold ratio sees a defect class the prepared one cannot — it is the looser instrument of the two, because the fixed cold cost only it pays deflates its ratio. A diagnostic (`-PcheckerColdJfr`) run asserts nothing at all |
| H5-c: prepared checking — **LANDED 2026-09-15** | a warm lane at `N` and `4N` with interleaved pairs and the median of within-pair ratios — the ratified paired estimator, reused rather than reinvented: `:adapter:checkerGrowthPerfTest`, with typed authoring and the silence preflight (which doubles as the warm-up) outside every clock | the same preflight, and the ratio measured against the ratified `PerfBounds.SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX` floor rather than against a latency: **5.33×** over a 4× scale-up, 51 → 261 ms at 200 → 800 programs ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#growth-2026-09-15)) | no absolute bar; the growth statistic is the claim. Unlike H5-b this lane ASSERTS, because a cubic checker returns exactly the right answers and no correctness test can see one |
| H5-g: the cold boundary's BROWSER arm — **LANDED 2026-09-17**, closing the host grid | the third host §8's cold row names, and the one no other arm stands in for: a bundler's output, an HTTP transfer and a document, where Node has its own module graph and a filesystem. One observation per **fresh browsing context** in a system Chrome, 51 interleaved pairs; the page imports the checker DYNAMICALLY so the chunk fetch lands inside the clock, and the sampler computes no statistic — it visits the scales in the asked order and prints raw observations, leaving the ratified estimator in `PerfHarness` rather than copied into TypeScript | the silence and report-shape preflights, a per-SCALE observation count (the estimator reconstructs pairs from per-scale queues, so a compensating mis-count clears a total-only check and then dies on an empty pop), a refusal of any module load under 1 ms (what an inlined chunk looks like, which would make the page time a warm call), and the ratio against `SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX`, measuring **3.88×** against the Node arm's 3.90×, agreeing to within half a percent on the same engine family. Its `module` column is a free positive control: model-independent at 23.2 → 25.2 ms while `check` moves 3.9×, which is what a correctly split timer must show ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-browser-2026-09-17)) | no latency asserted and no anchor written; the durations differ from Node's and no mechanism is offered for that; and off every automated lane, needing pnpm and a system Chrome as the functional browser arm already does |
| H5-f: the cold boundary's PLAIN-NODE arm — **LANDED 2026-09-17** | the host §8's cold row names beside the JVM and nothing had measured: 51 interleaved N/4N pairs of fresh **Node** processes, each importing `a12-consistency` by package name out of a consumer project's `node_modules` — so it is also the first cold observation of an INSTALLED package on any host, the JVM arm having always resolved `:consistency` as a project dependency. `:adapter:checkerColdNodePerfTest`, with the runtime pinned by `checkerNodeMajor` and `NodeIdentity` refusing any other major | the same silence preflight, plus a report-SHAPE preflight (an empty rejecting set is also what a report with no findings looks like), a closure preflight (`node_modules` holds the checker and nothing else) and a one-runtime check across the children; the ratio asserted against the already-ratified `SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX`, measuring **3.90×** — essentially linear — with the floor-lowered control refusing on three re-measurements ([measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#cold-node-2026-09-17)) | no latency asserted and no anchor written, on this runtime least of all; NOT a control for H5-d's fixed-cost hypothesis, a different engine moving more than one variable; and not wired into any automated lane, a schedule implying a bar |
| H5-h: prepared checking on **Node and in the browser** — both halves **LANDED 2026-09-17** | the two cells §8's prepared row leaves unmeasured once the host axis is read as owner-confirmed. The quantity is the one `:adapter:checkerGrowthPerfTest` clocks — verified by reading it rather than assumed: it times `check(ModelSource(text))` on a warmed runtime, a repeated whole-model check and **not** a snapshot-reuse call, so the analogue is `checkModelJson` after a per-scale warm-up and the checker's published two-function JS surface suffices. (A prepared/snapshot entry point is deliberately unexported to JS; timing one through an unexported Kotlin function would report a cost no consumer can obtain. That distinction is what turned this row from looking blocked into being schedulable.) `:adapter:checkerPreparedNodePerfTest` runs ONE warm child for the whole run, where H5-f pays a launch per observation, because warmth is what this boundary is about | the ratified `SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX` on each host, each with its own floor-lowered control, since a bar holding on a new carrier is a claim and not an inheritance | no latency asserted on either host; not a control for the JVM arm's ratio, a different engine moving more than one variable. **The three prepared ratios rise with distance from the JVM — 5.33×, 6.42×, 6.59× — and no mechanism is offered for that**; the one rival this arm could test, an in-call JSON serialization the JVM quantity excludes, is ruled out by `reportChars` being identical at both scales on both JS hosts |
| H5-i: after-edit analysis on **Node and in the browser** | the two cells §8's after-edit row leaves unmeasured. It needs L's edit surface reachable from JS, so it is ordered AFTER row 12's L-b rather than schedulable now — naming the dependency is the point of the row | the ratified `1.0×` keystroke ceiling per host, with the stateless-equals-stateful preflight the JVM arm already runs as its correctness oracle | no new ceiling derived from these hosts' own spread; the JVM arm's two permanently-unset arms stay unset here too |
| H5-j: retained memory on **plain Node** | the one memory cell the owner's qualifier does NOT excuse: `process.memoryUsage().heapUsed` after a forced GC is a supported observation on Node, where a browser offers no reliable retained-heap figure | whatever bar the owner ratifies, gated as the JVM arm is; the collector comes first with informative output, per H5's ordering rule | the BROWSER memory cell, dispositioned under the owner's "where the host supports measurement" qualifier rather than left as a silent gap |
| H5-k: the **JVM** delivered-closure size ceiling | the reverse gap, and the cheapest of the four: delivery is bounded on the npm tarball and on nothing the JVM ships, so a consumer's Maven closure has no ceiling at all. A byte count over the published jars plus their resolved production closure, which the build already assembles | an absolute ceiling, the one bar type H5 permits without a sampling regime because bytes are machine-independent; a planted addition must breach it | not a trend collector — `scripts/interpreter-delivery-footprint.init.gradle` owns the cross-revision view and a ratchet is a different instrument |

**Owner ratification, 2026-09-16: split by what the quantity can honestly bear.** <a id="h5-ratification"></a> The three boundaries ratified here are not interchangeable, so one posture across all three would have to either over-claim or under-claim. Each carries the strongest bar its own quantity supports, and nothing beyond it:

| number | the bar it bears | why not a stronger one | where it is held |
|---|---|---|---|
| resources/delivery — the packed npm tarball, which is the CLOSURE size half of the boundary only | an **absolute ceiling of 1 MiB** (1 048 576 bytes) | none needed: a byte count is machine-independent, which makes delivery the one checker boundary where an absolute bar is honest | `:consistency:verifyConsistencyDeliveryCeiling`, named in push CI |
| resources — retained memory, the boundary's other measured half | the **growth ratio** against `PerfBounds.SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX`, ratified and asserted 2026-09-17 ([disposition](#memory-disposition)) | the growth ratio against `PerfBounds.SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX` is the honest bar and an absolute byte ceiling is declined, because a post-GC heap reading moves with the JDK, collector and heap size where a tarball's length does not | `:adapter:checkerMemoryPerfTest`, asserting both ratios under `TimingEnforcement.GATED` |
| prepared checking | the already-ratified **growth ratio** `PerfBounds.SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX` | an absolute latency is machine-dependent and no checker latency has a certified basis; a ratio survives a faster machine, a slower one and a JIT change | `:adapter:checkerGrowthPerfTest` (H5-c above), which already asserts it |
| cold first result | the same growth ratio | as above, and its absolute figure additionally carries ~130 ms of fixed cold cost that belongs to the JVM rather than to the checker | `:adapter:checkerColdStartPerfTest` (H5-b above), asserting since H5-d — and since 2026-09-17 the same ratified ratio is asserted on **all three hosts §8 names**, by `:adapter:checkerColdNodePerfTest` (H5-f) and `:adapter:checkerColdBrowserPerfTest` (H5-g). Each carries its own measurement rather than inheriting the ratio, because a bar holding on a new carrier is a claim and not an inheritance; the two JS arms agree at 3.90× and 3.88× and the JVM's 2.75× is the outlier the row's own note explains |
| after-edit use (L) — the **keystroke** arm | a **reuse ceiling of `1.0×`**, ratified 2026-09-17 ([disposition](#keystroke-ratification)) | the ceiling comes from L-a's work COUNT — `checkDraft` runs two checks and a snapshot's draft runs one — so it is fixed by the feature rather than by the lane. Tightening it toward the measured 0.474–0.502× would fit the bar to the implementation it gates, which is the failure the six-point gate exists to prevent | `:adapter:checkerAfterEditPerfTest`, asserting since 2026-09-17 |
| after-edit use (L) — the **build-snapshot** and **saved-edit** arms | **UNSET, permanently** | both are ~1.0× BY CONSTRUCTION (building an analysis contains one check; `withSource` re-prepares because the source set changed), so any ceiling would have to be read off this lane's own spread — and the saved-edit arm straddles 1.0 across four runs (0.958–1.006×), so such a bar would refuse healthy runs | the same lane, printing them |
| combined application (N) — additivity | **UNSET, permanently absent a consumer-stated tolerance** ([disposition](#combined-ratification)) | measured 2026-09-16 and re-measured three times since, so this is a decision rather than a gap. [§4](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) promises NO sharing, so a bar at or below 1.0 would assert what the proposal declines to promise — and the controlled arm measures 0.996–1.010×, straddling 1.0, so it would refuse healthy runs. A bar ABOVE 1.0 would gate interference, and nothing in H5 or §4 says how much above the sum is tolerable, so its threshold could only come from this lane's own spread | `:adapter:checkerCombinedPerfTest`, printing |

**Two of the three are ratios, and that is what keeps this inside H5's own ordering rule.** The rule above forbids deriving a budget from the checker's own measurement, and the reason is that such a budget certifies whatever happened to be built. A ratio escapes it outright: `SUB_QUADRATIC_MAX` was ratified as a mechanism long before `:consistency` existed, so applying it here is a scope transfer onto a new carrier — which needs a measurement on that carrier, and has one at 5.33×, not a new number. **The byte ceiling does not escape it, and the honest thing is to say so**: 1 MiB was set from the measured 634 443 bytes and cannot be read as "the checker is small enough" — there is no independent basis for how many bytes a portable checker should be, and the nearest comparator (the interpreter's 1 311 139-byte tarball) is a different product. What it certifies is **bounded growth from a known state**, a ratchet with 1.65× of headroom, and a ratchet can only be set from a measurement.

**The delivery number's six-point disposition** ([IF137](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if137)): (1) the scenario is the real packed tarball a Node or browser consumer receives, and the "estimator" is a byte count; (2) the runner needs no pinning, which is this quantity's whole advantage; (3) no sampling regime applies — re-packing one revision is bit-identical, measured 2026-09-16 as the same SHA-256 across two packs, so the ratified ≥5-fork footprint default binds retained memory rather than a file's length; (4) the semantic preflight is the consumer test that packs the same tarball, clean-installs it and imports by package name, which is the checker analogue of point 4's tri-engine agreement — there is no second consistency engine to agree with, and the substitute has to be that the measured artifact is the working one; (5) reproducible, as (3) records; (6) ratified. The ceiling lives in one place, beside the task that packs the bytes; the cross-revision comparison stays informative and stays in its collector, because a ratchet and a trend are different instruments.

**The keystroke ratio's six-point disposition, and point 4 needed translating rather than waiving (2026-09-17, owner-delegated).** <a id="keystroke-ratification"></a> (1) The scenario is the synthesized flat workload at 200 programs and the estimator is the ratified paired one — interleaved stateful/stateless pairs, median of the within-pair ratios. (2) `JdkIdentity.requireSupported` pins the runtime. (3) The required sampling is the median of within-pair ratios, which is what H5-d's cold ratio was certified on; **no p95 is taken and none is required**, because a p95 describes a latency distribution and the quantity here is a ratio of two arms measured in one load slice. (4) **[IF137](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md#if137)'s preflight is written for an interpreter number** — `unsupported.isEmpty()` plus tri-engine agreement with the Groovy-dynamic anchor — and a checker has no `unsupported` set and no second engine to agree with, so read literally the clause would make **no** checker number promotable, ever. The translation is the one the delivery number already used: the measured operation must be the working one. Here that is two preflights outside every clock — the model draws no rejecting finding, and the snapshot's answer equals the stateless one compared as whole objects. (5) Reproducible: **eight independent estimates on 2026-09-17 span 0.474–0.502×, 5.9 %**, five from accepted runs and three from the control's re-measurements. (6) The owner delegated ratification on 2026-09-17; the bar and its derivation are recorded in `KEYSTROKE_CEILING`, and a control run with the ceiling lowered to 0.4 refuses across three re-measurements, which is the only evidence the assertion is live.

**The combined boundary is DECLINED rather than deferred, and the measurement is what declines it (2026-09-17, owner-delegated).** <a id="combined-ratification"></a> Points 1, 2, 4 and 5 are met on the same footing as the keystroke ratio — fixed scenario and estimator, pinned runner, three preflights (the checker accepts, the interpreter loads, both read the same `entryModelId`), and four independent estimates per arm order. What is absent is a **basis for the threshold**, and no further measurement can supply one: the proposal explicitly promises that an application choosing both "may hold two prepared representations and pay for two parses", so the quantity is certified to be ~1.0 by design, and a bar is only meaningful on the other side of it. Four controlled runs put that arm at 0.998 / 1.010 / 1.006 / 0.996× — it straddles 1.0, so the natural-looking `≤ 1.0` would refuse a run in which nothing is wrong. **Re-open trigger:** a consumer states a combined-cost tolerance, or the two preparations begin sharing work — at which point additivity becomes a promise rather than a non-promise and has a basis to be gated against.

**The prepared ratio needed no new code to certify; the cold one needed one restructuring.** H5-c's lane already asserts the floor, and its six points were met when it landed: the scenario and the ratified paired estimator are fixed, `JdkIdentity.requireSupported` pins the runtime, the estimator IS the required sampling, the silence preflight passes at both scales, and the ratio reproduced either side of the cubic fix. So ratification alone promotes it from a self-imposed guard to a release bar. H5-b's lane was already measuring both scales — but as two sequential campaigns, which is not the ratified estimator and yields no within-pair ratio to assert. **H5-d** interleaves them and asserts the median, for no extra process.

**The checker's cost axis is MODEL size, not document rows, which is why none of the interpreter's scenarios transfer.** Its scenarios scale rows (`b1-100000-rows`) because an evaluator's work is per row; the checker never sees a document. So the `N`/`4N` axis is declarations-and-rules, which is exactly what the synthesizer's `programsPerKind` already varies — and the three shaped profiles (compact, breadth, depth) give the topology variation a single scale cannot.

**Where each half runs, and the boundary it respects.** Synthesis needs the typed kits and so belongs where they are visible; MEASUREMENT must not, because H5's standalone boundary is the checker's own closure. Splitting it at the model TEXT settles both: the generator writes model JSON, and the timed process loads only the checker to read it. A lane that measured the checker with the kernel on its classpath would be measuring something H5 does not ask about.

**H5-a landed as a fourth SILENCE population, and that is where its value is (2026-09-15).** Framed only as benchmark scaffolding it would have waited on the timing lanes it feeds; framed as a population it pays immediately, because it sits on the other side of an axis every existing sweep shares. All three differential sweeps read the committed and reference model trees, and every model in them is hand-authored and small — the largest carries a few dozen declarations. So each of the checker's families was written, and is measured, against models of ONE size, and *"this family over-reports only above some declaration count"* is a shape none of them can see. The synthesized population is generated at 200 and 800 programs — 200 rules plus 200 computations over 400 fields, then four times that — and it asks A12 first (`BoundModel.ofJson`) so its silence is a claim about the checker rather than agreement with a rejection. It is a live oracle rather than a green by construction: a planted mutant in the arity gate (`EXACTLY_ONE` demanding two entities) is killed by this class alone in 35 s.

**H5-b's first run changed what H5-c has to answer, and that is the most useful thing it produced (2026-09-15).** The lane was built to supply a cold latency figure and it did — 552 ms median at 200 programs, 8.6 s at 800 — but the pair is not the finding. The **per-doubling ratio grows**: 1.67× from 100 to 200 programs, 2.83× from 200 to 400, 5.48× from 400 to 800. A quadratic cost holds 4× per doubling, so the measured curve is steeper than quadratic at the top of the range, and the flattening at the bottom is the fixed ~130 ms cold cost dominating a small model rather than better scaling. It is not the garbage collector: the 800-program scale re-measured under a fixed 6 GB heap reproduces its median within 1.4 %, which was checked precisely because the 4.4–10.0 s spread there reads like memory pressure. **No locus is claimed** — the curve says the cost is super-linear in declarations, not which family or which layer of preparation carries it, and attributing that from a source reading would be a mechanism wearing an observation's confidence. Two consequences for the remaining partition. H5-c's growth statistic is no longer a formality to confirm a linear expectation; it is the measurement that pins the exponent on the prepared path, where this one pins it on the cold path. And every row is the FLAT topology the synthesizer emits, one root group holding everything, so it transfers to the shaped profiles only as a hypothesis — the axis this measurement does not cross.

**And the curve was a defect, found and closed the same day — which is the strongest argument in this record for measuring before budgeting.** A flight recorder on the lane (`-PcheckerColdJfr=<dir>`, which drops the sample floor because a recorded run is a diagnostic rather than an observation) put 95 % of its samples in two places: `declaredReferences` with its `resolveRelative`, and `computationSelfReadFindings` with its `computedTargetOf`. `declaredReferences` is a whole-model walk that resolves one reference per element by SCANNING the resolvable elements — quadratic on its own — and fifteen families call it, one of them **per element**, which made the whole check cubic in declarations. Two derived values on `PreparedModel` close it: the walk computed once, and the resolvable elements keyed by path. A re-profile then put the new top at `DeclaredKind.of`, re-run by a `get()` on every access, and storing it per element took another 9 %. 800 programs went from 8.6 s to **824 ms**, and the per-doubling ratio from 5.48× to 1.75× — exactly linear once the fixed cold cost is taken out ([both curves](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#hoisted-2026-09-15)). No family changed and none had to remember to hoist anything — a rule no guard can enforce, which is exactly how the cubic arrived unnoticed. **Had a budget been ratified from the 8.6 s figure it would now be ten times too loose**, gating nothing for the rest of the project; H5's "measure the implementation, then ratify" ordering is what made that impossible rather than lucky.

**H5 stays a proposal, not a number.** No checker budget is asserted here: the four missing observations are exactly the workloads a budget would have to be measured over, and [H5](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#hard-acceptance-conditions-confirmed-by-the-owner) requires each budget to pass IF137's six-point gate — including owner ratification — **before** the implementation it gates is measured. The calibration spike §5 schedules after A therefore has a defined shape: add the four missing observations to their named owners above, run them against a disposable checker spike, and bring the measurements to the owner as candidate budgets. Any number written before that ratification would gate nothing.

### 5a record — the walking skeleton, and the failure only it could see (2026-09-13)

**The JS target emitted nothing importable, for the module's whole life, while every lane stayed green.** `js { nodejs() }` compiles and runs tests without `binaries.library()`, so the module had a passing JS test lane and no consumable artifact — and no Kotlin test could have noticed, because every one of them has the checker on the compile classpath by construction. That is precisely the class row 5a exists to catch before order 8, and it was caught in the first hour of building the skeleton rather than at qualification.

**The sample imports BY PACKAGE NAME, which is the only arrangement that tests what a consumer does.** `:consistency:nodeConsumerTest` runs `npm pack` on the compiled library, clean-installs that tarball into a staged copy of [`consistency/consumer-tests/node`](../consistency/consumer-tests/node), and runs `node --test` against it. A relative import into `build/` would prove a file exists; `node_modules` resolution is the step that fails in the ways that matter. It reaches no network — the packed package declares no runtime dependency — and it is deliberately NOT on `:consistency:check`, matching `:interpreter`'s consumer tests, because a JVM lane that silently needs node and npm fails on machines where the rest of the build is fine.

**The JS surface is one function and the report's JSON shape is hand-written, both deliberately.** `@JsExport` cannot carry `List<Finding>`, `Set<CheckFamily>` or a sealed `ConsistencyStatus` without a parallel bridge hierarchy, which `:interpreter` carries because its surface is settled and this one has not earned. Rendering the report by hand rather than annotating the data classes `@Serializable` keeps every field name and enum constant from becoming a wire contract silently, the moment somebody adds one — `ReportJsonTest` pins the key set for the same reason.

**The shared leaf is `:predicates`, and what it had to be was decided by the CONSUMERS, not by the predicate.** A predicate two consumers share is a few lines; the reason C1 stayed blocked is that one consumer is JVM/Java (`:rulekit`) and the other is multiplatform (`:consistency`), so a JVM-only leaf would have served the kits and not the checker — which is why the leaf is Kotlin Multiplatform and published as `dm-predicates` rather than being a class somewhere convenient. It depends on nothing at all, enforced by `verifyPredicatesPurity` rather than by convention, because a leaf that grows dependencies stops being a leaf and starts being a second checker.

**The law it collapsed had already drifted, which is the evidence that the duplication was live rather than theoretical.** `vk_vk` reserved-namespace rejection had three statements: `Rule.Builder` raising `MVK_RULE_NAME_INVALID`, `Computation.Builder` raising a bare `require` with no code — added only 2026-09-12, and only because the reader noticed the law reads as a rule's — and the checker's own `reservedNameFinding`. Three copies, three spellings, one A12 law. All three now call `isReservedElementName`.

**Extracting it exposed a Gradle footgun worth a build guard.** `:predicates` first shipped a `publishing { }` block without applying `maven-publish`; Groovy then resolves the unknown method against the PARENT project rather than failing, so the block configured ROOT's extension — renaming root's `dmkits` aggregator publication to `dm-predicates-aggregator`, giving it the leaf's POM, and leaving `:rulekit`'s POM pointing at a `predicates` coordinate nothing published. Every lane stayed green; only reading the installed POMs showed it. `verifyPublishingIsDeclaredLocally` now fails a subproject that writes `publishing { }` without the plugin, and was confirmed to fire on exactly that mutation.

**And `verifyPredicatesPurity` itself shipped one round green-but-blind, which is the more useful lesson.** Its stdlib exemption read `group.startsWith('org.jetbrains.kotlin')`, and `org.jetbrains.kotlinx` starts with `org.jetbrains.kotlin` — one missing boundary character, so the guard that defines this module's whole contract silently exempted the entire kotlinx namespace: serialization, coroutines, datetime. A planted `kotlinx-serialization-json` SURVIVED while a planted `jackson-databind` died, which is the shape to watch for — a guard that catches the dependency you imagined and misses the one you would actually reach for. It now matches the group exactly, and both arms are mutation-confirmed: the forbidden-edge arm fires on kotlinx and on Jackson, and the non-vacuity arm fires when the stdlib probe is made unmatchable. **The transferable rule: an exemption in a dependency guard is matched on the WHOLE group, never on a prefix** — namespaces here are deliberately built by suffixing (`kotlin` → `kotlinx`), so prefix matching fails in exactly the direction that makes a guard useless rather than noisy. **And it shipped green-but-blind a SECOND time, on a different axis (2026-09-17).** The same guard's module filter drops anything whose group equals this project's own — which is exactly what an intra-repo `ProjectComponentIdentifier` carries — so a planted `project(':consistency')` on the leaf that claims to depend on *nothing at all* was invisible to it. It now has a project arm, running FIRST, because a project edge drags its transitive modules in and the module arm would otherwise report five stray kotlinx artifacts instead of the one edge ([the measurements](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-RESULTS.md#project-edge-2026-09-17)). Twice is a pattern: this guard's failures are never the dependency it was written against.

**5a is met.** The leaf lands here, and the clean-install run went to CI on 2026-09-13 (`:consistency:nodeConsumerTest` on every push, wired in `ci.yml` and pinned by `GateTierWiringTest`). The API document is [CONSISTENCY-API](CONSISTENCY-API.md).

### F2 record — acceptance matrix for the first Order 7 slice, frozen before any code (2026-09-14)

**The over-rejection guard has a population problem nobody had named: every model it sweeps was written to be VALID.** `CheckerSilenceOnCleanModelsTest` runs the whole checker against every model A12 builds — the committed trees plus both reference corpora, about 80 models — and that is the project's central safety net. But a model authored to be correct exercises each family's *negative* path only where its author happened to write an unusual shape, so the shapes closest to a family's decision boundary are exactly the ones the sweep does not contain.

**Two over-rejections were found by hand on 2026-09-14 that this sweep cannot see, and they share a mechanism.** `REPETITION_SCOPE_GROUP` would have reported the repeatability class on a FIELD scope, and `FILTER_WILDCARD` did report the wildcard class on a filter whose leaf resolves to nothing. Both models are REJECTED by A12, so the silence sweep excludes them by construction; both were caught only because a mirror test stated the declined rows explicitly. **A guard that depends on somebody writing the row is not a guard.**

**So the first F2 slice is a MUTATION sweep with A12 as the arbiter of its own population.** Perturb each committed model mechanically, ask A12 whether the result still builds, and require the checker's silence on every mutant that does. It needs no expectation files and no corpus format change, because the oracle is in the loop: a mutant A12 rejects is simply not in the population, and one it accepts is a model the checker must not refuse.

| acceptance item | oracle | non-goal |
|---|---|---|
| every mutant A12 still BUILDS draws no `justifiesRejection` finding | `BoundModel.ofJson` + `check`, in the lane | — |
| the sweep is not empty: a floor on mutants that survived A12 | the lane's own count | a floor that tracks the corpus size |
| each mutation kind actually produces mutants A12 accepts | per-kind counts asserted, so a kind that silently stops mutating fails | proving a kind is exhaustive |
| a mutant A12 REJECTS is excluded, not judged | stated; the classes both engines draw on an invalid model have different denominators — A12 stops, the checker accumulates | comparing classes on rejected models |

**Landed 2026-09-14 as `adapter.laws.CheckerSilenceOnMutatedModelsTest`, with its reach measured rather than argued.** Four mutation kinds over the three committed model trees produce **141 mutants A12 still builds** (floor 40, and each kind must contribute or the sweep fails as vacuous). Inverting `RepetitionWildcard`'s repeatability test is killed by it; dropping `ErrorTargetIteration`'s repetition guard SURVIVES it and is caught by that family's own mirror — so the sweep is an additional net over the families, never a replacement for their grids, and that bound is stated in its KDoc rather than left for a reader to assume. **The reference corpora are mutated too**, on the same read-only terms the clean sweep already uses: the committed trees were authored against this project's own reading of A12 and every model anywhere was authored to be VALID, so mutating a corpus A12's own team wrote removes both confounds at once. *(The first version excluded them on a licensing reading that does not hold — a mutant lives only in memory, exactly as the Kernel-expanded original already does — and on an untested claim that they would reach the same shapes.)*

**It paid on the day it landed, and the payoff was not the sweep but its PROBE.** Extending the same machinery to the mutants A12 REJECTS — repoint each model's first rule at a newly added unreferenced field, then compare classes — turned up seven models where the checker named `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED` and A12 named `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`. That is a live wrong-class defect, fixed the same day, and no grid in the estate could have found it: every row of `ErrorFieldReferenceReachLawsTest` held the condition flat, so "the target is unreferenced" and "nothing iterates" were one thing across the whole file ([KF350](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf350)).

**The class comparison was measured before it was landed.** Over 45 rejected mutants: 13 draw no keyed class from A12 at all (the not-buildable tier, where the comparison is undefined), 25 have the checker silent or reporting a subset, and the 7 above were the only disagreements — all one shape. *(Corrected 2026-09-14: this paragraph previously concluded the comparison was not landable, because a strict subset assertion cannot tell "a class A12 masks behind an earlier one" from "a class A12 would never draw". Fixing the defect emptied the disagreement set, so the assertion is landable TODAY with an explicit `masked` set that is empty and a new pair failing the test — the distinction is made by hand when a pair first appears, rather than by the guard. It landed as `adapter.laws.CheckerClassAgreementTest`, 32 mutants comparing.)*

**A third open item, found by landing a family rather than by reviewing the lane (2026-09-14).** Nothing checks the COUNT of classes on one declaration, only their identity, so two families both answering one condition text is invisible here — A12 stops at the first gate that answers, so a second positioned class is an over-report by construction. The `DATE_CONSTRUCTION_SLOT` entity arm would have shipped exactly that against `OPERAND_REPETITION_SCOPE` and only a hand-measured precedence row caught it. **Landed 2026-09-15** as the `coFired` assertion, with the named exemption list (`coFiring`, empty) the invariant needs — it is not blanket, because `REPETITION_NOT_UNIQUE_COMBINATION` and `H_MODEL_PATH_SUFFIX` deliberately report a true second defect. It is written against the finding COUNT per declaration rather than against `POSITIONED_CONDITION_CODES`, which is private to the checker and not the adapter's to read. **What it catches today is narrower than it reads, and its KDoc says so**: A12 answers each mutant of this population with exactly one notification, so `ours ⊆ kernel.classes` already forces at most one class and this check is implied by the disagreement assertion — it becomes load-bearing the moment A12 answers two, or `masked` gains a row. Its positive control was run rather than argued: relaxed to fire on any positioned finding it reports all 98. Two over-reports of exactly this shape were nearly shipped the same day and BOTH were caught by a family mirror rather than by this lane, the corpus carrying no model of either shape — so the residual gap is the POPULATION, not the invariant. **Acted on the same day:** the sweep's error-field kind only ever repointed OUT of a repetition, so every mutant it built sat on a non-iterating rule and the carrier behind both near-misses was unreachable by construction. A kind that repoints INTO one took the population to 116 and found two further silences immediately — a parallel level the target iterates jointly with, which the not-referenced family's containment test could not see. Widening the population is cheaper than widening a family, and it is what the pinned map depends on.

**What this slice is NOT**, stated so it does not read as delivered — **and narrowed by measurement on 2026-09-16.** Its class comparison was ONE-DIRECTIONAL: it asserted that every class the checker names is one A12 named too, never the reverse, which was read as demanding the accumulation contract F2 separates from Kernel comparison, since A12 stops at the first gate that answers. That reading was too broad by exactly one distinction. The reverse splits in two, and only the second half needs a contract: **where A12 KEYED a declaration it said everything it had to say there**, so comparing against that needs no model of A12's stopping at all, while what A12 would say about declarations it never reached does. The reachable half now asserts, as `unreached` in the class-agreement sweep.

**And the half that looked unreachable largely is not, because the premise behind it is false across declarations (measured 2026-09-16).** "A12 stops at the first gate that answers" was read as meaning A12's verdict on a multi-defect model hides its verdict on every declaration after the first — which would indeed need a contract, since A12 cannot be asked what it withheld. It does not need one: for two defects on DIFFERENT declarations, what A12 would say about a model carrying both is what it says about each alone, so the accumulated verdict is CONSTRUCTED from single-defect observations the sweep already makes. Built that way over **23 independent mutation pairs**, A12 reported the **full union of its own single-defect verdicts on all 23** — it never reported less. The stop is intra-text ([KF340](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf340)) and does not hide a sibling declaration, so there was no cross-declaration accumulation to contract about, and that direction now asserts too (`everyClassA12WouldNameOnATwoDefectModelIsOneTheCheckerNamesThere`). The union being what A12 reports is itself pinned, because it is the fact that makes a direct comparison legitimate.

**What is left is the INTRA-TEXT stop, and it is smaller than the row it came from.** Two defects inside one condition string are the one shape A12 genuinely withholds, and the checker mirrors that withholding deliberately (`resolvedFirst`, `withoutPostParseClassesBehindAPositionedDefect`). Comparing there would measure the mirror rather than the checker, which is why it stays out — a scope statement now, not an unbuilt contract. *(The other half of this paragraph — "it is not profile-scoped, no profile machinery reaches this lane" — was closed 2026-09-15 as scoped-out under [A3](#a3), with the trigger that is real under one profile delivered instead; the row above carries it.)* Note what the silence closure does and does not buy here: the checker now names SOMETHING at the right declaration on all 116, so the weaker half of the missing direction holds by measurement, and what remains unasserted is only the accumulation A12's early stop hides.

**And it supersedes [A4](#a4)'s schema change for this slice, measured rather than preferred.** A4 calls adding a consistency op kind to `corpus/case.schema.json` "one bounded schema change". It is not bounded: `CaseJson.parse` hard-requires `document`, and five reading sites across four modules and two languages parse every file under `corpus/cases/` through it — the adapter's `CorpusFiles.all()`, the interpreter's `CorpusFs` on both targets, its JS shim, the browser consumer's `qualification.ts`, and `SemanticsMapResolveTest`. A documentless case in that tree breaks a published browser qualification path. When a consistency corpus with recorded expectations is genuinely needed, it belongs in a sibling tree sharing `corpus/models/`, which keeps the one property A4 actually argued for — one model owner — without teaching five runtime readers to skip a case kind no runtime can replay.

### F1 record — the partition, and F1-a's acceptance matrix frozen before any code (2026-09-16) <a id="f1-partition"></a>

**F1's row couples five mechanisms, so it is partitioned rather than attempted whole.** [Its acceptance row](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) asks for engine selection on two verbs, a receipt contract, a mechanical artifact-identity equality against the published JS package, packaged-and-native qualification, and a source-versus-artifact doc sync. Those need different oracles — a CLI envelope test, a build-resolution assertion, a native gate — so attempting them as one slice would let each review invalidate the framing of the last, which is what the convergence gate exists to stop.

| slice | what it lands | why it is its own commit |
|---|---|---|
| F1-a | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — `model check --engine` (`kernel` or `portable`), the portable verdict's result contract, and the `:cli` → `:consistency` edge | it settles the receipt SHAPE every later slice publishes |
| F1-b | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — `rule check --engine`, the edit attribution beneath it, and with it H's mechanism (`checkEdit`/`checkEditJson`) | a candidate rule must be spliced into a model before a whole-model checker can see it — a different algorithm, so its own semantics record |
| F1-c | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — `:consistency:verifyCheckerArtifactIdentity`, in push CI: the npm name and version, the jvm jar's resolved coordinate, and the `CHECKER_VERSION` compiled into that jar, all three read out of build OUTPUTS ([the record](#f1c-projections)) | a build-resolution oracle, not an envelope one. The corpus comparison in F1's row is a behaviour check and cannot carry this claim |
| F1-d | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — the recapture → build → gate sequence ran, and packaged/native identity is measured on both engines rather than argued ([the record](#f1d-qualification)) | release-grade, and it must follow the mandatory recapture → build → gate sequence |
| F1-e | **LANDED 2026-09-16** — the source-versus-artifact doc sync, applied on explicit owner go-ahead to all three sites ([the record](#f1e-wording)) | the three false clauses are replaced: the engine/artifact conflation in rule (B), the "JVM-only" oracle and "native carries no kernel" in the license-boundary bullet, and root `CLAUDE.md`'s EUPL scope naming only the JVM distribution. Each rests on H3, never on the refuted "only CLI tool" reason |

**F1-a's foundational decision, taken before any code, because the portable path's DOMINANT outcome has no existing encoding.** `:consistency` cannot emit VALID by construction — it reports INVALID or INCONCLUSIVE until the coverage registry lifts that bar — so `model check --engine=portable` on a model A12 accepts is INCONCLUSIVE, and that is the ordinary case rather than an edge. Meanwhile the envelope's `valid` is a nullable Boolean whose *absence* already means "no verdict was established", and `exitCode()` maps a `VERDICT` family with absent `valid` to **0**. Exiting 0 on a check that established nothing is the silent-green class, so the existing encoding cannot be reused as it stands.

| candidate encoding | why it is refused |
|---|---|
| `valid:false` for INCONCLUSIVE | over-rejection at the CLI boundary, which is the one forbidden direction. The checker did not find the model invalid; it could not conclude |
| `valid:true` for INCONCLUSIVE | fabricates a green — and the checker's own report shape exists precisely to prevent it |
| `valid` absent, staying in the `VERDICT` family | honest on the wire and wrong on the process: a script reading the exit code sees a pass |

**So F1-a adds a family and an exit code, which is exactly what the envelope's design anticipates** — "a new family cannot be added without deciding, at the compiler's insistence, what it exits with". [CLI-SPEC](CLI-SPEC.md) §3b spends `0`, `1`, `2` and `70`, and none of them means *ran and established nothing*, so the new code is **3**. The payload reuses the checker's own published report verbatim — `status`, `complete`, `findings`, `diagnostics`, `checksCompleted`, `gaps` — rather than re-encoding it, because that shape was designed for this exact question and already carries the reason `status` is a string where a boolean would do: three states do not fit in two.

| acceptance item | oracle | non-goal |
|---|---|---|
| the default and `--engine=kernel` reach the same gate with the same verdict, diagnostics and exit code as today | the existing `model check` suite, unchanged | no change to the Kernel path's *behaviour*. Its envelope does gain the engine receipt, because F1's row requires requested/executed identity on all three request shapes — default Kernel included — and a receipt only the portable path carried could not be asserted there. Amended 2026-09-16, before any code, against the governing proposal row |
| `--engine=portable` on an A12-accepted model reports INCONCLUSIVE, exit 3, `verification: PORTABLE_CHECKED`, and no `valid` key | a CLI test over a committed accepted model | not VALID — the coverage registry lifts that bar, not this slice |
| `--engine=portable` on a model the checker refuses reports `valid:false` and exit 1 | a CLI test over a model with an already-measured portable rejection | no new checker family |
| the receipt names the requested AND the executed engine | the envelope's own JSON | no auto-selection on any input, ever |
| the portable engine's version is DERIVED from the resolved artifact, never a constant in CLI source | a guard that fails when the version is a literal in `cli/src/main` | the equality with the JS package's coordinate, which is F1-c's |
| **substitution fails even when both engines agree** — a mutant running Kernel under `--engine=portable` must be killed | that mutant, run against the one guard that should catch it | — |
| **no fallback, in both directions** — a portable INCONCLUSIVE never consults Kernel, and a Kernel failure never becomes a portable answer | one test per direction, asserting the EXECUTED engine rather than the verdict | no availability-, discovery-, coverage- or exception-driven selection |
| `CommandContracts` and the manifest/help/schema projections carry the new mode selector | `CommandContractsTest` and `:cli:publicContractTest` | native qualification, which is F1-d's |

**What makes this slice stop rather than grow.** A finding enters F1-a only when it violates one of those rows. An adjacent defect in a checker family, a wording gap in a doc F1-e owns, and anything at all about `rule check` go to their owning slice instead.

### F1-b record — why the portable arm must ATTRIBUTE, and the measurement that picks the mechanism (2026-09-16) <a id="f1b-attribution"></a>

**The foundational difference from F1-a, and it is forced by the over-rejection rule rather than chosen.** `rule check`'s `valid` is a verdict about *the candidate*, but the portable checker reports over a whole model, and the candidate only reaches it by being spliced in (`RuleValidator.inject`, already public for the `eval-doc --condition` path). So a portable arm that published the spliced model's report as-is would answer `valid:false` for a perfectly good candidate the moment the base model carried any ERROR finding of its own — an **over-rejection**, which is the one direction this project forbids. The Kernel arm does not have to solve this: it reports every notification from the spliced model too (`RuleValidator.checkInjected` filters nothing), but `rule check` is used on models that already pass, so attribution is implicit there and explicit here.

**Two mechanisms were available, and the probe was built to discriminate them** — set DIFFERENCE against a baseline run of the same checker, versus structural attribution by each `Finding.at` path. Measured over a 2×2 of {clean, dangling-reference} model × {two drafts}, with the Kernel put to the identical spliced bytes as the control:

| what was measured | result | what it settles |
|---|---|---|
| does splicing perturb the base model's own findings? | `vanished = 0` on all four rows; the base `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY at /Order/BillingAddress/PostalCodeFormat` survives byte-identical into the spliced run | set difference is SOUND here — `Finding` is a data class, so structural equality is the identity, and the baseline does not move under splicing |
| where is a candidate-caused finding addressed? | at the candidate's own path (`/Order/<draft name>`), while the model's stays at the model's path | path attribution would also separate the two on these rows |
| does the checker over-reject a spliced draft the Kernel accepts? | **no** — the Kernel rejects the same bytes with `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_MISSING` *and* `MVK_MISSING_ERROR_FIELD`; the checker reported one of the two | the checker UNDER-reports, the safe direction. The probe's own hand-built draft was malformed, which is why both of its drafts returned the same code and its candidate axis measured nothing — the model axis alone varied |

**Decision: the differential, not path attribution.** Both are safe on over-rejection, and both agreed on every row measured — so the tie is broken by what each one ASSUMES. The differential needs only baseline stability, which is measured above. Path attribution additionally needs the claim that *every* candidate-caused finding lands under the candidate's subtree, and the probe could not measure that at all: its draft never got past A12's error-field gate, so no row exercised a candidate finding addressed elsewhere. That is an unmeasured scope-transfer assumption, and one check run is not worth buying with one. **Re-open trigger:** measure a candidate whose defect A12 reports at another declaration's path (a cross-reference or error-target conflict is the likely shape); if none exists, path attribution becomes the cheaper equivalent and this row can be revisited.

**Consequence for the contract.** The candidate's verdict is decided by the findings the splice ADDED: INVALID when any of those `justifiesRejection`, INCONCLUSIVE otherwise, never VALID. The base model's own findings are not the candidate's verdict and must not be published as its findings — but they are not discarded either, because an author checking a candidate against an already-broken model needs to know that, so they ride the envelope as a disclosure rather than as a candidate finding.

### J record — the graph predicts, the check decides, and the one row no verdict can see (2026-09-16) <a id="j-containment"></a>

**J's acceptance asks for "agreement of candidate validity with a fresh check", and the cheapest way to get that agreement is not to re-derive validity at all.** `impactOf` delegates the whole verdict half to `checkEdit`, so agreement holds by construction rather than by comparison, and the slice's own claim narrows to one thing: which declarations a change REACHES. That leaves a clean division of labour — the dependency graph predicts, the check decides — and it is what makes the load-bearing property expressible.

**The property is CONTAINMENT, and its oracle is stronger than the claim.** Over-reporting costs a reader a minute; under-reporting means an author was told a declaration was unaffected and it broke. So `everyNewlyInvalidDeclarationIsAlsoReportedAsAffected` runs six candidate changes at once and asserts that every declaration the check found newly invalid appears in the affected list — a carrier the graph misses fails a test rather than under-reporting quietly. Its twin, `aDeclarationTheChangeCannotReachIsNotReported`, is what stops "report everything" from passing.

**Two widenings were forced by that direction, and both were measured rather than assumed.**

| what a naive graph misses | why | the row |
|---|---|---|
| a group RESCOPED | nothing in a model textually references the groups a path passes through, so equality on the referent reaches no declaration at all. An element dependency therefore reaches a referent's ANCESTORS | `aGroupScopeChangeReachesTheDeclarationsAddressingThroughIt`; the mutant narrowing the match to equality dies there and on containment |
| a bare name REBOUND | a namesake declared in the reading declaration's own group takes the reference over through the local resolution tier, touching nothing the reference names. **Both models are acceptable, so no verdict on either side marks it** — this is the one row only the graph can see, and the reason a path-keyed analysis under-reports silently | `aBareNameTheChangeCouldREBINDIsReachedWithoutReferencingIt`; the edge is emitted only where the model-wide fallback did the resolving, which the inverted mutant proves is the right side of the test |

**Two precision decisions, each with the noise it prevents.** A group's `elements` are excluded from the modified-declaration comparison, so adding a field does not report its group as re-declared and, through the ancestor rule, every declaration addressing anything inside it — the mutant that keeps `elements` in the comparison fails six of the twelve rows. And a computation does not read its own output: the transitive frontier remembers which computation wrote each field, so a reached computation is not re-reported against the field it contributed.

**An unfollowable dependency is a gap, and that is a different list from the report's own coverage gaps.** A reference resolving to nothing has no position to match a change against, so a declaration reachable only through it can be missed — a limit of the ANALYSIS, where the model also being defective there is a FINDING. An unexpanded include is the stronger case and is answered by refusing: the fact base lacks declarations that exist, so nothing can be reported as unreached and the report carries the mount alone.

**Deliberately NOT in this slice: a wire shape and a JavaScript export.** `checkEdit` earned both because its consumer is a JS editor; `impactOf`'s only named consumer is `L`, which is in-library, so publishing a JSON shape now would freeze a report whose members are still moving for nobody's benefit. Two small additions to the fact base were needed and made where they belong: `DeclaredReference` now carries the relative path as AUTHORED (a report naming only the resolution sends its reader looking for a string the model does not contain), and the type-definition id is read through one internal helper beside the family that judges it, rather than through a second copy of two wire names.

Ten mutants planted, ten dead. One of them could not be written at all: removing the unreadable-source refusal does not compile, because the prepared models are nullable and the refusal is what discharges that — a structural guarantee rather than a tested one, which is worth more.

### I-a record — the draft goes into the FACT BASE, and the JS trap that cost the lane (2026-09-16) <a id="ia-draft"></a>

**The decision that shapes this slice: where does unsaved text go?** Three candidates, and only one survives an editor's actual loop.

| candidate | why it is refused |
|---|---|
| check the text on its own | every reference in it resolves against the model, so the answer would be about text in no context — and the ONE class an editor most needs, an unresolved name, is exactly the one that needs the model |
| write it into the source and check that | correct, and it makes the consumer undo a write on every keystroke. It also forces a JSON edit on a consumer that has a string |
| **apply it to the prepared FACT BASE** | the declaration is re-declared internally, the check runs over the result, and the source is never touched |

That needed one seam — `checkPrepared(prepared, modelId, inherited)`, factored out of `check` without moving a decision — and one rule about the element: `DeclaredElement.body` is DERIVED from `declaration[kind]`, so a draft rewrites the whole declaration. Writing into the body object would leave every other reader seeing the old text, which is the silent half of that indirection.

**The attribution is inherited rather than re-derived.** `checkEdit` and `checkDraft` now share one `attribute(before, after)`, because the attribution IS the semantics — a second copy is where one of the two would quietly start blaming a baseline defect on the author. And the property this buys is the one an editor would otherwise violate on every keystroke: half-typed text is `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT`, a class A12 owns, and nothing promotes it to a rejection.

**One structural improvement fell out, and it removes a divergence rather than adding a feature.** The DSL member vocabulary now has ONE enumeration: `draftableMembers` lists what a declaration could carry, `conditionTexts` is that list filtered to what it does carry, and `replaceConditionText` writes one member of it. The names are built in one place and taken apart in one place — before, a second enumeration would have been the copy the reader and the writer eventually disagreed about.

**The measured trap, and it cost the whole JS lane: a top-level `private val` can be `undefined` on Kotlin/JS.** Taking an alternative's member name apart with a file-level `Regex`, read from a private top-level function, left **359 of 615 `jsNodeTest` rows** failing with `Cannot read properties of undefined` while **all 615 `jvmTest` rows passed**. Kotlin/JS initialises file-level properties through a per-file init function it calls at the entry of the functions that need one, and that insertion did not reach this private function. The fix holds no file-level state at all. Recorded in [BUILD-ENVIRONMENT](BUILD-ENVIRONMENT.md) beside its sibling stale-ordinal trap, because the lesson generalises past this slice: **a green `jvmTest` is not evidence about the JS target.**

Six mutants planted, six dead — but one survived its first hearing and the reason is worth keeping. Making the alternative-name parse fail closed left every carrier still analysed with the right code, because the draft then wrote a FLAT member whose key was the spelling `computationAlternatives[0].operation` and every family read it as the member it was named after. What a write BESIDE the original cannot do is make the original go away, so the discriminator is a baseline whose alternative is already broken: replacing it resolves the defect where writing beside it leaves it standing. A row asserting only that the right code appears cannot tell a replacement from an addition.

### I-b record — a location on a finding, and the identity question it forced (2026-09-16) <a id="ib-spans"></a>

**Adding `span` to `Finding` is not only a new member; it changes what "the same finding" means.** `checkEdit` and `checkDraft` attribute by set difference over findings, and a span moves whenever a character is typed EARLIER in the same text. Comparing whole findings would therefore report one defect as resolved and an identical one as introduced on a keystroke that changed nothing about it — on every keystroke an editor makes, which is the one consumer this slice exists for. So a finding's identity is its DEFECT: `Finding.sameDefect` drops the location and the attribution compares that. The row asserting it carries its own control, because a test where the span did not move would pass without the fix.

**Where a span is published, and the rule that decides.** Only where the family that judged the text already knew the offset — an unresolved reference, characters the lexer cannot read, half-typed input, a shape the grammar refuses. A finding about a declaration's STRUCTURE has no characters to point at, and a family that would have to re-derive an offset publishes none: **an editor underlining the wrong words is worse than one underlining nothing.** That is the same under-report-rather-than-invent direction the whole checker follows, applied to position instead of to verdict, and it has its own row (`aFindingAboutTheDECLARATIONCarriesNoSpanAtAll`) so "spans are populated" cannot come to mean "populated with anything".

**Three decisions inside the reference span, each with the reading it rejects.**

| decision | the rejected reading |
|---|---|
| the FIRST occurrence when a name appears twice | the last, which `associateBy` would have given — and A12 stops at the first defect in a text, so the first is the one it is talking about |
| the whole path RUN, `..` steps and repetition markers included | the last segment, or the normalized spelling: a reader edits the characters that are there |
| an empty range at the end for a parser that ran out with a bracket open | a range over the last token, which would underline something that is not the problem. Nothing is left to underline, so an editor places a caret |

**The spans cost no second tokenization.** `referencesIn` and the new `referenceSpansIn` run the same scan, and `conditionReferences` — which is NOT memoised and is called by eleven families — asks for the spans and derives the distinct names locally, so the hot path does exactly the work it did before.

Every assertion reads a SLICE of the text rather than a pair of offsets, which is both how an editor uses the span and the only form that cannot pass for a wrong range: a self-consistent wrong pair of numbers reads as right. Six mutants planted, six dead, including the two that only a slice assertion can catch — the last-occurrence key and the last-token-only range.

### L record — the partition, and its acceptance matrix frozen before any code (2026-09-16) <a id="l-partition"></a>

**L's row couples two reuse granularities with completely different costs, so it is partitioned before anything is written.** [Its acceptance](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) asks to "update analysis from an earlier immutable snapshot, reuse unaffected work, and invalidate every affected dependency", with counters that an always-full-recheck implementation fails. Two readings of "unaffected work" are available and only one is reachable now.

| slice | what it reuses | why it is separable |
|---|---|---|
| L-a | the SNAPSHOT: an admitted source set, one prepared fact base, and the baseline report — so a consumer that asks repeatedly about the same model pays for them once | sound by construction. The reused values are pure functions of inputs that did not change, so no invalidation rule can be wrong |
| L-b | per-DECLARATION results inside one check | needs every family to declare the model footprint its verdict depends on. There are 103 of them and each footprint is a hand-kept set — a plan-scale change, not a slice. *(Superseded 2026-09-17 as to the METHOD, which is what carried the price: the footprint is observed rather than declared, so there is no hand-kept set to keep — [the design](#l-b-design).)* *(Corrected 2026-09-16, both halves measured: a wrong footprint is an OVER-rejection rather than a silent under-report, and the per-FAMILY reading of this row is worthless rather than merely dearer. The probe is [below](#l-b-footprint-probe).)* *(Partitioned 2026-09-17 into L-b1/L-b2/L-b3 before any code, and reading the estate moved two matrix items — the dispatch is ONE concatenation rather than 103 sites, and L2's enforcement becomes the compiler rather than a lint: [the partition](#l-b-partition).)* |

**What decided the order is a measurement this session already holds, not a preference.** The checker's own hot path is the reference walk and the families, which is why `declaredReferenceList` was memoised after a flight recorder put 95 % of samples there — admission is not the cost. So reuse of *admission alone* would ship a counter proving reuse of a quantity that does not matter, which is precisely the "unmeasured universal speedup claim" the row's non-goals forbid.

**The consumer that makes L-a worth landing is I, and its cost is countable rather than timed.** `checkDraft` performs **one admission, one preparation and TWO checks** per call — the baseline and the drafted fact base — and an editor calls it per keystroke while the baseline side cannot have changed. A snapshot makes a keystroke cost **one check and nothing else**. That is a claim about work performed, counted, not a latency claim; no wall-clock budget is asserted, and H5 still owns the after-edit boundary it deliberately left UNSET.

**L-a's acceptance matrix, frozen here.**

| acceptance item | oracle | non-goal |
|---|---|---|
