| a snapshot's own report equals `check` of the same source set and entry | the existing check, compared on status, findings, diagnostics, `checksCompleted` and gaps | no new verdict, ever |
| `snapshot.draft(...)` equals `checkDraft(...)` on the same input | those two calls, compared | not a second draft semantics |
| an updated snapshot's report equals a FRESH full check of the updated set, across every change kind the row names — a rule/computation edit, a field-kind change, a model-config change, a type definition, an include, and a removed source | one table, fresh against incremental per row | no per-declaration reuse, which is L-b's |
| counters show reuse: a draft performs no admission and no preparation, and an update re-admits only the source that changed | the counters themselves, on operations whose reuse differs | no timing assertion. A counter counts work, and this slice claims nothing about latency |
| the OLD snapshot is unchanged after an update | the old snapshot's own report, read again after | no mutable session object |
| a poisoned snapshot is caught by the equivalence guard | a mutant that reuses a fact base belonging to a DIFFERENT source, which the equality must refuse | — |

**What makes this slice stop rather than grow.** A finding enters L-a only when it violates one of those rows. Anything about per-declaration reuse, any family footprint, and any latency number goes to L-b or to row 8.

**L-a landed 2026-09-16, and two matrix rows needed narrowing to stay honest.** `ConsistencySnapshot` / `snapshotOf` ship with `withSource`, `withoutSource` and `draft`; the public account is in [CONSISTENCY-API](CONSISTENCY-API.md#holding-one-analysis-across-many-questions--snapshotof).

The **stateless-equivalence row** compares `snapshot.draft(...)` against `checkDraft(...)` on a fixture with **no mount**, and the narrowing is a contract rather than a concession: given one, the stateless call cannot expand it, so its reference families are suspended for an incomplete fact base while the snapshot's are not. Comparing the two there would measure the unexpanded-mount rule, not the snapshot. The rows now agree on the *whole* `EditReport` rather than on a projection of it, which is strictly stronger than the frozen row asked for.

That left the mounted fact base with no oracle at all, which the **poisoned-snapshot row** then exposed: a snapshot prepared with `emptyMap()` providers passes the equivalence table (whose reports come from `workspace.check`, not from the fact base), both counter rows, and the value-semantics row. The mutant is killed by a row added for it — a draft whose reference resolves into the **mounted provider**, with the missing-field spelling as its positive control. Six mutants planted, six killed: the formula-instead-of-identity counter, `withSource` dropping the other parses, a draft re-checking its baseline, a draft re-preparing, `withoutSource` as a no-op, and the poisoned fact base.

**One documented claim was false when written and is now true.** `Reuse`'s doc said every field was an identity measurement; two readings are structural — making a snapshot always re-prepares and re-checks, and answering a draft has no admission call to measure — and the code said so with `prepared === prepared`, a tautology that would have agreed with any implementation. The doc now names which two fields are measured and which two are structural, which is the only version of that claim a mutant can test.

**L-b's method was probed rather than assumed, and the probe falsified half of it (2026-09-16).** <a id="l-b-footprint-probe"></a> The row above prices L-b as 103 hand-kept footprints with no detector for a wrong one, and the obvious rescue is to make a footprint mechanically falsifiable: mutate a member OUTSIDE it and require the family's verdict to be unchanged, mutate one INSIDE and require that it can change. A throwaway probe measured both directions over four families of deliberately different shape — `GROUP_REPETITION_COUNT` (one body member), `ELEMENT_NAME_UNIQUENESS` (tree-wide over names), `CONDITION_REFERENCES` (DSL text plus name resolution), `COMPUTATION_CYCLE` (relational) — against eight single-member edits of one e-commerce model, with each family carrying a NON-EMPTY baseline finding so that an unchanged verdict could not silently mean "read it and had nothing to say".

**The invariance direction holds; the sensitivity direction is refuted by the same matrix.** All **24 out-of-footprint cells** came back verdict-identical with no exceptions. But **4 in-footprint cells did too**, in two families and two different member kinds: changing `CalcVolume`'s `computationAlternatives[0].operation` from `[Weight] * 2` to `[Base] * 2` left `CONDITION_REFERENCES` verdict-identical although `conditionTexts` provably reads that member for a `Computation` (`draftableMembers`, source-read), and adding an unreferenced `/Order/Note` field moved neither `ELEMENT_NAME_UNIQUENESS` nor `CONDITION_REFERENCES` although both read the element-name tree. A family legitimately reads a member without its verdict moving, so a guard requiring per-member sensitivity would fail honest declarations.

**So the polarity is one-directional: a mutation sweep can prove a footprint TOO NARROW and can never certify one.** What the sweep adds is the detector the row says does not exist — and because it can only ever prove a declaration too narrow, it is a backstop rather than the mechanism, which is why [the design](#l-b-design) observes the reads instead of declaring them. That detector is worth building because a too-narrow footprint is not the harmless direction — it reuses a stale `Invalid` after the author fixed it, which is an over-rejection.

**A census then says which reuse granularity is worth the price, and one of the row's two readings is measured-worthless.** Of 131 module sources, **72 read DSL text** (`conditionTexts` / `conditionReferences` / the syntax scanners) and **48 resolve against the element-name tree**, 78 touching one or the other; the ~30 remaining families are the narrow ones. Per-FAMILY reuse therefore cannot help the dominant editor edit — typing in a rule condition invalidates roughly seven families in ten however exact their footprints are — while per-DECLARATION reuse survives it, since that edit invalidates one declaration's cell across those families and leaves every other declaration's cells valid. **L-b should be read as per-declaration only.**

**And per-declaration reuse meets one constraint inside `check`'s own composition — L-b's to settle, and a rule rather than a barrier.** `check` filters the COMPOSED finding list through four model-wide passes — the broken-error-target stop, the first-unresolvable-reference stop, the variant-filter suppression, and the post-parse class withholding — so even exact per-declaration results compose through state read across declarations. *(Corrected 2026-09-16: this said that constraint "is the accumulation contract [row 7](#stage-7) is already blocked on". It is not, and the resemblance is the trap — row 7's was about what A12 withholds, which measurement then showed it does not withhold across declarations, while this is about what OUR `check` withholds in its own composition. Row 7 closing leaves this exactly where it was, and it is L-b's own to settle.)* *(Corrected 2026-09-17, by reading all four rather than counting them: they DECOMPOSE, and "state read across declarations" overstates three of them. `declarationsWithAnUnresolvableConditionReference` filters `conditionReferences()` down to `declaredAt` and reads nothing else (`consistency/.../DeclarationGates.kt:102`); `variantFilterSuppressed` reads each element's own `variantFilter` member (`consistency/.../VariantFilter.kt:58`); `withoutPostParseClassesBehindAPositionedDefect` groups findings by their anchor `at` and never across anchors (`consistency/.../Check.kt:1264`). Only `declarationsWithABrokenErrorTarget` reads across declarations, and what it reads is one path→declaration-count multiset — its `declarationsPerPath[it] == 1` guard exempts a path carrying several declarations (`DeclarationGates.kt:74`), which is also the only population the anchor grouping can couple. So the real constraint is narrower and states itself: **cache the PRE-composition cells, never the composed report**, and maintain two cheap global aggregates over the finding list. That is a rule, not a barrier.)*

**L-b's design is DECIDED, and it is recorded reads rather than declared footprints (2026-09-17).** <a id="l-b-design"></a> The row prices L-b as 103 hand-kept footprints, and [the probe](#l-b-footprint-probe) measured that such a set is unverifiable in the only direction that matters: a mutation sweep proves a footprint TOO NARROW and can never certify one, while a too-narrow footprint reuses a stale `Invalid` after the author fixed it — an over-rejection, the one direction this checker must never take. Correctness resting on 103 acts of care with a one-directional detector is not a design, it is a standing over-rejection risk. So the footprint is **observed, not declared**: a cell records the members the family actually read while producing it, and the cell's key is a digest of that transcript. A transcript cannot be too narrow, because it *is* the read.

| the rule | why it is the rule | what enforces it |
|---|---|---|
| **L1** — a cell holds PRE-composition findings; the four passes always re-run | they are filters over the composed list, so re-running them costs `O(findings)` and never a family — the amended paragraph above | the existing equivalence oracle: an incremental report must equal a fresh full check |
| **L2** — every fact-base read goes through the tracked accessor | an untracked read *is* the too-narrow footprint, one layer down, and it is the only failure this design can still have | a lint of the shape `:consistency` already runs two of (`verifyConsistencyPurity` asks what the compiled classes link) — the enforcement 103 hand-kept sets have no way to have |
| **L3** — a derived query is itself a tracked cell, keyed per declaration | a model-wide derived value (`conditionReferences()`, the element-name tree) hands every reader a model-wide transcript and collapses the granularity back to L-a's | the census's own consumer: typing in one condition must not invalidate the 78 families that read DSL text or names |
| **L4** — no transcript, no reuse | absence has to mean work, so a family added later is slow rather than wrong | — |

**What this decision buys is that the price the row quoted disappears.** The 103 hand-kept sets were what made L-b plan-scale; a read barrier is one uniform mechanical change to the fact base's read surface plus the cache, and its correctness is carried by a lint and the equivalence oracle instead of by 103 separate judgements. L-b stays after the current order and stays sizeable — but it is no longer priced as a plan, and no part of it is descoped, which [the proposal's completion rule](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) does not permit anyway.

**L-b is PARTITIONED into three commits before any code, and reading the estate moved two things the frozen matrix had wrong (2026-09-17).** <a id="l-b-partition"></a> The [convergence gate](../CLAUDE.md) asks for the partition up front rather than after a review invalidates a framing, and the estate answers two questions the matrix could only guess at.

**The dispatch is ONE locus, not 103.** `checkPrepared` invokes the families from a single concatenation expression — 80 call sites in one function — so the barrier threads through one place. That is the fact that makes L-b a bounded mechanical change rather than a survey.

**Nothing outside `:consistency` calls a family function, and nothing outside names the checker's `PreparedModel`** (the hits in `interpreter/` are a *different* `PreparedModel`, `…dm.interpreter.PreparedModel`, in another module — checked by package, since the short name collides). So the 103 published family functions are public **by omission**, which [the ABI disposition](#stage-8-abi) already recorded for the estate at large. This is not cosmetic: a public entry point taking the *untracked* `PreparedModel` is a bypass of the read barrier by construction, so matrix item L2 cannot hold while it stands.

**And L2's enforcement improves from a lint to the COMPILER.** The matrix assigned "every fact-base read goes through the tracked accessor" to a lint. Once a family's receiver is a tracked type that does not expose the model, an untracked read does not compile — illegal states unrepresentable, which this project prefers to a runtime or build-time check. A lint survives only for the narrow residue a type cannot state: that no family file names `PreparedModel` at all, which is the one way the barrier could leak.

**The granularity crux has a concrete answer, and it is the transcript's ALPHABET.** The matrix names the risk — a family reading `resolvableElements` or the by-path index wholesale gets a model-wide transcript and collapses reuse to L-a's per-source level. The resolution is that iterating the declarations records the SET SHAPE (a digest of paths, kinds and origins) and *not* any body, while reading one declaration's body records that path alone. Typing in a condition changes a body and leaves the set shape identical, so the iteration dependency survives the keystroke and only the edited declaration's cells fall. A transcript whose alphabet did not separate those two would be correct and worthless.

| commit | what it lands | acceptance items it discharges |
|---|---|---|
| **L-b1** (foundational — takes the earlier review) — **first increment LANDED 2026-09-17** | the tracked fact base, the transcript, the declaration cursor, and the first family migrated through it. Two corrections from building it, below | L2's enforcement locus, L3's per-declaration cursor |
| **L-b2** (elaboration) — **COMPLETE 2026-09-17** | the remaining families migrated to the cursor, in batches sized by the facts and helpers they share. Its cost was measured rather than guessed, below | L2, L3 across the estate |
| **L-b3** (terminal review) — **PARTITIONED 2026-09-17, [below](#l-b3-partition)** | the cell store keyed by transcript, wired into `ConsistencySnapshot`; the four composition passes always re-run | L1, L4 |

**Building L-b1 corrected the partition twice, both in the cheaper direction.** (1) **There is no separate "reduce the surface to `internal`" step**, because Kotlin will not let a `public` function expose an `internal` receiver — so a family becomes `internal` in the same edit that makes it tracked, and the public-by-omission surface drains family by family as L-b2 proceeds. The first migration's golden diff is exactly that: `EnumSpecialValueKt` gone from the JVM dump, its extension gone from the KLIB dump, **deletions only**, which also confirms the barrier itself adds no published surface. (2) **The transcript needs no digest.** A digest was specified to key a cell; Kotlin's structural equality over the read set does the same job with no collisions and no cross-platform hash to pin, so the key is the transcript.

**One acceptance item moved out of L-b1, having been caught as vacuous before it ran.** A test asserting that an unrelated field's edit leaves a family's transcript unchanged *cannot fail* for a whole-model family: `enumSpecialValueFindings` must read every field's body to discover which fields are enumerations, so both transcripts name both paths and the assertion passes on the reading it was written to forbid. The invariance claim needs per-declaration cells and is L-b3's oracle. What L-b1 can and does lock is the transcript's ALPHABET — iterating charges the set shape and no body, reading one body charges one path, a model-wide member charges itself — each mutant-controlled (3 planted, 3 killed) on both carriers, with the module's 643 tests unchanged as the equivalence oracle.

**L-b2's cost was MEASURED before batching it, and it is far smaller than 101 unknowns.** Grepping the family files for the fact-base members they name: **84 of ~101 families read only the declarations plus `conditionLanguage`**, both of which the barrier already carries, so the bulk is a receiver change with no new tracked surface at all. The tail is small and named — 5 families add `hasBaseYear`, 3 `declaredLocales`, 2 `declaredTypeDefinitionIds`, and single families the remaining members. *Recall limit, stated because it bit once already: the grep counts a member named in a COMMENT, so it over-reports what a family needs — `EnumTextForbiddenCharacters` appeared to need `supportedCharacters` and reads it nowhere.* Sharing is equally concentrated: **6 helpers carry all cross-family use** (`declaredFieldType` 26 files, `enumerationBody` 14, `authoredMessages` 12, `waivesValueValidation` 8, `stringTypeBody` 5, `baseTypeClass` 5), each a thin reader over the body and so each a one-line bridge; the other ~33 helpers are single-file, and the ubiquitous `finding(...)` is a *per-file private* in ~31 files, needing no bridge at all.

**Batch 1 landed the enum cluster: 5 family entry points across 3 files**, `EnumSpecialValue` having gone with the foundation. Both carriers' 643 tests unchanged, and the goldens shrank another 19 lines — the surface draining family by family exactly as the correction above predicts. **`EnumComputationSource` was deliberately left out of the cluster**: it reads `declaredReferences()`, a derived model-wide query, which is the case the design record says must itself become a per-declaration tracked cell rather than a read. Migrating it as if it were an ordinary fact would hand every reader a model-wide transcript — the collapse this whole alphabet exists to prevent — so it gets its own increment.

**A bridge or an accessor lands with the batch that reads it, never ahead of it, and the reason is correctness rather than tidiness.** An unexercised accessor is an untested one: a member recording a neighbour's `ModelFact` returns the right value, leaves every verdict correct, and keys its cell on a fact the family never read. That is a silent wrong-key bug and the one class that makes reuse *unsound* instead of merely coarse — invisible to all 643 verdict tests, which is why each accessor now arrives with a row in `ReadTranscriptTest` asserting the fact it charges. Controlled: swapping `DECLARED_LOCALES` for `CONDITION_LANGUAGE` in one accessor fails that row and nothing else.

**The DERIVED-QUERY case is solved, and it needed no cell layer: push the derivation onto the CURSOR.** The design record says a derived model-wide query must itself become a tracked per-declaration cell, which read like a second mechanism to build. It is not one. `TrackedDeclaration.declaredReference()` answers for ONE declaration and charges exactly what that answer depends on — its own body, for the relative path it spells, and the set's shape, because resolving that path consults the by-path index. No other declaration's body can change it, so none is charged. The `by lazy` memo stays on `PreparedModel`, so the walk is still computed once per model and the granularity costs nothing. Its sibling `declarationAt(path)` charges the shape ALONE, leaving content to be charged if and when the body is read through the returned cursor — so a family that only asks "is anything declared here" does not pay for a body.

Controlled, and the control is the collapse itself: making `declaredReference()` charge every declaration, exactly as the model-wide `declaredReferences()` would, turns one charged declaration into four and fails on the sibling's body. A **positive control sits beside it** because the charge is recorded *before* the lookup, so the granularity assertion would otherwise pass on an implementation that found no reference at all.

**This increment's frame expanded once and was stopped rather than pushed, which is the [convergence gate](../CLAUDE.md) working as intended.** `EnumComputationSource` was the intended first consumer; part-way through it turned out to reach `bareReferenceField`, i.e. the RESOLVER surface, which is a separate foundational piece with its own migration. The half-done migration and a bridge that no longer had a consumer were reverted, and `ComputedTargetKind` — 85 lines, zero resolver references, and a user of both new accessors — took its place. **The resolver is its own increment**, and `EnumComputationSource` waits for it rather than dragging it in sideways.

**The RESOLVER was the increment to fear and turned out to be the cheapest, because of what it reads.** It is reached by 37 files, so a resolver that charged bodies would put every reference-reading family back on a model-wide transcript. Measured in its source rather than assumed: `resolveEntity` and its short-name tier consult the by-path index and the resolvable set, and read `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` — and **open no declaration's body at all**. So resolution charges the set's SHAPE plus one model flag, and a cell that resolved a reference survives every edit leaving the declarations where they are, which is every keystroke in a condition or a message. The one body read nearby, `indexFieldNameOf`, belongs to group ordering rather than to resolution.

That makes the barrier's method plain: **charge, then delegate.** The tracked resolver records the dependency and calls the existing implementation, exactly as the per-declaration reference does with its memo — the barrier's job is to observe reads, never to hold a second copy of behaviour measured elsewhere. Controlled by making the resolver charge bodies, which fails the new row and nothing else; a positive control asserts the reference really resolved, since the charge is recorded before the work.

**Correction to L-b2's measured cost: the IN-MODULE TESTS are first-class consumers of the family surface, and the first measurement missed them.** The enumeration behind "the bulk is a receiver change" established that nothing *outside* `:consistency` calls a family function, which is true and was the wrong denominator — **14 test files call one directly across 65 sites**, and the earlier batches compiled only because the enum families' tests go through `check(ModelSource(…))` instead. A batch of 8 condition/name families then failed to compile on the JS carrier's test source set, which is where it surfaced. The cost is real but small and bounded: a test-side `PreparedModel.tracked()` puts a throwaway transcript behind the call, so the verdict assertions are untouched and only the receiver moves (24 sites in this batch). **Counted from now on as part of a family's migration**, not discovered per batch.

**All 107 family entry points are migrated** — `checkPrepared` dispatches every one through the barrier and none through the untracked receiver, which is what discharges L2 and L3 across the estate rather than in principle. (The denominator is counted from that dispatch rather than from a file grep; an earlier "~111" was an estimate over top-level declarations, which counts helpers too.) *The DENOMINATOR was re-derived on 2026-09-17 and "106" was short by one; it is quoted here only from that enumeration, never carried forward.* The method the foundations bought is what makes a batch cheap: script the receiver change, then let the COMPILER name every fact-base read and every helper whose parameter no longer fits — no grep has to be trusted, and a read named only in a comment cannot be caught by mistake. Both carriers run **658 tests** with the barrier's own locks added and no verdict test changed, and the published surface has shed **500 golden lines** (394 JVM, 106 KLIB, measured against the commit before the barrier landed; pure deletions, so the barrier itself publishes nothing). A helper shared with an unmigrated family gets the body-parameter bridge (`unreadableEnvelope` moved to `kind`+`path`, both arms with live consumers); a helper private to its family simply moves with it.

**Four things learned while batching, each of which changes how a batch is chosen.** First, **a helper's charge must cover everything it READS, and "narrower than what was read" is the one direction that makes reuse unsound** rather than merely coarse. `isRepeatable` reads a body member, so it is charged as that declaration's CONTENT — not folded into the set's shape, which would have been defensible (repeatability is structural, like the variant filter) and would have required redefining the shape in the same change to stay sound. The precise charge needed no design change at all.

Second, **the "what does this family need" filter is DIRECT-ONLY, and a transitive need does not show up in it.** A batch of 8 was selected as needing nothing new; 5 had to be handed back because they reach shared helpers, one of them (`declarationsWithUnsoundDsl`) sitting on `conditionReferences()` — the condition-reference frontier — two calls away from the family that looked clean. The batch was shrunk to the 3 that compiled rather than pushed through, per the convergence gate. **A candidate list built by grepping one file is a list of leads, not a denominator**; the compiler remains the only enumeration that is actually complete.

Third, **a helper that keeps BOTH arms needs its own agreement lock, and the mutant that proves it narrows ONE arm's input.** The repetition-scope cluster was the named blocker of two shrunk batches, and its members have live consumers on both sides of the migration, so each got a shared decision core with a `PreparedModel` arm and a `TrackedFactBase` one. That makes the DECISION structurally identical and leaves the INPUTS free to diverge — one arm builds its list from `elements`, the other from the cursors — which would split the estate silently, the unmigrated families answering one way about a repetition and the migrated ones the other, on one model, with every family's own test green because each family only ever calls one arm. `RepetitionScopeArmsTest` locks the two arms whose consumers landed, and the mutant grid shows why both halves of each case are load-bearing: emptying the TRACKED arm's repeatable-group list and blanking the tracked declaration's body are each killed by the equality, while emptying the SHARED core is killed only by the positive control — an equality between two empty lists would have reported that mutation green. **Then the shrink bit the arms themselves**, which is the part worth carrying forward: two of the four arms written for this cluster (`crossesARepetitionOutside`, `operandSpec`) turned out to be read only by the families that got handed back, so they were reverted rather than left standing. The rule that a bridge lands with the batch that reads it governs an ARM exactly as it governs an accessor — a cluster is worth arming only for the members whose consumers land with them, and "it will be used next increment" is how speculative surface accretes. So two of the cluster's six members are dual-armed, five families came with them, and the remaining four sit on a resolver CHAIN rather than on a list: `starredLevelsOf` and `addressedTargetOf` need `iterationGroupOf`, which needs `resolveRelative` and `groupOfTargetField`, and `resolveRelative` needs a new barrier member for the resolvable set, charged as the set's shape. Three families (`EntityListArity`, `RangeOperandGates`, `ValueAsDateOperandGates`) are handed back behind that chain and it is the next increment — the convergence gate taken twice on one cluster rather than pushing a batch through.

**The chain then landed, and a MUTANT found the case the test was missing** — which is the increment's transferable result, because the missing case is the one the code's own comment warns about. `addressedTargetOf` composes four now-dual-armed helpers (the resolver, the star reader, `iterationGroupOf` over the relative resolver and the target-field group, and the iterated levels), and `ResolverChainArmsTest` locks it at the top as well as at two members. Five of six planted mutants died at once; the sixth — keying the chain on the DECLARING group instead of the declaration's iteration group, the defect the code comment records as having made "every family behind it yield on an operand A12 admits" — **survived**, because every row in the test carried a STAR, and a starred level is addressed either way. The case that discriminates is the unstarred operand on a declaration that does iterate the repetition, and it now exists. So: a positive control proves a row is not vacuous, and only a mutant proves the ROW SET is not. The barrier also gained its first new member since L-b1, `isResolvable`, charged as the set's shape because the resolvable set is the declarations minus what the default variant deletes; it is named for the question rather than the act, since two families already declare a local `resolves` that a member of that name would sit shadowed beside.

Fourth, **a cursor is not a data class, so the receiver change silently changes what `==` means** — and the barrier now carries the structural answer rather than a note about it. `declarations()` allocates fresh cursors per call, so two cursors for the same declaration were unequal where the `DeclaredElement` data class they replace compares equal; a migrated family comparing two declarations would have flipped answer with nothing to catch it, since the families migrated so far all compare by `path` and that had to be established by hand-reading eight files. `TrackedDeclaration.equals`/`hashCode` delegate to the wrapped element and `TrackedDeclarationIdentityTest` locks it, mutant-controlled in both directions (identity `equals` and an always-equal one both fail it). Charged nothing: which declaration a cursor stands for is the set's shape, already paid by whoever obtained it. **The same substitution also made the cursor list worth memoizing** — `iteratedLevels` and `lowestRepetitionOf` are each asked once per operand, so a fresh walk per call allocates a cursor per declaration where the untracked code read a list it already held; `TrackedFactBase` now builds them once, with the charge deliberately left outside the memoization because the transcript is what a cell is keyed on and a cached list must not hide a read (mutant: dropping the record from the memoized walk fails `ReadTranscriptTest`). And that memoization nearly voided the equality lock in the same pass, which is the transferable part: handing out the same object twice makes an identity `equals` pass, so the lock had to move to two fact bases over ONE model — the shape a draft actually holds beside its baseline — and the identity mutant was re-run there rather than assumed to still fire.

**Fifth, the batching rule that turned a per-cluster crawl into a 23-family batch: flip the entry points and the FILE-PRIVATE helpers, never a shared `internal` one.** The earlier batches moved every `fun PreparedModel.` in a file, which silently moved shared helpers too and broke unmigrated families that still call them — the reason each batch had to be small. A shared helper needs an ARM and a file-private one needs only a move, and Kotlin's own visibility says which is which, so the script can tell them apart. With that rule the whole remaining estate was flipped at once (45 files), the compiler named 68 errors in 27 files, and those files were handed back wholesale by `git checkout` plus a dispatch revert derived from what each file now declares — no judgement per family. Four model-wide accessors (`hasBaseYear`, `supportedCharacters`, `unreadableLocaleCodes`, `declaredTypeDefinitionIds`) landed with their `ReadTranscriptTest` rows and unblocked most of the rest.

**The 27 that remain are blocked on four NAMED helper clusters, counted from that compile so the next increments are partitioned before they start** — the reference readers (`bareReferenceField` 13 sites, `expandToFields` 3) — **done, six more families with them** — the repetition/index chain (`repeatingLevelsOnThePathTo` 5, `baseTypeClass` 4, `parallelVerdictOf` 2, `keyedGroupsAtOrAbove` 2, `indexFieldAt` 2, `levelClassOf` 2) and the filter-body readers (`referencesOutsideFilterBodies`, `withoutFilterBodies`, `crossedRepetitions`) — **both done, ten more families with them, locked by `IndexChainArmsTest`** — and the date chain (`aDateSlotAnswersFirst`, `readDateConstruction`, `declaredScale`), which is what remains. One cluster per increment, each with the arms lock its dual-armed members earn.

**Sixth, the batch script must not touch a file that already carries two arms — a trap that cost a rework and would have cost a silent defect.** Its three blanket substitutions (`elements` → `declarations()`, `DeclaredElement` → `TrackedDeclaration`, and the receiver rewrite) are correct for a file being MOVED and wrong for one holding a dual-armed helper, because they rewrite the UNTRACKED arm too. Worse, the handback step reverts a non-fitting file with `git checkout`, which silently discards arms written into that file in the same increment — both happened here on `OperandRepetitionScope.kt`, and the tell was a cross-family call failing to resolve rather than anything naming the cause. So the rule is: arm a file, or flip it, never both in one pass, and hand back only files the pass itself flipped.

**The prune that follows every such pass: an untracked arm whose consumers have all migrated is dead code, and the COMPILER is the only honest detector.** A scan attributing call sites to their enclosing receiver flagged eleven candidates and was wrong about several, because a property read (`it.isRepeatableGroup`) does not match a call-shaped search — the same "a grep is a list of leads, not a denominator" lesson in a new costume. Deleting each candidate and letting the build decide is both cheaper and complete — and it settled the eleven at **four dead, seven still used**, which no reading of the scan would have got right. Each removal takes its orphaned core with it (a shared body-parameter core with one caller left is indirection, not sharing) and its arms case, since an arms lock over a single arm compares one arm with itself.

**A starred operand is never typed, and nothing said so — the reference-reader increment's real finding (measured 2026-09-17).** <a id="starred-operand-never-typed"></a> `isBareEntitySpec` decides whether a text reduces to one entity spec, and a dozen families type their operand through it. Its accepted-punctuation set listed `"*"` behind a comment promising that a spec "may carry … its brackets, its path steps, and a repetition star" — and it could never admit one: the tokenizer classifies a star as `OPERATOR_SYMBOL`, which the token test refuses before punctuation is consulted. So `Claims/Amount` is a spec and `Claims*/Amount` is not, and every family behind that reader is SILENT on a starred operand.

It was found by an arms test failing on a fixture chosen to be realistic, which is worth noting: the entry was **dead data**, so no test could have caught it — a mutant putting `"*"` back survives every class in the module. The corrections are the comment, the dead entry, and `BareEntitySpecStarTest`, which locks both the mechanism (the token kind) and the consequence (the star refused where the same path unstarred is admitted) so the claim now has an enforcement locus.

**The silence is NOT widened, and the re-open trigger is explicit.** It under-reports, which is the safe direction; admitting the star would make a dozen families judge an operand shape none of them has been measured against, and widening what a checker judges is exactly the direction ["a client-side refusal needs a measured kernel basis"](../CLAUDE.md) governs. Re-open when a kernel measurement establishes what A12 types a starred operand as at each of those gates — one `rule check` grid over the affected operators, per operator, since a per-operator gate order is already known to differ.

**Completing the migration DRAINED the second carrier, and that is the part worth planning for next time.** While the estate was part-migrated a shared helper needed two arms; once the last family crossed, every untracked arm lost its last caller and the generic cores that existed only to be shared collapsed back to one implementation — `levelClassOf`'s seven-lambda walk and `parallelVerdictOf`'s six became ordinary functions on the tracked receiver again. The compiler drives that drain: delete an arm, and it either builds or names the caller. What does NOT drain by itself is the three arms tests, whose whole subject was the agreement of two carriers; with one carrier left they would compare an arm with itself, which is the vacuity their own headers warn about. Two were converted into behaviour locks over the same fixtures (`ResolverChainTest`, `IndexChainTest` — their measured outcomes are worth keeping, and no family test reaches the index comparison), and `DeclarationGateArmsTest` was deleted, its gate now having one arm and its families their own tests. **So the arms-lock cost is temporary by construction: it is scaffolding with a known removal date, and its expectations are what survive.**

**What remains on the untracked receiver is composition, not a family.** Twelve `PreparedModel` readers are still live and all of them are read either by `checkPrepared` itself before it builds the barrier (`conditionReferences`, `declaredReferences`, `declarationsWithABrokenErrorTarget`, `gaps`, `emptyFactBase`), by the barrier's own delegation (`resolveEntity`, `resolveRelative`, `isDeletedAtDefaultVariant`), or by a surface that is not a check at all (`draftedWith`, `dependencyEdges`, `ownDeclarations`). That is the intended boundary: the composition passes are outside the per-family cells by design, which is exactly what L-b3's acceptance says of them.

**And a model-wide family legitimately gets a model-wide transcript.** `TrackedFactBase.declaredReferences()` assembles the whole list from the per-declaration cursor, so every declaration it reads is charged — the transcript is wide because the family really is. What the cursor buys is ATTRIBUTION, one entry per declaration; per-declaration *reuse* then comes from keying a cell per declaration in L-b3, never from charging less than was read.

**The condition-reference frontier is crossed, and it needed one new idea: a DUAL ARM over a single decision.** `conditionReferences()` decomposes exactly like the declared reference — one declaration's body for its DSL texts, plus whatever the language and the resolution charge — so the cursor pattern transferred unchanged. What did not transfer is the gate above it: `declarationsWithUnsoundDsl()` has **ten unmigrated callers**, so its receiver cannot simply change. Both arms therefore live while the estate is part-migrated, and both call one `unsoundDslIn`, because this gate decides whether a whole class of message and error-text families says anything at all.

**That duplication has a test, because the shared core does not cover all of it.** The core makes the DECISION impossible to diverge; it says nothing about the two arms feeding it equivalent INPUTS, and if either narrowed its declaration list some of those families would go quiet and others loud on the same model with every family's own test still green. `DeclarationGateArmsTest` asserts the arms name the same declarations, with a positive control (two empty sets would satisfy the equality while proving nothing) and a mutant that narrows one arm's list to rules alone — which drops the unbalanced computation and fails, as it must.

**A batch script validates every assertion BEFORE writing any file.** One did not, hit a wrong entry-point name at the fifth of nine files, and left four already rewritten — a partial state that then has to be recovered by hand. The two-phase shape costs nothing and the recovery is the only expensive part.

**The sharpest hazard so far, and it is in the TEST-side mechanics rather than the code.** Routing test call sites through `tracked()` with a blanket regex rewrote `DeclarationGateArmsTest`'s *untracked* arm too — so the test compared one arm with itself, was exactly as green as before, and had lost the only thing it existed to check. A self-confirming failure: nothing fails, and the guard that was supposed to catch the divergence is the thing that broke. Two further rewrites in the same pass silently moved tests onto a receiver they were not written against.

The rule that follows is narrow and mechanical: **a rewrite of call sites must exempt any test that deliberately exercises BOTH arms**, and after such a pass the arms tests get their mutant re-run rather than a green build taken as evidence. Re-proved here by dropping computations from one arm, which fails as it must.

**Two shapes cover every family, and the migration is a choice between them rather than a rewrite.** A family is either **per-declaration** — `elements.…mapNotNull { element -> … }`, the common case, whose cacheable unit is the per-element body — or genuinely **model-wide** (`computationCycleFindings` is relational and has no smaller unit), cached once against its own transcript. `Snapshot.kt`'s note that per-declaration reuse "would need every family to declare the model footprint its verdict depends on" describes the design [the probe falsified](#l-b-footprint-probe) and is corrected when L-b1 lands.

**L-b3 is PARTITIONED before any code, and the sizing moved it (2026-09-17).** <a id="l-b3-partition"></a>

**What a cell can be keyed on is settled by what the families actually read, so that was measured first — and the first measurement was wrong in the direction that would have killed the slice.** A file-level grep said all 107 families walk the whole declaration set, which reads as "every family depends on every body, so cells buy nothing". That conflated two different reads, and the transcript's alphabet exists precisely to separate them: **a family's zero-argument signature makes the SET SHAPE a certainty** — it has no path to start from, so it must walk — while the BODIES it charges are only the ones it opens. `enumCategoryFindings` filters to fields by `declaredKind`, which is free, and opens only those bodies; a keystroke in a rule's condition cannot invalidate it. The grep was a file-level read of a per-function property, which is the "a grep is a list of leads" lesson wearing its third costume in one slice.

**So the per-family charge PROFILE is the sizing input, and nothing measures it today**, because `checkPrepared` shares one transcript across every family — deliberately, since a read is a property of the model rather than of its reader. Making the profile observable is therefore the first partition rather than an aside.

| commit | what it lands | acceptance items |
|---|---|---|
| **L-b3a** (foundational — takes the earlier review) — **LANDED 2026-09-17** | a registry pairing each `CheckFamily` with its producer, so `checksCompleted` DERIVES instead of being hand-listed, and each producer runs in its own transcript scope; plus the measured charge profile. Its matrix moved once, [below](#l-b3a-landing) | attribution; the duplicated denominator removed |
| **L-b3b** — **LANDED 2026-09-18**, [below](#l-b3b-landing) | the cell store keyed by (family, the facts it read → their values), wired into `ConsistencySnapshot.draft`, with `Reuse` extended and MEASURED rather than derived | L1, L4 |
| **L-b3c** — **PARTITIONED 2026-09-18, [below](#l-b3c-partition)** | the per-DECLARATION key, which is where the other three quarters of the reuse is, then the review, the public documentation of what reuse guarantees, and the guard that an added family cannot bypass a cell | L3 |

**L-b3a's acceptance matrix, frozen before code.** (1) `checksCompleted` is computed from the registry, and `CheckFamilyWiringTest`'s denominator assertion — which already fails when a constant is unwired — still passes. (2) Two families' scopes differ on one model, shown by a test, so attribution is real rather than claimed. (3) No behaviour change: both carriers' full suites stay green and the report JSON is unchanged. (4) The charge profile is measured and recorded. **Non-goals:** no cell store, no `Reuse` change, no `ConsistencySnapshot` change — a finding about any of those goes to L-b3b rather than enlarging this one.

**One thing L-b3a must NOT do, and the reason is already paid for.** The cursor list is memoized per fact base, so giving every family its own `TrackedFactBase` would rebuild 107 cursor lists per check. The scope belongs on the TRANSCRIPT — one shared fact base, one shared cursor list, a per-family bucket — which is also the honest shape: a scope says who read a fact, not which model it came from.

#### L-b3a landed, and the sizing it produced argues against its own successor — which the row's acceptance overrides (2026-09-17) <a id="l-b3a-landing"></a>

`CHECK_PRODUCERS` is now the dispatch: ninety-eight registrations, each pairing the `CheckFamily` constants it completes with its findings and its gaps, run in registration order inside its own transcript bucket. `checksCompleted` is the union of what ran plus the two families `checkPrepared`'s own composition answers for, so the hundred-and-three-constant literal beside the dispatch is gone. Both carriers: **663 tests, 0 failures**, goldens unchanged (everything added is `internal`), and the packed-Node and Chrome replays green on the regenerated corpus.

**Four mutants, four killed, each filtered to its control class** (`scripts/mutant.sh`): a registration claiming no family, and one claiming a family it does not report, die in `CheckFamilyWiringTest`; `whileProducing` not switching the bucket dies in `FamilyChargeProfileTest`, because every producer then reads into the composition's bucket and none can key a cell; emitting `checksCompleted` in set-iteration order again dies in `ParityCorpusTest`.

**The attribution guard is what makes ninety-eight hand pairings safe, and it was already sitting in the estate.** A mis-pairing is invisible to every other test: the derived `checksCompleted` is right however the pairs are scrambled, because the UNION does not care, and each family's own test calls its function directly and never asks who claims it. `CheckFamilyWiringTest` already owns a `(family → code, path)` table for a model that breaks one declaration per family — so running ONE registration against that model and asking which rows it carries settles the pairing mechanically, in both directions. The forward direction catches a family claimed by a producer that does not report it (under-reporting); the inverse catches a family reported by a producer that does not claim it, which is the unsound one, because that cell would be keyed on the wrong reads.

**Acceptance item 3 was wrong and is amended: the report JSON DID change, and the reason is a root-cause fix rather than a concession.** `checksCompleted` is a `Set<CheckFamily>` in the API and a JSON ARRAY on the wire, so something has to decide the order — and until now that was the order a literal in `checkPrepared` happened to list its members. The registry reshuffled it and the parity corpus diffed on element 4. `ReportJson` now emits the enum's own declaration order, which is the only candidate that does not couple these bytes to something unrelated, and the corpus was regenerated: **the change is a pure permutation of that one array in all ten cases and nothing else**, proved by comparing both revisions' parsed documents with `checksCompleted` sorted. The gap order also follows the registry now rather than a second hand-written sequence; no corpus case observes it, which is stated as the measurement it is rather than as a claim about every model.

**And the sizing, measured on the landed dispatch rather than on a probe's imitation of it.** Eight declarations, ninety-eight producers; bodies charged per producer `{0:6, 1:10, 2:3, 4:11, 6:1, 8:67}` — **sixty-seven of ninety-eight charge every body**, and the ninety-eight producers hold only **nineteen distinct transcripts** between them. The number the L row actually asks about is per-edit: a keystroke in one declaration's body fells **69–79 of 98** cells and spares **19–29**, the editor's own case (typing in a rule's condition) being 75 felled and **23 spared**. So per-family cells reuse roughly a quarter of the family work, not most of it.

**That measurement recommends deferring L-b3b, and the recommendation is WRONG — checked before acting on it.** The case for deferral was that no ratified target needs the cell store: [the keystroke ceiling](#keystroke-ratification) is `1.0×`, L-a already measures 0.474–0.502× against it, and tightening a bar toward a measured implementation is the failure the six-point gate exists to prevent. All true, and all about LATENCY. [L's acceptance](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) asks for something a latency number cannot satisfy: *"Counters must show reuse on an independent-declaration edit and recomputation of affected declarations; **an always-full-recheck implementation fails the reuse control**"* — and L-a is exactly an always-full-recheck of the drafted model, reusing the admission and the preparation and re-running every family. So the reuse control is a FUNCTIONAL clause, the deferral premise answered a different question, and 23-of-98 spared cells is a counter that demonstrably shows reuse. L-b3b is required. *(The generalizable half: "no ratified bar needs this" is an argument about one acceptance column, and a row with a functional clause beside its performance clause will look satisfied from the wrong column.)*

**A record-keeping defect found while reading the matrix, fixed rather than worked around.** The L-b partition rows cited acceptance items "L1, L4, L5, L6" and the design table they refer to has **four** rules, so two of those citations had no referent at all — a resolvable-looking citation pointing at nothing, which is the class [`docs/CLAUDE.md`](CLAUDE.md) warns is permanent because nothing flags it again. The four rules now carry explicit **L1**–**L4** labels and the citing rows name only what exists. *Searched:* `grep -n 'L1\b\|…\|L6\b'` over this file — five sites, all read, two wrong.

**One lead for L-b3b, recorded as a lead.** The sixty-seven all-bodies producers are the population worth understanding, and the obvious hypothesis is that they reach the model-wide `declaredReferences()`/`conditionReferences()` readers instead of `TrackedDeclaration.declaredReference()`, which charges one declaration. *Searched:* `grep -rln 'declaredReferences()\|conditionReferences()' consistency/src/commonMain` — 24 files, of which seven are not families (`Preparation`, `Dependencies`, `Impact`, `Check`, `DeclaredReference`, `ConditionReference`, `TrackedFactBase`). That is a file-level count of a per-function property, which is the exact mistake [the paragraph above](#l-b3-partition) records, so it sizes nothing: what settles it is the per-producer transcript, which L-b3a now makes readable. Check it there before moving any family.

#### L-b3b landed: the cell store, and what a quarter of the family work is worth (2026-09-18) <a id="l-b3b-landing"></a>

A cell holds one producer's PRE-composition findings and gaps plus the VALUE every fact it read had; a later check over a nearby model reuses that cell when each of those facts compares equal, and runs the producer otherwise. `ConsistencySnapshot` keeps the cells its own report was built from and `draft` hands them to the drafted check, so a keystroke pays only for the families that could have changed. `Reuse` gained `families: FamilyReuse(reused, recomputed)`, measured from the store's own decisions rather than derived from anything.

**Soundness needs no knowledge of what any family does, which is the whole point of observing reads.** A producer is a pure function of the facts it reads through the barrier, so if each of those facts has the same value in another model the execution is identical — same branches, therefore the same reads, therefore the same output. Every fact in the alphabet was audited for that property rather than assumed: resolution reads the by-path index, the resolvable set and `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` and opens no body; `isRepeatableGroup` reads `repeatability` and is duly charged as CONTENT (its own KDoc says why); `Reference.Resolved` carries a path and nothing else, so nothing learned from resolution escapes the shape. The one place that needed care is the shape's VALUE — bodies out, resolvable paths in, and both halves argued at `setShapeValue`.

| the figure | measured |
|---|---|
| re-typing a rule's condition | **24 of 98** families reused, 74 recomputed |
| re-typing text the rule already had | **98 of 98** reused — the validator's own ceiling |
| a refused draft | 0 / 0, there being no check to count |

**The ceiling row is what makes the partial row mean anything, and it was added because the partial row alone cannot.** A comparison that was subtly too wide — the shape value dragging bodies in, a member left out of the projection — would fell cells on an unchanged model too, and 24-of-98 reads the same either way. The unchanged-model row reaches 98, so the store is narrow because it is right rather than by accident.

**What the figure says about the next partition.** 24 % is the per-FAMILY ceiling and the census already predicted it ("typing in a rule condition invalidates roughly seven families in ten"); the families that fall are the ones that read the edited declaration, and 67 of 98 read every declaration. The rest of the reuse is behind a finer KEY — one cell per family per declaration — which is [L3](#l-b-design)'s rule and is L-b3c's work, not a change to the mechanism landed here. Nothing about the store changes when the key does.

**A mutant SURVIVED, and what it exposed is that most of the key is unreachable from the only consumer.** Zeroing the resolvable half of the shape value — `to resolvableByPath.keys.toSet()` → `to emptySet()` — passed every end-to-end row. The reason is not a missing test so much as a missing *consumer*: cells are carried across a DRAFT, and a draft changes one text member of one declaration, so no model-wide member, no variant filter and no duplicate path can move underneath them. The whole key except `DeclarationContent` and the shape's rows is therefore correct-but-unexercised, which is exactly the shape of a defect that ships.

So the controls were written at the level where the claim is made rather than deferred to the consumer that could reach it. `FamilyCellReuseTest` gains the variant-filter row: an `ONLY` filter on a FIELD deletes it ([KF305](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md)) while leaving its path, name, kind, id and origin untouched, so the shape's ROWS are provably identical across the pair and only the resolvable half can tell the models apart — the row then asserts that every producer blind to that field's body loses its cell, and the mutant dies. `CheckCellKeyTest` closes the rest as a 7×7 grid: for each `ModelFact`, a model pair moving that member must move THAT fact's value and leave the other six alone, so a mapping reading the wrong member and a fixture moving two members fail different halves of one comparison. It also locks the two content rules a path cannot express — a second declaration at one path is covered, and a body change never moves the shape.

**One thing landed that was not in the matrix, and it is a fix rather than a widening.** `ConsistencySnapshot.of` prepared the entry model, then called `workspace.check`, which prepared it AGAIN — so the snapshot's report came from a fact base the snapshot did not hold. That was merely wasteful before and is wrong with cells, because the cells would then describe a different object from the one a draft is applied to. It now prepares once and checks that model, with the workspace still owning the unknown-entry envelope; the existing equivalence rows are the oracle, and they pass.

**An internal type now appears in the JVM golden, deliberately.** A private constructor reached from a companion lowers to a `public synthetic <init>`, so `CheckCells` is named there — the first `internal` type in this dump to be. Nothing can call it (javac refuses a synthetic member, Kotlin a private one) and the KLIB golden does not carry it; the alternatives were a mutable field against this class's value semantics, a public wrapper advertising a name with no meaning, or re-running the check the cells came from. The reason sits at the field rather than only here.

Both carriers: **673 tests, 0 failures**; the packed-Node and Chrome replays green with no report bytes changed.

**Six mutants, six killed — two of them only after the control each needed was written, which is the increment's transferable result.** Killed outright: a cell reused without revalidating its facts; a shape value comparing `elements` and so dragging every body in; a member mapping reading the wrong member. Survived first: the resolvable half zeroed (above), and a content index built with `associate` instead of `groupBy`. **The second survivor is worth its own sentence, because the control that missed it looked right.** `associate` keeps the LAST entry for a duplicated key, and the row edited the SECOND declaration at the duplicated path — so the mutant's value moved too. One edit cannot discriminate against an index that keeps one body; the row now edits both declarations and requires each to move the value. *Generally: a control over a duplicate-key index has to move every member of the duplicate, because a mutant that keeps one of them agrees with a control that only ever changes that one.*

#### L-b3c is partitioned before any code, and one measurement decided the shape (2026-09-18) <a id="l-b3c-partition"></a>

**Why the 67 wide producers are wide, measured rather than assumed.** A temporary marker on `declaredReferences()` and `conditionReferences()` split the population on the 8-declaration fixture: **31 producers are already narrow**, **25 are wide because they reach a model-wide derived reader**, and **42 are wide by their own walk** — they open every declaration's body themselves. The two halves need different work, which is the whole reason to measure before starting: the 42 are fixed by moving the WALK out of the family, and the 25 need the per-declaration reader ([L3](#l-b-design)) as well, because a family invoked per declaration that still asks for the whole reference list charges the whole model anyway.

**The prize, counted rather than timed.** With the walk in the dispatcher and a cell per `(producer, declaration)`, editing one declaration fells 98 of 98×8 cells rather than 74 of 98 — **~87 % reuse against today's 24 %**, and a keystroke costs about 98 producer-declaration units instead of ~592. That is the reading of L-b's row title ("per-DECLARATION results inside one check") the census already argued for, and the reason per-family keying was only ever the instrument.

| commit | what it lands | acceptance |
|---|---|---|
| **L-b3c-1** — **LANDED 2026-09-18, [below](#l-b3c-1-landing)** (foundational — takes the earlier review) | the per-declaration producer shape, the dispatcher's walk, the `(producer, declaration)` cell key, and a proof set of families migrated to it | the matrix below |
| **L-b3c-2** | the remaining own-walk families, in batches | L3 for the 42 |
| **L-b3c-3** | the 25 model-wide-reader families narrowed to the per-declaration readers | L3 for the 25 |
| **L-b3c-4** (terminal review) | the review, the public account of what reuse guarantees, and the guard that an added family cannot bypass a cell | — |

**L-b3c-1's acceptance matrix, frozen before code.** (1) A per-declaration producer is asked once per declaration by the dispatcher, and the report's findings and gaps keep the order they have today — producer-major, declarations in declaration order, which is what a family's own walk already produced. (2) The cell key distinguishes two declarations at ONE path; a model A12 refuses may declare them, and a key that collided would drop one declaration's findings from the report rather than merely lose reuse. (3) A migrated family's cell falls when ITS declaration's body changes and survives when another's does, shown per family rather than in aggregate. (4) No behaviour change: both carriers green, report JSON unchanged. (5) `checksCompleted` still derives, and the wiring guard's per-producer attribution still holds for a producer the dispatcher now drives. **Non-goals:** the other ~90 families, the model-wide readers, and any latency claim.

**One thing L-b3c-1 must NOT do.** The dispatcher's own `declarations()` call charges the SET SHAPE, and it must not be charged to the per-declaration buckets — a per-declaration cell that recorded the shape would fall whenever any declaration was added or removed, which is most of what an editor does and would undo the partition's whole point. The set membership is handled by the walk itself: a declaration that is gone is never asked for, and a new one has no cell. So the shape belongs to the composition's bucket, and that is a soundness claim rather than a tuning choice — it holds only because a per-declaration family's verdict depends on its own declaration and not on the set, which is exactly what the migrated families must be checked for one at a time.

#### L-b3c-1 landed: the per-declaration key, and the ancestor cost nobody had priced (2026-09-18) <a id="l-b3c-1-landing"></a>

`CheckProducer` is now a sealed pair. A `ModelWide` registration is asked once per check, as all ninety-eight were; a `PerDeclaration` one is asked once per declaration by `schedule(tracked)`, which walks `declarations()` **in the dispatcher** and hands each cursor in. A cell is keyed by `CellKey(producer, DeclarationKey(path, amongSamePath)?)`, the transcript buckets by that key, and the four condition-text families — lexicality, completeness, connective agreement, grammar — are migrated to it: the dominant editor case, and the four that read nothing but their own declaration's body plus `conditionLanguage`.

The mechanism landed **before** any family moved, and the whole suite was green with zero per-declaration registrations — which is the cheapest possible proof that the dispatcher's walk reproduces a family's own walk, order included.

| the figure, on the 8-declaration fixture | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| cells scheduled | 98 | **126** = 94 model-wide + 4 × 8 |
| a keystroke in a rule's condition, **re-parsed** model | 21 of 98 reused | **45 of 126** — 21 model-wide, 24 per-declaration |
| the same keystroke through `snapshot.draft` | 27 of 98 | **51 of 126** |
| a draft that retypes the text it already had | 98 of 98 | **126 of 126** |
| bodies charged per cell | `{0:6, 1:10, 2:3, 4:11, 6:1, 8:67}` | `{0:6, 1:42, 2:3, 4:11, 6:1, 8:63}` |

The two "before" reuse figures are **derived**, not separately measured: the 94 model-wide cells were measured in place, and each migrated family charged all eight bodies before it moved, so each had exactly one cell that read the edited body and would have fallen.

**Per-declaration cells reuse 24 of 32 — and the eight that fall are the surprise worth the row.** A GROUP's `body` nests its `elements`, so a rule's text is part of its containing group's body too: editing one declaration moves TWO content values, its own and every ancestor's. Each migrated family therefore falls at exactly `/Order` and `/Order/CodeRequired`, measured per family rather than in aggregate, and the root group's cell falls on every keystroke in the model. That is sound and it is the ceiling on what a per-declaration key can buy on a deep model — priced here rather than discovered while sizing L-b3c-2.

**The two keystroke rows differ for a mechanical reason, and it is not a defect.** `draftedWith` rebuilds only the edited element's declaration and substitutes it into `elements`; the containing group's JSON object is untouched, so under a draft the ancestor's content value does not move and six more model-wide cells survive. A cell reused on that basis is sound with respect to the fact base it is validated against — the group's body genuinely is the same object. Whether a DRAFTED fact base is a faithful stand-in for the re-parsed edited model is a separate, pre-existing question about `draftedWith`, and it is answerable: no family reads a group's `elements` at all. *Searched:* `grep -rn '"elements"' consistency/src/commonMain` — two matches, both read, neither a family (`Preparation.descendGroup`, which builds the element list, and `Impact`, which strips the key before comparing a group's configuration). Recall limit: a family that enumerated a body's keys generically rather than naming one would not appear in that search.

**One lead for L-b3c-2, from that same search.** `Impact` already excludes `elements` when it compares a group's body, so the precedent for a narrower group content value exists in the estate. Comparing `body - "elements"` for a GROUP would remove the ancestor cost entirely — the root group's cell would survive a descendant edit — and its soundness rests on the enumeration above rather than on a new argument. It is deliberately NOT in this commit: it changes the store's content value, which is L-b3b's mechanism, and this partition's acceptance says nothing about it.

**Two controls were written because the migration made them reachable, and neither is a variation on an existing row.** `CheckCellKeyTest` gains the twinned-path row: two rules named alike carry defects of two different families at ONE path, so a key made of the path alone would collapse two cells into one map entry and DROP one twin's code from the report — which is the acceptance item's own discriminator, and it is a lost finding rather than lost reuse. `FamilyChargeProfileTest`'s body-implies-shape invariant split in two, by producer kind: a model-wide family still charges the set it found every body in, and a per-declaration family must charge **no** set shape at all, which is the soundness condition of the finer key rather than a second opinion about it. Both halves carry their own vacuity control, because each is a negative existential that a registry with nothing in it would satisfy.

**Corrected 2026-09-18, one batch later: the per-declaration key was INCOMPLETE, and a vacuity guard is what said so.** A cursor's shape members — `name`, `kind`, `id`, `origin` — are free, which is the whole point of charging the set once; a family that WALKS the set therefore charges the shape and covers every row it reads off a cursor. A family the dispatcher hands one declaration to never walks, so nothing in its bucket said WHICH declaration it answered about, and the key carries only the path: a verdict that turned on `declaredKind` would have been reused for a declaration whose kind had changed at that path. Two models, one declaring `/Order/Check` as a `Rule` and one as a `Computation` with a byte-identical body, are indistinguishable to such a cell. The dispatcher now charges [FactRead.DeclarationRow] to the cell it is about, so the cell is a function of what it read again, and `CheckCellKeyTest` holds that pair. **What found it was `FamilyChargeProfileTest.everyProducerChargesSomething` failing** on the next batch's first family — a kind-filtering family returns before reading anything, so its cell keyed on the EMPTY transcript and would have been reused unconditionally. That guard was written to refuse a producer sharing a cell with every other silent producer; it caught an unsoundness instead, which is the argument for refusing a vacuous key outright rather than tolerating it.

**A zero-argument family form deliberately does not exist in production.** Twenty-two test call sites asked `conditionLexicalFindings()` with no declaration; they now go through `everyDeclaration(TrackedFactBase::…)` in the test source set. A convenience overload in main source would have to walk the set, charge the shape, and undo the key it was migrated for — so the shape lives where only a test can reach it.

Both carriers: **676 tests, 0 failures**; `ParityCorpusTest` green, so the report JSON is unchanged byte for byte. `CheckFamilyWiringTest`'s attribution still holds for a producer the dispatcher now drives — all four migrated families are rows in its `(family → code, path)` table, so the assertion is not vacuous there.

**Six mutants, and the two that survived are the increment's real result.** Each was run filtered to its control class (`scripts/mutant.sh`, `:consistency:jvmTest`). Killed outright: the dispatcher charging the set shape inside a per-declaration bucket (`FamilyChargeProfileTest`); the ordinal dropped from `DeclarationKey` (`CheckCellKeyTest`); the declaration dropped from `CellKey` altogether (`FamilyCellReuseTest`); only the first declaration asked (`CheckFamilyWiringTest`).

**The survivor: `declarations().reversed()` in the dispatcher.** It passed `ParityCorpusTest` and every family test in the module — 675 tests — because a corpus model carries at most one defective declaration per family, so no case can see a reversed walk *or* producer-major reporting. The only failure in a full-lane run was `FamilyCellReuseTest`'s, incidentally, because the paths in its diagnostic list came back the other way round; that is a control over which cells fall whose assertion happens to be a list, not a control over the report's order. So acceptance item (1)'s advertised order had **no enforcement locus at all** — an invariant this partition claimed and nothing held. `DispatchOrderTest` now holds it, on the smallest fixture that can fail: two declarations per family so a reversed walk shows, two families so producer-major shows.

**And that new control's first fixture was itself confounded, which is the transferable half.** Its four rules were named `EndsEarlyFirst`, `MixesFirst`, `EndsEarlySecond`, `MixesSecond` — names that sort into their family groups — so a mutant reporting declaration-major *by path* produced the identical list and survived, while the KDoc asserted the interleaving ruled that out. The names now interleave alphabetically as well as in the model, and both reorderings die. *Generally: when a fixture's identifiers participate in the ordering under test, the names encode part of the answer, and the assertion cannot tell you so.*

### F3 record — the partition, one blocked acceptance clause, and F3-a's matrix frozen before code (2026-09-16) <a id="f3-partition"></a>

**F3's row names THREE consumers with three different estates, so it is partitioned before anything is written.** [Its acceptance](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#7-implementation-slices-and-finite-acceptance-matrices) asks that "published dmkits expose portable profile verdicts; schemakit can use that gate under its purity guard; interpreter consumers can obtain an explicit consistency preflight". Those share a checker and nothing else: the first is a JVM library surface, the second is a build-guard boundary, the third is a new integration product with its own published-closure rule.

| slice | the consumer | what it needs that the others do not |
|---|---|---|
| F3-a | a published-dmkits caller holding DM-JSON and a `ModelResolver` | a dmkits-shaped result — `ok()`, `diagnostics()`, a gate receipt — and a diagnostic ORIGIN for a finding no kernel made |
| F3-b | schemakit, under `verifySchemakitPurity` | that the gate is reachable without widening schemakit's scope or its compiled closure |
| F3-c | an interpreter consumer wanting a static precondition | an explicit preflight distinct from execution preparation, in a façade neither core's package contains ([§1](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md)) |

**One acceptance clause is BLOCKED and is not this partition's to satisfy.** The row asks that "a supported accepted model yields portable VALID". `:consistency` cannot emit `Valid` by construction — `ConsistencyStatus` is a sealed interface with `Invalid` and `Inconclusive` alone, which is the ratified reading of [CONSISTENCY-API](CONSISTENCY-API.md)'s first section: a checker with no coverage registry cannot certify what it did not check, and inventing a `Valid` here would be an over-claim in the one forbidden direction. So every F3 slice reports INCONCLUSIVE on an accepted model, with the checker's own coverage evidence beside it, and the clause stays open against the coverage registry rather than against F3. The row's neighbouring instruction — "do not leave complete supported checking labeled merely STRUCTURAL_ONLY" — IS satisfied, and separately: `Verification.PORTABLE_CHECKED` exists for exactly that and is what these consumers stamp.

**The coverage registry is far smaller than the artifact this plan assumed, because its denominator is A12's CODE vocabulary and not the family list (measured 2026-09-16).** VALID needs one thing: that every code A12 could report on a model is either reported by a family that ran or cannot arise from stored DM-JSON. That is a per-CODE question, and [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md) has been answering it per code for months — 306 rows, each carrying a disposition. Read against it, the registry is not an artifact to build but a residue to drain:

| the denominator | size | what it leaves open |
|---|---|---|
| the ledger's four sources | 306 rows | **at most 20** are neither checker-reported nor marked unreachable from stored DM-JSON |
| `NoMetaModelCheckErrorType` — the fifth source, in none of those rows | 26 codes | 7 the checker reports, 1 dead on a cited path, 1 measured and deliberately unreported, so **17** unclassified |

**"At most 20" is an upper bound and is stated that way on purpose**, because it was derived mechanically — the disposition column filtered for neither `checker-reported` nor `unreachable` — and the ledger's bucket vocabulary was written for the AUTHORING story rather than for a checker reading stored JSON. Hand-reading shrinks it: `MVK_SEVERITY_MISSING` ("none of absent/null/empty fires this code"), `MVK_CATEGORY_REF_OP` ("this code is never reported" — it is a message label) and `MVK_NAME_RULES_DUPLICATE` (A12 throws before the metamodel rule can fire) are three of the twenty that are not gaps at all. So the honest statement is a **bound of 37 rows, most of which a read will dismiss**, against a blocker previously priced as a per-family artifact across 103 families.

**And the fifth source now has the keeper the ledger said it could not have.** That paragraph records the obstacle as needing "a build dependency on the codegen tool", since `MvkLedgerTest` reaches its enums reflectively off the kernel classpath and this one is not on it — and it asks for the count to be re-derived by hand, which the same paragraph shows going wrong three times. It needs no classpath: the constants are read from the consult-only sibling checkout as SOURCE, which is this project's primary kernel oracle by standing rule. `CodegenTierCodeDenominatorGuardTest` does that, pins the tier as a SET rather than a count so a change names the code that moved, and skips when the sibling is absent.

**The registry's other half — which FAMILY covers a code — is derivable too, and already built.** VALID needs more than an empty residue: it needs the covering family to have RUN, which is the question `checksCompleted` answers. `CheckerReportedCodes.bySource()` already maps each code to the checker sources that state it, and a family is one source file, so no per-family `covers` declaration has to be authored at all — the artifact this plan priced across 103 families is a join between two things that already exist and are CI-locked. What remains is genuinely the residue above, not the registry.

**The residue is now DRAINED, and the criterion that drains it is sharper than the one this plan had been using (2026-09-16).** <a id="coverage-residue"></a> The bar was read as *every code A12 could report is reported by a family that ran*. That is stronger than VALID needs and it inflates the blocker: what a VALID verdict must exclude is a model **A12 rejects that comes back non-INVALID**. A code that can only fire *on top of* a code the checker reports therefore cannot produce a false VALID, however unreported it is — the model is already INVALID on the other code. `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_INVALID` is exactly that case and was counted as a gap under the old reading: it is reachable ([KF331](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf331)) but only ever arrives on top of `MVK_INVALID_INDEX_FIELD`, which the checker reports from `GroupOrderingReferences`.

Re-derived mechanically: **307 ledger rows = 306 codes + one section-summary row**; 207 checker-reported, 77 marked unreachable, leaving **22 codes** — not the "at most 20" stated above, which was low. All 22 are dispositioned below.

| disposition | count | codes |
|---|---|---|
| **never reported at all** — a message LABEL interpolated into another code's text ([KF354](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf354)), or nothing fires it ([KF291](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf291)) | 4 | `MVK_CATEGORY_REF_OP`, `MVK_FIELD_REF_OP`, `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_REF_OP`, `MVK_SEVERITY_MISSING` |
| **not reachable through `checkConsistency`** — a different service's door (`getChangesAsSpecifiedByRules`, `ChangeRuleSupport`), an H-model-only branch, or pre-empted by a throw or a sibling code | 6 | `MVK_MAX_GROUP_RESTRICTION_TO_ONE`, `MVK_MAX_LFDNR_RESTRICTION_TO_ONE`, `MVK_ENTITY_MOVE_IN_A12_MODEL_CONDITION`, `MVK_ENTITY_MOVE_IN_A12_MODEL_ERRORTEXT`, `MVK_NAME_RULES_DUPLICATE`, `MVK_CONDITION_WITH_WILDCARD` |
| **co-fires with a reported code** — harmless under the sharpened criterion | 1 | `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_INVALID` |
| **DELIBERATELY not replicated** — the law is one ~806-line truth-propagation traversal (`IterationNotValidVisitor`); F4 is the gate | 2 | `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`, `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_INVALID` |
| **parser catch-all, PROBED and not reached** — 12 malformed conditions across five failure kinds drew a specific code every time, never either catch-all (below) | 2 | `MVK_CONDITION`, `MVK_COMMON_PARSER_ERROR` |
| **METAMODEL axis, unmeasured** — no JAVA raiser in kernel `30.8.1`, which [KF418](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf418) measured is NOT dormancy, because the metamodel raises codes absent from the checkout | 7 | `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_VALID`, `MVK_INVALID_PREDEFINED_TYPE`, `MVK_NOT_ON_ERRORFIELD_PATH`, `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_CMP_DATE_ALLOWED_NO_FIELD`, `MVK_RULE_GROUP_IS_EMPTY_OR_INVALID`, `MVK_CALCULATION_FIELD_AND_NO_CALCULATION_RULE`, `MVK_ERROR_ITERATION_UNCHECKED` |

**Read the two halves together before scheduling either: the codegen residue is NECESSARY but NOT SUFFICIENT, and the binding constraint is the METAMODEL axis.** Seven ledger codes sit on it ([KF415](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf415) — a zero raiser count over the kernel's Java sources is not dormancy, because the metamodel raises codes that appear in no file of the checkout; its witness is [KF418](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf418), which measures `MVK_INTERNAL_VALUE_INVALID` firing with a zero count everywhere. *Citation corrected 2026-09-16: this named KF418 for the qualification, which KF418 does not carry — it is the four-character enum predicate — so a reader following it landed on the measurement rather than the reasoning. The blocker itself re-verified and stands.*), and that axis is [row 5](#stage-5)'s named capability blocker, which nothing in this estate reaches. So finishing every remaining codegen row would still leave VALID blocked. The codegen rows are worth doing — each one is a live false-pass route, as the repeatability fix showed — but they must be scheduled as *defect removal*, never as the path to lifting the VALID bar.
