A session that grinds them expecting VALID to follow will finish the tier and find the bar exactly where it was.

**So VALID is blocked by 11 codes, not by an unbuilt registry — and they fall into three kinds with three different owners.** The 11 are the last three rows. Seven are on the **metamodel axis**, which is [row 5](#stage-5)'s named capability blocker and nothing in this estate reaches; two are a **deliberate scope decision** already taken and recorded, so they are a disclosed permanent under-report rather than unfinished work; and two were **measurable here, so they were measured rather than parked**.

**The parser probe: 12 malformed conditions through `rule check --engine kernel`, and neither catch-all was reached.** Every input drew a specific code — `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` (8), `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` (2, both unbalanced parentheses), `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT`, `MVK_LEXER_STANDARD_ERROR`. **This is an enumeration with its recall limit stated, not a proof**: a catch-all is a negative existential, and 12 authored inputs cannot close it. What it does establish is that the ordinary failure kinds a stored model carries are answered specifically, so neither catch-all is a *likely* false-VALID route.

**And the probe corrected a claim this plan carried: the checker reports at least FIVE parser codes, not four.** `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` was in none of the four-code lists here, and an unclosed parenthesis is the commonest malformation there is. Run through both engines on the same six inputs, the portable engine returned **`valid:false`, exit 1, on every one** — which is the criterion that actually matters, and it held. Two code-level DISAGREEMENTS showed up and are not VALID-blockers, because both engines still reject: `FieldNotFilled()` draws `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` from A12 and `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID1` from the checker, and a lone `#` draws `MVK_LEXER_STANDARD_ERROR` from A12 and `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` from the checker (the documented `#` gap is in the illegal-CHARACTER family; the grammar family rejects it anyway).

**The fifth source is NOT drained, and its shape is different.** `NoMetaModelCheckErrorType` declares 26 codes, of which `CodegenTierCodeDenominatorGuardTest` pins **7 as checker-reported**, leaving **19**. These cannot be dispositioned by reading the ledger, because they have no ledger rows — and the tier's own "unreachable" claim was already measured WRONG once (the scope-boundary note at the top of [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md)), so inheriting it is not available. The gating question was **does A12's `checkConsistency` report `NoMetaModelCheckErrorType` codes at all, or only its codegen path?** — and it is already answered YES by a lock this estate holds: `validate.laws.GroupIndexAndSortReferenceLawsTest` expects `MVK_INVALID_INDEX_FIELD,MVK_INDEX_FIELD_INVALID` from the real kernel for a semantic index over a mis-placed index field, with a no-semantic-index control drawing only the first. `MVK_INVALID_INDEX_FIELD` is a `NoMetaModelCheckErrorType` member, so the tier is reachable through `checkConsistency`, measured, with a within-model control — and that same lock is what settles the co-firing row above. It is now also settled DIRECTLY rather than inferred from a row about index fields — [KF425](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf425), locked by `validate.laws.CodegenTierReachedByCheckConsistencyLawsTest`: an unparseable `modelConfig.timeZone` draws `MVK_INVALID_TIME_ZONE` from `checkConsistency`, with the same model at `Europe/Berlin` admitted as the within-model control. **And one reachable class settles the whole tier rather than one code**, because `NoMetaModelChecks` exposes exactly one public entry point (`check()` over `CodeGenData`) and raises all 26 from private methods beneath it — so the question was never per class but whether that single door opens on the consistency path.

**The witness, which is what makes this a measured blocker rather than an argued one.** On that same invalid-timezone model the portable engine returns **INCONCLUSIVE with zero findings**, exit 3, while A12 returns `valid:false`. Under today's contract that is HONEST — the checker cannot emit VALID at all and says so in its summary — but it is precisely the row that becomes a **false VALID** the moment that bar is lifted. So VALID is blocked by a reproducible model, not by an unbuilt artifact.

**So each unreported code needs its own reachability row**, which is the one genuinely open work item this drain produces: one model plus one `model check --engine kernel` each, on the pattern the timezone row and the 12-input parser probe just used. **The denominator is 18, not the 19 this paragraph carried** — `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INVALID` left the set the moment `GROUP_REPETITION_COUNT` gained it, so the figure was stale by one from the commit that fixed it; re-derived 2026-09-16 as 26 declared − 8 reported, both pinned by `CodegenTierCodeDenominatorGuardTest`. The triage table above is complete over those 18, and as of the 2026-09-16 pass **the tier is fully dispositioned: 18 of 18, with `MVK_INVALID_TIME_ZONE` the one reachable code and a DELIBERATE, disclosed under-report.**

**Why that last one is a decision rather than unfinished work — measured, and it reverses this session's own first recommendation.** The law is `checkTimeZone`: literal `"GMT"` passes, otherwise `java.util.TimeZone.getTimeZone(s)` must not fall back to `GMT`. So the admitted domain is **whatever tz database the running JVM carries** — measured on the pinned JDK 21 (`21.0.12.1`): **632 ids**, including the legacy aliases `EST`, `PST`, `Greenwich` and custom offsets like `GMT+05:00`, while `Not/AZone` and `""` both fall back and are refused. Two consequences, and the second is the one that decides it:

- an **IANA-only** vocabulary (kotlinx-datetime's `availableZoneIds`) would refuse `EST`/`PST`/`Greenwich`, which A12 accepts — an **over-rejection**, the one direction this project forbids;
- and a table **derived from our JDK and committed** fails the same way against the future: the Kernel runs on the *consumer's* JVM, a newer tzdb adds ids, and any id newer than our snapshot would be refused here and accepted there. That is not drift to be managed by a `KERNEL-UPGRADE` trigger — the divergence is driven by a runtime we do not control, so no committed snapshot can be correct for every consumer.

A complete portable check for this code therefore cannot be made safe, and **silence is the only sound behaviour**: the checker reports INCONCLUSIVE, which is honest under its contract. A family covering only the JDK-independent sliver (a blank `timeZone`) was considered and **rejected**: publishing findings under this code's name while its real domain stays unreported would tell a reader the code is covered, which is worse than reporting nothing. *(Recorded after this session first recommended deriving the table from JDK 21 — the recommendation did not survive measuring what defines the domain.)* The last three were closed that day without a registry: two by probe (`MVK_FIELD_NO_PARENT` refused a tier earlier, `MVK_PARENT_ENTITY_MISSING` shadowed by a reported code) and six at once by re-verifying [KF296](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf296)'s dead gate at the import. **So the whole codegen tier now costs one check family, not eighteen** — and that is defect removal, which [the metamodel paragraph](#coverage-residue) above already establishes cannot lift the VALID bar. Two of the 18 are candidates by co-location in a method the consistency path demonstrably executes — `checkEntity` raises the reported `MVK_PATH_INVALID`/`MVK_PATHS_INCONSISTENT` alongside `MVK_FIELD_WITH_CONTAINED_ENTITIES`, `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INVALID`, `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INCONSISTENT` and `MVK_PARENT_ENTITY_MISSING`, and `checkIndexField` raises the reported pair alongside `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_NO_TABLE_ENUM`. That co-location establishes the METHOD runs, **not** that each branch is satisfiable from stored DM-JSON, which is the remaining per-row question and the reason the rows are not simply assumed.

**A source-read TRIAGE cuts those 19 roughly in half, because each raiser's satisfiability is decided by which of four inputs its branch reads.** `NoMetaModelChecks` is constructed over `CodeGenData`, and its checks draw on `getEc()` (the entity container — the model), `getModelSpecificSettings()` (the model's own settings), `getParams()` (the CODE GENERATOR's parameters) and `getPss()` (project settings). Only the first two can be driven by a stored DM-JSON file, so a branch reading solely the last two cannot produce a false pass no matter what a model says.

| reads | disposition | codes |
|---|---|---|
| `getModelSpecificSettings` | **reachable — MEASURED, and the witness above** | `MVK_INVALID_TIME_ZONE` |
| `getEc` / `getModelSpecificSettings` | **CLOSED — the checker now reports it**, so it is no longer a route at all | `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INVALID` |
| `getEc` | **already dispositioned in our own estate** — excluded on a cited dead path, not an open row | `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_NO_TABLE_ENUM` (its gate needs a present `IEnumerationType.getTableName()`, and the shape that would supply one is unreadable, unconstructible and unimplemented on all four links [KF296](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf296) attacks — [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md)'s scope note) |
| `getEc` / `getModelSpecificSettings` | **PROBED — no stored spelling reaches them**, so neither is a false-pass route | `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INCONSISTENT` (it compares max ARRAY lengths, which `CodeGenValidationDataCollector` derives from nesting depth, so the relation holds by construction — four nesting combinations admitted, and a `null` count draws only `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INVALID`; the same shape as the `MVK_MAX_MISSING` silence this family already documents) · `MVK_FIELD_WITH_CONTAINED_ENTITIES` (its only spelling is a `Field` body carrying `elements`, which A12's reader refuses outright as an unrecognized member, so the source never loads) |
| `getEc` | **PROBED 2026-09-16 — the stored shape is refused one tier EARLIER**, so the branch is never reached and the model is rejected regardless | `MVK_FIELD_NO_PARENT`. A `Field` placed directly in `rootGroups` draws a codeless build failure from the Kernel — *"Cannot process model with top-level element of type FieldWrapper"* — before `checkEntityContainer` runs. The `else` arm that raises this code therefore guards a programmatically-assembled container, not stored DM-JSON |
| `getEc` | **PROBED 2026-09-16 — SHADOWED by a reported code at an earlier gate that returns** | `MVK_PARENT_ENTITY_MISSING`. A nested group named `Missing/Ghost` draws `MVK_PATHS_INCONSISTENT`, which the checker reports, and `checkEntity` returns there rather than falling through. Structurally the two gates cannot both be satisfiable: passing the path gate requires the derived parent to EQUAL the walk's `parentPath`, and the walk only descends from an entity the container holds, so `getEc().get(parentPath)` is non-null by construction. Stated as one probed lever plus that argument — **not** an exhaustive lever enumeration |
| `getEc` | **UNREACHABLE — one dead gate holds SIX codes** ([KF296](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf296), re-verified at the import 2026-09-16) | `MVK_ENUM_TABLE_MISSING` and, for the same reason, the four `MVK_TABLE_ENUM_*` codes and `MVK_TABLE_ENUMS_NOT_ALLOWED_FOR_PROGRAMMING_LANGUAGE`. `checkEnumTables()` keeps only fields whose `getTableName().isPresent()`, then returns at `tableEnumFields.isEmpty()`. KF296 attacked that presence on four links — the stored member does not exist and makes the reader refuse the model, the converter takes no table argument, `EnumerationTypeImpl.getTableName()` returns `Optional.empty()` unconditionally, and it is the interface's only implementor. **The layer check KF296 warns about was run rather than assumed:** `NoMetaModelChecks` imports `core.tool.a12internal.api.ado.IEnumerationType`, the interface KF296 attacked, not the identically-named wrapper in the `md.model.api` layer |
| `getParams` (default, never populated) | **UNREACHABLE through `checkConsistency` — every link read, 2026-09-16**, so it cannot produce a false pass | `MVK_DUPLICATE_DYNAMIC_ENUM` — see the seam below |
| — | **DEAD: declared, never raised** | `MVK_INVALID_CHAR_IN_RESTRICTED_CHARSET` — `grep` over the whole checkout excluding `build/` and `delombok/` returns exactly **3** occurrences: the enum constant, and the two message bundles, whose German side reads `Kein Fehler definiert!`. There is no `reportError` site. The live charset concern is carried by `MVK_ENUM_VALUE_WITH_INVALID_CHARACTER`, which the checker already reports, so the model setting (`getSupportedCharacters()`, passed into the params) is not left unguarded |
| `getParams` | **NOT model-drivable — MEASURED on one member** | `MVK_INVALID_NAME_4_GENERATED_CODE` (three invalid model ids — leading digit, `xml` prefix, embedded space — are all ADMITTED, so `getParams().getName()` is not the model's id), `MVK_INVALID_PACKAGE_NAME_4_GENERATED_CODE`. *(The four `checkTableEnum` codes sat here until 2026-09-16 and have moved to the dead-gate row above, which is the stronger reason: they are not merely param-driven, they are behind a filter that is provably always empty.)* |
| `getPss` | **NOT model-drivable** — project configuration | `MVK_NO_PROJECT_LANGUAGE_SETTINGS`. *(`MVK_TABLE_ENUMS_NOT_ALLOWED_FOR_PROGRAMMING_LANGUAGE` moved to the dead-gate row above. **Corrected 2026-09-16, same day:** a first pass put it here reasoning that the consistency path fixes the language at Groovy, so no model content can vary it. That inverts the logic — Groovy is exactly one of the two languages this code fires FOR, so a fixed Groovy dialect would make it fire, not stay silent. What actually silences it is the empty `tableEnumFields` list one line earlier.)* |
| — | **no raiser in this file at all**, so its site is elsewhere or it is dormant | `MVK_NO_FORMAL_ERROR_RESOURCE_BUNDLE` |

**The seam from `checkConsistency` into this tier is now NAMED, and it was load-bearing while it was only inferred (source-read 2026-09-16, kernel 30.8.1).** `ExternalDocumentModelService.checkConsistency` does **not** generate code itself — it runs `DocumentModelConsistencyRulesProvider`'s eight rules, and the **seventh, `CodeGenerationRule`, is the door**: it calls `generateValidationCode(model, new GroovyCodeGeneratorConfig(null), null, notifications::add)` and reports every returned notification as a consistency problem. So the tier is reached through one named rule with a **fixed Groovy dialect and a caller-built parameter object**, not through a general "consistency re-runs codegen" equivalence. The measurement this plan already had stays correct and is in fact sharper than it claimed: `MVK_INVALID_TIME_ZONE` has exactly **one** raising site in the checkout (`NoMetaModelChecks:145`), so observing it out of `checkConsistency` identifies this tier uniquely rather than circumstantially.

**That seam is what settles `MVK_DUPLICATE_DYNAMIC_ENUM`, and each link was READ rather than carried by the argument's shape:** `CodeGenerationRule` supplies the config → `generateValidationCodeKernelInternal` builds params via `createCodeGeneratorParameters`, whose `new CodeGeneratorParameters(…)` call passes **13 arguments, none of them dynamic-enumeration information** → the concrete `CodeGeneratorParameters` does **not** override `getDynamicEnumerationInformation()` → `ICodeGeneratorParameters`' default returns `Collections.emptyList()` → `NoMetaModelChecks.checkDynamicEnumerations()` groups that empty list, so no group can have `size() > 1` and the code is never reported. Its own `reportError` call reads model content (`data.getEc().get(enumFieldName)`) purely to locate the finding, which is why the row previously read as model-driven. **Re-open trigger:** any of those four links — a kernel bump that populates the member, an override, a different config in `CodeGenerationRule`, or a second caller of the tier.

**The first candidate probed turned out to be a FIXABLE gap rather than a blocker, and it is fixed.** A stored `"repeatability":0` was reported by A12 as `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INVALID` and drew **zero findings** from the checker — the whole false-pass shape, in an ordinary construct rather than an exotic one. `GROUP_REPETITION_COUNT` already held that group's UPPER bound (the per-carrier cap and the unreadable ceiling) and simply had no lower half. It has both now, with the boundary measured on each side, and the packaged launcher returns `INVALID` / `MVK_MAX_VALUES_INVALID` / exit 1 where it returned INCONCLUSIVE before. A12's side was **already** recorded as [KF221](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf221) and was not re-derived as a new finding — it was extended there with the two boundary cells it lacked. The lesson for the remainder is that "candidate blocker" is the wrong default reading: several are probably the same shape, a family that covers most of a law and not its edge.

**And the cheapest step is the one that was skipped twice: GREP OUR OWN ESTATE BEFORE PROBING.** The repeatability law was measured on 2026-08-31 and sat in [KF221](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf221) the whole time, with the ledger's scope note even stating that the checker did not report it — the exact row being hunted. It was re-derived from the kernel instead, and only a builder's javadoc citing KF221 stopped a duplicate finding being minted. The same sweep then removed a second row from the candidate list: `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_NO_TABLE_ENUM` had already been excluded on a cited dead path. One `grep` over `KERNEL-FINDINGS` and `MVK-LEDGER` per code costs nothing and settled two of nine; the other seven have **no estate mention at all**, which is what makes them genuinely open rather than merely unread.

**Two harness defects in this probe run are worth more than the rows they nearly produced, because both returned a clean-looking result.** A quick `dmtool model check | grep '"code"'` **conflates ADMITTED with rejected-codelessly**: A12 refuses a model whose element name carries the path separator through a build failure that carries no class key, so the code grep finds nothing and the row reads *valid*. Next to a portable `INVALID`, that reads as an **over-rejection by our checker** — the one direction this project forbids — and it was written down as one before the full envelope was read, where `"valid": false` sits plainly. `KernelLaws` already models exactly this with four verdicts rather than two (`ADMITTED` · `UNREADABLE` · `NOT_BUILDABLE` · `REFUSED_CODELESS`), so the shell shortcut discarded a distinction the estate had paid for. **Read `"valid"`, never only the codes.** And the second: `set -- $pair` in a zsh loop passes ONE argument, so a two-value substitution silently produced `"repeatability" : ` and three rows of *malformed input* that had earlier read as three clean *valid* rows. Both tells are the same — a probe that cannot fail loudly will fail quietly.

**This is a TRIAGE, not a disposition, and the distinction is this project's own rule**: a reachability claim read off source stays a hypothesis until something is authored against it. It has two measured anchors, one per direction — the timezone row proves a `getModelSpecificSettings` branch fires from `checkConsistency`, and the model-id row proves a `getParams` branch does not follow the model — and the early-return structure of `check()` strengthens both, since all seven first-batch checks are assigned to variables *before* the return, so the timezone row proves the other six RAN and stayed silent on that model. What it does not do is settle the nine candidates individually. An attempt to isolate `MVK_NO_PROJECT_LANGUAGE_SETTINGS` by adding a model locale was pre-empted by `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_FOR_LANGUAGE_MISSING`, a rule-level class, which is a fair warning that several of these need a rule-free carrier to be observable at all.

**Where the façade lives, and why it is a module rather than a method.** [`:interpreter-bridge`](../interpreter-bridge/build.gradle) is the precedent and its comment states the rule: an integration that depends on two products sits **beside** them, never inside either, because the `api project(…)` edge that folds one into the other's published POM works equally well in reverse. So F3 gets `:consistency-gate` (`dmkits-consistency-gate`), depending on the kits and on `:consistency`, kernel-free under its own compiled-purity gate. Neither `:core` nor `:consistency` gains a dependency.

**F3-a's acceptance matrix, frozen here.**

| acceptance item | oracle | non-goal |
|---|---|---|
| a measured A12 rejection comes back `ok() == false` with the checker's own code on the diagnostic | a committed model A12 rejects, through the façade with no kernel on the classpath | not a re-derivation of the verdict: the façade maps, the checker decides |
| an A12-ACCEPTED model comes back INCONCLUSIVE with its coverage evidence, never `VALID` and never a fabricated pass | the same model the F1-b row uses, so the two consumers cannot disagree | no `Valid`; that is the coverage registry's bar |
| an unreadable source comes back as a diagnostic rather than a throw | a truncated DM-JSON text | no exception contract |
| the receipt is `PORTABLE_CHECKED` on every arm that ran the gate, and absent when it did not | `ValidationResult.consistencyGate()` | never `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`, and never `STRUCTURAL_ONLY` either |
| a finding's ORIGIN says the portable checker spoke | `DiagnosticSource` on the mapped diagnostic | not `KERNEL` (no kernel ran) and not `PRECHECK` (no rulekit heuristic ran) |
| an included model resolves through the dmkits' own `ModelResolver` | a two-file fixture whose reference the resolver supplies, and one where it does not — the second reporting the mount as a gap | no new include semantics; the checker's own expansion decides |
| the module is kernel-free as COMPILED and as RESOLVED | its own `verifyCompiledPurity` gate plus the root `verifyPublishedClasspathsAreKernelFree` | — |

**What makes F3-a stop rather than grow.** A finding enters it only when it violates one of those rows. The schemakit gate is F3-b, the interpreter preflight is F3-c, and anything about VALID belongs to the coverage registry.

**F3-a landed 2026-09-16.** `:consistency-gate` publishes `dmkits-consistency-gate`, whose `PortableConsistencyGate` returns a `PortableGateRun` carrying both halves — the checker's own report and the same run as a rulekit `ValidationResult`. The public account is in [CONSISTENCY-API](CONSISTENCY-API.md#calling-it-from-a-jvm-dmkits-consumer--dmkits-consistency-gate); the artifact note is [RELEASE-SPEC §11](RELEASE-SPEC.md)'s seventh.

Three decisions in it are worth keeping, because each had a cheaper alternative that would have shipped a false claim.

**`DiagnosticSource` needed a new value, and the code that refuses to guess one said so first.** `Diagnostic.gateRefusal` already threw on `PORTABLE_CHECKED` with the remedy written into the comment — "give `DiagnosticSource` a value for it, not to borrow `PRECHECK`, which would tell a reader that a rulekit heuristic refused their model". Both neighbours are live misstatements rather than approximations: `KERNEL` asserts an engine that never spoke, and `PRECHECK` asserts one that spoke about something else.

**It is deliberately NOT a `ConsistencyOracle` provider.** That port is the obvious place to hang a kernel-free checker and it is the one place it must not go: `Backends.verification()` answers `KERNEL_CONFIRMED` whenever a provider is present, so registering here would mint kernel provenance for a run no kernel arbitrated — undetectable, because the kernel is never asked. `Verification.PORTABLE_CHECKED`'s own doc already required the path to be "explicitly asked for", and a `ServiceLoader` registration is the opposite of that.

**The public-API lock needed a distinction it did not have.** Its hard rule refuses any non-kit type on a published signature, and this façade returns `ConsistencyReport` by design — restating the report in a second shape being the one outcome worse than naming its type. The existing escape hatch was `FOREIGN_SIGNATURE_EXEMPTIONS`, whose entry excuses a real LEAK (the bridge declares its interpreter dependency at `runtime` scope, so a consumer does not get those types). Filing this beside it would have recorded a leak where there is none, so `API_SCOPED_PUBLICATIONS` is separate and means the opposite: the artifact's own POM advertises `a12-consistency` at `api` scope, which is the rule's actual test. Both maps ride the same ratchet, so an allowance that stops matching a live signature is deleted rather than left standing.

Nine mutants planted, nine killed — and the ninth is the one worth recording: an unreadable entry on the RESOLVER arm reaches the header reader before the checker, and the self-contained row could not see it, so widening the catch to `Error` survived until a resolver-arm row was written for it. The other eight cover the receipt borrowing the ambient environment, the origin claiming the kernel spoke, source diagnostics dropped, an advisory flattened into a rejection, the locus claiming a rule handle, the closure stopping at one level, and typedef imports left unresolved.

**F3-b landed 2026-09-16, and the interesting part is what it bought rather than what it added.** Schemakit's own accept gate is one seam — `SchemaTestSupport.assertKernelAccepts`, called from 26 files — so arming it with the portable checker put the module's ENTIRE import corpus through both oracles at no authoring cost per test. Only over-rejection fails: A12 has just accepted the model, so a portable ERROR is the checker refusing what A12 admits, and the converse says nothing because this checker completes only the families it implements. **Measured across the 135-test corpus: no disagreement.** The dependency is TEST scope, for the same reason `:adapter` is — `dmkits-schemakit`'s POM is unchanged and `verifySchemakitPurity` reads main classes — which is this slice's "no widening of schemakit scope" non-goal discharged structurally rather than by care.

That arm needed a class of controls to be worth anything. It fires only on a disagreement, and there is none, so it is green whether armed or disarmed and a mutant deleting it survives all 135 tests. `PortableAcceptanceGateTest` is the fix: one row proves the predicate REFUSES when the checker reports an error, the other proves the checker examined a real model rather than finding nothing it could read. The general shape is worth naming — **a guard whose trigger condition is currently empty is unfalsifiable by its own corpus, and needs a control that supplies the trigger directly.**

**F3-c landed 2026-09-16, and its justification is a measurement rather than a worry.** `:interpreter-preflight` publishes `dmkits-interpreter-preflight`, whose `ConsistencyPreflight.of(sources, entryModelId)` takes exactly what `ModelWorkspace.fromSources` takes and answers whether A12 rejects the model. **A model A12 rejects PREPARES successfully in the interpreter** — measured in `ConsistencyPreflightTest`, not assumed — because preparation consults none of A12's constraints, so a consumer gating evaluation on preparation would evaluate a broken model and get answers about it. That is the whole case for the artifact, and it is the acceptance row's "preparation alone cannot claim consistency" turned into a test with the interpreter as its own oracle.

Its own module rather than a class in `:consistency-gate`, because the dependency sets do not overlap: a dmkits caller would otherwise carry the whole interpreter and an interpreter consumer would carry rulekit and modelkit. It builds no prepared graph and holds no interpreter, which is §623's "no common prepared graph" satisfied structurally.

**The three-way outcome is now the checker's, stated once.** `CheckOutcome` and `ConsistencyReport.outcome` live in `:consistency` because `ConsistencyStatus` answers a different question — `Inconclusive` covers both "every family ran and none rejected this" and "nothing ran at all", which are the same verdict and opposite instructions. The G slice's `ci-gate.mjs` keeps a wire-level mirror, since a CI gate classifies a JSON payload rather than a typed report; it now names this function as the rule's owner. **The durable fix is to publish the outcome ON the wire shape**, which is a report-shape change with its own tripwire (`ReportJsonTest`'s key set) and belongs to whichever slice next touches that shape.

**One thing the guards taught, worth keeping.** `verifyPublishedClasspathsAreKernelFree` carried ONE positive control — a Jackson group every published artifact resolved — and that was right while every one of them was a Java kit. The first published artifact sitting on the multiplatform modules resolves kotlinx and no Jackson at all, so the guard failed a module it had nothing to say about, which reads as a finding rather than as a miscalibrated control. The control is now per-module. A positive control is itself a claim about the estate, and this one had quietly become one.

**One duplication is now visible and is deliberately not folded in here.** `:cli`'s `PortableCheck` is a second witness over the same two checker calls, minted before this façade existed. Consolidating them touches `ConsistencyEvidence`, `WitnessTypesAreUnforgeableTest` and the CLI's portable tests, which is a different slice's blast radius; it is recorded here rather than left for someone to rediscover.

### F1-c record — one version, three projections, and the one that is not the artifact (2026-09-16) <a id="f1c-projections"></a>

**What F1-c had to avoid is a comparison that cannot fail.** Its acceptance asks that the coordinate and version `:cli` resolves equal what the JS package publishes, and every candidate value in reach derives from the same `project.version`. Reading it twice and asserting equality is the projection confound in its purest form — two columns that are really one — so the gate reads three build OUTPUTS instead, one per surface a consumer can actually meet:

| surface | value | written by |
|---|---|---|
| what a Node consumer installs | `name` and `version` in the packaged `package.json` | the Kotlin/JS plugin |
| what dmtool resolves | the version in the jvm jar's own file name | `jvmJar`'s archive coordinate |
| what dmtool's receipt cites | `CHECKER_VERSION`, read out of that jar's compiled class by reflection | our own `generateCheckerIdentity` |

**Stated narrowly, it establishes that the three projections still agree — no more.** It cannot say the version is the RIGHT one, and no build-time oracle can. That is enough, because the failure it forbids is a published id with two byte contracts, and diverging projections are exactly how one appears.

**The first draft read the GENERATED SOURCE for the third value, and that was one step short of the artifact.** Reading the constant out of the jar was the same amount of code — `URLClassLoader` with the platform loader reaches it, because a file holding one `const val` compiles to a static final with a `ConstantValue` attribute and needs no Kotlin runtime — and it closes the gap this project has already been bitten by: an artifact can lag its source, and did, when a catalog fact was committed and asserted as discoverable while the launcher still served the old one.

Three positive controls, each restoring byte-identically: perturbing the GENERATOR (so the disagreement is genuine rather than an inverted comparison) reports the version divergence and proves the reflection reads the recompiled jar; overriding `jvmJar`'s `archiveVersion` isolates the coordinate check; mutating the pinned name literal fires the name check. The name is pinned rather than derived on purpose — it is what consumers depend on, so it may not move as a side effect of a module rename.

### F1-e record — the stale claim is measured false, the class is enumerated, and all three edits LANDED on owner go-ahead (2026-09-16) <a id="f1e-wording"></a>

**Landed 2026-09-16.** Three sites were corrected after the owner chose "apply all three": `interpreter/CLAUDE.md` rule (B) (engine vs. artifact), its license-boundary bullet (the "JVM-only" oracle and "native carries no kernel"), and root `CLAUDE.md`'s EUPL scope, which named only the JVM distribution and so implied the native one was unencumbered. Every replacement rests on H3's "in both artifacts" and on the 2026-09-16 binary run, **never** on the "only CLI tool" reason, which this record refutes below. `docs/LICENSING.md` needed no edit and got none.

**What F1-e owes** is named in [proposal §1](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md): `interpreter/CLAUDE.md`'s license-boundary bullets call the consistency path JVM-only and the native runtime Kernel-free, while [NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM §4](NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM.md) retains Kernel `checkConsistency` for native `rule check`/`model check`. The older wording must not narrow H3.

**The contradiction is now settled by measurement rather than by comparing two documents.** Running the native binary — `model check -m examples/models/order-ruled.dm.json --engine kernel` — returns `verification: KERNEL_CONFIRMED`, `engineVersion: 30.8.1`, `valid: true`, exit 0. Two independent arguments make that a Kernel invocation *inside the native artifact*:

- **Nothing exists to delegate to.** A GraalVM native image is closed-world and self-contained: one Mach-O executable, no JVM, no classpath, no runtime jar loading. The Kernel is Java bytecode, so there is no subprocess and nothing loadable — a Kernel verdict out of that binary requires Kernel code compiled into it.
- **The portable path cannot have produced that answer.** `ConsistencyStatus` is a sealed interface with no VALID member, so the portable checker cannot emit `valid: true` at all; and `ConsistencyEvidence.requestedBy` throws when the executed engine is not the requested one. So `valid:true` + `KERNEL_CONFIRMED` is not the portable checker mislabelled.

*(A `strings` sweep of the binary finds 2194 distinct `com.mgmtp.a12.kernel*` names, 44 of them on the consistency path. That CORROBORATES and settles nothing on its own — GraalVM embeds class names for reflection metadata, resource bundles and messages, so a name proves a registration, not that the class's code is in the image. The enumeration of included code would come from the native-image build report. Recorded because the weaker instrument was the one first reached for.)*

**The class was swept, not just the reported site, and it is smaller than it looks.** Most estate hits for "JVM-only" and "native … kernel-free" are about the EVAL path and are true — the runtime verbs really do run on the Kernel-free interpreter. Two sites carry the stale narrowing, and **both are instruction files this session may not edit**:

| site | the stale wording | why it is wrong |
|---|---|---|
| `interpreter/CLAUDE.md` bullet, "MVK consistency oracle (`rule check`/`model check`, JVM-only) … the native/JS runtime carries no kernel and stays clean" | scopes the consistency oracle to the JVM | the native binary answers `--engine kernel` itself |
| `interpreter/CLAUDE.md` rule (B), "dmtool's native runtime a kernel-FREE engine, so that runtime carries no kernel code and is free of the EUPL" | true of the EVALUATOR, over-broad for the artifact | the shipped native binary does carry Kernel code |
| root `CLAUDE.md` interpreter section, "The JVM dmtool distribution still bundles the kernel-backed adapter … that distribution remains subject to the EUPL/commercial license" | naming only the JVM distribution reads as excluding the native one | same bundling applies to the native distributable |

**[LICENSING.md](LICENSING.md) is already correct and needs no edit** — its "bundled CLI distributable — EUPL-1.2" clause covers the native binary by construction ("the moment kernel bytecode is in a thing we hand out"), and it makes no Kernel-free native claim. That is the one place where a wrong statement would have had licensing consequences, so it was checked first. `plugin/CHANGELOG.md` is also accurate: its "JVM-only" qualifies the *removed* `--kernel` eval engine, not the consistency gate.

**Owner-confirmed 2026-09-16, and the context they gave is what the edit has to preserve.** The confirmation was *"wrong … if it refers to dmtool GraalVM native image"*, with the reason: **the interpreter shipped in dmtool is and must be Kernel-free, while `rule check` is deliberately NOT — it is the only way to reach the Kernel as an oracle from a CLI tool.** The first two halves are source- and runtime-confirmed below; **the third is refuted** — see the reason paragraph. So three claims have to be told apart, and the stale wording fuses them:

| claim | status |
|---|---|
| the interpreter shipped in dmtool is Kernel-free | **true, and a REQUIREMENT** — [interpreter](../interpreter/CLAUDE.md) rule (B), the EUPL boundary. Enforced by `verifyInterpreterPurity` (no `com.mgmtp.*` in any source set) plus a resolved-dependency guard; **run 2026-09-16**, native `model eval` answers `"engine":"DM_INTERPRETER"` and the verb exposes no Kernel option at all |
| `rule check` / `model check` are Kernel-free | **false by DEFAULT, and deliberately so.** `CheckEngine.A12_KERNEL` is "the default on every verb that can select"; **run 2026-09-16**, the native binary with no flag answers `engineExecuted: A12_KERNEL`, `engineVersion: 30.8.1`. The same verb's explicit `--engine portable` answers `PORTABLE_CHECKER` and must never reach the Kernel (H1), so the claim is about the default and the artifact, never about every invocation |
| the native artifact carries no Kernel | **false** — it bundles the Kernel to serve that oracle and executes it. The four-link source proof and its runtime corroboration are [NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM §3.8](NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM.md#native-carries-kernel)'s to hold, and the licence consequence is [LICENSING](LICENSING.md)'s; this row cites them rather than repeating either |

**Name the ENGINE, not the verb, in the replacement text.** Those verbs are served by two engines — [`CheckEngine`](../cli/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/cli/CheckEngine.java)`.A12_KERNEL`, the default, and `PORTABLE_CHECKER`, "only ever when explicitly requested" — so "`rule check` is Kernel-backed" is true of the default and **false of `--engine portable`**, which H1 requires to invoke no Kernel at all. Both halves of the boundary therefore run through the same verb, and the discriminator is the selected engine: the Kernel reaches the check path through exactly one seam, [`ConsistencyOracle`](../core/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/core/spi/ConsistencyOracle.java), implemented only by `:adapter`. A correction that says "the check verbs use the Kernel" replaces one conflation with another.

**This is not merely an imprecise sentence: it narrows H3**, whose acceptance row reads "dmtool retains the requested Kernel oracle **in both artifacts**" — so the Kernel *engine behind the check verbs* is an acceptance condition, not a tolerated leftover, and [proposal §1](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md) flagged exactly this risk in advance: *"the older wording must not narrow H3."* **The owner's REASON is FALSE as they stated it, and the replacement text must state the narrow version instead (measured 2026-09-16, Kernel source).** They gave it as *"it's the only way to access kernel as oracle via a CLI tool"*. The Kernel checkout ships its own CLI tools: **7** `public static void main` classes, one module named literally `kernel-md-cli`. `VkValidationCodeGeneratorCli` is javadoc'd "Provides a command-line interface to the generator of validation code"; it calls `generateValidationCode(…, notifications::add)` and then joins info/warning/**error** messages for the console, and `ValidationCodeGenerator:152` constructs `new NoMetaModelChecks(data, problemReporter)` — **this tier's own 26 codes**. So a Kernel-shipped CLI reports MVK findings on a document model, and dmtool is not the only CLI route to Kernel checking.

**The narrow version source DOES support:** none of those 7 `main` classes calls `checkConsistency`, and every source caller of it is a service class (`DocumentModelService`, `ExternalDocumentModelService`, `CombinationModelService`, `DecorationModelService`, `SelectionModelService`, `StructuralMappingModelService`) — no CLI among them. So for `IDocumentModelService.checkConsistency` **specifically**, dmtool is the only CLI route *in this checkout*. Two recall limits: only the sibling `../a12-kernel` was searched, so the A12 Tools installation is unexamined and "no other CLI anywhere" is not established; and `generateValidationCode` was confirmed to reach the codegen tier without tracing whether it also runs the full consistency check.

**Why this matters beyond the wording:** that Kernel CLI exercises the codegen tier this plan's coverage residue is about, so it is a second instrument for the rows below — not merely a correction to a rationale. And the retention itself never depended on the premise: §4's out-of-scope list excludes Kernel removal from **native** dmtool under the owner's explicit instruction, adding that "completing portable coverage does not authorize it", so H3 stands either way.

*(Method note, 2026-09-16: the first pass at this checked the claim by grepping `docs/*.md` for corroboration, which is the golden rule inverted — our own prose cannot establish which CLI entry points exist, and the only hits were the two sentences this record had itself just introduced. The owner rejected the instrument before the conclusion was published. A claim about what a program can do is settled by its source and by running it.)* The JS half of the clause is true and stays: the published JS/TS packages do carry none. So the edit separates **evaluation engine** from **distribution**, and states the oracle's presence as intent rather than as a defect — it must not read as though the native binary were contaminated.

**OWNER ACTION, and the only thing standing between here and `N`.** The three rows above need the distinction proposal §1 asks for — the Kernel-free *evaluator and portable library* versus the Kernel-*bundling* dmtool distribution, in both artifacts. This session does not write instruction files, and `N`'s own acceptance ("all preceding required slices are implemented") inherits this block.

### F1-d record — the capture stopped being a blocker, and the identity claim is measured on both artifacts (2026-09-16) <a id="f1d-qualification"></a>

**The recapture was environment-blocked, not unbuilt, and that is what changed.** Three attempts died: one as a Monitor's own command at its 30-minute ceiling, two as harness-backgrounded tasks under a memory watchdog — the second even after the traced fork got a 1 GB heap cap. The crack is that `config-merge-dir` already merges, so the suite can be traced in **foreground slices** that accumulate; foreground calls are not background tasks, and each slice is far inside every ceiling. 249 compiled test classes went through in 11 slices, every slice green, ~39 minutes, then a 13-second assemble. The mechanism and the completeness gate that keeps a sliced capture from being §3.5's own defect class are in [NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM](NATIVE-IMAGE-GRAALVM.md#capture-slices) §5.2.

| qualification step | oracle | result |
|---|---|---|
| recapture | `scripts/capture-native-config.sh`, sliced | 0 committed entries removed; +22 reflection, +1 resource |
| native build | `:cli:nativeCompile` | 87 s, 45.7 MiB |
| gate | `scripts/native-gate.sh` | GREEN — public contract clean, all demos byte-match, 192-check battery, 0 native-image signatures |
| **packaged/native identity** | both engine requests through `cli/build/install/dmtool/bin/dmtool` and the native binary, diffed | **byte-identical envelopes, equal exit codes**: portable → `PORTABLE_CHECKED`/INCONCLUSIVE/3, kernel → `KERNEL_CONFIRMED`/`valid:true`/0 |
| no substitution on either artifact | the envelope's own receipt | `engineRequested == engineExecuted` in all four runs |

**The delta the recapture added is NOT shown to be load-bearing, and measuring that is the point.** The 22 new reflection entries include exactly the surfaces this row introduced — `EngineOption`, its picocli converter, `PortableCheck` — so "shipping native without them would have broken `--engine portable`" is the obvious reading, and it is **false**: rebuilt against the pre-capture metadata, the native binary answers both engine requests identically. GraalVM's static analysis reaches those classes without a reflection entry. So the delta is the ordinary accumulation the unconditional pre-build recapture absorbs, not a defect it caught, and the recapture's value here rests on §3.5's argument — a reflective site no test reaches is invisible — rather than on this delta. The counterfactual cost one rebuild; asserting it would have put a mechanism dressed as an observation into this plan.

### G record — the CI gate has no pass, and the outcome a consumer would classify wrongly (2026-09-16) <a id="g-no-pass"></a>

**A CI gate is where "the checker cannot emit VALID" stops being a doc sentence and becomes a design problem.** A job wants one bit, and the obvious pipeline — no findings, therefore green — is precisely the silent pass the report shape exists to prevent. Worse, it is invisible: a consumer who writes it gets green builds and never learns. So the gate carries `validated: false` with no code path that sets it true, and lists per source why nothing was validated, while exit `0` means the weaker claim a job may still act on: *no rejection was found*.

Its exit codes are dmtool's own (`0`, `1`, `70` — [CLI-SPEC](CLI-SPEC.md) §3b) rather than a second numbering for the same three meanings, and an operational failure outranks a rejection: a run that could not read one of its inputs has not checked the set it was asked about, so reporting its rejections as the result would describe a smaller set than the consumer supplied.

**The classification a consumer gets wrong is which fact means "the tool failed".** "A diagnostic arrived" is the intuitive test and is wrong, and the discriminator is committed as a test row: a model with one declaration stripped of its `name` reports `DECLARATION_NOT_WELL_FORMED` beside **103 completed families** and no findings (measured 2026-09-16), because a broken element is skipped rather than allowed to hide its siblings' real errors. Keying on the diagnostic fails that build as a tool failure and sends the author looking in the wrong place — an over-rejection in the consumer rather than in the checker, landing on them just the same. The gate keys on `checksCompleted` being empty, which is the wire invariant the report shape already documents: absent and empty mean opposite things there.

**The fourth outcome exists for a report this package cannot produce.** An unknown `status` fails closed, because a gate pinned to today's package must not read a future `VALID` as a pass — it cannot verify what it does not model. No library call can stage that report, so the row exercises the classifier directly and says why. Five mutants were planted in the gate and all five died, including the fail-open one and the exit-code collapse, which is settled by SPAWNING the gate: an exported constant is a claim about a running pipeline.

### A4 — consistency corpus ownership (decided 2026-09-12) <a id="a4"></a>

**Reuse the conformance corpus's case convention; do not build a second corpus.** [`corpus/`](../corpus/README.md) already holds every property A4 asks for: 34 cases across 10 family directories, each carrying a stable `meta.id`, the oracle artifact identity in `meta.kernelVersion`, provenance in `meta.source`, `meta.sections` and `meta.tags`, and — the part that matters most for "one input owner" — a `modelRef` indirection into a shared `corpus/models/` tree rather than an inlined model. That is the reuse A4 requires across common replay, adapter differentials and packed consumers.

**One bounded schema change is needed, and it is the whole gap.** [`corpus/case.schema.json`](../corpus/case.schema.json) lists `['meta', 'modelRef', 'document', 'op']` as top-level **required**, and admits exactly three op kinds — `validateFull`, `validatePart`, `compute` — all of which evaluate a Document. A consistency case has **no Document by contract**. So A4 adds a consistency op kind and makes `document` conditional on the kind rather than unconditionally required, leaving every existing case valid and untouched.

**What a consistency case carries instead of a document:** the accepted / rejected / incomplete expectation, and for a rejected case the measured code. Everything else — `meta.id`, `modelRef`, `meta.kernelVersion`, `meta.sections`, `meta.tags` — keeps the existing convention, so the same tooling enumerates both kinds. Note the family is the **directory name only**: no case carries a `meta.family` and the schema does not model one, the normative taxonomy being `meta.sections`.

**Why not the other fixture trees — enumerated properly this time.** Counted as `.dm.json` models per tree (the first pass mixed three different quantities: two `models/`-scoped JSON counts and one whole-`test/resources` JSON count):

| Tree | Models | Owner's job |
|---|---|---|
| `cli/src/test/resources/models` | 18 | JUnit + eval lanes |
| `examples/models` | 14 | published demos, own CI gate |
| `adapter/src/test/resources/models` | 14 | Kernel differentials |
| `src/test/resources/models` (root module) | 7 | the `validate/laws` accept/reject tier — where the checker's own law locks live |
| `interpreter/src/commonTest/resources/**` | 47 | workspace-preparation, performance, footprint, retained-result KMP fixtures |

The first pass named only the first three and then asserted the corpus was "the only tree designed as a shared input owner" — a negative existential over a denominator two trees short, and the root laws tier is precisely the one a consistency checker's accept/reject cases are adjacent to. **The corpus is still the right owner** — it alone carries per-case oracle identity and a `modelRef` indirection — but that is now a comparison against five trees rather than an unmeasured claim of uniqueness. [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) keeps the demo and JUnit trees deliberately decoupled, so A4 repoints none of them.

**Per-case `kernelVersion` stays useful even with M deferred.** It records which artifact each expectation was measured against, so a corpus case cannot silently outlive its oracle — and if cross-version comparison is ever revived, it needs cases keyed this way rather than a parallel corpus.

### A2 — diagnostic ownership and portable delivery (decided 2026-09-12)

**The delivery decision is forced by the medium each fact lives in, not by preference.**

| Owner | Holds | Medium | Delivery |
|---|---|---|---|
| [operators.json](../rulekit/src/main/resources/catalog/operators.json) | 110 operator entries — 97 carrying gotchas, 20 carrying plural correctives, 110 carrying a valid example — plus top-level `verifiedAgainst` and `emptyOperandDefaults` | JSON resource, 293 KB | **Packaged directly.** It is already data and already proven readable as Node data ([proposal §12](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#12-project-synergies-additional-features-and-focused-cleanups), 2026-09-08), so no projection and no second authoring lane |
| [StructuralKernelFix](../rulekit/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/StructuralKernelFix.java) | code-level and cross-context correctives; **24** distinct `MVK_*` tokens appear in the file but `forCode` serves **21** keys, and `forMessage` serves a further class carrying no MVK code at all — so neither number is the served denominator | Java source | **Deterministic generated projection** |
| [RkCode](../rulekit/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/RkCode.java) | 52 enum constants; the constant name *is* the code minus the `RK_` prefix, each carrying its `Severity` and `DiagnosticSource` | Java source | **Deterministic generated projection**, applicable subset only |

A Node consumer cannot read Java source at all, which is precisely the condition the proposal names for preferring a projection over direct reuse. So the split is not a judgement call: the catalog ships as-is, the two Java-owned sources are generated into a resource the checker packages.

**`RkCode` applicability is NOT machine-decidable from `Severity` + `DiagnosticSource` — corrected after review.** The first pass proposed filtering out the `LINT`-sourced `WARNING` constants as advisory. Measured across all 52: `ERROR/PRECHECK` 34, `WARNING/LINT` 9, `INFO/ENVIRONMENT` 3, `WARNING/PRECHECK` 2, `ERROR/ENVIRONMENT` 1, `ERROR/INTERNAL` 1, `ERROR/KERNEL` 1, `INFO/LINT` 1. That filter removes 9 of 52 — not "most" — and fails in **both** directions: it keeps `GOTCHA` (`INFO/LINT`, literally the catalog-gotcha advisory), both `PRECHECK/WARNING` constants that describe themselves as "an advisory, not a refusal", and the environment/internal constants; while discarding `COMPUTATION_FORBIDDEN_CONSTRUCT`, whose own text says the kernel rejects it with `MVK_ERROR_INVALID_LANGUAGE_FEATURE`.

So the applicable subset needs a **per-constant disposition** recorded with the projection, not a field predicate. This is the same over-and-under-match shape the A1 method finding refuted for package paths, and it went undetected because no constant outside `WARNING/LINT` was checked — a filter proposed without a positive control.

**Reuse the established projection mechanism; do not invent one.** The repository already generates resources from typed sources through Gradle task pairs — root `dumpTemporalVocabulary` and `dumpKitApi`, `:cli:dumpOperatorIndex`, and the interpreter's `generate*Source` family. C2 extends that pattern rather than adding a fourth mechanism.

**Graph and guards.** The generator runs in the owning Java module at build time and emits a resource the checker packages, so there is no production edge from checker to rulekit and the build graph stays acyclic under H1/H2. A stale projection must fail a freshness/parity guard, which is the shape `DocSnippetFreshnessTest` already establishes for locked derived content. The 293 KB catalog is an input to H5's resources-and-delivery row and must be enumerated from the shipped package rather than estimated.

**Recall limits.** 25 and 52 are counts of distinct `MVK_*` tokens and of four-space-indented enum constants in those two files; neither is a verified count of the distinct codes each source ultimately *serves*, and the applicable-subset boundary is settled by the filter above rather than by these totals.

### Constraint/profile denominator (measured 2026-09-12, disposition partial)

**The denominator is bounded and enumerated at source level.** Counted by section from [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md) and by disposition column from [GRAMMAR-LEDGER](GRAMMAR-LEDGER.md):

| Source | Entries | In this profile? |
|---|---|---|
| `ParserErrorTypesEnum` — condition and expression parsing | 176 | **yes** — rules and computations |
| `CalculationErrorType` — computations | 18 | **yes** |
| `FehlertextErrorType` — error text | 1 | **yes** — the scope validates relevant messages |
| `MM_Common` — metamodel structure | 105 | **no** — structure is context, not subject matter |
| Grammar productions: 46 `rendered`, 22 `plumbing`, 16 `legacy`, 3 `entry`, 2 `directive` | 89 **rows**, covering the ledger's declared **119 productions** — 28 rows name two productions and one names three | the 16 `legacy` rows are the grammar-side counterpart of A1's exclusion, and GRAMMAR-LEDGER owns that bucket's definition. Rows and productions are different units; the other rows of this table count codes |
| `NoMetaModelCheckErrorType` — codegen | 26 *(reported by a12-kernel-lean and re-derived in [MVK-LEDGER](MVK-LEDGER.md); unclassified there, not dispositioned)* | **applicable subset yes** — `CodeGenerationRule.checkModel` calls `generateValidationCode`, so these surface when checking a stored model |
| `DynEnumErrorType` — dynamic-enum derivation | 12 *(proposal's figure, not re-derived)* | **candidate exclusion** — a separate derivation service, dispositioned in its own right rather than by absence from the four-source ledger |

The rule/computation MVK denominator is therefore **195 rows** (176 + 18 + 1), and 195 + 105 reconciles with the ledger's documented 300.

**Disposition is partial, and that is the finding rather than an omission.** A1 dispositioned the 60-row erased bucket per firing site. The remaining **135** in-scope rows carry ledger dispositions written to answer a different question — whether rulekit's typed API can author the shape — so each needs the same per-site treatment against "can this fire for an A12 model's rules or computations". Until they do, no complete coverage claim is available, which is exactly what this record's exit oracle requires.

**A code count is not a law count, and A1 measured why.** `MVK_INVALID_PARAMS_FOR_OP` is raised from three call sites, one legacy and one not; `MVK_CONDITION_WITHOUT_REPEATABLE_ROOTGROUP` is raised from a general utility whose only caller is legacy. So the 195 is a bound on *entries to disposition*, never a count of laws or of checks to implement, and the frozen implementation families in C/D/E take their size from measured law families rather than from this number.

### A1 — erased-bucket disposition (per-site, corrected after review 2026-09-12)

**Result: 54 in scope, 6 excluded as legacy.** The 52 rows with no legacy claim are in by default. Of the 8 claiming legacy, six survive — three on a cited gate, three measured on every carrier — and two do not. The count is stated as memberships rather than a headline for the reason the inversions below record: a re-derived total cannot catch errors that cancel, and this membership set has now moved twice on the same code.

| Code | Disposition | Basis, at the firing site |
|---|---|---|
| `MVK_DIFFERENT_ROOT_GROUPS` | legacy | raised by `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkAllEntitiesInSameRootGroup`; both callers are `hdv` **and** grammar-predicate-gated |
| `MVK_ERROR_ITERATION_UNCHECKED` | legacy | two raisers in `kernel-core-calc`'s `NormalisierteBerechnungsFormHelper`, both guarded `if (isHModel && isIterable)`, each carrying the kernel's comment that "for A12 DVs, the iteration check precondition is added automatically/implicity" |
| `MVK_INVALID_UNSPECIFIED_TYPE` | **legacy** | `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkUnspecifiedType` is `private` with a **single** caller, `checkCommon:103`, whose immediately preceding line is `if (fullParser.isHModel()) {` |
| `MVK_INVALID_PARAMS_FOR_OP` | **legacy** (measured, both carriers, five attacked asymmetries) | its live-looking raiser `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkAllFieldsSameL` really is ungated from `CheckFeldListenOpImpl:85` — and an A12 model still cannot reach it. Five ways to make two `SumOfProducts` operands differ at `indices[0]` are each refused by a DIFFERENT earlier gate, on the computation and rule carriers alike ([KF285](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf285)). Re-open on a topology outside that set — a third repeatable level, or several index fields |
| `MVK_EXTRACT_OP_NOT_ALLOWED_4_DATE_CONSTRUCT_WITH_4_PARAMS` | in scope | no `isHModel` gate on the `checkJahresParam` → `checkYearFormat` → `handleDatumExtractOpAtYearParam` path. Fires when the construct carries a fourth parameter **and** the century or short-year param is itself a `datumExtractOp`; the grammar's other two `datumParam` arms route elsewhere |
| `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` | **in scope** (measured, both carriers) | both raisers open with `if (fullParser.isA12Model()) throw`, and an A12 model reaches both: a rule condition and a computation operation carrying the construct each report the code, once the model's `conditionLanguage` is the one whose lexer has the keyword |
| `MVK_CONDITION_WITH_WILDCARD` | **legacy** (measured) | `VordruckAngegeben(Order/*)` in an A12 model reaches the form-condition checker and draws `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` from the name action, which pre-empts this code |
| `MVK_CONDITION_WITHOUT_REPEATABLE_ROOTGROUP` | **legacy** (measured) | same pre-emption, for `MindestensEinVordruckAngegeben(Order)` — the outer `formularBedingung` action that raises either code runs after the name action that refuses the construct outright |

**Two dispositions were inverted in the first pass, from the same two causes.** `MVK_INVALID_UNSPECIFIED_TYPE` was called in scope from reading the raising method without tracing its only caller. `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` was called legacy on the **package path** — the criterion this record's own method finding had already shown to over- and under-match. The totals stayed at 55/5 only because the two errors cancelled, which is why re-deriving a headline cannot catch this class: **the memberships, not the count, are the claim.**

**The two unresolved rows are now measured, and the first measurement was wrong in a way worth keeping (2026-09-12).** Both are legacy, but not for the reason first recorded. The initial rows put the form constructs through `RuleValidator.check` and got `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, which read as a parser-level exclusion. Every row shared one unnamed condition: `DocumentModelDraft` stamps `conditionLanguage: en_US`, and these constructs exist only in the German terminal bundle, so the spellings were not keywords at all. Re-measured with the language as the sole variable, an A12 model with `de_DE` conditions **does** reach the checker, and the codes are pre-empted there by `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` — which is the inner-action pre-emption the first pass had recorded as *refuted*. The mechanism was right; the experiment could not see it.

**So `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` returns to in scope, and the correction that moved it out is itself corrected.** The row's original basis — its checker's first statement is the `isA12Model()` throw — was sound. Both carriers are measured under `de_DE`: `AnzahlAngegebenerVordrucke(Order) > 0` in a rule condition, and `{ AnzahlAngegebenerVordrucke(Order) }` as a computation's operation, which parses through the grammar's `operation` entry rule instead of `bedingung`. The second carrier still had to be run rather than inferred, because inside `einfacheOperation` the H-model siblings `feldListenOpHModel`/`feldOpHModel` ARE predicate-gated on `isHModel()` while `anzahlAngegebenOpHModel` deliberately is not. Its ledger cell is corrected in place, for the second time in one day.

**The transferable failure is the CONSTANT, not the conclusion.** Every row was individually clean, each with its own control, and the harness control (`FieldFilled(NoSuchField)` → `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`) genuinely proved that ordinary conditions reach the semantic checks — it just could not see a variable that no row varied. The cheap falsifier existed the whole time and cost one line: build the same model with the other condition language. The lock now carries it, plus the control that keeps the German arm honest — a `zeilenBedingung` (`ZeilenLueckenlos(Code)`) is still refused at the parser in a German model, because the grammar predicate-gates that production on `isHModel()` while leaving `formularBedingung` un-predicated. So the parser layer is real and separable; it simply was not what refused these three. *(Locked: `rulekit.validate.laws.HModelFormConditionReachabilityLawsTest`, six arms; the vocabulary fact itself is [KF283](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf283).)*

**An independent second source agrees on the legacy SET and expands it by none.** The kernel team's own `documentation/ba/parser_errors_en.adoc` carries a triage table of 103 undocumented codes bucketed `only for H-models` (45), `not relevant` (34), `ExtMP-exclusive` (16), `unclear` (6) and awaiting translation (2). Of the ledger's 60 erased rows, exactly **5** sit in the H-model bucket, and they are 5 of the 8 whose ledger note claims legacy — so the kernel team's view corroborates the ledger's legacy claims without adding any. Two cautions keep this as corroboration rather than evidence: the table answers *why is there no description*, not *can an A12 model reach this*; and it disagrees with measurement in two places — it buckets `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR` as `ExtMP-exclusive` rather than H-model-only, and it buckets `MVK_INVALID_PARAMS_FOR_OP` as H-model-only although that code has an ungated raiser at `CheckFeldListenOpImpl:85`.

**`MVK_INVALID_PARAMS_FOR_OP` is settled by attack, not by one measurement (2026-09-12).** Its live raiser compares `EntityRef.getLfdNr()` — `indices.get(0)` — across a `SumOfProducts`' operands, and `getLfdNr()` is not an H-model-only quantity: `indices[0]` is written by the general index path and by `CheckSemanticIndexImpl` as well as by the nine `hdv` `setLfdNr(` sites. So the disposition needed reachability, and reachability needs every link ATTACKED. Five asymmetries were authored and each is refused by a different gate ahead of the comparison — `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, `MVK_WILDCARD_ONLY_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_ALLOWED`, `MVK_INVALID_OUTER_ITERATION`, `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NOT_ALLOWED` — while the two admitted rows share one marker at that level. Both carriers agree. The recall limit is stated in [KF285](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf285) rather than implied: one topology, five attacks, and the shapes that would reopen it named.

**Two MVK-ledger rows carry a legacy basis that measurement contradicts** — the two still marked in scope, one of which is `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP_CONSTR`, whose ledger row is corrected in place (above). `MVK_INVALID_PARAMS_FOR_OP` left that set the other way: its ledger row's legacy claim was right, for a reason the row did not give. `MVK_INVALID_UNSPECIFIED_TYPE` is **not** among them: there the ledger was right and this record was wrong, and the first pass's proposed correction would have introduced an error. Correcting the other three still needs the kernel oracle, since accept/reject is terminal only there.

**Recall limits.** Sites located with `grep -rl <code> a12-kernel --include='*.java'` excluding `/build/` and the enum declaration files, then callers enumerated with `grep -rn`. Read: every site and caller named. The exclusion "the enum declaration files" was stated as three; the tree holds **seven** files declaring `MVK_*` constants, though for these eight codes only one is ever matched. Constant indirection is not ruled out.

### Checker-owned types (decided 2026-09-11)

With no neutral leaf, the checker owns every type on its own side of the boundary. Named here so the closure and shipped-file guards have a list to check rather than a judgement to make.

| The checker owns | Why it cannot be shared or borrowed |
|---|---|
| Its analysis AST | Retains raw spelling, grouping and source locations that execution lowering discards; the interpreter's AST is shaped for evaluation |
| Its parser | Follows from owning the AST; ported from our original source, reusing its test expectations |
| Its source and limit types | The archived attempt reached `interpreter/ModelPreparationLimits` into the checker's published JVM API, which is the coupling the shipped-file guard exists to catch |
| Its model context | The ported `ModelIndex` query behaviour, over checker-owned preparation |
| Its diagnostic and report types | The public result contract; projects existing diagnostic concepts without importing their Java carriers |
| Its numeric representation | See the design question below — not `Dec` |

### Neutral leaf — NOT EXTRACTED, allowlist stays empty (decided 2026-09-11)

Measured by reading the import lists of the two files a neutral syntax extraction would have to move. [Parser.kt](../interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/parse/Parser.kt) imports `UnsupportedConstructException`, `dec`, the five AST types (`ArithOp`, `CmpOp`, `Cond`, `Expr`, `ValueListQuantOp`) and `ConditionLanguage`. [Ast.kt](../interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/ast/Ast.kt) imports `Dec`, `dec`, `decOrNull`, `DEC_ZERO` and `Kind`.

**So the leaf is not small: a neutral parser requires moving the whole AST, two model enums, an exception type and the decimal primitive.** `Dec` carries `big.js` as its JS actual, and its reach through the evaluator is wide — `ExprEval` operates on `Dec` directly and even the polarity-arithmetic path carries many `Dec` references. *That reach is an enumeration over the evaluator, not something the two import lists establish; the import lists establish only that extraction forces interpreter changes* — which is by itself decisive under [proposal §5.1](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#51-standalone-checker-with-independent-preparation)'s "prefer a checker-owned port if a shared implementation forces interpreter changes" and under NFR #1.

**Consequence for the closure guard:** the permitted-type allowlist is **empty**, so any production edge from the checker into interpreter code is a failure with no exceptions to encode. **Enforcement locus:** that guard does not exist and cannot until a checker module does — it lands with **B**'s Kernel-free module/test dependency setup (stage 2) as the mirror of the existing `verifyInterpreterPurity` lint, which guards only the interpreter→kernel direction. Until then this is declared intent, not an enforced invariant. Extraction stays available as a later reviewed change carrying its own H4/H5 evidence.

**One design question B inherits.** The checker still needs numeric handling for numeric literals and declared field bounds, and it must not reach for `Dec` to get it: `Dec` carries the kernel's rounding and precision model, while a checker comparing declared configuration needs the value's text preserved. B decides that representation on the [transport-value rule](../CLAUDE.md) — a lexical or `BigDecimal`-style handling, never an evaluation type.

### Bare-reference resolution has FOUR owners; `ConditionBridge` DESIGNATED (corrected after review 2026-09-12)

`git grep -ln 'fieldsByShortName\|shortNamesAllowed' -- '*/src/main/*' '*/src/commonMain/*'` — all four read:

| Implementation | Module | Uniqueness test |
|---|---|---|
| [ParsedModel](../interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/ParsedModel.kt) | `:interpreter` | `singleOrNull()` |
| [ConditionBridge](../interpreter-bridge/src/main/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/bridge/ConditionBridge.kt) | `:interpreter-bridge` (JVM) | `matches.size == 1` |
| [ReferenceFragmentRemapper](../interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/workspace/ReferenceFragmentRemapper.kt) `appendShortName` | `:interpreter` | `matches.size != 1` |
| [ConditionReferenceRemapper](../core/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/core/ConditionReferenceRemapper.java) | `:core` | `matches.size() != 1` |

All four implement the same rule shape: candidate `base/name` first (declaring-group precedence), then a **unique** model-wide field short name only when the flag is on, with ambiguity and absence both falling back to the candidate.

**The fourth is the one that matters, and the first pass missed it entirely.** `ConditionReferenceRemapper` is invoked from `ModelReader` at lines 501, 543 and 589 — inside the **Expansion** responsibility the boundary table designates for reuse. It is therefore the owner B1 would most want to designate, and an arithmetic that counted two owners could not have seen it.

**Designate `ConditionBridge` and `ConditionReferenceRemapper`.** Both sit outside the interpreter, and the second already lives in a designated responsibility. **Residual owners after B1 are three, not two**: the shared resolver, plus `ParsedModel` and `ReferenceFragmentRemapper`, which stay inside the interpreter under NFR #1 because their fact source is the execution model.

**Two first-pass claims do not survive, and B1 must not inherit them.**

- **The laziness boundary does not exist.** `ConditionBridge.resolveBareReference` materializes the full `groupBy` over every field path on the **first bare reference**, before testing `shortNameRefsAllowed` — not on first fallback use. "The shared resolver must not force eager construction" was derived from a mechanism that is not there; any cost claim here needs measuring rather than reading.
- **"The same rule over different fact sources" is an equivalence claim and stays a HYPOTHESIS.** Two divergences are visible in source: short-name keys use `path.trimEnd('/').substringAfterLast('/')` in the interpreter's `Model.kt` but omit the `trimEnd` in `ConditionBridge` and `ReferenceFragmentRemapper`; and the candidate sets differ — `ModelDef.entityPaths` is built from fields, repeatables, group paths **plus rule anchor groups and computation groups/targets** with ancestors, while `ModelIndex.elementPaths()` is `reader.paths()`. B1 owes a differential over a model that separates those sets before one implementation may replace another.

### Stage-1 freezes (2026-09-12)

**First implementation family: reference binding (B1).** Its cases are [proposal §3.4](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#34-reuse-existing-static-reference-checking-distinguish-it-from-execution-preparation)'s four rows plus declaring-group precedence, relative and absolute references, and applicable indexed/category references. Fixture authoring follows [ShortNameTierLawsTest](../src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/ShortNameTierLawsTest.java), which builds through `DocumentModelDraft` and carries **raw bare-name condition strings on purpose**, because the claim is about a path form the typed renderer never emits — exactly the typed-valid-model-plus-minimal-raw-mutation discipline A4 requires.

**One fixture gap blocked B0's positive control — discharged 2026-09-12, by a different vehicle.** §3.4's four-row model is still **not committed**: `git ls-files` finds no `StaticReferenceReviewProbe`, and the proposal records that probe as session evidence rather than a regression lock. What the freeze actually required was a committed Kernel *acceptance* row — the matrix's only one, and the row the [testing spec](TESTING-SPEC.md)'s all-refusals argument makes mandatory — and [`ShortNameUniqueTierLawsTest`](../src/test/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/validate/laws/ShortNameUniqueTierLawsTest.java) holds it: `unique model-wide, flag ON` → ADMITTED through `checkConsistency`, beside the rejection it must be distinguished from. Landing §3.4's own model as an A4 corpus case remains worthwhile when that corpus kind exists; it is no longer what the positive control waits on. **§3.4's model is now committed after all, in a different vehicle again (2026-09-17)**: `consistency/consumer-tests/shared/models/catalog-reference-*.dm.json` carry the grid as five document models, driven end to end by both packaged arms and compared as full reports across all four readers. An A4 corpus case would still add the Kernel's own verdict beside ours; these carry the packaged behaviour, which is the half F's row 8 was missing.

**Performance workload identities: the existing four.** `interpreter/build.gradle` defines `performanceModelFixtures` as `B1`, `B2`, `MANY_RULES_48` and `MANY_RULES_192`, authored once through modelkit/`EditSession`/rulekit in `:adapter`, committed, then inlined into KMP test source by `generatePerformanceFixtureSource`. Those four names are the frozen workload identities, and the checker's rows attach to them rather than to a new set.

**Why not select workloads from the committed corpus.** Measured: `corpus/models` spans 6.4–24.0 KB — a 3.7× range, with 17 of 23 files clustered at 23.6–24.0 KB and six below 11 KB. So it does spread, contrary to the first pass's claim of a single cluster; what it lacks is a *rule-count* ladder, which is the axis `MANY_RULES_48` → `MANY_RULES_192` supplies. Declaration-count spread across the corpus was never measured — only file size was — so the reason to prefer the existing workloads is that they vary the axis we need, not that the corpus is uniform.

### A3 record — single pinned profile (decided 2026-09-12) <a id="a3"></a>

**The project keeps one A12 version, and A3's two-version basis is withdrawn with M.** The pinned profile is whatever the sibling `a12-kernel` checkout carries — today `2025.06-ext5` / kernel `30.8.1` — recorded with its artifact identity and oracle access. No second version is required, sought, or admissible while M is deferred; the reasoning is in [proposal §4](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#4-required-functionality-and-scope-boundaries)'s deferral record.

**What was measured before the deferral, kept because it is the evidence a revival would start from.** The sibling's `origin/main` is `f10b920` "Release 2026.06-ext0 - 31.1.0" and its local `main` is `cb66e51`, one commit apart, fetched 2026-07-17 — so a second version's *source* was never the obstacle. A runnable oracle was: `31.1.0` is not in `mavenLocal`, and publishing it means bootstrap Phase 0 over `f10b920`, carrying an unresolved a12-base version identity (`30.0.1` upstream against the kernel's pinned `30.0.0`). Those costs, plus the `28.4.0` → `29.4.0` format move, are what made the deferral the better trade.

**One route stays forbidden regardless.** The abandoned attempt scanned the wider machine Gradle cache and found facade jars for "30.7.1, 31.1.0 and 31.1.1" *(its measurement, quoted)* while conceding "a cached facade is not a verified transitive closure" and advertising no second profile. Machine-scanning for A12 releases is ruled out by [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md)'s sourcing rule; the sibling is the only admissible origin, and it defines the single version.

### B2 record — preparation reports what the Kernel's reader raises (decided 2026-09-12, corrected after review)

**Landed:** admission (well-formed source, retained tree, authored numeric scale, source identity, A12's comment parity), the declared element set at its canonical path, `content.modelConfig.fieldRefByShortNameAllowed`, the §5.4 category separation, and B2's five acceptance cases. Kernel-free module and test dependencies are guarded by two instruments on `:consistency:check` — `verifyConsistencyPurity` over the JVM target's compiled main and test classes (`jdeps`, the shared `verifyCompiledPurity`), which also refuses a source set that does not compile into that target, and `verifyConsistencyDependencies` over both targets' resolved runtime classpaths with an allowlist of module groups, which is the only instrument that can see a dependency nothing imports. Both replaced an import-text scan. `check` rather than `test` is what runs them, and a Kotlin Multiplatform module registers no `test` task at all, so the workflow's unqualified `test` reached neither them nor any checker test until `:consistency:check` was named explicitly.

**The canonical path is the join of `name` segments, NOT the element's `id` — corrected before landing.** The first implementation read `id` as the path, which is the claim [KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES](KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md#dm-json-shape-for-hand-authoring-fixtures) already carries a dated correction against: `id` is an opaque identifier in a namespace disjoint from the path, and keying by it once resolved ~0% of a foreign model's references. The Kernel derives the path by appending `getName()` (`DocumentModelVisitorWithFullNames:56`); `ElementDeserializer` merely *stores* `id`, so it could never have settled the question it was cited for.
