**The mechanism of that error is the one this project keeps a table about, so it is recorded rather than just fixed.** A fixture sweep found `id == path` on 934 elements of 52 models — and every model in that set was one we authored, where the two are equal by habit. The counterexample class was committed in this repository the whole time (`src/test/resources/models/idmismatch.dm.json` with hash ids, plus ten `workspace-preparation` opaque-id fixtures), and the selection excluded it. One constant across every row, no row on the other side of the axis, and the agreement of 934 of them established nothing. The prior-art search missed it too, for the adjacent reason: it grepped the terms the new work used (`ElementDeserializer`, `envelope`, `discriminator`) and the record states the fact in different words.

**A declaration is retained whole, not projected.** `DeclaredElement.declaration` is the entire authored node — envelope, element-level `annotations`, and the two descriptions, all of which `ElementSerializerUtil.serializeElementBase` writes and a per-kind projection would drop. A checker-side record of a rule's parts would be the parallel A12 model [proposal §5.3](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#53-retain-invalid-input-long-enough-to-diagnose-it) forbids, and it is also what would lose an *invalid* declaration.

**Where the Kernel's reader raises, preparation diagnoses — and nothing makes it raise.** A declaration missing `type` or `name` cannot be descended into (one names the envelope, the other places the subtree); a missing `id` is reported yet the element is still placed, because `id` no longer locates it. A kind outside the Kernel's closed four — including a mis-cased `"group"`, which hits its `default:` arm — is reported rather than passing as an inert declaration, since input A12 refuses being analysed here without a word is the direction no consumer can detect. Every array and boolean member is read through a diagnosing accessor, so no well-formed JSON escapes as an exception; that class was the alternative to a verdict or a coverage gap, and [proposal §6](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#6-public-contract) admits neither.

**Only a measured A12 constraint, violated at ERROR, rejects a model.** §5.4's three categories are `FindingCategory`, and `Finding.justifiesRejection` is the single locus that grants the power to one of them; an `AUTHORING_POLICY` or `EVALUATOR_SUPPORT` finding stated at ERROR does not reject, and an `A12_CONSTRAINT` finding at WARNING does not either. The ERROR threshold is **ours**, not a Kernel rejection: `checkConsistency` returns `void` and emits notifications, and every dmkits consumer draws the line at ERROR independently. Whether A12 treats a WARNING-only model as acceptable is an `:adapter` probe nobody has run.

**`ConsistencyStatus` has no `Valid`, and the control is behavioural rather than the compiler.** An exhaustive `when` over the sealed type forces a later `Valid` variant to *edit* the test, not to fail it, so the guard iterates what `statusOf` actually returns for every category × severity instead. `complete` is a constant `false` on both variants and carries no information yet — the coverage evidence that makes it informative is F's report.

**And an empty fact base now says so, rather than reading as a clean result.** A source that declares no elements produced the same findings-free report as one whose references all resolve, so a report meant "nothing was found to check" and read "nothing was wrong". It is a coverage gap and not a rejection because A12 accepts such a model: `DocumentModelDraft.typeDefModel` emits exactly an empty root, and the Kernel reads an absent `rootGroups` and an absent `modelRoot` too ([KF286](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf286)). The one shape its reader cannot take — a missing `content` — is the single admission refusal this stage adds, and `header.modelType` is deliberately not one, because the DocumentModel deserializer never consults it.

**Source identity is EXTRACTED, not asserted.** `ModelSource` has no id parameter: `admit` reads `header.id` and rejects a source without one (`SOURCE_HAS_NO_IDENTITY`), matching `WorkspaceModelSource`, which takes the same shape for the same reason — A12 puts a model's identity there and every cross-source reference resolves through it, so a caller-supplied id is an echo rather than the retention this stage's exit evidence names. It also closes the case where any well-formed JSON was admitted as if it were a model.

**Still open, logged rather than folded in.** `PreparedModel` collides by name with `:interpreter`'s unrelated type. `Finding.at` is a flat path where [proposal §6](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#6-public-contract) and `rulekit.validate.Diagnostic` both carry a structured `where` with a span.

**Carried out of the B3 cold review, each outside that slice's acceptance denominator.** A `Finding` names no SOURCE, so a workspace report cannot say which of several files a finding's declaration lives in — §6 requires that identity and `DeclarationOrigin.modelId` already holds it, so this is a projection question for F. `ConsistencyWorkspace` takes no resource limits, where the `ModelWorkspace` it mirrors takes `ModelPreparationLimits`; a chain mounting each level twice expands 2ⁿ (measured: depth 18, 415 ms) with no limit gap, and §6 requires a limit to be visible as incomplete coverage. A provider declaring a different `conditionLanguage` has its keywords read in the entry model's language, so a German provider in an English entry draws `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` where A12 says `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` — the checker has no parse-failure family yet, which is C's. An unresolvable alias is a gap here where `core.ModelReader` falls back to the alias as the reference; ours is the conservative arm and the divergence is named rather than silent. The module declares no JVM interop annotations at all, so a Java consumer writes `Companion.fromSources` — F's publication boundary. And `verifyConsistencyPurity` has no does-fire control proving its forbidden prefixes match a real class name, where `:interpreter-bridge` commits a plant for exactly that.

These are F's and B1's to settle; none violates a B2 or B3 acceptance item.

**Carried out of the reconciliation review, and the first two are a JVM-reader class rather than a checker one.** A reviewer's source read says the Kernel's *standard* Jackson binding accepts `"True"`/`"TRUE"` for `content.modelConfig.fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` where our four readers (`ModelReader`, `ModelDocument`, the interpreter's `ModelLoader`, `InterpreterConditionReadBack`) read `asBoolean` and say false — the dangerous direction, and a different carrier from the exclusion members this slice fixed, so it needs its own kernel measurement before anything changes; the interpreter's `IncludeAssembly` refuses every string spelling outright, which is a third reading of one member. The same shape covers `includeLevel`: our JVM readers expand an unreadable spelling as SINGLE_RG where the Kernel refuses to load the model at all, which `:consistency` already suspends. Separately, `:consistency` retains a mount group's authored `usageType` where expansion replaces it with the provider root's, INCLUDING its absence ([KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3)) — invisible until a family reads `usageType`, and the shared matrix cannot separate it because every attachment case suspends first.

### B3 record — a mounted model is checked as a closure, not one file at a time (2026-09-12)

**The whole analysis used to stop at a mount, and that was most models.** A group carrying `Group.modelAlias` declares elements this source does not hold, so every reference into it reads as undeclared and the only safe report was INCONCLUSIVE with a gap. `ConsistencyWorkspace.fromSources(...).check(entryModelId)` is where a caller supplies the rest — the interpreter's `ModelWorkspace` shape (finite, caller-supplied, EXACT-ID, admitted at construction, entry named per check), owned by the checker because the published artifact carries no interpreter dependency. `check(ModelSource)` stays as the one-shot the proposal's §6 also asks for.

**Nothing is rewritten to mount it.** Descent continues into the provider's root-group children with the mount's path as the parent, so every declaration is still the node its own author wrote — B2's retention, unchanged. Three Kernel facts decide the placement, all in [KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3): the provider's root group is REPLACED by the mount group rather than added above it (so the provider's own root name appears in no path, and a relative reference is move-invariant), the provider is fully expanded before it is mounted, and a mount group that already carries local `elements` is an ALREADY-EXPANDED mount whose reference is silently ignored.

**A reference that named the vanished root is rehosted, and that is the half a checker gets wrong silently.** `DeclarationOrigin` carries the model a declaration was authored in together with the prefix substitution its own absolute paths need. A relative path needs none; an absolute one needs exactly the substitution the Kernel applies before integration.

**The short-name fallback turned out to have two owners, which one denominator cannot express.** [KF287](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf287) measures the grid: a provider whose OWN `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` is on has its bare names bound before integration and searches that provider alone, while every other reference is bound over the whole expanded tree under the ENTRY model's flag — so a host field of the same name makes the second ambiguous and leaves the first untouched. The first implementation scoped every reference to its author and passed its own tests, because the fixture had no name both models declared; the surviving mutant is what exposed it.

**The cold review found the denominator wrong in both directions, and the doc sentence is what caused it.** [KF287](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf287) read "searches that provider alone", so the first implementation keyed the bound fallback on the AUTHORING MODEL — which drops a field the provider's own nested include contributed, and finds both copies when one provider is mounted twice. Both reject a model A12 accepts. The denominator is the MOUNT INSTANCE's expanded subtree; the finding is corrected in place and the two shapes now have rows, because the original eight-cell grid has one mount and no nesting and could not separate them. Three further review findings were the same shape — a value read by its text rather than its type (`excludeRules: "true"` is FALSE to A12), an `includeLevel` spelling A12's enum refuses being expanded as the default, and a provider's unreadable declaration diagnosed under the entry model's identity.

**The same misreading was live in the JVM readers this slice measured against, and is fixed there too.** `dm.core.ModelReader` and modelkit's `AuthoredStateReader` both read the two exclusion members with Jackson's `asBoolean`, which coerces `"true"` and `1` — so the clean-room expansion dropped an executable the Kernel keeps, and `ModelDocument.authoredState()` (and the model review built on it) reported an exclusion A12 never applies. The kernel lock that was supposed to bound this measured `{}` alone, which is *also* what the coercion reads as false; widening it to twelve spellings, including the near-misses `"True"` and `0.5` that the coercion reads as the Kernel does, is what made the divergence visible and what bounds it, and the fix is the [KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3) rule the checker already used. Fixing the component you measure against is the standing directive, not scope creep.

**The mounting now answers to the Kernel's own answers, not to fixtures I wrote.** `WorkspaceMatrixReconciliationTest` runs the 20-case shared raw-workspace matrix — authored in `:adapter` through the typed kits, its expanded facts locked against the real `expand` and the JVM reader by `WorkspacePreparationParityTest` — and compares the checker's prepared paths and kinds case by case. Eleven cases agree exactly, including a repeated mount, imported typedefs, a seven-source diamond and ten sources whose element ids are OPAQUE while the locked facts are keyed by path; nine name the mount they suspend. Condition TEXT is deliberately outside the comparison: the Kernel rewrites it at expansion where this checker retains the author's own node and rehosts only when resolving a reference. The oracle costs no classpath edge, because it arrives as committed data.

**Imported typedefs need no resolution at this stage.** A type definition is a FIELD TYPE keyed by id ([KF143](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf143)): it is inlined into a field's `fieldType`, so it cannot move or rename an element — the reason the three typedef-carrying cases prepare identically to the Kernel without the checker reading a single type is that mechanism, and the matrix confirms rather than establishes it, since a path-and-kind comparison is invariant under type resolution by construction. Resolution becomes load-bearing for the first family that reads a field's KIND, and belongs to that slice rather than to this one.

**Parse-once is asserted by IDENTITY, because nothing else can count reads.** A reparse and a defensive copy both produce an equal tree, so `PreparationReuseTest` measures sameness instead: a provider mounted twice yields one retained declaration node bound at two mount prefixes — §5.6's "reuse its syntax, resolve each mount separately" as a pair of assertions on one Kernel-locked workspace — and preparing the same admitted sources again reads nothing again.

**It found a silent under-report on its first run, which is the class that matters most.** A provider whose OWN root group is an unexpanded mount had its (empty) `elements` read directly, so the include contributed nothing and the inner alias was never visited — no facts, and no gap saying so, which would have turned every reference into that subtree into a finding against a model A12 accepts. The mount now gives the provider's root group the same treatment as any other group, which is what "a provider is fully expanded before it is mounted" ([KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3)) means one level down. The nineteen hand-written rows in `WorkspaceMountTest` had not distinguished it: every one of them mounts a provider whose root group holds its own elements.

**What is deliberately not expanded, each suspending the analysis by name rather than guessing.** `includeLevel: MODEL_ROOT`, `excludeRules`, `excludeComputations` — each changes which declarations survive or where they land, so an unimplemented one is a coverage gap at the mount. *(All three landed 2026-09-15: the level in [this record](#model-root-include), the two exclusions in [this one](#mount-exclusions). No mount DIRECTIVE suspends the analysis any more; the remaining suspensions are about the provider — a cycle, an unresolvable alias, an unsupplied source, a root count `SINGLE_RG` refuses, and an `includeLevel` spelling A12's enum cannot read.)* A malformed exclusion value is NOT one: the Kernel reads it as false and the content survives (KF3's coercion matrix), so suspending there would refuse an include A12 expands in full. A cycle, a provider with other than exactly one root group, an unresolvable alias and an unsupplied source are gaps too. So is an `includeLevel` spelling A12's enum cannot read — deliberately UNDER-reported, since A12 refuses to load such a model at all and reaching `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` from the mount path would need it to carry findings as well as gaps.

### B3 record — the second include level, and the substitution a single prefix could not express (2026-09-15) <a id="model-root-include"></a>

**A whole include level was suspended, and the oracle for it was already committed.** `includeLevel: MODEL_ROOT` mounts every top-level group of the provider, each KEEPING its own name as a subgroup of the mount, where `SINGLE_RG` replaces the mount with the provider's sole root ([KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3)). The checker suspended every such mount, so four of the shared matrix's cases reported no facts at all — and the expected placement for each was already in the matrix, generated from the real `expand`. Implementing a directive is therefore mostly a reading exercise: the matrix rows move from *suspended* to *agrees*. It is not entirely one — the cold review found three questions the matrix cannot answer, and they needed the kernel (see [the review record](#mount-review)). This landing took fourteen of the twenty COMPARABLE cases to agreeing, with the six exclusion cases following in [the next one](#mount-exclusions). The matrix carries 21; the twenty-first declares an `expectedPreparationDiagnostic` rather than a tree — it is a model the reader REFUSES, so it has no expansion to compare against and the generator drops it. Any count of "the matrix's cases" in this plan therefore means those twenty.

**The rehosting is what a second level actually costs.** `DeclarationOrigin` carried ONE prefix substitution, which is exactly right for `SINGLE_RG` (`/Address` → `/Order/ShipTo`) and cannot express `MODEL_ROOT`, where each provider root keeps its name (`/Address` → `/Order/ShipTo/Address`, `/Contact` → `/Order/ShipTo/Contact`). It now carries a MAP, one entry per root the mount placed. The interpreter's own kernel-parity-locked assembly reaches the same conclusion by a different route — a `List<MountedPrefix>` behind `SegmentPrefixIndex`, a trie walked one SEGMENT at a time that returns null rather than pick when two prefixes both match ([`IncludeAssembly.kt`](../interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/workspace/IncludeAssembly.kt)). This slice's map with first-match is not that structure, and the one invariant that makes it sufficient is worth stating rather than leaving implied: a mount's authored prefixes are its provider's root NAMES, one segment each and distinct, so no two can nest and nothing is ever ambiguous to pick between — and `rehost` matches on `"$authored/"`, which is the same `/` boundary that keeps `/Address` off `/AddressBook`. A mount whose prefixes could nest would need the interpreter's refusal, not this map.

**That alternative is an over-report, and naming it is the point.** Prefixing every absolute path with the mount instead would need no map and passes every same-root row. It also rewrites `/Order/Code` — a provider reference that happens to spell a HOST path — into a path nothing declares, so the checker would report `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` for a reference A12 resolves. The Kernel rewrites a reference because its TARGET moved, and that one's never did. A per-root map is safe in the other direction too: if A12 turned out to rewrite more widely than KF3's mechanism states, this checker stays silent where A12 speaks.

**A row that expects silence cannot see a suspended mount, which is how the first draft of the test was vacuous.** Every finding-based assertion about a mounted model passes while the mount is unexpanded, because a suspension silences every family — measured, on the first run of these rows, before the implementation existed. The completed-family assertion (`CheckFamily.CONDITION_REFERENCES in report.checksCompleted`) is what turns the empty finding list into a measurement, and it belongs in every future row about a mount.

**What the matrix still cannot see, so the hand-written rows stay.** Its multi-root providers are only ever mounted at `MODEL_ROOT`, which admits them, so `SINGLE_RG`'s refusal of a multi-root provider remains `WorkspaceMountTest`'s; and no matrix case carries a cross-root absolute reference, which is why the sideways row above is authored here with its direction of risk stated rather than left to an unmeasured assumption.

### B3 record — the mount exclusions, and why a filter became a refusal to collect (2026-09-15) <a id="mount-exclusions"></a>

**A mount's `excludeRules`/`excludeComputations` drop the provider's executables before A12 checks anything, so the checker must not have them in its fact base at all.** An excluded rule is ABSENT rather than silent, and the difference is a report: its defects are not A12's verdicts, so reporting them would be the checker speaking alone about a declaration A12 discarded. The instrument for every row is therefore a provider executable that IS defective — an unresolvable error target, an unresolvable computed field — because retention and exclusion then differ by a finding rather than by nothing.

**A12 filters the fully expanded provider; this walk declines to collect. Same filter, one phase earlier, and no second tree.** The consequence that has to be modelled either way is ACCUMULATION: a nested mount's content answers to its own flags and to every enclosing mount's, which is one carried value on the descent (`MountExclusions`) rather than a post-pass.

**The `attachment` shield is where the placement of the test does the work.** An attachment-usage group shields its subtree from any exclusion applied from above it, and the shield is read from the group's FINAL expanded `usageType` — so reading it from the mount group that named the provider gets two measured halves backwards. Testing it on the body being DESCENDED reproduces both for free: under `SINGLE_RG` the provider root's slot replaces the mount's, so an `attachment` marker authored on the mount is cleared by a provider that declares none and shields nothing, while the provider root's own marker shields the subtree that landed on the mount's path. The mount group's own `usageType` is consequently never consulted — A12 filters the mounted CHILDREN, so the shield is always some descended group's.

**The shared matrix's remaining six cases now agree, so all twenty do.** They are the ones worth having: rules excluded with the computation retained and its mirror, an outer exclusion carried into a nested mount, and the two attachment templates mounted at DIFFERENT levels. A suspension appearing in that table is now a regression rather than a status.

**One clause is inert and needs a row of its own.** `excludeRules` on an ordinary group is accepted by A12 and changes nothing (KF3), so a checker reading the members wherever they appear would drop declarations A12 checks — an under-report that hides real defects, and invisible without a row, since the flag has no other effect to notice.

### B3 record — a provider root A12 refuses is a gap, not a tree to judge (2026-09-15) <a id="unmountable-root"></a>

**Three provider-root shapes the Kernel refuses were being mounted, and two of them silently.** `SINGLE_RG` needs exactly one non-repeatable root group carrying its object body; a known non-Group root and a Group root without a body are measured refusals ([KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3), `adapter.WorkspaceIncludeStructureKernelTest`). The checker read the missing body as an empty subtree, so the mount contributed nothing, no gap said so, and every reference into it would have been a finding against a model A12 never built — the same silent under-mount class B3's first run turned up one level up, which is why the shapes now suspend by name.

**The repeatability matrix is not the rule the member's name suggests, and that is the whole finding.** A12 coerces the declared value to an INTEGER before its non-repeatable test, so `1.9`, `0.5`, `-1`, `-1.5`, an explicit null and the numeric string `"1"` are all ACCEPTED while `2` and `2.1` are refused, and anything that is no number at all — a Boolean, a container, `"one"` — is refused too. An implementation that demanded exactly `1` would suspend mounts A12 expands in full, which is why the grid is KF3's measured matrix rather than a derived predicate. **Corrected 2026-09-15 after the cold review:** the first landing generalized that numeric rule to STRINGS, where A12 binds an INTEGER instead — so `"1.5"` is a reader refusal where the number `1.5` is admitted, and the checker was mounting providers A12 refuses. Both matrices are now measured and the two refusals are reported apart (KF3, extended).

### B3 record — what the terminal review found, and the three questions it sent to the Kernel (2026-09-15) <a id="mount-review"></a>

**The review's first finding was a live OVER-REPORT on half the oracle's own cases, and the oracle could not see it.** A field whose `TypeDefType` names a definition a MOUNTED provider declares drew `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, because the type-definition id set was read from the ENTRY model's `content.typeDefinitions` alone — while A12's `TypeDefCache` keys definitions by id across the whole loaded closure ([KF3](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf3)). Ten of the twenty comparable matrix cases were affected and the matrix's own kernel-generated effective field types prove the Kernel resolved every one of them. Five of the ten were suspended before this row's landings, so implementing the directives turned the checker from silent to falsely-INVALID on five models A12 builds.

**Why it stood: the reconciliation compares paths and KINDS.** A family reporting an ERROR about a mounted declaration agrees on placement and refuses the model anyway. The net that closes the class is one assertion with an unimpeachable oracle — the matrix IS the Kernel having expanded each case, so `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` on any of them is a defect by construction, whatever the model says. It is deliberately the ONLY finding-level claim made there: nothing in the matrix measures `checkConsistency`, so asserting general silence would assert something unmeasured.

**Three findings were claims about A12 that neither side had run, and all three went to the Kernel.** The string arm of `repeatability` (the checker had generalized the numeric truncation to strings, where A12 binds an integer — a live refusal of models A12 admits and a live admission of models it refuses); a `MODEL_ROOT` include of a provider with no root group (refused, so the new suspension is measured rather than chosen); and whether a `MODEL_ROOT` mount group's own `attachment` marker shields its subtree. The third is the one worth recording: KF3's replacement clause is measured for `SINGLE_RG`, so the review's KF3-derived expectation was that the marker survives and shields. It does not — expansion CLEARS it — and the row that says so needed two controls, because without the exclusion-free row the marked and unmarked measurements agree vacuously. Our own clean-room readers preserve the marker and are wrong about it, with no reachable witness ([INTERPRETER-GAPS](INTERPRETER-GAPS.md) IG151).

**Two silent mounts and one lost diagnostic, each the same class as a defect this row had already fixed one level up.** A non-object entry in `rootGroups` was filtered out and mounted around (so a MODEL_ROOT mount placed the survivors and a SINGLE_RG mount satisfied its single-root count) and a rootless `MODEL_ROOT` provider mounted nothing at all — no facts and no gap, which is exactly what `unmountableRoot`'s own KDoc condemns. And an excluded declaration lost the diagnostic for an envelope A12's reader cannot bind, though the argument for keeping the missing-`id` one applies verbatim.

**Three mutants survived the whole suite, and each named a real missing control** rather than a defect: the provider-bound fallback's segment boundary in `Reference.kt` (losing it makes a sibling mount at `/Order/ShipToBilling` ambiguous with `/Order/ShipTo`), the nameless-root refusal at `MODEL_ROOT`, and the mount-marker shield — the third settled by the Kernel rows above. The refusal rows also asserted only that SOME gap existed at the mount path, which is how a gap reading "is repeatable" about `{}` survived; each now asserts its reason.

### B1 record — reference binding classifies three outcomes, not two (2026-09-12)

**A12 rejects an undeclared name and an ambiguous one DIFFERENTLY, so the checker must too.** `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` and `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE` carry different correctives — declare the field, versus qualify the reference so it names one — and the interpreter's existing static helper reports both as the same unresolved path ([proposal §3.4](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#34-reuse-existing-static-reference-checking-distinguish-it-from-execution-preparation)). `Reference` is therefore a three-arm sum: `Resolved`, `Undeclared`, `Ambiguous`.

**The tier rule is the Kernel's, not a re-derivation.** The declaring group is tried first and wins outright; only on a miss does the model-wide fallback apply, and only when `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` is on — the flag gates the second tier alone, which is why it changes the verdict for a name the group lacks and changes nothing for one it has. The fallback indexes **fields**, not every element; a resolver that indexed groups too would resolve a group short name and never be noticed, so that is its own control.

**The grid's accept-side row had no committed oracle until now.** `ShortNameTierLawsTest` holds the ambiguous pair, where the flag only chooses between two rejections — which cannot show the fallback SUCCEEDING, so on its own it leaves open whether the flag enables a lookup or retitles a refusal. `ShortNameUniqueTierLawsTest` adds the unique pair (`MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` → accepted), moving exactly one thing: the sibling group declares `Other` rather than a second `Code`. §3.4 had recorded those rows as executed session evidence and explicitly not locked.

### C2 record — the reference rejections, and where their correctives live (2026-09-12)

**Both codes ride the finding, and both are A12's.** `Reference.Undeclared` → `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`, `Reference.Ambiguous` → `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`, each `A12_CONSTRAINT` at ERROR because each is a locked Kernel verdict rather than a policy of ours. The explain names the ambiguous candidates, since a reader cannot qualify a reference to one of them without seeing them.

**A gap in the corrective owner was fixed in the owner.** `StructuralKernelFix` had a hint for `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` and none for `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`, so the CLI's enricher said nothing on the harder of the two — and it is the harder one precisely because its corrective is the *opposite*: qualify the reference rather than declare the field, and turning `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` off converts this code into the other rather than fixing anything. That hint now exists, which serves every JVM consumer.

**Sharing that guidance with the portable report is C2's remaining piece, deliberately not improvised.** The checker is Kernel-free and carries no project dependency, so it cannot read `StructuralKernelFix`; restating the prose in Kotlin would mint the second live statement the doc rules forbid. The portable findings therefore carry the code and the observation and no `fix` text, rather than advertising guidance with nothing behind it. The single-owner mechanism — a shared resource both sides read, or generation from the Java owner — belongs with F's report contract, which is where the `fix`/`explain` fields become public.

### B0 record — the reference class that needs no parser, and the one that does (2026-09-12)

**Two reference classes, and only one of them is behind the syntax layer.** A12 declares a rule's error target and a computation's target as **structured members** (`errorEntityRelPath`, `computedFieldRelPath`), not as DSL text, so `declaredReferences()` resolves them with no condition parser at all. The references inside condition and operation text are the other class, and they wait for syntax.

**The relative path resolves from the declaring ELEMENT, not from its group**, which matters because the two readings agree on almost everything anyone writes: a rule at `/Order/Check` reaches its sibling as `../Code`, and dropping that step names a child of the rule. `ErrorFieldRelativePathLawsTest` locks it, and the row that discriminates is the only one in this repository where a group-relative resolver would have disagreed with A12.

**Two neighbouring gates were measured in the same pass, because each rejects a path that resolves perfectly well** and a reader seeing only the rejection would blame the path: the condition must also reference the target (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`), and that gate is why the lock's rows vary the error field together with the condition's own reference rather than the path alone — varying the path alone moved two variables and produced three wrong expectations before the Kernel said so. With the reference present, a target inside a repeatable group is ADMITTED, which bounds the second gate rather than leaving it as a guess.

**Consequence for the checker's contract:** `declaredReferences()` answers "does this path name a declared element" and nothing more. A resolvable path is necessary and not sufficient for A12 admission, so this slice's findings must never be read as a rule-level verdict — the remaining gates belong to D.

**The first public operation is `check(ModelSource): ConsistencyReport`.** It admits, prepares, resolves the structural references and reports — INVALID when one does not resolve, INCONCLUSIVE otherwise, never VALID, because `ConsistencyStatus` has no such arm and the coverage registry that would earn it does not exist. `findings` and `diagnostics` stay separate arrays on purpose: a finding is A12's verdict on a declaration, a diagnostic is the checker saying it could not read something, and folding them together is precisely the conflation `ModelLoader.loadAndCheck` makes on the Java side.

**`checksCompleted` is evidence, and every report also states what it could NOT check.** `CheckFamily` has one constant today, so "the reference check completed" would read as "the analysis completed" if nothing said otherwise; each report therefore carries an explicit gap naming the unimplemented families, and each unreadable declaration becomes a gap of its own rather than a silent omission. An unreadable source reports at the SOURCE level with no check completed, instead of inventing a rule location for it.

**The syntax layer is the next partition, and it is not small.** The Kernel's condition lexer resolves operator keywords *dynamically per language* (`ID` carries `setTypeInLexer`, driven by the multi-language handler), so a faithful checker-owned tokenizer needs the per-language keyword catalog rather than fixed literals — and the neutral-leaf record already rules out sharing the interpreter's parser. That is B0's remaining half and slice C's foundation, with its own acceptance matrix.

### B0 record — the syntax layer reads condition references without a parser (2026-09-12)

**Acceptance matrix, all measured on `referencesIn(text, language)`.** A keyword is not a reference and its argument is; an INFIX keyword is not a reference either, which is the case a structural guess gets wrong because `And` is a bare name with no parentheses; a string literal and a comment contribute nothing however condition-shaped their content; a path is kept whole; a quoted name IS a reference whatever it spells; a category after `->` is not one; `@From` is a single terminal, so its second half is not a name. Non-goals are stated on the function: `$` is dropped because it selects an outer repetition of the same declared element, an all-digit name is read as a constant, and nothing here decides whether the text parses.

**The keyword table is PER LANGUAGE, and that is a correctness requirement rather than completeness.** A model declares `content.modelConfig.conditionLanguage.code`, the Kernel resolves a name against that language's terminals alone, and the two vocabularies are not translations of one set ([KF283](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf283)). A merged table reads a German model's field named `And` as an operator and drops the reference with no diagnostic — a silent false negative, which is the worst shape a checker can have. The table is generated from rulekit's catalog plus the terminals that catalog deliberately omits, declared with their reason in `consistency/build.gradle`, and `consistency.ConditionKeywordProjectionTest` pins the union to the Kernel's own bundles in both directions so neither list can drift.

**The discriminator against arithmetic is the OPERAND, not the spacing — and getting that wrong is the slice's main correction (cold review, 2026-09-12).** The first version read `[A] / [B]` as division and `A/B` as a path by requiring path tokens to touch, and justified it in a KDoc and here with "the lexer's `entity` rule admits no whitespace between a path's parts". That is false and this repository already said so: whitespace rides the lexer's HIDDEN channel and is insignificant between any two tokens ([KF76](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf76)), which `:interpreter` had already hit, root-caused and locked (IF75/IF91, `PathWhitespaceParseTest`). KF76 also gives the real discriminator in one sentence — a bare identifier is never an arithmetic operand, so `/` and `*` after a bare path segment are always path syntax — and the adjacency check had no control, surviving its mutant against the whole lane. Three of the review's findings were the same shape: re-deriving what the interpreter's findings already record, and getting wrong the cases it names as its hardest.

The tokenizer's own two departures from a naive lexer stand, and each now has a control: a quoted name keeps its own kind, because the Kernel keeps the escape in the token TYPE (`NameOhnePunktMitQuotes` is a different terminal from the keyword the name spells); and a digit run followed by a letter is one NAME, because `NameOhnePunktZifferAmAnfang` is `ZiffernFolge STR …` — splitting `1City` hands a consumer the reference `City`, which usually exists.

**The references are now bound, and binding turned up a shipped defect in the codes.** A condition's relative path resolves from the rule's GROUP while `errorEntityRelPath` resolves from the rule ELEMENT — one level apart, both spellings natural — and the floor is the root group rather than the model root, with `MVK_TOO_MANY_PARENT_GROUPS` of its own ([KF284](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf284)). The same pass measured that the code for an unresolvable reference depends on WHERE it was written, and the checker was reporting `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` for all three: it is `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_MISSING` for a rule's error target, `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` inside a condition, and no code at all for a computation's computed field.

**That last one is why the report grew a gap rather than just a finding.** The Kernel cannot BUILD a model whose computed field is missing, so it reports that and stops — no consistency verdict is produced, not even for defects the same model plainly has. A portable report listing findings for such a model would present its own reading as A12's, so the report says so at the declaration, and the code it uses (`CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`) is the one code this checker owns, minted only after establishing that A12 defines none. It carries every shape with that outcome rather than one of them — an unresolvable computed field, a declared target that climbs past the root, and an unsupported `conditionLanguage` — because what a consumer must act on is the same in all three: A12 never judged this model at all.

**Three further rejections the review's measurements added, all of them cases where reporting the wrong thing rejects a model A12 accepts.** A candidate at ROOT-GROUP level draws `MVK_INVALID_ROOT_GROUP` rather than `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`. A group that MOUNTS another model (`modelAlias` with no `elements`) leaves the fact base incomplete, so the reference families are suspended with a gap instead of reporting every reference into it as undeclared. And an unsupported `conditionLanguage` is a REFUSAL, not a fallback — A12 rejects the model outright, so no reading of its conditions exists to report ([KF283](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf283), corrected).

**What this does not yet do.** Reference EXTRACTION is complete and BINDING covers the paths above; what a reference is allowed to be — the iteration gates, the wildcard rules, the operand types — belongs to D, and a resolvable reference remains necessary and not sufficient. Declared non-goals, each stated at its locus: `$`, an all-digit name and the H-model row specifiers on `referencesIn`; and the turning-point label's ANCESTOR check (A12 verifies it, this does not, which under-reports). (`Group.indexFieldName` / `Group.sortFields[].name` were listed here as unexamined until their own C family landed; see its record below.)

### Syntax-layer record — partitioned, and its first phase landed (2026-09-12)

**The partition exists because the layer is not one slice, and A12 itself says where the seams are.** A condition that does not parse draws one of FOUR phase-specific codes, measured through `checkConsistency` ([KF300](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf300)): `MVK_LEXER_STANDARD_ERROR` when the text cannot be tokenized, then `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` and `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT` from the parser. The phases differ in RISK as much as in code, which is what makes them separate increments rather than one.

| increment | decidable from | over-rejection risk | state |
|---|---|---|---|
| **lexical** — the text tokenizes at all | the characters alone | none: a lexical failure does not depend on the grammar | **landed** (`CheckFamily.CONDITION_LEXICALITY`) |
| ~~**balanced structure** — delimiters pair up~~ | — | — | **WITHDRAWN, measured 2026-09-12** |
| **completeness** — the text ends mid-expression with every delimiter closed | the last significant token, in delimiter-balanced text | none: the balanced region is measured end to end | **landed, narrowed 2026-09-12** (`CheckFamily.CONDITION_COMPLETENESS`) |
| **connective agreement** — `And` and `Or` are not mixed within one bracket PAIR | the token stream plus a bracket-pair stack | none: the rule is scoped to a pair, and every pair is delimited lexically | **landed 2026-09-13** (`CheckFamily.CONNECTIVE_AGREEMENT`, [KF301](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf301)) |
| **grammatical** — the tokens form a condition A12 accepts | a parser over the token stream, deliberately LOOSER than the grammar | **high — this is the one that can reject a legal model** | **landed 2026-09-13** (`CheckFamily.CONDITION_GRAMMAR`), always-on populations green and the opt-in `a12-kernel-mm` arm met |

**The middle increment was withdrawn the day it was planned, and the reason generalizes.** It assumed a delimiter imbalance could be reported without a grammar by counting. A12 does not partition the failures that way: only the shape where the input ENDS with something still open draws `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND`, while an unclosed value bracket (`FieldFilled([Tier)`) and a bracket closing a parenthesis (`FieldFilled(Tier]`) both draw `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` — the parser meets a token that is wrong where it stands before it ever runs out of input. The distinction is POSITIONAL, not arithmetic, so counting can detect an imbalance but never pick which of the two codes A12 gives, and a checker that guessed would report the wrong one on three of the five measured shapes. Two of the five predictions in that grid were wrong in exactly this way. The layer is therefore two phases, not three.

**The first increment reports only the shape that was measured.** An unterminated string literal, which the checker's tokenizer already recognized and the report simply never surfaced. A12's own message text ("Character '%s' at position %d is incorrect") shows it also refuses illegal CHARACTERS, and which those are is unmeasured — `@@@` lexes cleanly and fails a phase later — so nothing is claimed about them and the report carries a gap saying exactly that.

**What replaced the withdrawn increment — and it was itself narrowed within the day, on the same kind of error.** A12 reports `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT` when it runs out of input, measured as one class across word operators, a comparison, arithmetic and a trailing comma. That was first stated as "whether it happens depends only on the LAST significant token, not on the structure before it", with the operator catalog's `OPERATOR`/`PATH_OP` split said to track A12's parse behaviour so the generated keyword table's kind column was safe to key on. **Both halves are retracted** (KERNEL-FINDINGS KF300): `FieldFilled(Tier For` — the same `PATH_OP` with one bracket removed — is `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT`, `FieldFilled(Tier And` is `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, and the two `PATH_OP` members that were never measured (`@From`, `Having`) both behave opposite to the one that was. The deciding variable is an unclosed delimiter EARLIER in the text — precisely what the account denied could matter — and the account rested on one table member generalized to a kind. The family now fires only where every delimiter is closed, a boundary around the measured rows rather than a mechanism, and is silent elsewhere.

**The lesson the two withdrawals share, since this layer has now produced it twice in one day.** Both increments were justified by a rule that reads the text without a grammar — count the delimiters; look at the last token — and A12 defeated both the same way, by deciding the code from the parser's STATE. Which is the finding the grammatical increment inherits: there is no third grammar-free phase to find, and any future proposal for one starts already refuted unless it carries a discriminating case the two grids do not already cover.

**One candidate has now cleared that bar, and the bar is what it was made to clear (measured 2026-09-13, [KF301](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf301)).** Mixing `And` with `Or` inside one bracket pair draws `MVK_BRACKET_MISSING`, and unlike the two withdrawn rules the code does not depend on parser state: the refusal is a semantic ACTION on the `bedingung` context, and exactly three productions open one — the entry rule, `geklammerteBedingung`, and the entity filter's `'Mit' filterCond=bedingung` — each of which a tokenizer already sees. The two discriminating cases neither earlier grid covers, both of which reject a cheap implementation that would otherwise look right: **five conditions carrying BOTH spellings at different levels are admitted**, so "both spellings appear in the text" over-rejects five times; and **two sibling calls one parenthesis deep, one carrying `And` and the other `Or`, are admitted**, so a depth COUNTER over-rejects while a bracket-PAIR stack does not. The arity half of the same measurement refuted a claim this plan's own scope rested on — [GRAMMAR-LEDGER](GRAMMAR-LEDGER.md) said a bracket level takes at most three parts; four are admitted in both connectives.

**The gate the GRAMMATICAL increment must pass before it lands, recorded now so it is not negotiated later.** A parser stricter than A12's rejects models the Kernel accepts, which is the one direction this checker must never take, so that increment is not shippable on unit tests alone: it needs the corpus `adapter.laws.ParserCorpusSweepTest` already drives — every catalog `validExample` and `computationExample`, their German localizations, and the opt-in real corpora — replayed against the checker's parser with a 100% acceptance bar on the always-on populations. The catalog lives in `rulekit` and the checker must not depend on it, so the corpus is GENERATED into `:consistency`. *(Corrected 2026-09-12: this said "the way its keyword table already does", i.e. generated into the committed source tree and held by a regenerate-and-diff guard. The module carries a second, stronger pattern and the corpus should use it — `generateWorkspaceMatrix` writes into `build/generated/.../commonTest/kotlin`, registered as a `commonTest` srcDir, so the artifact cannot drift from its source by construction and needs no diff guard at all. The committed-tree pattern earns its place for the keyword table, which is read by humans and cited in findings; a test corpus is not.)* That generator is part of the grammatical increment, not a prerequisite to be done separately. **Landed 2026-09-13, ahead of the parser and deliberately so** (`:consistency:generateConditionCorpus`, `ConditionCorpusTest`): the corpus is 154 conditions — every `validExample` and every `invalidExample` — plus 8 operations, each with a German localization, and the bar it enforces today is that the three landed syntax families report NOTHING about any of them in either language. Standing it up now rather than with the parser bought the first independent evidence that those three do not over-reject, on a population no unit test of theirs could supply; the parser inherits the same gate with acceptance added to silence.

**What the parser checks, and what it deliberately leaves to A12 (landed 2026-09-13).** It reads the shape the productions SHARE — a call is a name and a parenthesised argument list, an argument is an expression, a path is steps joined by separators — and not each operator's own argument arity or operand kinds. Reproducing those per operator would be a second copy of the catalog, and every place the copy disagreed would refuse a condition A12 admits; the price is silence on a wrong-arity call, which the report's gap states. Which of the two codes a rejection draws is decided by where the parser stopped, and both halves are measured in [KF300](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf300): running out of text with a delimiter still open is `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` — the only shape of the delimiter grid that does — and everything else, including reaching the end with nothing open, is `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`.

**The phases run in A12's order and the first to speak owns the text.** A member that cannot be tokenized is the lexical family's, one that ends on a dangling operator is the completeness family's, and only what survives both reaches the parser. Without that ordering one defect would draw two findings with two codes.

**The matrix is met on every population that exists on this machine (2026-09-13).** The always-on ones are green — 154 conditions and 8 operations, each in both condition languages, all accepted, with the nine measured reject shapes still refused and four valid controls still accepted. The **opt-in real corpora arm now runs too**, through `adapter.laws.CheckerParserCorpusSweepTest`: `:adapter` gained a test-scoped dependency on `:consistency` and the harvest moved to a shared `ParserCorpora`, so the checker's parser and the interpreter's meet the SAME strings rather than two populations that can drift. Over `a12-kernel-mm` — **657 stored conditions and 4 operations from 59 models, none of which this project wrote** — the parser refuses nothing and its lexer refuses nothing. The A12 Tools workspaces arm skips here as every workspaces harness does, so that half of the corpora row is unmeasured rather than met.

**A mutation sweep over the parser's spelling sets then found what no population reaches, which is the class the corpora could not.** Every text in the corpora and the generated corpus is evidence that the parser does not over-reject; none of it is evidence that a WIDENING is load-bearing, and the two questions look the same from a green build. Removing `/` from the parser's arithmetic set left the entire estate green, and so did dropping `RegelKontext`, then `To`/`TO`/`to`/`Zu`/`ZU`/`zu`. All three are correct — division and exponentiation are ADMITTED ([KF302](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf302)), and the value-list infixes and the rule-context entity are pinned into the generated keyword table by `ConditionKeywordProjectionTest` against the Kernel's own terminal bundle — so each gained a control rather than a narrowing. The transferable half: a corpus proves the absence of over-rejection and says nothing about whether a widening is exercised, so a parser built to be deliberately loose needs a mutation pass over its own admission sets, not only a sweep.

**The sweep's own first run was the reason its language is now read rather than assumed.** It reported 655 over-rejections and looked exactly like a parser defect; all 59 kernel-mm models declare `en_US` outright and the sweep was putting them through German, under which every operator keyword reads as an undeclared name. The corpus therefore cannot show the harvest reading a declaration at all — one constant across every row — so `ParserCorporaTest` supplies the model that varies it. The transferable half is that a corpus sweep's own configuration is a claim needing an oracle, and a wrong one fails in the direction that indicts the code under test.

### Grammatical increment — acceptance matrix (frozen 2026-09-12, before any parser code)

**The denominator is finite and already classified.** `docs/GRAMMAR-LEDGER.md` classifies all **119** parser productions of the Kernel's condition grammar: **70 rendered, 23 plumbing, 3 entry, 2 directive** — the **98** this increment owns — and **21 legacy** (H-model multi-form/Vordruck/USB and bounded row iteration), which are the named non-goal, the same boundary the interpreter draws. Nothing is `queued`, so the scope cannot quietly grow.

**ACCEPT — the floor, always-on, 100% required.** A parser stricter than A12's rejects models the Kernel admits, so this half is the gate, not the reject half.

| population | count | why it is an ACCEPT population |
|---|---|---|
| catalog `validExample.dsl` | 110 | CI-executed against the real Kernel |
| catalog `computationExample.operation` | 8 | same |
| catalog **`invalidExample.dsl`** | 44 | **grammatically VALID — measured: 0 of 44 carries a parse code**, they fail on 27 distinct SEMANTIC codes (`MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`, `MVK_NO_CONST`, …). `[CustomerName] <= [ProductCode]` parses perfectly and is a type error |
| the German localization of all of the above | ×2 | the keyword table is per language; a parser that only handled English would pass every row above and be half a parser |
| the opt-in real corpora (`a12-kernel-mm`, A12 Tools workspaces) | unbounded | skipped when absent; every failure must fall in an explicitly classified bucket, never a bare count |

**The `invalidExample` row is the trap this matrix exists to prevent.** The name invites reading it as a reject population, and a parser built to reject those 44 would refuse 44 grammatically correct conditions — over-rejection, in the one direction this checker must never take.

**REJECT — each row with the code A12 gives, all measured ([KF300](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf300)).**

| shape | code | state |
|---|---|---|
| unterminated string literal | `MVK_LEXER_STANDARD_ERROR` | **landed** |
| trailing infix operator, comparison, arithmetic, comma | `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT` | **landed** |
| everything still open at end of input | `MVK_EXPECTED_TOKEN_NOT_FOUND` | this increment |
| stray punctuation · unknown name used as a call · foreign-language keyword · bare field reference as the whole condition · extra closing parenthesis · unclosed value bracket · bracket closing a parenthesis · stray closing bracket · dangling path operator | `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` | this increment |

**NON-GOALS, named rather than left silent.** The 21 legacy productions. A12's illegal-CHARACTER class (its message text implies one; no row measures it). Operand TYPE compatibility — the 27 semantic codes above are a separate layer needing model-resolved types. ARITY, which is **not derivable from the operator catalog at all**: `operands` is a per-slot type contract and 15 of 110 operators pass a different argument count than their slot count in their own kernel-executed example ([CATALOG-SPEC §2](CATALOG-SPEC.md)).

**Oracles.** Every REJECT row is settled by `checkConsistency` through the laws tier (`rulekit.validate.laws.RuleConditionSyntaxLawsTest`), never by reading the grammar. Every ACCEPT row is settled by the generated corpus below. A production's presence in the ledger is a denominator, not an oracle.

**The gate, and it is a build gate rather than a promise.** The catalog lives in `rulekit` and `:consistency` must not depend on it, so the corpus reaches the module the way its keyword table already does: GENERATED into `commonTest` by a Gradle task, with a `verify…` twin that regenerates and diffs, wired to `check`. Until that task exists and those 162 EN texts plus their localizations parse, the increment does not land. **Production coverage of the corpus is NOT claimed** — nobody has mapped examples to productions, and asserting that 162 texts exercise 98 productions without measuring it would be the completeness claim this record is written to avoid.

**Family ownership is settled, and it was not obvious.** An unknown name used as a call, a foreign-language keyword, and a bare field reference as the whole condition are each `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` — parse failures, not reference failures. So an unresolvable name never reaches A12's reference check, and the syntax layer owns it: reporting one of them from the reference family would name the wrong defect, and reporting from both would report it twice.


### C record — first frozen family: a declaration's own SLOTS (2026-09-12)

**Frozen family: the `name` and `errorMessage` slots of a stored rule or computation.** It is the family that can land before the checker owns a syntax layer — every row reads members A12 declares as structured JSON — and it is the first one where the Java builders' knowledge is a real asset rather than an analogy, because [proposal §3.2](PORTABLE-CONSISTENCY-PROPOSAL.md#32-java-already-contains-valuable-validation-knowledge-embedded-in-authoring)'s named sources (`Rule.Builder.build`, `LocalizedText.checkDeclared`) enforce exactly these five shapes today.

| shape | A12 | the checker reports |
|---|---|---|
| `name` begins `vk_vk`, any letter case | rejects | `MVK_RULE_NAME_INVALID`, `A12_CONSTRAINT` / ERROR |
| a RULE's `errorCode` absent or blank | rejects | `MVK_ERROR_CODE_MISSING`, same |
| a rule's `severity` absent, or spelled outside its enum | **admits** the absent one; refuses to READ the others | nothing — `Rule.Builder`'s `MVK_SEVERITY_MISSING` has no Kernel basis |
| no text collected for any declared locale — the member absent or empty, every `text` blank, or every entry naming an UNDECLARED locale | rejects | `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING`, same |
| one declared locale's entry absent or blank, others filled | rejects | `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_FOR_LANGUAGE_MISSING`, same |
| `header.locales` empty | rejects, and then collects no text at all | `MVK_SUPP_LANGUAGES_MISSING` model-level, same, beside a per-declaration `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING` |
| a message entry without a string `locale`/`text`, or any locale code A12's parser refuses, on either side | **never reads the source** | `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, same — and that declaration's messages go unjudged |
| an entry for an UNDECLARED locale | **admits**, never collecting the text | `RK_UNDECLARED_MESSAGE_LOCALE`, `AUTHORING_POLICY` / WARNING |
| DUPLICATE entries for one locale | **admits**, keeping the LAST | `MVK_LANGUAGE_DUPLICATE`, `AUTHORING_POLICY` / WARNING |

**The two admitted rows carry as much weight as the three refusals**, and they are why C1's "authoring policy remains separately classified" needed a family with real rows rather than prose: `LocalizedText.checkDeclared` refuses both, deliberately and for good reasons, and a checker that inherited either refusal would reject a model the Kernel accepts. `Finding.justifiesRejection` already withholds that power from the category; these are the first findings to exercise it.

**Both carriers measured, and the one that looked settled was not.** `MVK_RULE_NAME_INVALID` reads as a rule's law and rejects a computation too — the Kernel calls both elements "Rule" in its diagnostics ([KF160](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf160), widened). The prediction going in was the opposite, and it is exactly the scope transfer that needs no new branch and so prompts no question. It also exposed a live gap in the component being measured against: `Computation.Builder.build` had no such pre-check where `Rule.Builder.build` has had one since 2026-06-10, so it now has one.

**One CONSTANT ran through every fixture this family inherited, and two rows were added to break it.** Every message measurement in [KERNEL-FINDINGS §13](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf158) held two declared locales and blanked *all* of them, so "a blank text is no message" and "this locale has no message" were one observation; blanking only `de_DE` separates them and draws the per-locale code. And emptying `header.locales` is the only row on the other side of that axis: the message demand survives, so the checker may report `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING` without first knowing what the model declares.

**Which locale set a MOUNTED declaration answers to had no answer, and A12 gives it one layer earlier than expected.** A provider that does not declare every locale of the entry model cannot be expanded at all ([KF288](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf288)), so no mounted declaration ever faces a narrower set and the entry's is the only one in play. Preparation now suspends such a mount by name — under-reporting deliberately, on the same footing as an `includeLevel` spelling A12's enum refuses.

**The family's first landing read `errorMessage` and `locales` by rules the Kernel does not use, and a cold review found six symptoms of that one mechanism.** The fixtures it was measured against all came through `ModelWriter`, which emits one canonical spelling of everything, so the checker's reader was written for canonical DM-JSON and every departure from it was a guess: locales compared as TEXT (`en-US` would have drawn a coverage gap on a model A12 admits), `header.locales` read only in its `{code}` shape (a bare-string header silently switched off every per-locale demand), the FIRST usable entry kept where A12 keeps the last, messages collected for any locale rather than only declared ones, and a shape A12's own reader refuses reported as `MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING` — presenting the checker's opinion as A12's about a model A12 never judged. The fix is one mechanism, not six patches: both sides go through one `localeKeyOrNull` mirroring A12's parser, including which spellings it REFUSES, and a refusal is reported as a refusal. [KF290](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf290) holds the measurements, taken with the two sides varied independently because a table that moves both attributes every refusal to the header.

**The family's second arm found the over-rejection C1 exists to prevent, in the component it was porting FROM.** `errorCode` and `severity` are both `pre-checked` in the [MVK ledger](MVK-LEDGER.md) — `Rule.Builder.build` refuses each — so what A12 itself does had never been asked. Measured ([KF291](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf291)): the `errorCode` refusal holds, and the `severity` one does not. An ABSENT `severity` is accepted by `checkConsistency`, and `MVK_SEVERITY_MISSING` fires for none of absent/null/empty, so the builder refuses a model the Kernel admits. **The checker therefore has no severity row at all** — translating the builder's refusal, which is what "translated from Java" invites, would have shipped that over-rejection into a second consumer. Two neighbouring shapes are likewise not verdicts and are not reported: a NUMERIC `errorCode` is coerced by A12 to the code `"7"`, and an explicit JSON `null` on any rule member refuses A12's reader outright.

**One prediction of this author's was refuted outright and is worth keeping visible**: "with no declared locale, one message is enough" — the Kernel answers `MVK_SUPP_LANGUAGES_MISSING,MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING`, because collection is keyed by declared locale and an empty set collects nothing. It had been asserted in a green checker test, which is what a self-confirming reading looks like.

**C1's compile-time extraction is BLOCKED by publication order, and that is a measured constraint rather than a preference.** C1 asks for one shared finding-producing function both consumers call, which means a dependency edge from `:rulekit` to `:consistency`: the shared function must live in `commonMain` to reach the JS target, and nothing else is reachable from both. `:rulekit` is published as `dmkits-rulekit` and `:consistency` is not published at all, so that edge would put an unresolvable coordinate in a shipped POM. Publishing `:consistency` to fix it is F's decision, taken with F's API and packaging review — [§2](#2-execution-order-and-landing-boundaries) puts packed delivery there on purpose, and F3 is where the dmkits edge is already required. So the family lands in the checker now with the Kernel as the shared oracle for both consumers, and C1's extraction rides F3. **Re-open trigger:** the first `:consistency` publication. **Resolved 2026-09-13 — and the blocker's own framing is what delayed it:** it assumed the shared function had to live in one of the two consumers, so the edge had to run between them. A third module both depend on costs one `include` and needs neither consumer published. The leaf is `:predicates`, the shared function is `isReservedElementName`, and `:consistency` remains unpublished; see the [5a record](#5a-record--the-walking-skeleton-and-the-failure-only-it-could-see-2026-09-13).

### C record — second frozen family: a computation's ALTERNATIVES (2026-09-12)

**Frozen family: the `computationAlternatives` list of a stored computation.** It reads structure only, like the slots family, and it is the list D's "every precondition/alternative" scope has to reach — so getting its shape wrong would put D on a fact base that misreports which alternatives exist.

| stored shape | A12 | the checker reports |
|---|---|---|
| the member absent, or an EMPTY list | rejects | `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT` **and** `MVK_CONDITION_MISSING`, `A12_CONSTRAINT` / ERROR |
| an alternative with no `operation`, or a blank/whitespace/`null` one | rejects | `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT` alone, same |
| ONE alternative, unguarded | **admits** | nothing |
| TWO or more, any of them unguarded — first, last or both | rejects, with ONE notification for the computation | one `MVK_ERROR_EMPTY_PRECONDITION` naming which alternatives lack a guard, same |
| a `commonPrecondition` declared beside them | changes neither row above | unchanged — it is a different slot |
| a precondition present but BLANK or whitespace | rejects, as a PARSE error | nothing — left to the syntax family, and the report says so |
| the member as an object, a scalar or an explicit `null`, or an alternative that is not an object | **never reads the source** | `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, same |

**The precondition law is the one a reader gets wrong, and locating it took three rows.** A single unguarded alternative is admitted, so "an alternative may be unguarded" looks settled; add a second and the pair is refused. Two accounts fit that one observation — *the last is the fallback and owes nothing* versus *each of several owes a guard* — and they disagree on every real model, so a checker built on the wrong one either rejects legal models or passes illegal ones. Unguarding the first, the last and both all draw the same code, which settles it.

**One CONSTANT ran through every row that established it, and the rows breaking it were added deliberately.** None declared a `commonPrecondition`, so "one alternative may be unguarded" and "an unguarded alternative is fine while nothing else guards the computation" were the same observation. Declaring one separates them and changes nothing: the common guard is a different slot, not a substitute.

**Three inferences from the sibling family were each refuted or confirmed by measurement rather than carried.** `errorMessage` ADMITS an object member — its deserializer iterates the node's elements — and `computationAlternatives` as an object is UNREADABLE: two list-shaped members, two readers. A blank string counts as ABSENT for `errorCode` and a message `text` and does NOT for a precondition, which reaches A12's condition parser whatever it spells and fails there as `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`. And an explicit `null` refuses the reader on the LIST while being an ordinary verdict on the `operation` and `precondition` inside it — the null law splits inside this one member. All three are in [KF292](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf292); the one that was predicted rather than measured is the one that was wrong.

**Consequence for the report:** `COMPUTATION_ALTERNATIVES` joins `checksCompleted`, and the report gains a standing gap saying that a precondition is checked for PRESENCE only. Advertising a precondition check while silently ignoring whether it parses is exactly the "parsed but never consumed" shape, so the boundary is stated where a consumer reads it rather than only here.

### C record — third frozen family: a rule's CONDITION and TARGET members (2026-09-12)

**Frozen family: `errorCondition` and `errorEntityRelPath` on a stored rule, checked for PRESENCE.** It is the rule twin of the alternatives family and closes the "an executable with no work in it" class on both carriers.

| stored shape | A12 | the checker reports |
|---|---|---|
| `errorCondition` blank or whitespace | rejects | `MVK_CONDITION_MISSING` **and** `MVK_INCOMPLETE_INPUT`, `A12_CONSTRAINT` / ERROR |
| `errorEntityRelPath` blank or whitespace | rejects | `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_MISSING` **and** `MVK_MISSING_ERROR_FIELD`, same |
| either member ABSENT or an explicit `null` | **never produces a verdict** | `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, same |

**The family exists because the natural report is backwards.** Three sibling slots read a blank string as absent, so "missing" reads as one shape with one code; here the blank member is A12's verdict and the OMITTED one is A12 saying it never got a model to judge — the condition's absence fails the model build, the target's absence refuses the reader one tier earlier still ([KF293](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf293)). Both predictions going in were the ordinary ones and both were wrong, which is what a fourth slot family measured rather than inferred is for.

**Each blank row draws a PAIR of codes**, and one pair is a naming trap worth stating once: `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_MISSING` and `MVK_MISSING_ERROR_FIELD` are two codes from two enums that fire together. The checker reports both, because naming either alone under-reports what A12 said.

**No overlap with the reference family, checked rather than assumed.** A blank `errorEntityRelPath` resolves to the declaring rule's own path and is therefore `Resolved` — `declaredReferences()` reports nothing for it — so this family adds the only finding that shape gets, and the two families cannot double-report.

### C record — fourth frozen family: element-name uniqueness (2026-09-12)

**Frozen family: no group holds two children of one name.** It reads nothing but the fact base the earlier phases already build — a duplicate path IS the collision — and it is the first family whose subject is the model's shape rather than a declaration's members.

| stored shape | A12 | the checker reports |
|---|---|---|
| two children of one name under one group, **any kinds** | cannot build the model | `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, once at the shared path |
| the same name in two different groups | **admits** | nothing |
| two names differing only in letter case | **admits** | nothing |

**Two boundaries were measured because the one known row under-determined them** ([KF294](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf294)). The demand spans KINDS — a rule may not take a sibling field's or a sibling subgroup's name — which a checker indexing only executables cannot see at all. And it is case-SENSITIVE, where the `vk_vk` prefix law on the very same member is case-INSENSITIVE; two laws on one slot with opposite case rules is the pair that gets unified by accident, so the differing-case row is an admission the checker must keep silent about.

**The outcome is a TIER, not a verdict.** A12 throws while building the model from inside `checkConsistency` and reports no `MVK_*` code, so the report's existing `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` gap already says what every other finding beside it is worth.

### C record — fifth frozen family: a group's ORDERING references, closing a standing report gap (2026-09-12)

**Frozen family: `Group.indexFieldName` and `Group.sortFields[].name`.** Every report since B0 named both as relative field references the checker does not resolve; that gap is now deleted rather than re-worded.

| stored shape | A12 | the checker reports |
|---|---|---|
| absent, or a bare name of one of the group's own fields | **admits** | nothing |
| a spelling that resolves to a field whose short name this group has no field for | rejects | `MVK_INVALID_INDEX_FIELD` / `MVK_INVALID_SORT_FIELD`, `A12_CONSTRAINT` / ERROR |
| a spelling that lands outside the group on a name the group DOES carry | **admits** | nothing |
| a direct-child field declared `noValueValidation` | rejects | nothing — the field's own TYPE gate is a family this slice does not own, and every report names it as unexamined |
| blank, whitespace, a bare name that is not one of its fields, a child group's name, a path naming nothing, an absolute path, a `sortFields` entry with no `name` | **never produces a verdict** | `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`, same |

**They could not ride the existing reference machinery, and that is the reason the gap stayed open rather than an oversight.** The resolution has no short-name fallback — a bare name is looked up among the group's own fields alone — and each member carries its own code, so reusing `ReferenceRole`'s codes would have reported a rule's law on a group's member.

**TWO gates, and the second reads a NAME** ([KF295](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf295)): the spelling must land on a field at all, and that field's SHORT NAME must then name a field of this group. **The first landing of this family got the second gate wrong and shipped an over-rejection**, caught by the terminal review and settled against the Kernel: it judged where the path LANDED, so a model with the short name `Sku` at two levels — `../Sku` written in `/Order/Items`, which A12 admits because that group has a `Sku` of its own — was rejected. No fixture in the family could see it, because every one had disjoint short names per level, which made "landed outside" and "no field of that name here" the same predicate on every row. The discriminating row is now locked on both members.

**Two further boundaries were measured because the mechanism alone does not settle them.** The check runs on EVERY group, not only a repeatable one. And an ABSENT member is legal while a BLANK one is fatal — the blank-counts-as-absent law three sibling slots follow, inverted as far as it can go.

**One row is a third member of [KF259](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf259)'s class:** an absolute spelling makes the Kernel raise past every typed channel. The checker reports the model as unusable, which is what a consumer acts on; that A12 delivers it untyped is A12's reporting defect, recorded rather than reproduced.

### C record — sixth frozen family: the ordering members' FIELD TYPE, and one gate that is dead (2026-09-12)

**Frozen family: whether the field a group's `indexFieldName` / `sortFields[].name` resolves to is one A12 accepts as a key.** The fifth family closed resolution; every report since then carried a gap saying the landed field's own type was gated and unexamined. That gap is now deleted.

| stored shape | A12 | the checker reports |
|---|---|---|
| a plainly typed field of the group | **admits** | nothing |
| a string declared `noValueValidation`, as the index | rejects | `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION`, `A12_CONSTRAINT` / ERROR, **at the field** |
| the same, as a sort field | rejects | `MVK_SORT_FIELD_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION`, same |
| the same field named by both members | rejects | both codes, one per member |
| the flag `false`, absent, or carried by a type that is not a string | **admits** | nothing |
| a reference that never resolves, whatever the field's type | **never produces a verdict** | `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE` only — the gates are exclusive |

**It is its own `CheckFamily` but not its own file, and both halves of that are deliberate.** `NoMetaModelChecks.checkIndexField` returns at the first gate that fires, so a type verdict is only ever reached by a reference that already resolved; splitting the implementation would duplicate the resolution and let a resolution fix read as a type approval. The report still separates them, because "it resolves" and "A12 will accept it" are different claims a consumer acts on differently.

**The finding attaches to the FIELD, not to the group** — A12 reports it against `indexField`, and the corrective is to retype that field or key the group by another. Every other finding in this family is attached to the group, which is why the checker test locks the path rather than assuming it.

**The family is SMALLER than its source suggested, and that is the finding.** `checkIndexField` has a third gate, `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_NO_TABLE_ENUM`, that no stored A12 model can reach; `checkSortField` has no such gate at all. The asymmetry is real in source and turns out not to matter. Four links were attacked separately ([KF296](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf296)): the reader REFUSES a table reference outright (measured), the constructor takes no table argument, the accessor returns `Optional.empty()` unconditionally, and the interface has exactly one implementor among its 23 importers. So the code is excluded on a cited dead path — the A1 treatment — rather than left as an unmeasured silence.

**The trap this family walked into, and the reason the denominator link was worth attacking: two interfaces named `IEnumerationType` live in different layers.** A search for the name returns both, and the wrong one — `EnumerationTypeWrapper`, which binds the stored model and is in the a12internal package — is the more plausible-looking answer. Only `md.model.internal.service.mapping.type.EnumerationTypeImpl` implements the interface `FieldUtils` actually reads. Picking the wrapper would have produced a confident claim that the gate is live and a checker branch that can never fire.

### Terminal review of the five C families, and what it changed (2026-09-12)

One cold adversarial review over the frozen range, as [§2](#2-execution-order-and-landing-boundaries) order 5 requires. It found four things the fixtures could not see, and every one was settled against the Kernel rather than against the reviewer's reading.

| finding | settled by | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| the ordering family judged the LANDING, not the short name | a new row: the same short name at two levels | **over-rejection, confirmed and fixed** |
| a non-object kind envelope reached the member families as "the member is absent" | a new row: a `Computation` envelope that is not an object | **tier collapse, confirmed and fixed at the root** |
| `MVK_CONDITION_MISSING` was explained by "no alternative means no precondition slot" | two new rows declaring a `commonPrecondition` | **mechanism refuted**; the codes were right, the explanation was not, and it is now stated as an observation |
| the checker emitted one precondition finding per unguarded alternative | the notification stream, which the code table de-duplicates | **confirmed**; A12 raises one, so the checker now reports one |
| a direct-child field is "the only thing that is legal" | two new rows on a `noValueValidation` field | **over-claim, confirmed**; the type gate is a family this slice does not own, and every report now names it |

**The root fix for the envelope class is `DeclaredElement.body`, and it is a THREE-state answer where the code had two.** An omitted envelope is legal — A12 default-constructs the element, so every member genuinely reads as absent — while a present non-object one is a binding failure the source never survives. Casting `declaration[kind] as? JsonObject` collapses those onto one `null`, which four families then read as "the member is missing".
