**The denominator was wrong in the ledger and the source settled it in one read.** The row described the kit's three pre-checked constructs as "the representable forbidden constructs; rest kernel-delegated", implying A12 forbids more. Its listener carries exactly three overrides. A checker built on the ledger's wording would have gone looking for constructs that do not exist.

**The new gate exposed a parser defect one layer down, and the first attempt at this record had shipped it as a boundary.** The construct gate reported nothing for `CustomCondition`, and the reason looked like scope: the syntax family answered `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` first. It was a defect. `ConditionShape` spelled the keyword-plus-bare-name predicate in `conditionPart`, where a condition is expected POSITIONALLY, but a clause operand reaches the parser through `expressionOrCondition`, which fell through to the expression reader — so `Having (… And CustomCondition Foo)` was a token error on a text A12 parses. The fix threads the permission explicitly and **stops at the clause**: in a plain aggregate argument the same text IS an error, and widening it there would have traded a wrong class for silence. One helper now carries the form for both readers.

**The tell was that the silence had the wrong SHAPE for a boundary.** A family's own boundary is silence; this was a different family reporting a class of its own. Both estates refuse every model concerned, so no guard could have caught it and nothing was red — it surfaced only because the three rows were written out and compared against A12's three, rather than one representative row being taken as the family's answer. **With the parser fixed the three rows land on A12's own three classes** — the construct class, `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` — the last of which was predicted as silence and measured as agreement.

**The conjunct was measured rather than transferred.** Both existing kernel rows carried the construct with no separate in-scope conjunct, and the rule the family needs is about a body that has one. Since the conjunct changes what the gate BEFORE this one sees, the transfer was not free; one row in `FilteredOperandGateOrderLawsTest` settled it for 29 s.

### C record — two gate-order measurements cashed in the same lane (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: one more gate in each of two families already landed.** `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX` for a message parameter whose semantic-index key is itself keyed ([KF412](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf412)), and `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_AMBIGUOUS` for an `$index(…)$` argument matching the index field of more than one group on the error entity's path ([KF414](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf414)). No new family, so the count stays at 92; two more classes of A12's unprefixed error-text vocabulary are now reported.

**Both were licensed by an ordering measured for a DIFFERENT purpose, which is what made them cheap.** The two slices before this one each measured a gate order to justify what they would NOT report; the same two orderings then said exactly which further class each family could report for free. `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_AMBIGUOUS` is the second gate and the argument gate is the only one before it — already reproduced — so it needs no machinery the family did not already have. `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX` is thrown by `checkSemanticIndex` as the KEY sub-rule completes, before `checkFieldRef` consults the condition at all, so it needs *less*: no membership work whatsoever. **The transferable move is to ask, of every ordering measured to bound a family, which gate it also unlocks** — a measurement taken to justify silence is the same measurement that licenses the next class, and reading it only in the direction it was taken leaves that free.

**The membership row is the discriminator, and this is the one place a nested key could have been reported wrongly.** A chained key on a group the condition never reads draws `ILLEGAL_SEMANTIC_INDEX`, not `MVK_INDEX_FOR_ERROR_TEXT_INVALID` — a membership-first reading predicts the wrong class there, and the checker would have named it on every unread field. Predicting both rows and measuring them together is what settled it.

**The condition-side reduction deliberately kept refusing a nested use.** Only the MESSAGE side was measured, and the same helper reduces both surfaces — so widening it for the message would have silently changed what counts as a pairing the condition wrote, on a carrier with no measurement behind it. The nested class is answered before that helper is reached instead, which is the scope-transfer rule applied to a shared reader rather than to a fixture.

### C record — a family built from a grid that already existed (2026-09-15)

*(Superseded in part 2026-09-15 by the record above: this family now reproduces the first TWO gates, not one. Its argument for the three it still leaves alone is unchanged.)*

**Frozen change: the `$index(…)$` terminal's ARGUMENT gate.** [KF414](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf414); one class of A12's unprefixed error-text vocabulary newly reported — `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD`. New family `ERROR_TEXT_INDEX_TERMINAL`; the count goes 91 → 92.

**No new kernel measurement was needed for the family, only ONE row for its soundness.** `RuleMessageIndexTerminalLawsTest` had measured this form richly and nothing reported it, which is the cheapest class of slice in the estate: measured-but-unreported. The one row that had to be added is the one that makes reporting a single gate defensible — an unknown argument with NO suffix, which draws the argument class rather than the missing-suffix class and so proves the ordering the family relies on.

**Five gates, one reproduced, and the ordering is the whole argument.** Being FIRST is what makes a gate safe to report alone: nothing pre-empts it, so no reproduction of the other four is needed to know it was reached. The four after it each need the repeatability machinery computed from the condition's references, or a shape stored DM-JSON cannot spell — so their silence is a disclosed under-report with a stated reason rather than a gap. Read the other way, this is the rule the slice before it had to learn the hard way: a class drawn after an unreproduced gate cannot be claimed to have been reached.

**A row that PASSES the gate and fails a later one is the mirror's risk, not its noise.** `$index(ClaimNo)$` — the right argument, no suffix — is A12's `INVALID_INDEX_FIELD_PARAMETER` and this family's silence, and the mirror asserts that silence explicitly with the reason. Left unasserted it would read as an untested shape rather than a decided one.

### C record — the same tier, batched by FORM rather than by code (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the error-text parameter's category suffix.** [KF413](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf413); three classes of A12's unprefixed error-text vocabulary newly reported — `NO_CATEGORY`, `FIELD_NO_ENUM`, `INVALID_CATEGORY`. New family `ERROR_TEXT_CATEGORY_SUFFIX`; the count goes 90 → 91.

**Eight of eight predicted on the first run**, against four runs for the slice before it. The difference is not skill but the depth of the read: these three classes are thrown by two short methods with no delegation, so the source says what runs. The previous slice's law lived partly in `refEntities`, whose matching rule no reading settles — which is the standing signal for when to predict and when to dump an empty map.

**Batching the TIER by parameter FORM is what made both slices cheap.** A12's message grammar is its third DSL and each form — plain, semantic index, category suffix, row specifier, base-year terminal — carries its own gate and its own classes. Taking a form at a time gives each family a boundary it can state, and the membership family's own KDoc had already enumerated the remaining forms, so the backlog was written down before the work started.

**Both families AGREED WITH A12 on a carrier neither had measured, and that is why both were narrowed.** The category family answered `FIELD_NO_ENUM` for `$index(Note)->Band$` — which is what A12 draws there, so nothing was red and nothing would ever have been. But `checkIndexFieldRef` owns the `index(…)` terminal and has eleven classes for it, several about an ENUM index field, so the agreement held on the one carrier tried and the families disagree exactly where this one is confident. Found by reading the NEXT slice's existing grid and noticing a row whose shape the just-committed family would also match. Each family now claims only its own parameter form, and the row is locked as deliberate silence rather than left to luck. The transferable rule: when a tier is split by FORM, every family needs an explicit refusal of its neighbours' forms — the shapes overlap textually, so silence has to be written rather than assumed.

**The membership ordering is the row both new families leave alone, and they say so.** `checkFieldRef` resolves against the condition first, so an unread field draws `INVALID_FIELD` whatever its suffix or key says. Neither family names a class there; the plain-form family that owns `INVALID_FIELD` reads only the plain form, so that row is an under-report disclosed in both docs rather than a class anyone reports.

### C record — the first family reading A12's THIRD DSL against its first (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: the error-text parameter's semantic index.** [KF412](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf412); one code drained — `MVK_INDEX_FOR_ERROR_TEXT_INVALID`. New family `ERROR_TEXT_SEMANTIC_INDEX`; the count goes 89 → 90. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 126 reported**.

**A12 resolves a message parameter against the rule's CONDITION, not against the model**, which is what makes this family a comparison of two DSLs rather than a check of one. The message grammar is A12's third, and its indexed form reduces to the same `(field, key)` pair the condition writes — so the checker reuses `semanticIndexUsesIn` on both texts and compares. Reusing one reader for both sides is also what made the first draft wrong in a way worth recording: the value half was resolved with a bare-name reader, which silently judged nothing for every multi-segment path and left four of nine rows unreported.

**The discriminating pair is the finding.** A bound key does NOT make its repetition readable: `$Shipments/TrackingNo For "T-1"$` is admitted on a condition that never names `TrackingNo`, while `$Shipments/Carrier For "T-1"$` — a plain sibling at the same key — is refused. Either row alone yields a wrong law in a different direction, and only the pair gives the real one: the condition's own pairings, plus the field that names the key. The rival mechanism was named and its discriminating case run before the clause was written, rather than after a reviewer asked.

**A grammar file is one layer, and this one says the opposite of what runs.** `BaseErrorTextParameterLexer.g4` carries `FOR: 'Fuer'`, so the grid was first written with every row spelled `Fuer` and measured nine parse errors; an `en_US` model takes `For`. One run corrected it, which is the argument for measuring the spelling in the same grid rather than trusting the read — the cost was one invocation and the alternative was a family that reported nothing on every English model.

### C record — one question, three scans, wrong in both directions at once (2026-09-15)

**Frozen change: what "the checker reports this code" MEANS, extracted to one reader.** No A12 code drained and no family changed — the count stays 89 at **125 of 142 reported** — but **17 ledger rows** were disclosed and the guard that should have demanded them was fixed at its root. New support class `CheckerReportedCodes` with its own controls; `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest` and `CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest` now read it, and `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` keeps its deliberately coarse token scan with the pattern corrected.

**The root cause is a character class, and all three guards had REASONED about it.** Each spelled a code as `MVK_[A-Z_]+[0-9]*`, and two carried a KDoc explaining that the trailing digits belong to the code because `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1` and `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` are two different laws — correct, cited, and beside the point. Digits also appear in the MIDDLE, and twelve ledger codes spell them there: the whole `_A12_DV` family, `MVK_ERROR_FIELDGROUP_ITERATION4_INVALID`, `MVK_POINT_IN_A12_MODEL`. A documented rationale for the case you thought of reads, to the next session, exactly like a rationale for the case you did not.

**The two scans failed in OPPOSITE directions and both stayed green.** The narrow one followed a helper literally named `finding(…)`, so it saw 99 of the 193 codes this checker states — 27 of the 34 `Finding`-returning helpers carry other names, and `nameFinding(` has no word boundary before its `F`. The coarse one took every string literal, so the four codes held only as members of `Check.kt`'s phase and suppression lists read as reported. Neither failed: the narrow one by never looking, the coarse one by luck, because every set member but one happened to be named in some laws test. Correcting the pattern alone would have turned the coarse guard RED on honest content — `MVK_ITERATION_RESTRICTION_IN_A12_MODEL` is `erased` for a shape rulekit cannot render and could not be given a measurement.

**The right boundary is not "reaches a `Finding`" — it is "stated, versus compared against".** Narrowing to a `Finding` construction was the first fix attempted and it misses 86 genuine reports, because codes travel through intermediate defect records (`ListDefect("MVK_…", …)`, a `String?` returned two frames up) that no amount of following helper NAMES reaches. The one shape that is not a report is a code parked in a declared `setOf`. That rule was measured both ways: it drops all four false positives and keeps every genuine report.

**The fix carried the defect it was fixing, twice, and the same question caught both.** Written first as `setOf`/`listOf`/`mapOf` — "a declared collection is membership" — it hid four codes, because three families key their explain fragments by code (`SemanticIndexStructure.ADVICE` and two siblings) and look the key up while building the finding, which makes every key a report. And its type annotation was `[^=]*`, which crosses newlines: `enum class ConstantArm(val code: String, val admitted: Set<String>)` scanned on to the next file-level `= setOf(` and cut out the enum between them, taking three more reported codes. Both are unanchored spans, the defect class of the day; neither would have failed a guard, since all seven rows already said `checker-reported`. What found them was asking the reader the question it exists to answer — *which codes are stated only inside a collection?* — rather than trusting it once it went green.

**The tell was a disagreement inside the ledger, found while ranking the remaining work.** Five rows said "Reported by the portable checker (`CheckFamily.ELEMENT_NAME_ALPHABET`)" in their prose while the cell beside them did not, which is the exact drift the disposition guard exists to prevent — so the guard's silence was evidence about the guard. Two of the 17 were worse than cosmetic: `MVK_ERROR_FIELDGROUP_ITERATION4_INVALID` and `ITERATION5` still read `kernel-delegated` for classes `ErrorTargetIteration.kt` had been reporting for two days, and this session had edited one of those rows without the cell going right, because nothing could see it.

**Which direction of error to leave in place, stated rather than assumed.** The reader can still over-count: a literal stated outside a set but never emitted reads as reported. That was chosen — over-counting only ever demands a truer ledger row or an earlier measurement, while the under-count it replaces hid nine stale rows and a guard's whole purpose.

### C record — the first family reproducing a gate from A12's CALCULATION module (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `NormalisierteBerechnungsFormHelper.checkBerechnungsFunktion`'s enum arm.** [KF411](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf411); one code drained — `MVK_ERROR_INVALID_ENUM_CALCULATION`. New family `ENUM_COMPUTATION_SOURCE`; the count goes 88 → 89. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 125 reported**.

**Every family so far reproduced the PARSER; this one reproduces the module that runs after it, and that is what shapes its scope.** A computation is lowered to a comparison against its target, so the comparison walk answers first wherever it has a class — three of the ten rows are pre-empted, and a reader building the gate from its kernel source alone would report all three. The TEXT source is what keeps the gate from being redundant: a plain text against a TEXTLESS enumeration is ADMITTED by that walk, so only the calculation gate catches it.

**Two rows had to be a PAIR or the law is unmeasured.** Containment runs one way, and a mirrored source passes both an equality test and a subset test — so "declaring FEWER" and "declaring MORE" together are the measurement, and either alone reads as a clean row that proves nothing. The category rows are the same shape: one covered, one not.

**Three mispredictions, all of them earlier gates rather than wrong laws.** The pre-emptions were invisible from the calc module's source, which is the standing lesson about reading one layer: a locally correct read of `isEnumValueCompatible` gives exactly the wrong answer for three of its own inputs. The fourth miss was a spelling — `Concat(...)` is not the operator, `+` is — and it hid a row that turned out to confirm the allow-list arm.

### C record — the slice that went looking for a code and found an over-report (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `IterationChecker`'s phase, not its classes.** [KF410](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf410); no code drained and one OVER-REPORT removed — the checker named `MVK_ERROR_FIELDGROUP_ITERATION5_INVALID` beside the parse-time `MVK_MULTIPLE_INDEX_FIELDS` on any model whose repeatable ROOT carries an index field. No family changed; the three `IterationChecker` classes joined `POST_PARSE_CONDITION_CODES`. The count stays 88 at **124 of 142 reported**.

**The phase list had been read off ONE syntactic form, and that is the transferable defect.** Its own comment says the members are "the `throwError` calls in `ParserService.checkNormalRule`'s own body, read off kernel `30.8.1` in source order" — a careful, citable enumeration that misses everything the method DELEGATES. That body makes two such calls and they carry five A12-model classes between them; only the one the checker already reported made the list, so nothing went red. A denominator assembled by grepping a keyword is bounded by the keyword, not by the question.

**The new-code pre-flight was too narrow, and two runs paid for it.** It read "a NEW code the checker REPORTS costs up to three guard rows"; the trigger is actually a code the checker's main source SPELLS, because the corrective projection scans text. Adding an inert member to a phase list duly projected an entry no report can deliver, and `CorrectiveTest` went red twice. The residue is now a named, asserted set rather than an empty one — a code with real owner guidance and no emitting family is a legitimate state, and naming it keeps the guard catching the dead entry it exists for.

**The sweep is what made it a class fix rather than a patch**, and it found two more members after the defect was already green: `MVK_ITERATION_RESTRICTION_IN_A12_MODEL` from the same delegate, and `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` from the second call. Both are inert today, which is precisely why they belong in the list — the next family to report one would repeat this defect with nothing going red, and the list exists for that and not for the members already live.

**The target code was not drained, and saying so is the result.** `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_AND_ITERATION_INVALID` guards level 0 — the level its two siblings' loop explicitly skips — and seven spellings failed to reach it, each for a different reason. That is an enumeration of attempts, not a proof of unreachability, so the ledger row records the attempts and stays delegated. Claiming the class unauthorable from seven failures is exactly the reachability claim the standing rule forbids.

**An exploratory grid asserting an EMPTY map is the cheapest instrument for an unpredictable chain.** The level model here could not be predicted from source — `getMax`, the index sizing, and the wildcard pre-emptions all interact — so the accelerator's "predict the whole grid" does not apply. Asserting `Map.of()` turns one run into a complete measurement of fourteen cells, and the round-two probes cost one more. Two runs bought a finding, a fix and a bounded negative result; predicting would have bought a wrong prediction.

### C record — the slice written whole before it was measured (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckAnzahlWertOpImpl`'s string arm past its type walk.** [KF409](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf409); one code drained — `MVK_NO_DATE_CONSTANT`, whose single caller in the estate this is — plus a further carrier for `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. An EXTENSION of `VALUE_IN_FIELDS_CONSTANT_KIND` rather than a family: same kernel method, same switch, two more statements of the same arm. The count stays 88; the measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 124 reported**.

**A ledger row named a construct the code does not have.** It read "date extraction takes fields/expressions, not string literals" — plausible, wrong, and it would have sent a reader to the extractors. One grep for the callers settled it. The general form is worth carrying: a `kernel-delegated` row's PROSE is an unmeasured claim, and the cheapest moment to check it is when the code is drained.

**The accelerator went one step further and the step paid for itself.** The last four slices measured the grid first and then wrote the checker. Here the laws grid, the family change, the reader promotion and the mirror were all written from one source read, and ONE invocation measured all four — twenty-six kernel cells and twenty-six mirror cells, every one right. A wrong prediction would have cost two text edits and no extra build, which is strictly cheaper than the two builds the split order spends every time.

**The near-misses are the measurement; the positives are almost free.** Seven spellings that look like dates are counted as text — wrong length, wrong shape, or a real pattern behind a non-lenient parse. A reader matching the shape alone would refuse all seven, and no positive row would have caught it.

**Separating "no offender" from "cannot judge" was the whole implementation risk.** The type walk returned one `null` for both while the type class was all the family reported; adding two gates behind it makes the conflation a live defect, because a list A12 has already refused would draw a later gate's class. The walk now answers with three cases and the tail gates run only on the third.

### C record — the slice whose kind gate answers for eleven declarations (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckFeldOpMitDatumSpecialImpl.check`, `ValueAsDate`'s four-gate operand chain.** [KF408](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf408); one code drained — `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE_FOR_OP` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED`. New family `VALUE_AS_DATE_OPERAND`; the count goes 87 → 88. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 123 reported**.

**One class for eleven declarations is why the whole type domain had to be measured rather than sampled.** The gate is `isDatePartialKnown`, two questions behind one code, and the second question is the one the operator's name hides: A12's ADO layer gives `TimeType`, `DateTimeType` and `DateFragmentType` the same `IDateType` interface a plain date carries. A checker keyed on the interface would have admitted three kinds A12 refuses, and no row short of the full domain would have caught it — every date row it looked at would have agreed.

**A whole-grid prediction cost one run and was wrong in exactly one cell.** Twenty-seven cells from one source read; the miss was the ROOT group, predicted `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED` and measured the plain group class. The lesson generalises past this operator: the root class belongs to the methods that ACCEPT a group and exclude the root one, so a method with no group arm never reaches it — a class is not available to a chain merely because the chain's operand could draw it elsewhere.

**The pre-emption row is what licenses the family's silence, and it needed the anchored twin to say anything.** An unstarred read into a repetition draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` from the reference reader and hides all four classes — but the SAME read from the row's own rule reaches them, so the silence is keyed on the declaring group rather than on the operand's spelling. A grid carrying only the outer row would have justified a blanket silence that drops two real findings.

### C record — the slice that had to fix the authoring kit before it could measure (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`'s DISPLAY-value half.** [KF407](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf407); two codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_STRING` and `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_ENUM`. New family `ENUM_DISPLAY_COMPARABILITY`; the count goes 86 → 87. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 122 reported**.

**A fixture the typed builder refused to author was the finding, not an obstacle.** The grid needed an enumeration labelled only for an undeclared locale; `DocumentModelDraft` threw. The refusal turned out to be over-strict in two ways and was fixed first, as its own commit ([AF149](API-FINDINGS.md#af149), [KF406](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf406)) — which is the dogfood rule behaving exactly as written: the friction was the signal, and working around it with hand-written JSON would have hidden a live defect in a shipped kit.

**Its own family rather than an extension, on the one criterion that separates them.** `STRING_ENUM_COMPARISON_AGREEMENT` owns the same kernel walk's kind and domain halves, so the pre-emption rule points at extending it. The display question is about the PAIR — the same enumeration is fine opposite one partner and refused opposite another — and that family's reader is a per-SIDE walk whose type cannot carry a pair. Carrier-disjointness held: the halves partition the comparison, measured, so the two never contend.

**Two rows that look like implementation detail are the ones that bound the whole family.** A label for an undeclared locale leaves an enumeration TEXTLESS, and the value-label law forces the declared locales to be answered together — so A12's "shared display locales" intersection can never be narrower than the declared set, and a reader walking the declared locales is exact rather than approximate. Without those two measurements the family would have carried an intersection it could not justify.

### C record — the slice measured in one grid rather than five (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckFeldOpMitParamsImpl.check`, the two substring operators' one checker.** [KF405](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf405); two codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_TYPE` and `MVK_INVALID_INTERVAL` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED` and `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. New family `RANGE_OPERAND_GATES`; the count goes 85 → 86. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 120 reported**.

**The whole grid was written from one source read and measured once, and every cell was right.** The slice before it grew its grid across five runs because each measurement suggested the next question — which is the accelerator half-applied. The method here has six gates, so the questions are enumerable before any of them is asked: every gate against every other, and every declared type against the kind gate. Writing them all costs one file and one run; discovering them costs a build each.

**A code the kits PRE-CHECK is still a code the checker must report.** `MVK_INVALID_INTERVAL` was dispositioned `pre-checked`, which is a claim about the typed authoring surface and says nothing about a stored model — and the checker reads stored DM-JSON, where no builder ran. The disposition is now `pre-checked / checker-reported`, and the general form is worth carrying: a `pre-checked` row is a CANDIDATE for this checker, not an exclusion from it.

**Two readers were promoted rather than copied.** The `isLikeString` complement and the operand-with-category reduction both already existed, private, in the family that needed them first; the second consumer is what makes them shared readers. The alternative — a second copy of the type allow-list — is the failure mode this plan has already paid for once, where a set spelled `CustomType` silently matched nothing.

### C record — the slice that corrected the chain it was extending (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckSemanticIndexImpl.checkIndexField` and its containment test.** [KF404](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf404); two codes drained — `MVK_INDEX_TYPE_INCONSISTENT` and `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_CONTAINED_IN_INDEX` — plus a fourth carrier for `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. No new family: the residue sits in the same kernel method on the same carrier, so `SEMANTIC_INDEX_STRUCTURE` was extended and the count stays at **85**. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 118 reported**.

**Measuring a residue re-opened the chain it was joining, and the wrong class was already shipped.** `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_BELOW_WILDCARD` had been placed second in the chain from a source reading — `getIndexGroup` runs before the key is examined — and it is in fact LAST, raised by a later pass over the finished index list. Nothing in the earlier grid could have caught it: every one of its rows paired that star with a class that does answer ahead of it. **A gate's position is only measured against the gates it was actually paired with**, so a chain locked by N rows is not a chain locked end to end, and the cheap check is to pair the newcomer with every class on both sides of it rather than only with its predicted neighbour.

**A per-family type comparison had to be written rather than reused.** The shared `BaseTypeClass` reader splits the temporal declarations, and A12's model layer has ONE date interface — so reusing it here would have refused a time key against a date index field that A12 admits. The family carries its own table with the temporal declarations merged, which under-reports inside that class and cannot over-reject; the shared reader stays as it is, because its own three consumers measured their carriers separately.

**Reading the rule's ANCHOR is what made the second code reachable at all.** The family had treated every key inside a repetition as opaque, which is right for an unanchored rule and wrong for a rule declared in that repetition — and a key inside the indexed group is anchored by definition, so the blanket reading made the class unreportable rather than merely deferred. The helper that decides it already existed in another family, private; it is now a shared reader, and the two rows that license the widening are the anchored key indexing a THIRD group, measured admitted and refused.

### C record — the slice whose subject was a MODEL declaration rather than a construct (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckKonstanteReferenzjahrImpl` and `CheckDatumKonstruktImpl.checkJahresParam`.** [KF403](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf403); two codes drained — `MVK_NO_BASE_YEAR` and `MVK_MISSING_YEAR`. New family `BASE_YEAR_REQUIREMENT`; the count goes 84 → 85. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 116 reported**.

**A family keyed on a MODEL-LEVEL declaration is a shape the plan had not produced before, and it earns its own constant for the reason the others do not.** Nothing about the `BaseYear` constant and the year-less `Date` construction is shared at the construct level — different grammar rules, different checker classes, no common carrier. What they share is a property of the MODEL, so the law belongs to the declaration and the family's first line is its precondition: a model that declares one reports nothing at all.

**The declaration is not one switch in one direction.** It ENABLES these two shapes and DISABLES the year-agreement walk of the range-overlap operators, landed two slices earlier. A family assuming "a base year relaxes year questions" would have got half of that backwards, and only holding the three together makes the asymmetry visible — which is the argument for landing related slices close in time rather than by convenience.

**Widening one family's arity gate closed a silence in another, and the stale row was in a MIRROR that had never been measured.** `DATE_CONSTRUCTION_SLOT` declined every two-parameter `Date` as an unjudged arity, so its mirror asserted the checker says nothing there — and nothing had ever asked A12. This slice's grid did: the day and month gates run whatever the arity, so the written slots are judged and only the missing year is the new family's. **A mirror row expecting silence is a claim about A12 that no laws grid covers unless someone put that shape to the kernel** — the same class as the empty-cell finding one slice earlier, arriving from the opposite direction.

### C record — the slice where the empty MIRROR cell was the finding (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckPredefinedTypeValidityConditionImpl` and `CheckDateValidityConditionImpl`.** [KF402](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf402); three codes drained — `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM_OR_EXT_ENUM`, `MVK_NO_EMPTY` and `MVK_ONLY_CONSTANTS_USED` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. New family `VALIDITY_CONDITION_OPERANDS`; the count goes 83 → 84. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 114 reported**.

**The mirror is not a transcription step — it is the only instrument that finds a gate with NO owner.** Twice in two slices, a gate assumed to belong to some pre-gate family turned out to belong to nobody, and both times what surfaced it was an empty cell in the mirror opposite a kernel class. The laws grid cannot find it (A12 answers, as expected) and neither can a source read (the gate is plainly in another method). **So write the mirror's expectation from what the checker SHOULD say, never from what you assume some other family already says** — and when the cell comes back empty, the question is whether anything reads that carrier at all.

**One keyword, two grammar rules, two checker classes: the operand SHAPE is the discriminator.** `Valid(Field, "Type")` and `Valid(Date(…))` share a keyword and share nothing else, so the family branches on arity and on whether the sole operand is a construction. The two arms are carrier-disjoint by construction, which is what licenses one family for two kernel classes.

**A code's NAME listed a kind that is not one.** `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM_OR_EXT_ENUM` reads as three kinds and `isStringEnumOrExtensibleEnum`'s third disjunct is a flag on the first, so a `CustomFieldType` is refused. The ledger row had carried the name's reading since it was written and is corrected; the catalog's constraint prose said the same thing and is sharpened in the same change, which is where an operator-level fact belongs.

**A neighbouring family's walk was REUSED rather than restated.** The date arm's question is asked after the construction's own slot gates, so it needs both "does a slot already fail" and the slot walk's own reading of "this slot names an entity" — an EXTRACT operator does not, which is the arm's most surprising row. Exposing `readDateConstruction` from the slot family keeps one live statement of what a slot demands; restating either half would have been two chances to disagree about one law.

### C record — the slice where one gate's escape hatch was the whole finding (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckEntityListenUtils`' two date-range walks.** [KF401](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf401); three codes drained — `MVK_NO_DATE_RANGE`, `MVK_INVALID_DATE_RANGE_FORMAT` and `MVK_DATE_WITH_AND_WITHOUT_YEAR` — plus a further carrier for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`. New family `DATE_RANGE_OPERAND_LIST`; the count goes 82 → 83. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 111 reported**.

**The selection method needed a second filter, and finding that out was worth the detour.** Ranking kernel files by unreported codes put `CheckFieldAndValueListConditionImpl` near the top with 4 of 4 open — and all four are table-backed-enum laws the ledger already records as UN-BUILDABLE, since a table enum is not DM-JSON-serializable. **A per-file open count must be intersected with reachability before it selects anything**, which is the companion to the previous slice's delegation caveat: one inflates the count where the kernel delegates, the other where A12 cannot be asked at all. Of 64 kernel codes this checker does not report, roughly 25 are genuinely open; the rest are unreachable, pre-checked by a kit, message labels, or declined by decision.

**A gate with an escape hatch is two claims, and only the pair of rows separates them.** `checkDateRangeConsistentRegardingYear` returns immediately on a model base year and `checkDateRangeWithoutSpecificInterpretationOfYear` does not, so "a base year settles year questions" is true of one walk and false of its neighbour. Both were measured with and without one rather than inferred from the first.

Simplifying it to "does this reference carry a year" would have been cleaner code and would have admitted a model A12 rejects — the checker reproduces the step and says why in the one place a later reader would try to tidy it.

**One gate was closed rather than disclosed because the mirror measurement asked the question.** The plain list's group gate was predicted to be some other family's and is nobody's: the arity family declines a group operand on its own account, its law being about how many FIELDS a list names. The mirror's empty cell is what surfaced it, and reporting it cost four lines — cheaper than the disclosure paragraph it replaced.

### C record — the slice keyed on a SLOT rather than on an operator (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckDatumAngabeImpl`, the date-argument slot every date operator hands its operands to.** [KF400](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf400); three codes drained — `MVK_NO_DATE`, `MVK_INVALID_DATE_STRING` and `MVK_INVALID_DATE_FOR_OP` — plus further carriers for `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`. New family `DATE_ARGUMENT_SLOT`; the count goes 81 → 82. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 108 reported**.

**A family keyed on the SLOT costs one map entry per operator; one keyed on the operator costs a table each.** Sixteen operators across four grammar families — difference, shift, numeric extraction, temporal extraction — share these gates byte for byte, because the kernel factored the slot out. `DATE_SLOTS_OF` therefore maps each operator to WHICH of its operand positions are date slots and nothing else. This is the same shape the previous slices reached by merging, arrived at from the source instead: **when the kernel has factored a check out, the checker's unit is that factor, not its callers.**

**The gate that is one layer up looks exactly like the gate that is here, and telling them apart is the whole slice.** `FieldUtils.isDate` tests `IDateType`, which covers time, stamp and fragment as well as date, so the slot ADMITS a `TimeType` and the carrier then refuses it as `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`. A reader that took "a date slot demands a date" from the class name would answer `MVK_NO_DATE` on a model A12 rejects with the other class — a correct rejection under the wrong name, which this project counts as a real defect. Four rows in the grid exist only to separate the two layers, and the same pair recurs on the constant form: the slot accepts `"01.02."` and the carrier refuses the MIXTURE as `MVK_DATE_WITH_AND_WITHOUT_YEAR`.

**Four of the grid's cells were predicted wrong and the measurement corrected all four**, three of them in that layering and one in the calendar: `"29.02."` is REFUSED, because the year-less pattern's missing year defaults to the epoch's 1970, which is not a leap year. The intuitive prediction — a year-less date being year-agnostic — went into a KDoc before it was measured and had to be corrected there too. **Predict the grid, but never write the prose before the run.**

**A LENGTH switch is a denominator question, not a pattern question.** `DateUtils.isStringAValidDate` picks its one pattern by the string's length, so the accepted shapes are four rather than the two the kernel's own javadoc names, and two of the grid's refusals follow from the switch rather than from any pattern: a stamp spelled with a space is 19 characters and fails the date-time pattern, and the year-less shape stripped of its trailing dot is a length no case handles.

**The three guard rows a NEW code costs are now pre-flight rather than discovery.** Recorded in §2 after they cost three lane reruns on the previous slice; all three were satisfied before the first lane here.

### C record — the slice that merged across two kernel METHODS, and the rule that let it (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: `CheckFeldListenAnzahlOpImpl`'s distinct-count arm.** [KF399](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf399); three residue codes drained — `MVK_STRING_ENUM_AND_NON_STRING_ENUM`, `MVK_NUMBER_AND_NON_NUMBER` and `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_CMP_DATE_ALLOWED` — plus a third carrier for `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`. Landed as a fifth carrier of `FIELD_LIST_OPERATION_OPERANDS`; the count stays at 81.

**The three previous slices merged within one kernel method; this one merged ACROSS two, which needs a different justification.** The pre-emption rule says two families reporting gates of ONE method over-report by construction — it says nothing about two methods. What licenses this merge is the weaker condition the rule is really about: the two methods never share a CARRIER, so neither family can name the other's class on any model. That plus an identical question (what kind must each expanded field be), identical pre-emptions (the shared pre-gates and the same `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN` arity gate) and an identical leader-decides-the-arm structure made a second family pure duplication. **The condition to check at a merge is therefore carrier-disjointness, not method identity.**

**The derivation that picked the slice overstated it, and the correction is worth keeping.** Subtracting reported codes from the codes each kernel file raises showed this file at 4 of 4 open — a whole unreported file. But the scan counts only literal `ParserErrorTypesEnum.X` references in a file's own text, and this file mostly DELEGATES: its arity, duplicate, root-group and temporal gates are attributed to `CheckEntityListenUtils`, where three families already own them, and one of its four codes is H-model-only. The real residue was three classes on one of three arms. **Per-file counts read high wherever the kernel delegates; re-derive with that in mind rather than citing the count.**

**A leader-decides-the-arm construct hides its hardest row in the branch that has no arm.** "Not string, not number, not date" reads like the numeric arm's territory and is a class of its own; three unclaimed leaders were measured separately rather than inferred, because the else branch is a complement and a complement is what a checker may not infer. The measured denominator now stands at **142 A12-reachable kernel codes, 105 reported**.

**One under-report has no owner and is disclosed rather than closed.** `checkNoCategories` is the first statement of this method and outruns even the duplicate walk — but the shared operand reducer declines a categorized operand, so no family sees such a call. Widening the reducer is a change to every family that reads an operand, which is its own slice; the silence is stated in the family, the finding, the ledger row and a locked mirror row so it cannot read as agreement.

Two families reporting gates of one kernel method independently is an over-report by construction, so the slice extended the incumbent rather than adding a neighbour, and the rename followed from the previous slice's own rule: name the family after the METHOD's subject.

**A gate's NAME is evidence about nothing, and this method has two that say the wrong thing about the same declaration.** `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED` names three kinds and admits a fourth, because `isLikeString` takes `IPredefinedType` beside `IStringType`; and `matchBaseType` groups those same two, so a CUSTOM `[field]` entry matches a STRING subject where an ENUMERATION entry does not. The grouping lives in this family with its own lock, and the shared map's KDoc keeps saying the grouping is unmeasured elsewhere — which is now true in a narrower way and stays honest.

**The refusal set was measured as seven rows rather than inferred as a complement.** A12 asks three POSITIVE predicates of the subject, so "what it refuses" is their complement over a thirteen-name vocabulary — and a complement is exactly the shape a checker may not infer, because an unmeasured member lands on the over-rejecting side. Three extra cells (Time, DateTime, DateFragment) cost one rerun and turned a transferred claim into a measured one.


### C record — the slice that found a WRONG CLASS in the family it was extending (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the rest of `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl`.** [KF397](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf397); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_DATE_RANGE_COMPARISON` and `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUMERATION_OR_CATEGORY` — plus a fourth carrier for `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED` and a second for `MVK_NO_CONST`. That kernel class is now fully reported. `VALUE_LIST_QUANTIFIER_OPERAND_KIND` is renamed `VALUE_LIST_CONDITION_GATES`; the count stays at 81.

**The defect was in the incumbent family and only a row it did not hold could see it.** It merged the field list and the value list into one same-type walk, which matches A12 wherever the field list is listable — every row its own grid held. Put a DATE-RANGE field list opposite a text one and A12 answers with the field list's own kind class, since the field gates run before the value list's type is looked at; the family would have answered with the disagreement class. **This is the constant-across-the-set confound in its plainest form: the set varied the disagreement and held "the field list is listable" fixed, so the account fit every row and was wrong.** The new slice exposed it by asking about the operator the old family had EXCLUDED, which is where the constant breaks.

**A silence that was really an exclusion turned into a locked handover.** The family had left `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` out entirely, justified by the classes its own arm owns — but the same-type gate runs ahead of the branch on the operator token and applies to all four. One measured row converted a mirror expectation of silence into an asserted code, and the operator is now a carrier.

**Two readings of "exactly one" separated by one row.** The date-range shape gate reads the REFERENCE list, so a group holding a single date-range field is refused even though every other operator in the same construct expands a group to its fields. A checker built on the expansion — which this family already had — would have admitted it.

**The rename is the second this session and the pattern is now clear enough to state.** A family named after one of its gates (`…_OPERAND_KIND`, `…_OPERATOR_ARITY`) stops describing itself the moment a neighbouring gate of the same kernel method lands, and the next slice of that method always arrives. Name the family after the METHOD's subject, not after the question the first slice happened to answer.

### C record — two codes that MERGED a family instead of adding one, and the measurement that forced it (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the type walk of `CheckOperationImpl`.** [KF396](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf396); two residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_NUMBER_FOR_CALC` and `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_NUMBER_FOR_ADD` — which completes that kernel class. `CALCULATION_OPERATOR_ARITY` is renamed `ARITHMETIC_OPERATION_GATES` and now carries all five; the count stays at 81 because nothing was added.

**The merge was not a tidiness call — a measured pre-emption made two families wrong.** `check` runs the type walk before both arity gates, so a string in a two-division chain is the kind class ALONE, with the all-numbers control drawing the division class. Two families reporting independently would have emitted a second class on a model A12 answers once, which is the over-report the plan's third open lane names and which no test in this estate can currently see. The slice found it by asking the precedence question before writing the family rather than after.

**The tell that a slice belongs INSIDE an existing family is a shared walk, not a shared code.** The two questions have disjoint code sets and disjoint kernel methods; what they share is one `OperationContext` and one order over it. A split by code — the shape every earlier slice took — would have cut that order in half.

**The renaming is the cost of having named a family after one of its gates.** `CALCULATION_OPERATOR_ARITY` was accurate when it held three codes and became a misdescription the moment the walk's first gate landed, and a constant that under-describes its family is read by the report's own consumers. Six sites plus three ledger rows; worth doing in the same commit rather than leaving a name that would have to be explained.

**One measured surprise, and it is the kind a source read produces the wrong shape of.** `+` refuses an enumeration whose values carry display LABELS, beside a plain text, under the KIND class rather than one of its own — two enumerations declaring the same tokens differ only in that property. The grid varies exactly it, with the labelless twin as the control.

### C record — the eighty-first family, and a construct where two operator NAMES point the wrong way (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what each of the four single-field value operators demands of the one field it reads.** [KF395](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf395); three residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_STRING`, `MVK_NO_CONVERT_IN_NUMBER` and the first REPORT of `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED`, on a construct that also widens `MVK_NO_NUMBER`, `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` to a further carrier.

**Slicing by kernel FILE landed the whole grid in one measurement again.** `CheckFeldOpImpl` is a straight-line head followed by a switch on the operator token, so every cell the source settles was predictable and only the cells A12 alone can settle needed a `?` — there were none, and roughly 45 cells went green on the first run. The three preceding slices behaved the same way; the pattern is now reliable enough to plan around.

**Two of the four operators are named for the opposite of what they demand, and only the grid says so.** `FieldValueAsString` refuses a string and takes a number; `FieldValueAsNumber` is the one arm that admits a CATEGORY, which its three siblings refuse. Neither is derivable from the names, from the sibling arms, or from the ledger rows — `MVK_NO_STRING`'s row had attributed the class to both `Length` and `FieldValueAsString`, and half of that was wrong.

**An unreachability claim cost two models rather than one, deliberately.** `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE` sits behind a gate whose demand excludes its own trigger, so the honest test attacks BOTH links: declare both and A12 answers the inconsistency class beside it, drop the pattern and the conversion class answers first. Measuring each link separately would have established neither, which is the reachability row's own rule applied rather than cited.

**The mirror's fixture trap recurred one layer out from where it was recorded.** A raw `"` inside the substituted condition breaks the embedding JSON and the model then carries no rule, which reads exactly like the family staying silent — recorded after the last slice for conditions written IN the raw string, and hit again for a condition assembled in Kotlin code, where `\"` is just a quote. Every `FieldValueAsString` row went silent at once, which is the tell: a whole operator failing while its siblings pass is a fixture fault, not a family fault.

### C record — four type sets matched a token A12 never writes, and one of them inverted into an over-rejection (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the custom field type's discriminator.** [KF394](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf394). A12 stores `CustomFieldType`; four of the checker's type sets matched `CustomType`. No new family, so the count stays at 80.

**A set that matches nothing is the quietest defect this estate can hold.** Three of the four sets were refusal lists, so the families reading them went silent on every custom declaration — an under-report, invisible from every direction at once: each family's mirror measured the checker against itself and agreed, no laws grid had put a custom field to the kernel, and the shared reader `baseTypeClass` returned `null` for a custom declaration exactly as three consumers' KDocs said it deliberately did. The accidental silence and the documented one were indistinguishable.

**The fourth was the forbidden direction, and it is the one that makes this a defect rather than a gap.** `ValueInFieldsConstantKind`'s text arm lists the declarations A12 ADMITS, so a token that matches nothing removes an admission: the family reported `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM` on a custom field under a text constant, which A12 takes. Its own KDoc table already said "String, Custom, Enumeration" — the intent was never in doubt, only the spelling — which is the tell worth carrying: **a set whose members are strings can disagree with the prose above it and nothing will say so.**

**The guard is `CheckerFieldTypeTokenGuardTest`, with both denominators derived.** The legal spellings come from `FieldTypeBinding.kt`'s own `DECLARED_FIELD_TYPES`, the table mirroring A12's deserializer switch; the scanned side is every checker source outside the `syntax` package, whose `ValidType`/`InvalidType` are operator ids — and the exclusion is itself asserted, so it cannot start hiding a real set. A shared constant would not have helped: the sets that bite are the literal ones a new family writes out, and there were seven when this landed.

**The measurement is five carriers wide because five methods ask the question separately.** Both rounding constructs, the summing and the ordering field-list arms, and a date shift's amount each refuse a custom declaration with their own class, and the value-list text arm admits it — with a number control beside it, since an ADMITTED cell means nothing without a row that fails. Every predicted cell landed on the first run.

### C record — a code the owning family had ruled out, re-opened by a helper that arrived later (2026-09-14)

**Frozen: an ITERATING semantic-index key, `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_INVALID_ITER`.** [KF351](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf351), amended with one new cell. It is not a new family — it is the first gate of `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY`, ahead of that family's own class, which is where A12 asks it.

**Its own KDoc had ruled it out, and the reason had expired.** The family said the gate "turns on the rule's anchoring, which this family cannot see" — true when it was written, and false once `OperandRepetitionScope` grew the anchoring lookup and the level helpers moved to a shared file. A recorded silence is a claim about the code as it stood; nothing re-reads those claims when the code beneath them changes, which is an argument for citing what a silence DEPENDS on rather than only stating it.

**The new cell is the one the reader could have got wrong in the safe-looking direction.** A NON-repeatable group inside a repetition, as the key: the key-kind class would have been a plausible verdict and the iteration gate takes it. Measured rather than predicted, because predicting it would have been a claim about which of two gates reads the key first.

**The reporting geometry is narrower than the gate**, stated in the family and in the row: only a repeatable level strictly below the error target's group, which is where both halves were measured. A PARALLEL level draws one of four index-keyed classes outside an index and nothing has measured which name it takes inside one, so that shape stays silent rather than guessing a rename.

### C record — the selection method's own input had drifted, and now a guard holds it (2026-09-14)

**33 MVK-LEDGER rows said the checker did not report a code it had been reporting for days or weeks.** Several of those rows' own prose already said the checker reported them, beside a disposition cell that did not — the two-copies-of-one-fact smell, in a registry whose column is read mechanically.

**This is not a tidiness finding; the column STEERS the work.** Family selection ranks `| kernel-delegated |` rows by how much measurement already sits against them, so a stale row puts a finished code at the top of the list. It happened twice in this session before the sweep: `MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE` was selected as delegated and was only half-true, and `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` sat at the top of the next ranking while the checker had been reporting it all along. The cost of a stale row is a session spent re-deriving finished work.

**The guard is `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest`, and its denominator is derived on both sides**: every A12 code appearing in a `Finding(...)` construction in the checker's own sources must have `checker-reported` in its ledger cell. The scan is deliberately NARROWER than the neighbouring `CheckerCodeBasisGuardTest`'s, which takes every string literal: over-counting is harmless when asking *was this measured* and is not harmless here, because the checker holds codes it never reports and demanding a row for those would fail honest content. Two exclusions have their reasons in the class — comments, where this estate's KDoc routinely names the neighbouring class a family stays silent on, and string interpolation, where a message tells an author which code the reported one is not.

**Only one direction is locked, and that is the honest limit.** A row claiming a report the checker no longer emits would need a scan that follows every helper, and the same over-counting returns. The drift that actually happens is one-way — the family lands and the row lags — so the guard covers the direction with the evidence.

### C record — the eightieth family, and the comparand confound on a second axis (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what the six rounding operators demand of the value they round and of the digit count.** [KF393](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf393); `MVK_INVALID_DIGIT_FOR_ROUNDING` and `MVK_NO_CONST` drained, and three more codes gained a carrier.

**One construct in the catalog turned out to be two in the kernel.** The `…Value` spelling and the bare one are different grammar rules with different operand gates, and nothing in the operator names says so — the `code-owner` file mode over `CheckOpUtils` returned four open codes spread across four unrelated callers, and reading those callers is what partitioned them into one coherent family and three other slices.

**The comparand confound cost the one round this slice spent, on an axis the previous note did not cover.** `ExtremumOperandKindGridLawsTest` records it for the operand KIND; here it is the SCALE — a rounded result's scale is the digits argument, so a field comparand fixes a scale of its own and `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` masked every admitted cell. The generalisation now in both classes' prose: **compare against a literal unless the row is about the comparand.**

**The remaining three `CheckOpUtils` codes are named and left.** `MVK_NO_NUMBER_FOR_CALC` types the arms of `^ * / + -`, which are operations this reader does not type; `MVK_NO_DATE_CONSTANT` extends the existing `VALUE_IN_FIELDS_CONSTANT_KIND` family rather than starting one; `MVK_NO_CONST`'s second carrier is `CheckFeldOperOpImpl`, a different construct. Each is a slice, not a widening of this one.

### C record — the seventy-ninth family, and a gate ORDER that inverts what the source seems to say (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a date-arithmetic operator demands of the date it shifts and of the amount it shifts by.** [KF392](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf392); `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE`, `MVK_INVALID_TIME` and `MVK_NON_NUMERIC` drained, and `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP` gained a second carrier.

**The finding is the ORDER, not any one gate.** Read on its own, "the format must carry a date component" says a day shift over a clock is `MVK_INVALID_TIME`; measured, it is `MVK_WRONG_DATE_FORMAT_FOR_OP`, because the component gate is asked first and a clock carries no day. That inverts which operators can reach the class at all — only the three clock ones — and it is the kind of claim a source read settles only if the read is of the CALLER rather than of the utility.

**Forty cells, one measurement, every prediction right.** Two slices running now have landed their grid first try, and the common factor is that both transcribed a caller method whose gates are literally sequential statements. When the source is a straight-line sequence there is nothing to leave as `?`; the `?` belongs to predicates whose admitted set the name misreports, which is where the previous two slices spent their rounds.

**Two shared readers were promoted rather than copied** — `declaresAPartialPrecision` into `TemporalFormat`, `PLAINLY_NON_NUMBER_TYPES` into `Reference` — and the amount slot reuses `bareReferenceField`, which already answers "one declared field, or nothing" for both the bracketed and the bare spelling. A third private copy of that reduction was written and deleted the same hour; the tell was that it handled one spelling and the measured fixture used the other.

### C record — the seventy-eighth family, and the slice whose grid was already on disk (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what kind of declaration each field-LIST operation admits in the entity list they share.** [KF391](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf391); `MVK_NO_BOOLY_ALLOWED`, `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` and `Sum`'s carrier of `MVK_NO_NUMBER` drained.

**Slicing by kernel FILE rather than by code paid for itself twice over.** `CheckFeldListenOpImpl` is one switch over one list, so its three open classes are one family and one KDoc — and reading the caller instead of the utilities is what showed that the extrema's numeric arm is the complement of an arm this repo already owned, rather than a new law.

**A third of the measurement was already on disk, and the accelerator is to look before cutting a grid.** `ExtremumOperandKindGridLawsTest` had measured the whole `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` axis in September — leader set, follower set, typedef resolution, group-expansion order, and the comparand confound — so the new grid covers only the confirm and `Sum` arms. `code-owner`'s locks column is what surfaced it: the code reads `reported by: NOBODY` while naming three lock classes, which is exactly the shape of a measured law with no reporter.

**Every predicted cell landed first try, on a grid of about seventy.** The source settles this family completely — `isYes` is `IYesType`, `isNumber` is `INumberType`, and `getOpTypeForField` fixes the leader arm — so nothing was left as `?`. The one thing worth measuring anyway was the BOOLEAN row, because the code's name says the opposite of what the predicate does.

**The handover between two families is now locked from both sides.** `TemporalAggregateOperandsTest` carried a row asserting silence on a number-led extremum with the comment "A12 answers `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` there"; that row now asserts the code, so deleting either arm fails a test that names the other.

### C record — the seventy-seventh family, and the grid that stopped an over-rejection (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what kind of field `FieldValuesNotUnique` admits, and how its temporal ones must be spelled.** [KF390](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf390); `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_DATE_ALLOWED` drained.

**The class NAME is wrong about its own law, and one extra grid round is what caught it.** "Only string, enum, number or date allowed" reads as a closed list, and a family built from it would have refused a `TimeType`, a `DateTimeType`, a `DateFragmentType` and an `EnumerationType` — four shapes A12 refuses under the NEXT gate's class, which is a wrong class on a correct rejection. The rows cost one run; the defect would have been invisible to every guard, since the model is refused either way and only the code differs.

**The prediction was exact on the six rows that were predicted.** Two text fields, two numbers, two dates alike, a date range, the base-type neighbour and the raw-type neighbour all landed first try; the four kind rows were left as `?` precisely because the class name was not evidence about them. That is the split worth repeating — predict what the SOURCE settles, measure what only the kernel can.

**Slice selection cost two commands.** `code-owner` over `CheckEntityListenUtils` returned the open set, and the per-code mode then showed that the neighbouring `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED` was already reported while this one was not — a distinction one letter apart in the code name and invisible to a grep.

**One code of the pair is deferred, and the reason is a library gap rather than a measurement.** `MVK_NO_BOOLY_ALLOWED` sits on `FirstFilledValue` and needs a Confirm or Boolean operand, which `DocumentModelDraft` cannot author (DEV-API-GAPS DG55). Closing DG55 unblocks it, and that is its own slice rather than raw DM-JSON smuggled into a laws fixture.

### C record — an EXTENSION rather than a family, and the instrument that decided that (2026-09-14)

**Frozen change: the date-aggregate base-type gate.** [KF389](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf389); `MVK_DATE_AND_NONDATE` drained into the existing `TEMPORAL_AGGREGATE_OPERANDS`, whose KDoc had carried it as a deliberate silence — "which the builders pre-check". No new family, so the count stays at 76.

**The silence was justified by the wrong fact, and the ledger's own semantics say why.** `pre-checked` classifies the TYPED LIBRARY surface; whether the portable checker reports a code is a third, orthogonal fact. A stored DM-JSON model carries whatever it carries regardless of what a builder would have refused, so "the builders pre-check it" is never on its own a reason for the checker to stay quiet. Any other family's silence resting on that phrase is worth re-reading.

**The slice was chosen and sized by `scripts/typescript/code-owner.ts` rather than by grep**, which is the first slice to have been. Its file mode over `CheckEntityListenUtils` returned 15 open classes; three of them clustered on one method, and the per-code mode then showed that two of the three were already reported and only this one was not. That turned what looked like a three-code slice into a one-code extension of an existing family — the same judgement the previous slice got wrong by hand, reached in two commands.

**The instrument had to be corrected twice first, and both corrections are the same defect one layer down.** Its source scan could not see a code reached through a file-local `finding(…)` helper, nor one parked in an enum constructor and read as `law.code`. The first gap was real and shared with `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest`, which had been green while fourteen reported codes carried rows that did not say so; the second is unfixable by scanning, so file mode now reads coverage from the guard-locked ledger instead. **A tool that under-reports coverage is the expensive direction here** — it sends a session to redo finished work, which is exactly what the count in this plan's stage-5 row was doing.

**One over-rejection was avoided by a check worth repeating.** `declaredTemporalFormatOf` returns null both for a non-temporal field and for a temporal one declaring no format, and a `DateTimeType` may legitimately declare none. Refusing on that null would have rejected a model A12 admits, so the gate refuses only on an allow-list of types it is certain about — `CustomFieldType`, `TypeDefType`, `UnspecifiedType` and `DateRangeType` are all left alone.

### C record — the seventy-sixth family: the decider was the CALLER, and the prior art was one carrier wide (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the duplicate and root-group gates an entity list meets before its operator asks anything.** [KF388](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf388); `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`, `MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2` and `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_REFERENCED` now reported on the field and value lists, which is the carrier boundary the ledger had carried as unaudited since 2026-09-13 and the group-list families' own KDoc named as out of scope.

**The prior art answered a narrower question than the one asked, which is the failure mode that terminates an enquiry.** `GroupListOperands` and `GROUP_LIST_ROOT_OPERAND` already reported all four classes — on six GROUP-LIST carriers. A grep for the codes returns them confidently and on topic, and reads as covered. What made the difference was running the module lane against the new family before writing anything else: nine of its tests went red at once, which is a far cheaper discovery than a review finding a duplicated family. The partition that followed is the one the existing KDoc already prescribed, so the fix was to narrow the new table rather than to widen the old one.

**A gate whose class depends on the CALLER is measured by moving the operator and holding the list fixed.** That is the accelerator's caller-decides variant, and it paid: 22 operators × 9 list shapes settled the whole table in six runs, including three rows no source read would have produced — `GroupFilled` admitting a lone root group, `NumberOfFilledGroups` answering with its arity class instead, and `RepetitionNotUnique` asking the duplicate gates ahead of its own key-list gates.

**A class that looks dead from every one-root fixture was reachable, and only a second ROOT GROUP shows it.** Every entity of a model is under its root group, so a list naming the root beside anything else is the nested-duplicate class — which makes `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_WITH_OTHER_PARAMETERS` unreachable on any ordinary fixture and not unreachable at all. The cost of finding out was one added `rootGroup` call and one run; the cost of writing "unreachable" would have been a published claim that a later fixture falsifies.

**Three families now share one reader instead of restating it.** `bareReferenceField` split into `isBareEntitySpec` and `addressedTargetOf`, and `EntityListArity` and `ValueInFieldsConstantKind` both cite the pre-gate predicate rather than carrying their own copy. That found a real defect in passing: the arity family's copy did not exempt a STARRED duplicate, so it stayed silent on `(Lines*/Amount, Lines*/Amount)` where A12 answers with the arity class — the shared reader reports it, and the grid had already measured the row.

### C record — the seventy-fifth family, and `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` DRAINED (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a pattern comparison demands of its two sides.** [KF387](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf387); four residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`, `MVK_MISSING_CONST_IN_PATTERN_COMP`, `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN` on its condition locus. **All thirteen reachable codes of the estate's largest check method are now reported, across six families.**

**The construct took six slices, and the ORDER of those slices was the whole method.** The operator gates first, because they answer ahead of everything and every later family has to know where they stop; then the four type-agreement arms in A12's own sequence — boolean, text-and-enumeration, number, temporal — each measuring its predecessors as pre-emptions rather than assuming them; the pattern arm last, being the one that RETURNS and so contends with nothing. A slice ordered any other way would have had to re-measure the same precedence from the far side each time.

**The pattern gate came for free because a predicate already existed.** `kernelPatternBlacklist` in `:predicates` is the reader [declaredPatternFindings] uses for a DECLARED pattern, and the condition locus needs exactly it under a different code. One import, one call — which is what the extraction to `:predicates` was for, and the first time a second consumer has arrived without new work.

**A 15-row grid cost one wrong prediction: an EMPTY pattern is admitted.** It compiles and spells no blacklisted adjacency, so the last question takes it. Worth the row because an empty regex reads as the obvious refusal and a checker built from the source alone would have guessed.

**The corrective table's reachability fixture grew twice in two slices, and that is the shape to expect.** A family that reports a code A12's owner explains needs a declaration in `CorrectiveTest` that draws it, and the guard names the code the moment the projection is regenerated — so writing the fixture row in the same pass as the family costs nothing and discovering it costs a red gate run.

### C record — the seventy-fourth family: the module lane caught what the family's own grid could not (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a date or date-range side of a comparison demands of its partner.** [KF386](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf386); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE`, `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_DATE_RANGE`. **`CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` is now drained but for its pattern arm**: eleven of thirteen codes, across five families.

**The co-firing defect is the case for running the module lane before the mirror, and it is the second time it has paid.** The family judged every comparison operator, so a TOLERANCE comparison with a date operand drew the date class ALONGSIDE the tolerance class A12 actually answers with. Nothing in this family's own 20-row grid could see it — a tolerance row reads as another family's business — and `ComparisonOperatorOperandsTest`, written for a different slice on a fixture built for other reasons, went red on the pair. The fix widened the grid by three measured rows (a date ordered against a number is the date class, a date ordered against a text is the operator gate's, a date in a tolerance is the tolerance arm's) and narrowed the family to the six operators that reach the walk.

**A written-out value is left UNJUDGED, and that is not conservatism but the forbidden direction.** A12 types a date-shaped string CONSTANT as a date, so a reader treating `"01.01.2020"` as text would refuse `[Start] == "01.01.2020"`, which A12 admits. Requiring both sides to be declarations is what makes the arm safe rather than what makes it small.

**Two branches in these two methods are written and cannot fire**, recorded because a checker that reproduced them would name a class on a correct rejection: the range arm's ordering refusal (a range is not orderable, so the operator gate answers) and the base-year branches of the date arm (BASE_YEAR is not a declaration). The first was measured, not read.

**The corrective table's standing exemption is gone.** `CorrectiveTest` had carried `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` as "the code the checker only compares against"; the number-scale family now reports it, so the fixture draws it and the expectation is the empty set. An exemption that becomes stale is worse than none: it reads as a measured limit long after the limit has lifted.

### C record — the seventy-third family, and a disposition that had been wrong since it was written (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what equality demands of two numbers' declared scales, and what a number may stand against at all.** [KF385](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf385); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` and `MVK_INCONSISTENT_TYPES_COMPARED`. Four of `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`'s thirteen codes remain, all of them the date, date-range and pattern arms.

**Two of five predictions were wrong, and the grid is the only reason that is a footnote rather than a shipped family.** The source read said "equality of declared scale", so `[UnitPrice] == 1` was predicted refused; it is ADMITTED, because a written-out number carries the multiplicative-constant capability the gate waives on, and the pair passes exactly when the SMALLER-scale side is the capable one. The ledger row for the code had said so for months, in the kit's language rather than the kernel's, and reading it first would have cost nothing. **A slice's own ledger row is prior art about the law, not only about the disposition.**

**The second wrong prediction was a fixture, not a law**: the computation probe read the field it computed, so the suppression row measured `MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD` instead of the gate. Worth naming because a suppression row cannot be read from the verdict alone — an ADMITTED that comes from the wrong refusal being absent looks identical to the suppression working.

**A disposition row was wrong, and landing this family is what exposed it.** `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` sat in `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` under a comment saying rulekit "names these only to EXPLAIN a kernel verdict … never to refuse anything itself". `NumberExpr.requireCompatibleScale` refuses on it, naming the code in the message. The row is now `SAME_LAW_SEPARATE_CARRIERS`. The guard did its job — it recorded that a decision was made — and the decision was simply wrong; what caught it was needing the row to be right for a new reason.

**A shared reader was short in a way nothing had noticed.** `isNumberLiteral` capped at three tokens, so a NEGATIVE decimal (`-1.00`, four tokens after the tokenizer's digit-run terminal) read as no number at all in three families. It is now shape-based and returns the scale, which this family needs anyway — one function, one correct definition, three consumers.

### C record — the seventy-second family: two questions in one kernel method that never contend (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a text or enumeration side of a comparison needs of its partner, and which written-out values an enumeration admits.** [KF384](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf384); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_ENUM_OR_STRING` (also closing an entry on the ledger's *still unaudited* list) and `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON`. Six of `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl`'s thirteen codes remain.

**Half a kernel method is a legitimate partition when the halves are DISJOINT, and that has to be shown rather than asserted.** `checkStringOrEnumType` carries four classes; this family reports two and leaves the display-value pair to A12. The justification is structural, not budgetary: the kind question fires only where the partner is a NUMBER, the display questions only where both sides are text-or-enum, and a written-out value opposite an enumeration takes the domain branch and returns before the display branch beside it. All three claims are measured on a display-bearing enumeration — the carrier that would have collapsed them had they contended.

**The `else if` was the whole argument, and reading it is what made the partition safe.** Had the display check run *after* the domain check rather than as its alternative, every constant row would have needed the display machinery to be right, and the slice would have been four codes or none.
