**It also closed a documented under-report in a neighbouring family.** [scalarValueListGateFindings] (then named `enumConstantDomainFindings`) states that a COMPARISON answers with `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON` and that it does not report it, "because each needs an operand shape this reader does not reduce". The comparison reader built two slices ago is that shape; the note stays, now naming where the carrier went.

**A third reader collapsed into a shared home**, `declaredEnumValues` in `EnumCategories.kt` — three private copies in two shapes (ordered and set-valued), with the same null-on-unreadable contract stated three times. The ordered one won and the set consumers call `toSet()`.

**A test-side defect worth naming, and the guard that already owns it.** The first grid used the same row label twice in one `LinkedHashMap`, so the second measurement silently overwrote the first on BOTH sides and the assertion passed over a denominator short by one. It was caught by eye and fixed before any gate ran — which is why `GridRowKeyGuardTest`, which owns exactly this class in `leakGuardTest`, never saw it. Re-proved by mutation on this slice's own grid: it fires. The lesson is not a missing instrument but the order of the loop — the targeted `-Pfast` runs a slice is written against skip that lane, so a collapsed grid can steer a whole family's implementation before anything says so. Read the row count off the result XML when a grid is the thing being written.

### C record — the seventy-first family, and a published grid that varied one axis too few (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a confirm or boolean declaration may be compared to, and with which equality operator.** [KF383](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf383); two residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YES`, `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_YESNO`. The first partition of `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` after the operator gates; eight of its thirteen codes remain.

**The grid found a defect in two entries this estate had already published.** [KF270](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf270) reported that a confirm "refuses every field on the right, including another CONFIRM", and [KF9](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf9) that it "compares only to `True` or another Confirm" — the first too strong, the second silent on the restriction. Both are right about `==` and wrong about `!=`, which A12 admits, and neither grid had varied the operator inside that arm. The kernel source carries the answer in plain sight: the two-confirm refusal is its own `throw` guarded on `eq.ne == null`, separate from the comparability test. **The confound is the one no control inside a set can catch** — every row of KF270's equality arm was individually clean and the whole arm held `==` fixed — and it was found only because a new family had to decide what to report for `!=`.

**A checker must not inherit an ERASURE, and this is the sharpest case yet.** The Java handles erase the whole confirm-versus-confirm comparison: `ConfirmRef` offers `confirmed`/`notConfirmed` and nothing else, so `[A] != [B]` between two confirms is unauthorable through the typed surface — and A12 accepts it. A checker that mirrored the handle's shape would refuse a legal model, which is the one direction this project forbids; the disposition row in `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` records that separation rather than the usual carrier split alone.

**The slice also widened the sixty-ninth family, on a measurement it had never taken.** `True` and `False` are the constants an author actually writes beside a boolean field, and A12 spells them as bare NAMES rather than as literals — so [comparisonOperatorOperandFindings]'s reader, which recognised only literals, left them outside all three operator gates. Four measured rows closed it ([KF382](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf382), extended). The general shape is worth naming: **a reader's constant vocabulary is a denominator, and a family that never wrote the missing member cannot see the hole.**

**Three helpers collapsed into shared homes in the same pass** — `bareReferenceField` (one entity spec, resolved, with the unaddressed-repetition pre-emption the corpus mutation found in the arity gates) and `isNumberLiteral`, both in `Reference.kt`, now serve three families that each carried their own copy. The pre-emption reaching the comparison family for free is the point: it was a defect there too, unmeasured, and sharing the reader closed it without a second corpus run.

### C record — the seventieth family, and the reader that unlocks the estate's largest construct (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what each comparison OPERATOR demands of the two sides it joins.** [KF382](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf382); three residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_CONST`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_TOLERANCE_OP`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`.

**The foundational half is `comparisonsIn`, and the gap it closed had been invisible because nothing failed.** This checker read CALLS, and a comparison is infix — A12's `vergleichsBedingung` — so the syntax module had no structure to hand a family, and `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` sat at the top of the ranking untouched with thirteen reachable codes, twice the next construct. The shape parser had been reading comparisons correctly all along in order to validate them; it simply never recorded one. Twenty lines in `ShapeParser.conditionPart` and a `ComparisonShape` beside `CallShape` is the whole reader.

**Both sides are recorded as TEXT, not reduced, and that is the load-bearing design decision.** A comparison law asks first what each side IS — a bare reference, a literal, arithmetic, a nested call — and a reduction that answered "a path" for the first and null for the rest would erase exactly the distinction those laws turn on. The first consumer proves it: the family types only a bare reference or a literal, and a side is a bare reference only when EVERY token belongs to one entity spec. An earlier draft reduced by "it names exactly one entity" and duly typed `{[Qty] + 1}` from the `Qty` inside it — a type claim about a result A12 computes and this reader does not.

**Three of the thirteen are drained; the other ten are their own partitions** (boolean agreement, string/enum agreement, the pattern arm, the decimal-places gate, the date and date-range arms). The slice stopped at three because the reader was the foundational part and a thirteen-code denominator is the shape the convergence gate exists to refuse.

**A NEW code costs three guard rows, and finding that out from the guard lane costs a rerun each time.** Measured over three slices: a code the checker has never reported before trips `CheckerLedgerDispositionGuardTest` (its MVK-LEDGER disposition cell needs `checker-reported`), `CorrectiveTest`'s reachability assertion (the generated corrective table gains an entry, and the `reachability` fixture must carry a declaration that draws it), and — where the code also appears in Java main source — `BuilderCheckerDivergenceGuardTest` (both estates state it, so the DISPOSITIONS map needs a classified row). None of the three depends on anything the lane computes, so do all three at family-writing time: list the codes the family newly emits, then grep the ledger cell, the corrective fixture and the Java main source for each. Three greps against three full lane runs.

**The verification order was also re-cut this session, and the reason is measured.** `:adapter:checkerSilenceGuardTest` — the corpus-mutation guard — now runs with the module lane straight after a family compiles, rather than last. It is the only instrument that generates shapes nobody thought to write, it caught a wrong-class defect in the sixty-ninth family, and it caught it AFTER all that family's documentation had been written. Cost of moving it: nothing; it was going to run anyway.

### C record — the sixty-ninth family: the accelerator pointed at a UTILITY instead of a construct (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: how many entities each entity-list operator's argument list needs.** [KF381](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf381); six residue codes drained — the whole `MVK_PARAMSIZE_*` set, across twenty-two operators and five construct classes.

**Every slice so far transcribed one CONSTRUCT's method. This one transcribed a UTILITY, and the enumeration is what made that safe.** Six arity gates live in `CheckEntityListenUtils`, and the variable is not the construct but the operator: thirteen call sites, `grep`-able in one command, each naming which gate one operator's switch reaches. The rule the previous slices established still holds — you transcribe the thing that decides the class — but the thing that decides it here is the CALLER, so the transcription is the caller table.

**Two predicates, and the difference is exactly where a rule of thumb would go wrong.** `checkIsMoreThanOneEntity` refuses only a lone FIELD, so `AllFieldsFilled(SomeGroup)` is admitted; `isMoreThanOneParameter` refuses a lone entity of any kind, so `AllGroupsFilled(SomeGroup)` is not. A family that had read all six as "needs at least two" would have refused a legal model on the first of those — the over-rejection this project forbids — and nothing in the six method names says so.

**Two classes differ only in an underscore.** `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID2` is the group conditions' "more than one parameter"; `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID_2` is `SumOfProducts`' "exactly two". Each is measured on its own carrier, never inferred from the other, because a transposition here is invisible to every guard.

**Two wrong-class defects were caught by the estate rather than by reading, and they were caught by DIFFERENT instruments.** The first implementation reported an arity class on lists A12 answers with `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`/`MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`; the existing `GroupListOperandsTest` caught it as a co-fire within a minute, which is the mirror suite working as a CROSS-family gate rather than a per-family one — worth remembering when deciding whether a row belonging to another family's carrier earns its place.

The second is the one no amount of source reading would have found, because the shape does not look like an arity question: an operand crossing an unstarred repetition (`Sum(Lines/Amount)`) is one entity, is a field, and carries no star — and A12 never asks its arity, the reference reader having refused it while the list was built. `:adapter:checkerSilenceGuardTest` found it by MUTATING five real corpus models (remove a star from a condition) and comparing class for class. **A synthetic grid cannot produce that row, because writing it requires already suspecting it.** The corpus-mutation guard is the estate's only instrument that generates the shapes nobody thought to write, and this is the second family it has corrected.

### C record — the sixty-eighth family, and the bucket that turned out to be the larger reservoir (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what a value-list quantifier's field list may carry, and what may stand opposite it.** [KF380](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf380); three residue codes drained — `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`, `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`, `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON`, all of `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl.check`.

**The selection instrument was wrong about which pool to rank over, and correcting it changed the plan's remaining size.** Ranking had been over `kernel-delegated` codes alone. But the codes this family drains are `erased` — the ledger's word for "the typed builders cannot author it" — and an erased code is precisely the one a checker over STORED DM-JSON is for: the builders never emit the shape, so nothing but a checker ever meets it. Counted on 2026-09-14: **50 purely delegated codes remain unreported and 54 erased ones do**, with 8 erased codes already reported, so the route was live and simply unranked. The pool the instrument now ranks over is both buckets minus the rows marked unreachable from stored DM-JSON — 110 codes.

**Re-ranked that way, the estate's shape is different.** `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl` — the comparison itself, the most common construct in any model — carries **13** reachable codes, twice the next; `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl` 10, `CheckFeldListenBedingungImpl` 9. The comparison is not yet takeable, and the reason is worth writing down as the next foundational investment rather than as a blocker: this checker reads CALLS, and a comparison is infix, so the syntax module has no structure to hand a family. One `comparisonsIn` reader unlocks a construct with thirteen codes and several partitions.

**One shared reader landed instead, and it is the smaller sibling of that idea.** Two operator families now put an infix `In` between two comma-separated lists, and the keyword sits INSIDE one operand because only a comma splits operands. `ConditionCall.splitAtIn()` is that scan, written once; the sixty-seventh family was refactored onto it in the same change rather than left holding a private copy.

### C record — the sixty-seventh family: one method, one switch, four classes (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: which field declarations each constant KIND of `NumberOfValueInFields` admits.** [KF379](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf379); four residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_TYPEYESNO`, `MVK_NO_TYPE_YES_OR_YESNO`, `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM`, `MVK_NO_NUMBER`, all of `CheckAnzahlWertOpImpl.check`.

**This is the accelerator at its cleanest, and the throw-site grouping is what found it.** The selection instrument introduced with the sixty-sixth family — group the remaining delegated codes by the kernel FILE that raises them — put `CheckEntityListenUtils` at the top with sixteen codes, and that file is a utility rather than a construct. Following its CALLERS instead named a 137-line construct class whose single method draws seven of those sixteen. One method, one grid, four classes: the largest slice of this plan so far, and it cost one measurement pass because every row of the grid is the same call with one variable moved.

**Every prediction in the grid was right on the first run, which is the accelerator's actual payoff.** Transcribing the method rather than the codes means the arms, their order, and the gates ahead of them all arrive together; nothing had to be discovered by a failed assertion. The work that remained was not finding the law but bounding it.

**Two codes of the construct were measured UNREACHABLE, and that is a result rather than a gap.** `MVK_DIFFERENT_YES_VALUE`/`MVK_DIFFERENT_NO_VALUE` compare a Boolean declaration's stored true/false representation, and the conversion feeding every consistency check replaces it with the literals — so a mismatch is admitted, and a family reporting the class would refuse models A12 accepts. The row that would normally settle it does not exist: the model reader ignores an unknown property, so a misspelled-key control is admitted exactly as the correct key is, and the mechanism had to be carried by a source chain instead. **Recording WHY no control is available is the transferable part** — a uniform column with no control behind it is indistinguishable from apparatus that never arrived.

**Four gates run ahead of the switch and each answers with its own class**, so the family refuses to judge a list that trips one. This is the same discipline as the previous three slices and the reason none of them over-reports: the risk in a construct-shaped family is never the class it names, it is the class it names on a model some earlier gate already refused.

### C record — the sixty-sixth family: the arithmetic the typed builders cannot author (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: how many divisions and powers one arithmetic expression may carry, and what an exponent has to be.** [KF378](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf378); three residue codes drained — `MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_DIV_FOR_CALC`, `MVK_TOO_MANY_POW_FOR_CALC`, all three of `CheckOperationImpl.check`.

**The selection instrument changed, and that is the transferable part.** The lock-count ranking that picked the last four families is now FLAT: every one of the 68 remaining purely-delegated codes cites exactly one lock class, so sorting by that count says nothing. What replaced it is a group-by over the kernel's own throw sites — for each remaining code, which file raises it — which immediately named `CheckEntityListenUtils` (15 codes), `CheckEntityUtils` (7) and then a tail of four-code CONSTRUCT files. That is the accelerator's own principle turned into a query: pick the method, not the code.

**These three are the clearest case yet for the checker existing at all.** `DslRenderer` braces a left power child and a left division under division, so the typed builders cannot emit any refused shape — the codes are reachable only from stored DM-JSON, which is exactly the input this checker reads and the builders never see.

**The measurement cost more than the implementation, and the reason is worth recording: the scale gate masks the whole construct.** A power or a division leaves an operation's scale undeterminable, so `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` answered every row until each was wrapped in `RoundDown(…, 2)` into a scale-2 target. Two false starts first — a hand-written model the kernel could not build at all, and a bare `BaseFee` where the grammar wants `[BaseFee]` — both of which read as a verdict about the construct.

**One row was dropped rather than asserted**, and the family is silent where it would have gone: an exponent field declaring no `maxFractionalDigits` at all. The kernel source refuses a null scale as plainly as a positive one, but the estate's fixtures cannot author such a field and a splice attempt did not resolve, so the claim stayed a source read and the checker stays silent on the shape.

### C record — a THIRD branch for an existing family, and the code that lives in two phases (2026-09-14)

**Frozen scope: the semantic-index branch of `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkIndicesInFilterCondition`, added to `FILTER_BODY_GATES`.** [KF377](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf377); one residue code drained — `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NOT_ALLOWED`. No new family: the class belongs to a method this estate had already transcribed, and the transcription had two of its three branches.

**The code had been filed under the wrong PHASE, and the ledger's own wording is what hid it.** It sits in `ParserService.checkNormalRule`, so the post-parse set took it on the strength of that one site. It has three, and the other two throw during the parse; every measured shape reaches the filter walk. The discriminating control is a rule violating the filtered-entity gate as well — a whole-rule gate checked two branches earlier, which loses anyway. **The general lesson for a set keyed by code: grep the class across the parser and read EVERY site, because a class is not owned by the phase that happens to name it.**

**Three under-reports taken deliberately, all in the same direction.** An entity-keyed index in a filter is left to the index's own spec gates; a filtered operand riding a parallel level is left alone by both families; and the filter walk's reader still refuses to judge an operand that crosses an unstarred repetition, which is where the second of those comes from. Each is a silence where A12 rejects, which the estate tolerates — what it will not tolerate is the class that was live instead.

**The slice also bounded a clause the estate had been applying too widely.** `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY`'s iteration gate is drawn against the RULE's error target; inside a filter the anchoring is the filtered entity, and two measured shapes there are answered by other gates entirely. It had been naming its class on both. That is the third time this month a gate measured on one carrier was applied to a second without measurement, and the tell was the same: no new branch was needed, so nothing prompted the question.

**Where the convergence gate bit, and what it bought.** The first implementation reported on every index in a filter; the mirror then showed two classes where A12 gives one, on two rows, and fixing that exposed the neighbouring family's over-wide gate. Partitioning at that point — report only the literal-keyed shape, leave the rest silent and say so — closed the slice with one measured claim per row instead of a third round.

### C record — the sixty-fifth family: a gate whose marked operands are not the ones it names (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: that no `Having` filter hangs off an operand A12 marked as parallel-iterated.** [KF376](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf376); one residue code drained — `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_FOR_FILTERED_ENTITY_NOT_ALLOWED`, the ranking's top row, and the second of `ParserService.checkNormalRule`'s seven `throwError` calls to get a reporting family.

**The gate's name is the wrong model of it, and one row is what said so.** `hasParallelIteratedFilterConditionId` reads as "the filtered operand reads a parallel-iterated group", and a family built on that reading passes every row but one: the marker is set by TWO passes, and the second marks the rule's OWN keyed group on every reference once any other operand parallel-iterates. The discriminator was written before the measurement precisely because the natural reading and the source reading differed — two conditions with byte-identical filtered operands, differing in a conjunct that names neither — and the kernel took the wider side.

**The family it neighbours had the same defect and was silent about it.** `PARALLEL_ITERATION_UNIQUENESS` excluded any reference carrying a star, where A12 marks per LEVEL: an aggregate over a keyed group with a star on a group beneath it still iterates jointly, and the rule is refused. One kernel row settled it and the fix is three lines. That under-report had been live since the family landed, invisible because a silence on a legal-looking condition is what a correct family also produces.

**One smell fixed on the way, and it is the reason it was fixable at all.** Promoting two helpers to `internal` collided with private copies in two other families — `OUTER_MARKER` and `indexFieldNameOf`, each stated three times byte-identically. They now live once, in `Reference.kt` beside the other cross-family readers. A duplicated helper is cheap until a third consumer needs it to change.

**A silence the report carries on BOTH sides of a boundary, now disclosed.** `OPERAND_REPETITION_SCOPE` does not judge a filter-carrying text at all — a filter body's references are bound to the filtered entity rather than the rule's target and the reader sees one flat list — so the operand's own levels go unjudged too. The measured case is an unkeyed parallel level under a filtered operand: A12 draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` during the parse and nothing in the report names it. The KDoc said nothing about the skip until this slice; an under-report nobody wrote down is indistinguishable from a partition.

### C record — the sixty-fourth family: the top of the ranking, and a gate that was pure transcription (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the two gates an entity-list aggregate asks of each temporal operand, its PRECISION and its COMPONENTS.** [KF193](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf193), amended with the gate's own mechanism, and [KF260](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf260). Two residue codes drained — `MVK_DATEFORMATS_NOT_COMPATIBLE`, which the ranking put at the top with three lock classes against it, more than any other delegated row, and `MVK_PARTIAL_DATE_NOT_ALLOWED`, which cost a second expectation in the same walk because A12 asks both of one operand before moving to the next.

**The second code is the argument for transcribing a whole METHOD rather than a code.** It was a separate delegated row with its own lock and would have been selected on its own merits eventually; landing it here cost one branch, because the loop that answers it is the loop already written. So when the accelerator says to transcribe the `Check…Impl` for a CONSTRUCT, the pay-off is not only that the chain's order comes out right — it is that every class the chain draws arrives in one slice.

**Nineteen mirror rows, green on the first run, and that is the ranking rule paying off rather than luck.** The kernel method is forty lines and every branch of it is decidable from stored DM-JSON: the component set is plain containment of six letters in the declared format string, the base year is a model-level presence, and the operand list is the expansion A12 already flattens. Nothing needed a grid, because three law classes had already measured the cells — including the two confounds this construct has cost the estate before, kind-versus-declaration and format-string-versus-component-set.

**The one cell that did not exist was the one the previous carrier-scope clause left open.** It named the admitted group operand and stopped, so "a group whose expansion DISAGREES" was a prediction the reader would have had to make. One kernel row settled it in twenty seconds, and the answer went into the finding rather than into the reproduction's comment.

**The row that was delegated says the opposite of what it meant, and that is worth naming.** It read "deliberately NOT pre-checked: this is exactly where a pre-check would over-reject" — true of the Java builders, which hold operand KINDS and provably cannot see a declared format, and false of a checker that reads the stored declaration. A disposition written for one estate had been sitting as if it governed both. The ranking surfaces rows like that on their evidence rather than on their prose, which is a second reason to run it rather than read the ledger front to back.

**Four new leak-guard dispositions, all PROSE_ONLY and three of them the MIRROR direction** — the kits enforce `MVK_DATE_AND_NONDATE`, `MVK_NOT_SORTABLE` and `MVK_NO_NUMBER` while this family only names them, in the KDoc table saying which gate answers ahead of the one it reports. Naming the neighbouring gates is what makes a silence locatable, and it costs a disposition row each time.

### C record — the sixty-third family: three containment classes, and the shipped over-report they exposed (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: where a `RepetitionNotUnique` KEY may sit relative to the scope that bounds it.** [KF375](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf375) carries the phase law and [KF355](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf355) was amended with the chain mechanism; three residue codes drained — `MVK_NOT_IN_GROUP`, `MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH`, `MVK_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP`. The previous record predicted this family by name: the ranking pairs two codes citing the same locks, and this construct's pair was already sitting at the top of it.

**The find was not the family, it was a defect already shipped in a different one.** `PARALLEL_ITERATION_UNIQUENESS` co-fired with every new containment class, and the tempting fix — teach that family to stay silent here — would have been the first of a series. A12 checks a condition in two phases, and the second runs only on a parse that completed, so a parse-time class removes the whole-rule gate's input rather than outranking it. The fix is therefore one rule over the finished finding list, not a condition inside any family, and the set it applies to is derivable from kernel source: a second-phase gate takes a built `ICondition` rather than a parser context.

**Amended 2026-09-14: the fix was itself too narrow, and transcribing the METHOD is what showed it.** The post-parse set held one class while `ParserService.checkNormalRule` draws seven, three of which this checker already reports — so the same over-report was live on two more shapes and was measured on one of them. Worse, the estate held the identical law TWICE: a `withoutRelationsBehindAPositionedDefect` written months earlier, byte-identical but for its code set, because the two halves were discovered separately and neither session read the other's. One function and one derived set now, and the set's members are read off the kernel method rather than off the shapes that happened to expose them. **The lesson generalizes past this fix: a suppression list assembled from incidents is a list of incidents, and the way to size it is to go and read what the phase actually draws.**

**A suppression needs a positive control or it is indistinguishable from a family that stopped working.** The control is within-model: the same index-keyed pair with a CLEAN key list beside a parallel read reports `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_NOT_ALLOWED` on both estates. Without that row the new silence would have passed as correct on a checker that had simply lost the class.

**The hour this cost went to a wrong catalog id** — `"From"` for a clause whose id is `"FromScope"` — which made the whole family return an empty list at its first elvis. That failure mode has no signal at all: a family that reports nothing looks exactly like a family correctly silent on every row, and three of the mirror's rows legitimately expect silence. What found it was the positive control at the end of the table, which is the same instrument that caught the vacuous JSON one family earlier.

**One row was deleted rather than asserted.** A chain control — two keys whose repetitions nest — was written with a predicted expectation, and the kernel answered `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`. A prediction in a lock is a claim nobody measured, so the row went rather than being back-filled to whatever ran.

### C record — the sixty-second family: a construct already measured to exhaustion, and what reusing that grid costs (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: which repeatable levels a `Having` filter's body may name, and the one it must.** [KF374](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf374) carries it. Two residue codes drained — `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` and `MVK_INVALID_ITERATION_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`, both of which had sat delegated while three separate laws tests measured them across some thirty cells.

**The cheapest family so far, and the reason is worth naming: the measurement already existed and nobody had noticed.** Three law classes had been built for peer exchanges about this construct and none of them had ever been asked whether a checker could report what they measured. The selection rule that found it is not "which code is next in the residue" but "which residue code has the most measurement already sitting against it" — a question the ledger answers directly, since a delegated row that cites three lock classes is a family waiting to be written.

**That ranking is mechanical, so run it rather than reading the ledger front to back.** Take every `| kernel-delegated |` row, extract the `…LawsTest` / `…DiffTest` / `…GuardTest` class names it cites, and sort by how many distinct ones each has. Measured 2026-09-14: 78 delegated rows, 38 of them citing at least one lock, and the top of that list is where a family costs a transcription and a mirror rather than a grid. It also names the pairs — two codes citing the same two classes are usually one construct, which is how `MVK_REPEATABLE_GROUP_MISSING` and `MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH` surface together.

**Reusing a grid needs its constants re-derived, and this time that was done first.** One new cell was measured before the family landed: every in-scope row in the borrowed grid was a CONJUNCT beside a failing reference, so "an in-scope reference clears the scope gate" and "an in-scope reference clears it when something else has already failed" were the same rule across all of it. The sole in-scope row is admitted, as predicted — but predicted is not measured, and the previous record's `positivesOnly` cell is what that sentence cost last time.

**Three silences, each locating another family's gate rather than declaring the shape safe.** A star in the body is `FILTER_WILDCARD`'s; an unaddressed repetition in the OPERAND is the reference reader's and answers first; and the `$` marker is invisible to this reader, because the shared reference scan normalizes it away — which is right for binding and wrong for a walk that must skip marked references exactly. The third is the one that would have produced a WRONG class rather than a missing one.

### C record — the sixty-first family: a construct whose SLOTS are the chain, and twenty predictions that all held (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: what each slot of a `Date`/`Time` construction admits, on all three of its arms.** [KF373](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf373) carries it. Four residue codes drained. The ENTITY arm landed in the same slice once its measurement turned out to exist already — `TemporalComponentDeclarationLawsTest` pins the number widths, the flexible year maxima, the scale/sign/near-miss refusals, all six recognised pattern spellings and the exact-`yyyy` date exception — so the arm needed no new grid, only one new cell. A `TypeDefType` component stays delegated, its body not always visible to the reader.

**Reusing a measured grid is a claim, and the claim had a hole exactly where the grid had a constant.** Every row of that older grid declared a `minValue`, so "the sign gate is a declared non-negative minimum" and "the sign gate is that OR the `positivesOnly` flag" were the same rule across all of it — and the kernel's own comment beside the flag says the data modeller cannot set it, which reads as a reason not to look. The flag is declarable in DM-JSON, the source takes it as a second satisfier, and the reader built on the grid alone would have REFUSED a component A12 admits. Two rows and one 29-second run settled it before the family shipped; the general form is that a borrowed grid's constants are the borrower's to re-derive, never the lender's to have covered.

**The accelerator's cleanest run so far: twenty cells, twenty correct predictions, one build.** Two subclasses supply the per-slot bounds and the expected operator, and the base class supplies the gates — so the whole law is a table read straight out of two files. Where the previous family needed four new measurements to find one surprise, this one needed none, and the reason is worth keeping: the constant bounds are LITERALS in the kernel source, not behaviour.

**A third arm means a new way to be wrong: two families answering one declaration.** The constant arm reads no reference and can collide with nothing; the entity arm reads one, and A12's reference reader runs ahead of every operator gate, so a component that is also an unstarred cross-repetition read draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` and the slot gate never runs. Both families would have reported. Nothing in the suite would have gone red — the mirror's own fixture is flat, so the collision was unreachable from every row the family already had — and the differential lane compares classes A12 never draws rather than counting them. The lesson is the reach one: an arm that starts reading REFERENCES inherits every precedence the reference reader has, and the question to ask of a new arm is which other family now shares its input.

**The one cell that could still have gone wrong, and why it was put in anyway.** `QuarterFromDate` is a date extract operator that no date slot expects. The natural rule — "one's own family misplaced, the other family barred" — predicts the wrong-slot class for it; the source lists the three slot operators by name, which predicts the barred class. Reading the switch settles it, but a family built on the natural rule would have named the wrong class and no other row in the grid would have caught it.

**The vacuity trap from the fifty-ninth record recurred, in the same shape, and the positive control caught it in one run.** Every constant probe carries quotes, and an unescaped quote inside the JSON `errorCondition` ends the string — so the three constant tables measured nothing while the operator table, which has no quotes, passed. A silent-row table is where this hides, which is why the leave-alone table now ends with a row that MUST report. Escaping in the fixture helper rather than in each probe is what keeps the next row from reintroducing it.

### C record — the sixtieth family: five classes of one WALK, and the block chosen by construct rather than by code (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: which repetitions an operand may cross unstarred.** `CheckEntityUtils.checkIndicesForA12Model` is one method, and the five classes it can draw — `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, `MVK_MULTIPLE_INDEX_FIELDS`, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NO_INDEX`, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX`, `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX_TYPE` — sat in the residue as five separate ledger rows, three of them annotated as needing "full iteration-model analysis". They needed one walk. [KF371](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf371) carries it.

**The selection rule keeps paying, and this is its largest single return.** Five residue codes drained in one family, and `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` is among the most common authoring mistakes in the corpus. Ranking by CONSTRUCT rather than by code is what found it: ranked by code, these five sit in three different ledger neighbourhoods with three different dispositions, and nothing pairs them.

**Most of the oracle was already on the shelf, which is the second half of the selection rule.** `RuleOperandScopeLawsTest` and `ComputationOperandScopeLawsTest` had pinned the sibling cells on both carriers; `ParallelIterationGateLawsTest` had pinned the type cell. Four new cells finished the chain — the target end of the index demand, the per-level walk, the multiple-index gate, and a star with an unstarred level beneath it — and the four old ones were re-measured on ONE fixture, because a checker reads one model and a table mixing three cannot say whether a verdict belongs to the chain or to a fixture.

**The prediction that failed is the one worth keeping: the WALK outranks the gates.** Transcribing `checkParallelIteration` gives a gate order in which the operand's own index chain resolves before the target's, so a doubly-keyed operand should draw the multiple-index class whatever the target looks like. Measured, it draws `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NO_INDEX` into an unkeyed target, because the OUTER level is refused first and the inner one is never asked. The accelerator reads a chain inside one level; it says nothing about the loop around it, and that is where the fifteenth prediction in this run went wrong.

**Two existing mirrors went red on landing, and both were the family doing its job.** `ErrorFieldReferenceTest` had two rows asserting SILENCE where A12 reports `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` — its own javadoc said so — and `ParallelIterationUniquenessTest` had two more, one of which had been reasoned rather than measured. All four now carry A12's class. A silence justified by "another family owns this" becomes a wrong answer the moment that family arrives, so the mirror tier is what converts the plan's under-reports into reports.

**A THIRD family was corrected on the way, and the lesson is the standing one about constants.** Writing this family's own "target outside the declaring group" silence exposed that `ERROR_TARGET_ITERATION` had no such silence and named the parallel class there. The grid that measured those two classes ([KF348](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf348)) had already had two constants pulled out of it; a fourth was still constant — every row declared the rule in the error field's own group — and moving the rule alone makes both classes vanish, the containment gate answering during the parse ([KF372](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf372)). The control for that measurement had to change fixture, because on a repeatable ROOT every reference iterates at level 0 and no non-iterating reference exists to control with.

**Its own silences, each resting on a gate that answers first.** A target outside the declaring group is `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP`, drawn at the top of the same method — and our `Rule.build()` refuses that pair while A12 admits it ([KF212](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf212)), so the checker must not inherit the refusal; the divergence guard now carries it as `STRICTER_IN_JAVA`. A star on a non-repeatable level, a GROUP operand, a `Having` filter, a semantic index and an `$`-marked outer reference are each another family's or another target's.

### C record — the fifty-ninth family: six classes of one chain, and the JSON that made three rows vacuous (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the semantic index's STRUCTURE.** [KF351](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf351) carries the chain; `SEMANTIC_INDEX_KEY` already owned its first gate, and this family owns the six below it that need no comparison of declared types. *(Extended 2026-09-14: `MVK_INDEX_TYPE_INCONSISTENT` and `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_CONTAINED_IN_INDEX` were held out here for want of a measured domain; [KF404](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf404) measured both and they joined this family, which also corrected where `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_BELOW_WILDCARD` sits in the chain.)*

**The measurement this needed was the ORDER, and it was the cheapest part.** Every single-defect row already existed; six cells putting two live gates in one index settled the descent, and all six predictions from the kernel source held. The gate order is the whole reason to implement these six together — three of them are about a star and differ only in where it sits.

**One class could not be decided index by index, and an existing mirror caught it.** A12 reports the first defect in a condition and nothing after, so a text with two semantic indices is not two questions: a group key in the first withholds the class the second would draw. The family therefore walks the indices in order and stops at the first it cannot read through — including one another family owns. `SemanticIndexKeyTest` went red the moment the family landed, which is what the mirror tier is for.

**A reader capability landed with it.** `semanticIndexUsesIn` reads the index as the PAIR its laws are about — the indexed reference, the key, and what the key carries — where the existing scan answered only what stands in the key position. Its nesting flag encodes A12's left-recursive parse: in `[A For B For C]` the OUTER index is the last one in the text, which is also why the family answers a nested index before the walk rather than inside it.

**A test-authoring trap worth naming, because it makes a mirror VACUOUS rather than red.** Three rows silently measured nothing: their probe carried a `"` that was interpolated straight into the model JSON, so the source never parsed and the whole check returned no findings at all. Rows expecting a code fail loudly; rows expecting silence pass. The tell was that every failing row had a string literal in it, and the fix is to escape the quote in the probe — worth remembering because the same shape reads as a correct test.

### C record — the fifty-eighth family: a budget that is per CALL, and the reader capability it needed (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the numeric operand-list `Min`/`Max` constant budget.** The law was already measured (`OpListMathConstantLawsTest`, since 2026-07-23), so this slice is what the seam looks like when the oracle is already in hand: read the kernel's counting method, add the one reader capability it needs, wire, mirror.

**The capability is `CallOperand.constant`, and it belongs in the reader rather than in the family.** A12's `CheckOpUtils.isConstant` asks whether an operand is ONE literal with `{…}` unwrapped, and the shape parser could not answer it — `literal` has always meant "a string constant" and says nothing about a number. Adding it to the operand rather than re-tokenizing inside the family is what keeps the next constant law cheap, and its narrowness is deliberate: a temporal constant reads as false, so a counting law under-reports rather than refusing a call A12 admits.

**Two spellings had to be measured before the reader could match them.** A12's number terminal is a digit RUN, so a decimal arrives as three tokens and a signed literal as two; both count exactly like a bare digit run, measured rather than inferred from the grammar. Under-recognizing either would have been silent, and over-recognizing would have refused a call A12 admits.

**What makes this family worth having at all is the pair of admitted rows.** A literal inside arithmetic and a literal inside a NESTED call both leave the outer budget untouched, so the naive implementation — count the literals in the text — refuses two shapes A12 accepts. Counting each call's own operands is the whole family.

### C record — the fifty-seventh family: four codes from ONE operator, and the gate order that decides which one (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: `SumOfProducts`' operand PAIR.** [KF370](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf370) carries the chain. Four residue codes land together — `MVK_DIFFERENT_GROUPS`, `MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED`, `MVK_WILDCARD_ONLY_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_ALLOWED`, `MVK_REPEATABLE_LEVEL_REQUIRED` — because A12 decides all four in one operator's gate chain and a slice taking any one of them would have had to guess the order of the rest.

**The construct rule paid again, and faster: the matrix was already measured.** `SumOfProductsLawsTest` had eleven rows including a precedence grid; the work was reading `CheckFeldListenOpImpl` for the gates the grid did not cover, which took one grid of seven cells. Two of those seven were surprises, and both would have shipped a wrong class: the arity code is `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALID_2` and not the sibling spelling, and a GROUP operand is taken by the DUPLICATE gate rather than by the kind gate, which makes the kind class unreachable on that pair.

**The class whose NAME is misleading is the one a reproduction most needs.** `MVK_WILDCARD_AT_LOWEST_LEVEL_REQUIRED` sounds like "a star above the lowest level" and is never that — the reference reader answers that shape with `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` first. It is reachable only through a `Having` filter, because `isWildcardRestricted` reads a filter as restricting every level. A family written from the code name alone would have reported it on the wrong shape and stayed silent on the right one.

**The precondition that keeps three foreign classes out is one equality.** Each operand must resolve to a FIELD inside the declaring group and star exactly the repeatable levels between the two; unequal either way, A12's answer is the reference reader's and belongs to another family. Counting those levels from the DECLARING GROUP rather than from the model root is load-bearing — the kernel fixture that exercises two nested repeatables has a repeatable ROOT group, where root-relative counting silences a legal pair.

**Residue arithmetic.** Six `kernel-delegated` rows re-dispositioned across this slice and the one before it, four of them to `checker-reported`. What the two slices share is the method: one construct, one `Check…Impl`, the whole gate chain in source order, then a grid for the cells the chain does not settle by itself.

### C record — the fifty-sixth family: one `switch` in the kernel, three arms, and the two operators a name-driven family would have over-rejected (2026-09-14)

**Frozen family: the repetition wildcard's legality in a GROUP-CONDITION operand.** [KF369](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf369) carries the grid. Two codes land at once — `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` on `GroupFilled`/`GroupNotFilled` and `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` on `AllGroupsFilled`/`GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled`/`NotAllGroupsFilled` — because they are the two arms of one kernel `switch` and a slice that took only one of them would have had to guess the other.

**The accelerator generalized, and this is the record of it: read the CHECKER, not the rule.** The `n/a-structural` seam was drained by reading `CodeGenValidationDataCollector`; the same move on the parser side is to find the single `Check…Impl` that owns a grammar rule and transcribe its gate order. `CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl.check` is 40 lines and gives the whole seven-operator family: which arm each operator sits on, that three of them run a root gate first, and that two run no wildcard gate at all. The prediction accuracy that follows is the same as the collector's — 14 of 15 rows first time, the miss being a control rather than a law.

**The two ungated operators are why this needed measuring at all.** `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` and `NoGroupFilled` are spelled like the three that refuse and take the same operand list, so a family assembled from the operators' NAMES over-rejects two of seven. That is the forbidden direction, and nothing but the switch or the grid separates them.

**One prediction from the kernel's own predicate was wrong, and its row is the family's shape.** `EntityRef.hasWildCardsInGroup` exempts a FIELD operand's own repetition index, which reads as "a field one level down escapes"; measured, it does not. So the family tests the REFERENCE and never the operand's kind — a rule read straight from a predicate's body, refuted by the run.

**Three stale ledger rows fell out.** `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_G_ALLOWED` was `kernel-delegated` on a row that said "in some positions" without naming them; `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` gained a second checker position; and `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` gained the strongest of its five triggers — the reference reader answers an unstarred repeatable operand ahead of every operator gate AND ahead of the root gate, so the three refusing operators admit no spelling of a repeatable operand at all.

**What this says about the remaining residue.** The `kernel-delegated` bucket is not one seam. Its per-operator operand-TYPING rows stay out of scope, but its per-operator PATH-SHAPE rows — wildcard placement, operand co-location, semantic-index admission — are ordinary declaration-reading work, and each one has a single `Check…Impl` that hands over the whole construct. That is the next block, ranked by construct as the queue record already directs.

### C record — second pass on the standing queue, and the unit that actually pays is the CONSTRUCT (2026-09-14)

**The queue's second item is `IterationLawsTest`, and greping its eight codes first — the method correction the queue's own record carries — said not to convert it.** Every one is already discussed elsewhere, several of them heavily; a grid over that file would mostly restate. What the grep also showed is why: the codes TRAVEL, so a file's assertions are a poor index of a law's coverage.

**Re-aiming the same pass at a CONSTRUCT instead of a file paid immediately.** `RepetitionNotUnique`'s eleven classes were measured one containment assertion at a time across four files, and reading A12's own `CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl` gives the whole chain in source order. Sixteen rows later: the complete key-list order, the complete scope order, that every key gate reports ahead of every scope gate, and that the `@From` and no-`@From` branches are EXCLUSIVE rather than ordered ([KF355](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf355)). Two of the eleven — `MVK_CATEGORY_NOT_ALLOWED` and `MVK_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP` — had no measurement anywhere in this estate.

**One carrier fact cost a build and generalizes a narrower record.** No placement row was reachable at all until the two repeatable groups were INDEX-KEYED together: otherwise the rule reads a repetition its error field does not sit in, and [KF350](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf350)'s iteration gate answers on every row at once. [KF349](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf349) had recorded that requirement as a property of `MVK_NOT_IN_GROUP`; it belongs to the carrier, and three further classes need it too.

**It also settled a policy question that had been implicit across seven families.** A key-list defect pre-empts `REPETITION_SCOPE_GROUP`'s class in A12, so does the checker stay silent there? No — the scope's defect survives fixing the key, so it is independently real and reporting it is the accumulation this checker exists for. The line is the one the combination family already draws: stay silent only where the second defect exists ONLY because of the first. That distinction is now stated in both families' mirrors rather than inferred from their behaviour.

**Queue method, refined: rank by construct, not by file.** Take one construct, find the single kernel method that guards it, transcribe the chain, then grep the estate per code to drop rows already answered. The file ranking still says WHERE containment assertions cluster, which is a hint about which constructs are under-measured — not a work list.

### C record — the family residue, classified: what is left is the per-operator gap plus four dead codes (measured 2026-09-14)

**The residue was re-derived after the seventh family and then CLASSIFIED, which is the step the earlier passes skipped.** Every `MVK_*` the ledger calls `kernel-delegated` minus every code the checker reports leaves 99; scoring each by mentions in a laws test and in KERNEL-FINDINGS leaves 32 with at most one. Of those, **8 already carry a disposition in their own ledger row** — dormant, unreachable, or no firing site in 30.8.1 — which an evidence count cannot see, so the ranking sends a reader at them repeatedly. Two were chased this way before the rows were read.

**Of the 24 that remain, sixteen are per-operator operand TYPING** — `MVK_NON_NUMERIC`, `MVK_NO_DATE_CONSTANT`, `MVK_NO_STRING_OR_ENUM`, the `YES`/`NO` value pair, the tolerance and rounding kinds — which is the standing gap this plan names and does not reproduce. Three are enum/category typing, two are number-config, and the rest are one-offs already answered elsewhere. **So the relational and whole-rule seam that produced seven families is drained**, and the next family in this area needs either the per-operator catalog decision or a new seam, not another pass over this list.

**Four of the 24 could never have been families at all, and finding that out closed a class** ([KF354](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf354)): they are message LABELS a checker passes into a shared helper, so no model draws them. Two had been corrected individually, months apart, each as a surprise — the mechanism was described twice and named neither time.

**The method correction to carry forward: rank by evidence, then READ the ledger row before predicting anything.** The row carries a disposition the count cannot, exactly as the earlier record found that the laws-file ranking measures where containment assertions live rather than where knowledge is missing. Both are the same mistake — trusting a derived score over the prose that owns the fact.

### C record — the `n/a-structural` seam is DRAINED, and what it cost and paid (2026-09-14)

**Every one of the ledger's 96 `n/a-structural` rows now carries a disposition.** The last block — the enumeration's per-language labels — gave one more class, `MVK_DISPLAY_VALUES_DUPLICATE`, whose whole content is that the law holds within ONE language: the same text for one value in English and another in German is admitted ([KF368](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf368)). Everything else in that block is unreachable through one mechanism, the collector walking the declared locale SET, plus a dead extensible-enumeration arm.

**The seam's total, so a later reader can judge whether a similar one is worth opening.** Seven blocks measured over one stretch: **eight new classes** reported by the checker (five number-budget, the group repetition cap, the enum special value, the reserved root-group name, the duplicate label — across two new families and four existing ones), **~45 rows re-dispositioned as unreachable or unmeasured**, and **four ledger rows corrected** that had been under-stating work already done. No block cost more than about half an hour once the method settled.

**The method that made it cheap, in one line: read the two COLLECTORS first, and grep the checker before implementing.** The collectors decide reachability before a fixture exists; the checker grep is what stops a code that is already reported from being implemented twice, which happened once and was caught only by three existing tests going red.

**What is left in the residue is now genuinely different work** — the `pre-checked` bucket, whose codes are about a rule's own slots rather than a declaration's shape, and the standing per-operator typing gap this plan does not reproduce. Neither is another pass over this list.

### C record — the collector sweep run at scale: sixteen rows closed, one class gained, one published claim corrected (2026-09-14)

**The accelerator from the previous record was applied to the whole remaining residue in one pass, and it holds** ([KF367](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf367)). Sixteen `n/a-structural` rows are now re-dispositioned on a collector read — the four `*_H_DV` name laws and `MVK_ROW_TOO_BIG` all gate on an `oldWorld` flag only an H model sets; the two header laws are the collector's own constants; the date-format laws, `MVK_NAME_MISSING` and `MVK_TYPE_MISSING` all test members written unconditionally. **No fixture was needed for any of them**, which is the whole point: the reachability question is decided one layer above the grid.

**One class came out of it, and it is the estate's only whole-name reservation.** A ROOT group called `Id` in any case draws `MVK_ROOT_GROUP_NAME_INVALID`; `Identity`, `BinId`, a nested group and a field of that name are all admitted. It rides `ELEMENT_NAME_ALPHABET` as its first gate, since a reservation and a bad character want different fixes and the codes already differ.

**And the sweep corrected a claim this session had been acting on.** [KF362](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf362) recorded that a hand-written model draws `MVK_MAX_DIGITS_MISSING` and `MVK_PROGRAMMING_LANGUAGE_MISSING` — both unreachable, as the collector shows. `ModelHeaderSlotLawsTest` now measures what actually refuses one: a single absent `header.modelVersion`, with no class, while `id` and `modelType` may both be left out. The correction cost one small grid and removes a fixture rule that had been slowing every declaration block since.

**The method note to carry: the collector answers WHETHER, never WHICH TIER.** Three of this stretch's surprises were all of that shape — an empty enum value list, a duplicated header locale and a table member are refused at the build or reader tier rather than by the law that names them. So a collector read is enough to close a row as unreachable, and never enough to predict a verdict.

### C record — the enum-declaration block, and the accelerator that found it: read the COLLECTOR, not the rule (2026-09-14)

**Fifteen Enumeration shape laws yield one family, ten codes closed on a single member list, and two that stay honestly unmeasured** ([KF366](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf366)). `ENUM_S_VALUE_DOMAIN` reports `MVK_S_VALUE_INVALID` — the kernel basis modelkit's one documented over-strictness (API-FINDINGS 96) had only prose for, now measured on the stored carrier too, where another tool may have written the declaration.

**The accelerator is worth more than the block.** Every `n/a-structural` code comes from a rule in A12's metamodel document model, and whether a stored model can reach it is decided by ONE pair of files — `CodeGenValidationDataCollector` and `FieldDataCollector`, which build that document from a stored model. Reading the collector before writing a grid turned prediction from a coin flip into a formality: the number block ran 12-of-13 correct, the group block 12-of-12, and the enum block's surprises were all in the tier a refusal lands in rather than in whether it fires. The rule to carry: **find the collector line for the member the rule reads, and the reachability question is already answered** — a member written unconditionally kills every `FieldNotFilled` law over it, a member never written kills every `FieldFilled` gate.

**Two things the collector cannot tell you, and both bit here.** A shape may be refused ONE TIER EARLIER than the law — an empty value list is unbuildable, a `tableName` is UNREADABLE — and only a run says which tier. And a metamodel CUSTOM CONDITION is opaque: `ungueltigerXMLWert` and `ungueltigerKategorieWert` were not reached by markup or by a control character, so what they refuse is unknown and nothing reproduces them. Those two rows are recorded as *unmeasured predicate*, never as unreachable, because the difference is exactly what a later reader needs.

**Three ledger rows were STALE rather than open, and the triage that found the block found them too.** `MVK_CATEGORY_VALUE_DUPLICATE`, `MVK_PATTERN_INVALID` and `MVK_SORT_FIELD_DUPLICATE` are all reported by the checker and locked, with rows that still read as untouched — one of them cost a wrong implementation that three existing tests caught within a minute. A ledger row is the queue the next session works from, so a row that under-states its own state is not a cosmetic defect: **before implementing any code from the residue, grep the checker for it.**

### C record — the fifth declaration block: the fourteenth family, on a cap that is two caps (2026-09-14)

**`GROUP_REPETITION_COUNT` is one predicate and its whole content is that A12 states the law TWICE** — a root group is capped at 999 999 999 and a nested one at 1 999 999 999 ([KF365](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf365)). Measuring one carrier and transferring would have produced a family that refuses a legal nested group or misses an illegal root one, and nothing inside a single-carrier grid could have shown it. Its own constant because it is a claim about the declaration's SIZE rather than about a reference, which is what every other group family judges.

**Three of the block's four codes have no stored spelling, and the two index ones needed both spellings attacked.** `MVK_MAX_MISSING` cannot fire because A12's collector writes the count it derived rather than the member it read; `MVK_INDEX_FIELD_DUPLICATE` and `MVK_TOO_MANY_INDEX_FIELDS` both need a group to declare several index fields, and a stored model declares one — an array under the singular member is refused by the reader, a plural member is ignored. Fifteen ledger rows are now re-dispositioned across five blocks.

**The block also produced a third tier boundary worth encoding.** Past a signed 32-bit count A12 stops READING, so the family reports the unbuildable tier there rather than the cap's class: naming `MVK_MAX_TOO_BIG` for a model A12 never judged would be a verdict the checker invented. Both cells of that boundary are measured.

### C record — the fourth declaration block pays five codes into an existing family, and the seam's rate is now known (2026-09-14)

**The number-type block is the productive one, and the reason is that the checker already owned the construct.** `CheckFamily.NUMBER_TYPE_DIGITS` was reading the same `NumberType` body for two laws, so five more — `MVK_FRACT_DIGITS_MISSING`, `MVK_ARITY_TOO_BIG`, `MVK_MAX_LENGTH_TOO_BIG`, `MVK_MIN_LENGTH_TOO_BIG`, `MVK_MAX_LENGTH_TOO_SMALL` — landed as three predicates in that file with no new constant, no wiring, and no divergence dispositions (none of the five is named in a kit main source). A sixth, `MVK_MIN_LENGTH_BIGGER_MAX_LENGTH`, gained its THIRD carrier ([KF364](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf364)).

**So the selection rule the sort block suggested is now confirmed and can be stated as a rule: prefer a block whose construct a family already reads.** Three blocks measured against constructs the checker did not own yielded one family and eleven closed rows; this one yielded five codes in a single file. The `n/a-structural` queue should be worked construct-first rather than code-first.

**The budget had to be DERIVED, because it is a member of A12's metamodel document and not of a stored model.** `/MVK/ModelProperties/maxDigits` is 15, and the length threshold is `15 + a sign + a decimal point` — established by walking a `maxLength` of 16 across `positivesOnly` and across a declared scale rather than by reading the constant anywhere. A grid that had assumed 15 flat would have under-reported every signed number by one place.

**One code in the block is unreachable, and both links of that were attacked separately** — the arm IS reached (`extractFieldTypeName` names an amount `GeldBetrag*`), and on that arm the member `MVK_AMOUNT_INT_PART_MISSING` demands is derived by `TypeConverter.convertNumber` and therefore never absent. Measuring only the first link would have read as a reachability result and been wrong.

**The row that changed the family's shape was the SUPPRESSED fractional pair.** With both members `-1`, `maxLength` 17 is refused where a real fractional part admits it, so A12 takes its `== 0` branch against a member its collector never wrote. Both rows of that pair were needed: the over-long one is consistent with either reading, and only the boundary one discriminates. A checker that had read `-1` as "some fractional part" would have allowed one length too many on every such field.

### C record — the third declaration block: no family, no gap, and one mislabelled row that nearly inverted it (2026-09-14)

**A name is scoped to its PARENT, so two of the three "model-wide" laws are not laws of a stored model at all** ([KF363](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf363)). `Insured` under two different root groups is admitted, and so are two fields of different groups sharing a name. The two collisions A12 does refuse — the same parent, and two root groups — are build refusals carrying no class, and BOTH are already covered: `ELEMENT_NAME_UNIQUENESS` and `CK_MODEL_NOT_BUILDABLE`. The second was verified against the checker rather than inferred from the family's wording, which speaks of a group's CHILDREN while root groups are not any group's children.

**Eleven ledger rows are now re-dispositioned across three blocks, against two families.** That is the seam's measured yield, and it is a good rate rather than a poor one: a row closed on a shape fact never comes back, while a row left as "outside rulekit's authoring surface" sends the next reader at it again.

**The row that nearly made the grid say the opposite.** The "two subgroups of DIFFERENT parents" case was first written as an insertion into the same parent's element list — it measured NOT BUILDABLE, agreed with the ledger's framing, and would have shipped a model-wide law that does not exist. Nothing about the output looked wrong; the label and the patch simply disagreed. The general form of that check is cheap and belongs beside the control rule: **read what the patch does, not what the row is called.**

### C record — the second declaration block measured, and it yields NO family at all (2026-09-14)

**Four yes/no laws, four unreachable.** A `BooleanType` carrying an identical true/false pair, only one of them, or two blanks is ADMITTED in every combination ([KF362](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf362)): `TypeConverter.convertBoolean` defaults each member, so "missing" never reaches a check, and the pair laws do not fire on a stored model either. Eight ledger rows are now re-dispositioned across the two blocks — four here, four in the sort block — against two families.

**That ratio is the seam's real shape and is worth stating plainly, because it changes how to spend on it.** The `n/a-structural` bucket's rows come from A12's own metamodel document models, which validate a model-under-edit; a stored DM-JSON model reaches only some of them. So the enumeration's output is roughly one family per BLOCK and a majority of closed-forever rows — still worth working, and not at the rate the raw count of 69 suggests.

**The grid needed a POSITIVE CONTROL to mean anything, and it nearly shipped without one.** Every row was ADMITTED, which is exactly what a patch that failed to land would produce. The control — the same patch with an unknown `fieldType.type` — came back UNREADABLE, proving the bytes reach A12's deserializer. Two rounds were also lost to a hand-written model whose header omitted members that are themselves laws, so every row read NOT BUILDABLE for unrelated reasons; the fixtures build through `DocumentModelDraft` for that reason and a grid that patches one should start from a built model.

**One incidental defect, recorded rather than fixed here.** Modelkit's `BooleanSpec` and `ConfirmSpec` are empty records and the reader drops A12's custom true/false members, so a read→modify→write silently rewrites a declared encoding — the same class the Enumeration S-value work closed once already. It blocks nothing in this plan, since the codes are unreachable, so it is a `DEV-API-GAPS` row with the established fix pattern named rather than an interruption to the loop.

### C record — the thirteenth family, and the first block of the declaration seam, measured (2026-09-14)

**A group that cannot repeat cannot be sorted, and A12 says so with `MVK_INVALID_SORTINFO`.** The family is one predicate and rides the existing ordering file, because it is the same member read for a third claim — `GROUP_ORDERING_REFERENCES` resolves the entries, `GROUP_ORDERING_FIELD_TYPE` judges what they landed on, and this judges the CARRIER. Its own constant for the usual reason: a report merging them would let a group that cannot be sorted read as a group whose sort fields are wrong.

**The discriminating row is the EMPTY list, and it is the one a reader would skip.** An empty `sortFields` on the same non-repeatable carrier is ADMITTED, so the gate is on the entries and not on the member — a family keyed on the key's presence refuses every non-repeatable group that merely carries it.

**Three sibling mirrors went red, and they were right to.** Their fixtures put sort entries on non-repeatable groups, so every sort row had been carrying a second, unmeasured defect since those families landed. The carriers are now repeatable with the reason written beside them. That is the third time in six families that the signal came from outside the family's own tests, and the second time from a sibling.

**The block's other four codes are unreachable, which is the more durable half of the result.** They are all about a sort PRIORITY, and the DM-JSON entry has no such member — so the twenty minutes that produced one family also re-dispositioned four ledger rows from "outside rulekit's authoring surface" to "cannot be expressed in a stored model at all". A residue row closed on a shape fact stays closed; one closed on an evidence count comes back.

### C record — the residue enumeration re-derived over ALL THREE ledger dispositions, and what is actually left (measured 2026-09-14)

**The standing residue figure was computed over one disposition and read as the whole estate.** It enumerated `kernel-delegated` rows, found 24 live candidates, classified 16 of them as the per-operator typing gap, and concluded the area was drained. Two further buckets were never scanned, and each paid on the day it was: `erased` gave two families, `pre-checked` gave two more. The ledger's disposition vocabulary is the denominator, and the question to ask of each is the same one — **what does this disposition say about a STORED model?**

| disposition | rows | what it actually claims | reaches a stored model? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kernel-delegated` | 101 | the Kernel arbitrates; we do not reproduce | yes — the standing queue, now mostly the per-operator typing gap |
| `n/a-structural` | 96 | the typed BUILDER's shape makes it unrepresentable | **yes** — 69 unreported, and a stored DM-JSON model is not built by that builder |
| `erased` | 51 | the RENDERER never emits it | **yes** — `rule check` and `modify --condition` take raw strings |
| `pre-checked` | 46 | a builder or CLI pre-flight refuses it first | **yes** — a stored model never took the authoring path |

**So the largest remaining candidate is `n/a-structural` — 69 unreported codes in blocks that are constructs rather than lists**: the five SORT laws (a construct `GROUP_ORDERING_REFERENCES` and `GROUP_ORDERING_FIELD_TYPE` already half-own), four YES/NO type laws, six number-type laws beside `NUMBER_TYPE_DIGITS`, three name-uniqueness laws beside `ELEMENT_NAME_UNIQUENESS`, two index laws, two group-repetition laws, and a large table-backed-enum group that KERNEL-SEMANTICS §8 records as un-buildable and is genuinely out of reach.

**But the first block measured says this bucket is a queue of QUESTIONS, not of families — corrected in place before it could mislead.** These codes come from rules in A12's own METAMODEL document models, which validate a model-under-edit represented as an A12 document; whether one also constrains a stored DM-JSON model is a per-code reachability question with no shortcut. The sort block splits four-to-one: `MVK_INVALID_SORTINFO` is live and now reported, while the four PRIORITY laws cannot be expressed at all, A12's DM-JSON `Group.SortField` carrying only `field` and `order` with the position derived from the list index ([KF361](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf361)). So expect roughly one family per block rather than one per code, and expect the enumeration's value to be as much in the unreachability results as in the families — four ledger rows were re-dispositioned on a type-level basis by the same twenty minutes that produced the family.

**The method correction, which is the transferable part.** A residue is only as good as the disposition set it enumerates, and a disposition is a claim about ONE consumer — the builder, the renderer, the pre-flight. The portable checker is a FOURTH consumer that none of those three claims was written about, so every row deserves re-reading from its point of view rather than being inherited. That is the same mistake as ranking by file rather than by construct, one level up: trusting a derived score over the prose that owns the fact.

### C record — the twelfth family, and a law whose CLASS depends on the carrier four ways (2026-09-14)

**The `pre-checked` seam's largest coherent block was the enum VALUE domain, and it is four laws wearing one name.** A string constant outside an Enumeration's declared values draws `MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON` from a comparison, `..._FOR_ENUMERATION` from a scalar value list, `..._FOR_ENUMERATION_CATEGORY` from a categorized one, and `..._FOR_ENUMERATION_OR_CATEGORY` from a multi-member quantifier checking the UNION of its members' domains ([KF360](KERNEL-FINDINGS.md#kf360)). The category splits the value list and not the comparison, which neither kernel method predicts alone.

**So the family takes two carriers of four and says so in three sentences of KDoc.** The comparison and the quantifier need operand shapes the shared call reader does not reduce, and a categorized operand is not a bare entity spec — `asPath` disqualifies `->` by contract, which every operand-reading family depends on. Widening that reduction is a change to all of them and is not this family's to make; naming the scalars' class on the other carriers would be the wrong class on a correct rejection.

**One reader gap was closed rather than worked around.** `CallOperand` carried the path an operand names and nothing about a LITERAL, so no family could judge a constant at all. It now carries the string constant with A12's doubled `""` collapsed, which is where that unwrapping belongs: at the reader, once, rather than at each consumer comparing a value against a domain.

**Two source-order predictions were refuted in one day**, this one and the tenth family's. A number field in a value list was predicted to draw the loop's fall-through kind class and draws `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON` instead, a comparison gate answering ahead of the loop. The firing-site method keeps paying for grid WRITING; the day's evidence is that it buys nothing at all for grid TRUSTING.

### C record — the eleventh family, a SECOND unscanned seam, and the differential earning its keep (2026-09-14)

**`pre-checked` is the same denominator hole as `erased`, one layer over.** A pre-flight runs on the AUTHORING path — `EnumRef.category(name)`, `Rule.build()`, the CLI's `ModelPaths` — and the portable checker reads a STORED model that never took that path. 46 ledger rows carry the disposition; 23 name a code the checker does not report. Twelve of those are the per-operator operand-typing gap this plan excludes, and the rest are a real queue.

**The family taken from it is the `->` category reference, and the checker was blind to it BY CONSTRUCTION.** Its reference scan steps over the name behind the arrow deliberately — a category is an attribute, not an entity, so resolving it would report every categorized reference as undeclared. What the law needs is the PAIRING of reference and name, which no existing reader produced. Six rows, six distinct classes, three of them never measured in this estate.

**And the differential caught an over-rejection the family's own grid could not have.** A first version reported `MVK_NO_ENUMERATION` on `[format->containsYear]` in a reference-corpus model A12 builds: the field is typed by reference to a type definition, whose body preparation does not keep — so it may well BE an Enumeration declaring that category. Every row of the family's own grid used inline types, which is the set-wide constant no control inside the set can catch, and `CheckerSilenceOnCleanModelsTest` is what sat on the other side of that axis. The fix is silence on `TypeDefType`, riding a coverage gap `Check.kt` had already declared.

**That is the second time in three families that the signal came from outside the family's own tests** — the eighth was caught by a sibling family's rows, this one by the corpus sweep. Both are the same lesson in different clothes: a family's grid is built from what its author imagined, so the instruments that see past it are the ones built from somebody else's models.

### C record — the tenth family, and the run that refuted a chain read straight from source (2026-09-14)

**The construct was the `Having` clause's OWN operand, and it is one kernel method — the firing-site method paying for the third time.** `CheckEntitySpecImpl.checkFilterCondition` runs four gates in order: field, wildcard, no semantic index, then the filter BODY. The body already had a family; the operand's three gates had none, and two of their classes had no measurement anywhere in this estate.

**Transcribing the chain would have shipped a wrong class, and one row caught it.** Source order puts `checkIsField` ahead of `checkHasWildCards`, so an operand that is BOTH a group and indexed was predicted to draw `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`. A12 answers `MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED`. The row cost one build and was run only because the family had to decide a precedence — which is the general case for the method: reading the firing site makes a grid cheap to WRITE, and does not make it optional to RUN. The order a method is written in is a hypothesis about the order it answers in.

**`MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED` does not mean what its name says.** An unstarred filtered field draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` — A12's iteration gate outruns the chain — so the class is reachable only through a semantic index on the operand, and then whether or not a `*` is written, because the index spelling consumes it. A family built from the name would have reported the wildcard class on exactly the shape A12 answers with a different one.

**The star turned out to be a non-variable on both defects, which is why each is measured twice.** A group operand answers the same starred or not; an indexed operand likewise. The previous family's rule — compare the operand's star count against the repeating levels on its path — therefore does NOT transfer here, and the pair of rows is what establishes that rather than an argument from the two families' similarity.

### C record — the ninth family, and the SEAM the residue enumeration never scanned (2026-09-14)
